## CURRENT STATUS (updated after Round 44, 2026-09-15) — read this first

**Round 44 update, read this first**: built the array-layer/large-slack synthetic repro Round 43 called for (`webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/array_layer_repro.js`) — a texture array written once per call at a rotating layer, read back at that same layer only `history_size` calls later, matching `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s real access pattern exactly. First run looked like a reproduction, but that was a math error in the repro's own expected value (fixed — see Round 44 for the derivation); with the corrected expectation, **this specific pattern, in isolation, does NOT reproduce Round 43's live finding** — `average` converges to the correct plateau and holds flat for 10 full ring-buffer cycles at real-GPU scale. Round 43's live measurement itself isn't in question (measured directly in the real engine, twice, consistent numbers) — what's now suspected is that the hazard needs **dispatch parallelism** to manifest: every repro tried so far (`race_repro.js`, `native_emdawnwebgpu_repro.cpp`, this round's) uses a single invocation per dispatch, while the real shader dispatches `probe_axis_count²` concurrent threads all touching the same two textures at once. **Next step, not yet done — full implementation spec written, not yet run**: extend the repro to dispatch many concurrent invocations touching different texels of the same shared textures, to test whether concurrent same-resource traffic within one dispatch is the missing ingredient. A complete, concrete spec (exact dispatch shape, WGSL kernel, texture dimensions, pass/fail check, runtime-scale caveats) is written up in `plan-of-attack.md`'s "Round 45 spec" subsection under Tier 1 Phase 1 — implement from there directly rather than re-deriving it. See Round 44 below for the full trail.

**Round 43 update**: a narrower live diagnostic read `ivalue` (the raw per-frame light contribution, from `lightprobe_history_tex`) alongside `average` directly. Once the scene's light reached the sampled probe, `ivalue` was a rock-steady constant `512` for 100+ consecutive calls — but `average` kept growing the entire time at a rate of **~512/render_pass, matching `ivalue` almost exactly**, long after `lightprobe_history_tex`'s 30-layer ring buffer should have made `prev_value` also read back `512` and caused convergence. **This is strong quantitative evidence that `average -= prev_value` is contributing close to nothing** — i.e. `imageLoad(lightprobe_history_texture, prev_pos)` is plausibly reading back `0`/stale data instead of the true value written 30 calls earlier, not (or not only) `lightprobe_average_tex`'s own same-frame RMW as Round 33/38 originally pinned it on. This is a genuine tension with Round 39's "more slack helps" finding, since `lightprobe_history_tex`'s access pattern has the *most* slack (30 frames) of anything in this investigation. **Next step, not yet done**: extend `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/` to test this exact shape — a multi-layer array texture written once per call at a rotating layer, read back at that same layer only once every `history_size` calls — since no prior synthetic repro tested large-slack array-layer reads specifically. See Round 43 below for the full trail and numbers.

**Round 42 update**: live-instrumented the user's real scratch project (`cameraSim`, `sdfgi_enabled=true`) directly, closing out the "does the bound texture pointer ever change" question with real data instead of code reading alone: **the driver-level texture pointer for `lightprobe_average_tex` was identical across all 60 samples of a 90-second, render_pass-20-through-1200 live capture** — genuinely constant through an actual runaway session. The same capture also caught the runaway itself live: probe (0,0)'s SH0 readback grows cleanly and unboundedly (~492/render_pass, no sign of leveling off), tied to a specific texel for the first time rather than only an aggregate `max_abs_average` metric. Diagnostic was temporary and fully reverted (`git checkout --`, native rebuild confirmed clean) — no lasting engine change. **Cache/texture-identity theories are now dead across three independent lines of evidence** (Round 40/41 proxies, Round 41 code reading, this round's live confirmation). Next candidates: the still-unexplained alternating-zero pattern in this round's readback, Emscripten's same-thread event-loop timing, or a narrower live diagnostic logging `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s own `ivalue`/`prev_value`/`average` triple directly instead of inferring it from outside. See Round 42 below for the full trail.

**Round 41 update**: two more angles closed with no new leads found. (a) Code reading of `gi.cpp`'s `SDFGI::create()` + the WebGPU driver's `uniform_set_create()` structurally rules out cached-bind-group staleness for `lightprobe_average_tex`/`lightprobe_history_tex` specifically — the uniform set is built once with a direct (non-cached) `uniform_set_create()` call and reused via a fixed `RID` every frame, and WebGPU bind groups pin their resource views at creation time; there's no per-frame cache lookup in this path at all. (b) Rebuilt Round 40's repro through the *real* `emdawnwebgpu` C API path (`webgpu.h`/`wgpuQueueSubmit()` via `--use-port=emdawnwebgpu`, matching the actual driver's submission path exactly, not raw browser JS) — still **zero divergence** at real-GPU scale (8,000 calls × slack 0-16, 1,000 calls × slack 32-128). Two independent theories (Dawn hazard-tracking race; stale cached bind group) now both lack supporting evidence. Remaining candidate: Emscripten single-threaded event-loop/rAF timing (though `threads=no` in this repo's actual web builds rules out cross-thread races specifically). **Next step is now to instrument the real Godot repro directly** rather than build more proxies. See Round 41 below for the full trail.

**Round 40 update**: Round 39's "probabilistic Dawn hazard-tracking race" theory just took a real hit. A minimal, from-scratch WebGPU repro (`webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/`, no Godot involved) built the exact same-texel telescoping-accumulator pattern Round 38/39 isolated and ran it at real-GPU scale (tens of thousands of touches, slack 0-128, including the zero-slack worst case) — **zero divergence, every time**. This doesn't rule the theory out everywhere, but it's a genuine negative result against the leading hypothesis, and shifts the next move toward plan-of-attack.md's "not reproducible" branch: Godot-specific state (`FramebufferCacheRD`/`UniformSetCacheRD` staleness, Emscripten's submission-timing relative to `requestAnimationFrame`) rather than continued Dawn-bug-hunting. See Round 40 below for the full trail.

**Round 39 update**: the Round 38 "ping-pong `lightprobe_average_tex`" fix proposal (below) has been **downgraded from leading candidate to insufficient-alone**, based on a direct test. Skipping the `MODE_PROCESS` dispatch on a schedule (simulating the write-to-next-read timing a ping-pong buffer would provide, without writing any shader/ABI code) shows the runaway's growth rate drops a lot with more slack — ≈27,472/dispatch at zero slack, ≈1,506/dispatch at a 2-frame gap (what a 2-layer ping-pong provides), ≈636/dispatch at a 4-frame gap — but **does not trend to zero**, and the per-doubling reduction is shrinking (18x then 2.4x), not showing a hard cutoff. This looks more like a **probabilistic race** in WebGPU/Dawn's automatic hazard tracking for same-texture compute-to-compute read-modify-write across separate `wgpuQueueSubmit()` calls than a fixed-latency visibility delay — more slack lowers the odds of losing the race per frame but doesn't structurally prevent it. **Recommendation**: don't implement the ping-pong fix as originally scoped; instead either look for an explicit WebGPU/Dawn synchronization primitive to replace the no-op `command_pipeline_barrier`, or build a minimal Dawn/Chromium test case outside Godot to determine if this is a reportable upstream bug. All temporary diagnostic instrumentation was reverted (`git checkout --`) and a native rebuild confirmed clean before ending this round. See Round 39 below for the full trail and rate table.

**Round 38 update**: the SDFGI brightness runaway (item 11) has its first genuinely decisive evidence in 38 rounds. A live diagnostic that clears `gi.cpp`'s `lightprobe_history_tex`/`lightprobe_average_tex` every frame (breaking their intended cross-frame persistence on purpose) was run in three configurations against the user's real scratch project: clearing both (flat, no runaway), clearing only `lightprobe_average_tex` (also flat, no runaway), and clearing only `lightprobe_history_tex` (runaway reappears, growing unbounded exactly like the untouched baseline). Careful reasoning about the shader's actual accumulator math (`average -= prev_value; average += ivalue;`) shows the third result is an expected mathematical consequence of forcing `prev_value` to always be `0`, not new evidence on its own — but the **second** result is genuinely informative: it directly probes whether `lightprobe_history_tex`'s 30-frame ring-buffer reads are self-consistent (they are: the `ivalue - prev_value` residual stayed pegged at exactly `512` for the entire ~10,680-frame capture), while the **first known-bad baseline** (both textures persisting normally) still runs away only on WebGPU, never on Vulkan, with identical code. This points at `lightprobe_average_tex`'s own cross-frame read-modify-write — read and written at the *same texel every single consecutive frame*, i.e. zero slack between one frame's store and the next frame's load — as the most likely hazard site, with `lightprobe_history_tex`'s much larger 30-frame-per-layer access spacing plausibly absorbing the same underlying WebGPU/Dawn cross-submission write-visibility gap without ever showing a symptom. **Original candidate fix (see Round 39 above: shown insufficient alone)**: ping-pong/double-buffer `lightprobe_average_tex` (give it 2 layers, alternate read/write layer by frame parity, mirroring how `lightprobe_history_tex`'s own ring-buffer design already avoids same-frame reuse) — this needs a shader push-constant addition and uniform-set/texture-allocation changes, scoped but nontrivial. All temporary diagnostic instrumentation was reverted (`git checkout --`) before ending this round. See Round 38 below for the full trail.



**Round 37 correction, read this before anything else about the SDFGI runaway below**: Round 36's cascade-array-binding-collapse hypothesis has been **implemented, fully fixed, and verified correct at the WGSL level — and the brightness runaway still happens unchanged.** The apparent "confirmation" Round 36/37 initially got from a driver-level experiment (binding the *last* array element instead of the first) turned out to be a false positive: with this scratch project's `cascades.size()=4 < MAX_CASCADES=8`, the last element is always `gi.cpp`'s own white-texture padding, so that experiment had silently disabled SDFGI's SDF ray-march (constant-white "distance field" = "no obstacle anywhere") rather than fixing its indexing. A clean isolation test (`sdfgi_enabled=false`, no engine changes) confirms the runaway is still genuinely SDFGI-caused, but the actual root cause is open again — Round 36's fix is being kept (it's real and independently correct) but does not by itself resolve item 11 below. See Round 37 for the full trail.

**Milestone: SDFGI genuinely renders end-to-end on WebGPU as of Round 27, and the last known *validation-error* defect reproducing under the user's real, single-effect settings (`SsaoInterleaveShaderRD`, item 8) is fixed as of Round 28.** SDFGI: a fresh 65s capture against the user's real project (SDFGI-only) shows **zero console errors** and a real, lit scene (not a solid-black viewport) — confirmed via screenshot; sandbox-verified only, not yet confirmed by the user's own hands-on testing (see "Suggested next round priorities"). SSAO: a genuine, pre-existing upstream Godot format inconsistency (a storage image's declared `rgba8` format never matched its real `R8_UNORM` backing texture, tolerated by Vulkan/Metal/D3D12's looser validation but rejected by WebGPU) is fixed — verified with SSAO enabled alone (the user's actual repro, never previously tested standalone) and with SSAO+SSR+Glow+SDFGI all stacked, both zero errors. **However, Round 29 found a new, real, silent (zero-console-error) SDFGI bug immediately after this milestone** — see item 11 below, now the top priority: SDFGI's brightness/exposure climbs continuously over time until the scene goes solid white, resetting only when an SDFGI field change reallocates the cascades. **Round 30 confirmed this is WebGPU-specific** (native Vulkan stays pixel-stable over the same 5-minute duration) and disproved the leading hypothesis: adding the packed-path's implicit clamp to the native-storage-format write sites does not fix the visible whiteout — it only relocates where the runaway pins, proving the underlying per-frame growth is genuinely unbounded, not just missing an overflow guard. **Round 31 instrumented the actual numeric magnitude on both backends and found something starker than expected**: native Vulkan's cascade-0 irradiance is bit-exact flat for the entire ~300s/~51,300-frame capture, while WebGPU's climbs continuously and unboundedly (reaching 42000+ by t≈278s) — ruling out the quantization-damping theory too and pointing squarely at the self-illumination bounce-feedback read/write cycle in `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl` as the most likely site. **Round 32 confirmed Vulkan's `dst_light` (direct_light's own output, upstream of everything Round 31 looked at) is also bit-exact flat across its entire 2M-texel volume for ~48,780 frames** — the stability starts at the very first write stage — but hit a real WebGPU API limitation (`texture_get_data()` on a 3D texture can only ever read back depth-slice `z=0`, which happens to be outside this scene's lit region) before getting a matching WebGPU number at that same stage. **Round 33 sidestepped that limitation by instrumenting a different, genuinely backend-symmetric resource (`lightprobe_average_tex`, a plain `TEXTURE_TYPE_2D` with no storage-format branching at all) and found the actual mechanism: a hand-rolled, non-atomic rolling-average accumulation (`sdfgi_integrate.glsl` lines ~330-346) whose own explicit per-frame clamp puts a hard theoretical ceiling on its output (~983,010 for the default 30-frame history) — and WebGPU's observed value overshoots that ceiling by ~481×, meaning the accumulation's subtract-old/add-new bookkeeping itself is broken, not merely saturating.** Since this code has no storage-format branching whatsoever, **this is no longer believed to be an SDFGI/Round-21 storage-format issue** — the leading hypothesis going into Round 34 was a read-after-write/hazard-tracking gap in how the WebGPU driver (which treats `command_pipeline_barrier` as a no-op, relying on WebGPU's automatic hazard tracking) handles a storage image read-modified-and-written across *separate frames'* compute dispatches. **Round 34 audited that barrier no-op (confirmed unconditional, as documented) and ran a cheap settings-only test — disabling `sdfgi_bounce_feedback` entirely — which rules out the closed self-illumination feedback loop as the *root* cause: the runaway still happens (just slower) with zero cross-frame feedback of any kind, meaning the SH-accumulation mechanism itself is broken on plain per-frame direct light, not something specific to the feedback loop.** **Round 35 then directly tested the remaining push-constant-delivery hypothesis (does `history_index` actually reach the shader correctly?) and ruled it out too: a 30-second, 14-sample capture showed the shader-reported value cycling in perfect lockstep with the true CPU value at a constant, exact offset the whole time — the signature of a benign readback-timing artifact in the diagnostic itself, not real corruption.** **Round 36 audited the actual Tint-generated WGSL directly and found a real, high-confidence root cause: `sdf_cascades`/`light_cascades`/`aniso0_cascades`/`aniso1_cascades` (arrays of `MAX_CASCADES=8` separate textures, dynamically indexed by runtime-computed cascade numbers in both `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s shadow/occlusion ray-march and `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s GI ray-march) get silently collapsed by the existing `flatten_binding_arrays` SPIR-V pass into a single resource — and `uniform_set_create()`'s `UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE` case has an explicit, pre-existing comment acknowledging this: `// Tint flattens binding arrays to single resources. Only provide the first texture for array bindings.`** This means every cascade-array texture read in these two shaders always samples cascade 0's data, regardless of which cascade the algorithm actually intends — for a scene with `max_cascades > 1` (this scratch project has 4), any ray-march step operating on cascade 1+ silently reads the wrong (far too small/near-origin) cascade's SDF/light/anisotropy data. This is a known, already-documented limitation, not something introduced by this investigation — but it had not previously been connected to the SDFGI brightness-runaway bug. See item 11 and Round 36 below for the full writeup, scope, and candidate fix approaches (not yet attempted — this needs a scope/priority decision before any fix is written).

**What this task is**: the "actual live repro" Task 9.1/9.2's closing notes called for. Started (Round 1, below) against the in-repo `ShaderCoverage` test scene under a sandboxed/software GPU; since Round 15 it has run against **the user's own real project** (a camera/photography simulator, `~/Downloads/cameraSim_.../testing` — always test against this real project, never `webgpu_tests/test_project`, for anything user-facing) on **real consumer Vulkan hardware** (user's machine, RTX 4080 SUPER — see Round 5 — and this sandbox, which Round 15 discovered also has genuine Vulkan/GPU access, not just software rendering).

**Testing infrastructure now in place** (the single biggest structural change since this task started):
- `WEBGPU_REAL_GPU=1 webgpu_tests/scene_smoketest/run_scenes.mjs` — Playwright/Chromium with real-GPU flags (`--enable-unsafe-webgpu --enable-features=Vulkan --use-vulkan --use-angle=vulkan --use-vulkan=native --ignore-gpu-blocklist --no-sandbox`), runs the 8 in-repo `benchmark_*` scenes headlessly.
- `webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/probe_check.mjs` (untracked/scratch, small and generic, safe to keep or copy) — serves an arbitrary exported build directory over local HTTP and reports console error count + a screenshot, using the same real-GPU flags. Used throughout Rounds 15-19 to test scratch copies of the user's real project with one `Environment` setting flipped at a time.
- **Workflow established over Rounds 15-19**: rsync the user's real project into `webgpu_tests`-adjacent scratch dirs (never edit the user's actual project files directly), flip one or more `Environment`/`WorldEnvironment` flags via script, export headlessly (`godot.linuxbsd.editor.x86_64 --headless --export-release "Web"`), probe with the script above, iterate. This turned every bug in Rounds 15-19 into a same-session, no-round-trip-to-the-user repro — a major speedup over Rounds 1-14, which needed the user to manually re-test on their own machine each time.
- Caveat: this sandbox's headless Chromium is still one specific (software-adjacent but real-Vulkan) environment. The user's own real desktop Chrome remains the final source of truth and has caught things the sandbox didn't (e.g. Round 17/18's whole investigation was kicked off by the user's own annotated `errors-chrome.txt`).

**Confirmed working right now** (verified via the real-GPU pipeline, and/or the user's own live re-test) on the user's real project: base Forward+ 3D rendering (Round 16), SSR (Task 8.3 fix), SSAO, SSIL (both Round 19), Glow, Fog, Volumetric Fog (Round 19), Adjustments/BCS (Round 16), and **SDFGI (Round 27, sandbox-verified only — not yet confirmed by the user's own hands-on testing)**; every item except SDFGI is also individually confirmed by the user's own hands-on testing as of Round 18's per-feature report.

**Important methodological correction from Round 20**: the "zero console errors" claim behind the SDFGI black-screen mystery (Rounds 17-19) was an artifact of short capture windows (12-20s). Dawn's `uncaptured error` GPUValidationErrors for the specific failures below are genuinely async and don't surface until well after SDFGI's cascade resources are created — a 60-second capture is what it actually takes to see them. **Any future "silent, zero-error" finding should first be re-checked with a 60s+ capture before being treated as true data corruption** — it may just be errors that haven't arrived yet.

**Second methodological correction, from Round 23**: this scratch project's `export_presets.cfg` had `variant/extensions_support=false`, which makes Godot's web exporter read a *different* template file (`web_nothreads_release.zip`, a symlink to a separate non-`dlink_enabled` build under `builds/4.7.2.stable/templates/`) than the one CLAUDE.md's documented build command (`dlink_enabled=yes`) actually produces (`web_dlink_nothreads_release.zip`). Rebuilding and copying the dlink zip into `~/.local/share/godot/export_templates/` silently had no effect on exports from this preset — two full rebuild/test cycles showed zero change before this was caught via `md5sum` on the exported `index.side.wasm`. **Any future round should verify `md5sum` on the exported build's `index.side.wasm` against the freshly built `bin/` wasm before trusting a "no change" or "still broken" result** — it may mean the fix was never actually in the tested build. Also worth a dedicated check: whether any of Rounds 20-22's "live-verified" fixes were actually exercised through this same stale-template gap (their scratch projects may predate or postdate this preset flip; not audited).

**Findings still open, needing action or investigation — in priority order**:

1. **[FIXED in Round 27, verified — SDFGI now genuinely renders] Two distinct pre-DCE-scan blind spots in `_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()` (Round 19's fallback mechanism), surfaced one after the other as each was fixed.** First: the function never resolved through `OpTypeArray`/`OpTypeRuntimeArray`, so it silently skipped every GLSL array-of-textures uniform (`texture3D foo[N]`) — `gi.glsl`'s `aniso0_cascades[]`/`aniso1_cascades[]` (declared but genuinely unused in every `GiShaderRD` mode, so DCE always strips them and there's no live WGSL text to fall back to either) defaulted to the wrong `WGPUTextureViewDimension_2D`, not the real `3D`. Second, found only after fixing the first and re-testing: the pre-DCE fallback only ever recovered storage-image *format*/*dimension*, never *sample type* — `voxel_gi_buffer` (Round 25's own fix target, `utexture2D`/Uint) is DCE'd out of `MODE_SDFGI`'s own WGSL too, so *that* mode's BindGroupLayout defaulted its sample type to the generic `Float` fallback, disagreeing with the genuinely-Uint cached texture `_get_compatible_bind_group()` carries over from `MODE_VOXEL_GI` — and no existing rebind fixup handled a Uint-source/Float-target mismatch (only Depth→Float was covered). **Fix**: extended `_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()` to unwrap one level of `OpTypeArray`/`OpTypeRuntimeArray` before the image-type lookup, and added a new `PreDceImageInfo::sample_type` field (resolved from `OpTypeImage`'s `SampledType` operand via a new `OpTypeInt` signedness table) seeded into `wgsl_tex_sample_type` the same insert-if-absent way `wgsl_tex_dims`/`wgsl_storage_tex_format` already are. Both gaps root-caused via targeted `print_line()` instrumentation directly in the WGSL scan/BGL-construction/rebind code paths (removed before commit) — see Round 27 below for the full diagnostic trail. Commit `fca56f2adf`. Verified: all three regression suites green; fresh-build-confirmed 65s live capture against the user's real project (SDFGI-only) shows **zero console errors and a genuinely rendered scene** (screenshot-confirmed, not solid black) for the first time in this task's history; a broader SDFGI+Glow+Fog+Adjustment combined test also came back clean. See item 8 below for the one pre-existing, unrelated bug a wider SDFGI+SSR+SSAO+Glow stress combo still reproduces.

2. **[FIXED in Round 26, verified] `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD` pipelines 11, 13 (variant 17) and 14, 15 (variant 8): `GPUValidationError: No attachment was specified` at `CreateRenderPipeline`, cascading into `Render pass has no attachments` and then every subsequent `CommandBuffer`/`BindGroup` for the rest of the session.** Root cause: `SHADER_VERSION_DEPTH_PASS_WITH_SDF`'s fragment stage declares zero color outputs (writes exclusively via `imageStore`/`imageAtomicOr` side effects during SDFGI voxelization), paired unconditionally with a genuinely empty framebuffer in `_render_sdfgi()` — needing `SUPPORTS_FRAGMENT_SHADER_WITH_ONLY_SIDE_EFFECTS`, the same requirement Round 12/13 already fixed for `ClusterRenderShaderRD` but never applied here. **Fix**: on a backend lacking that capability, the shader now declares a real dummy color output (`NEEDS_DUMMY_COLOR_ATTACHMENT`), `_create_pipeline()` gives `PIPELINE_VERSION_DEPTH_PASS_WITH_SDF` a matching one-attachment blend state, `_render_sdfgi()` creates a real `RGBA8Unorm` framebuffer instead of an empty one, and a fifth site — `_mesh_compile_pipelines_for_surface()`'s SDFGI pipeline pre-warming, found only by testing — needed a matching real-format pre-warm target too (`_get_sdf_color_framebuffer_format_for_pipeline()`), since it was pre-creating this pipeline against two stale zero-color-attachment shapes that produced a *second*, different mismatch error. Commit `02230c1782`. Verified: all three regression suites green; fresh-build-confirmed 65s live capture shows zero occurrences of this error class, down from being the single largest source of console noise; native/Vulkan re-confirmed unaffected. **SDFGI still does not visibly render** — item 1 above (freshly exposed once this was fixed) is now the sole documented blocker. See Round 26 below for the full trail.

3. **[FIXED in Round 25, verified] `GiShaderRD:0:set0`/`:2:set0` binding 28 (`voxel_gi_buffer`): `None of the supported sample types (Float|UnfilterableFloat) of [Texture] match the expected sample types (Uint)`.** This was the *original* finding from this whole task's very first live run (Round 1's "three more real issues", finding 3) — never fixed, just eclipsed for 23 rounds by louder, earlier-firing errors, until Round 24 cleared enough noise for it to surface as blocking again. Root cause: `gi.glsl` declares this binding `utexture2D` (Uint sample type); `gi.cpp`'s `process_gi()` falls back to `DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_BLACK` (Float/Unorm) whenever VoxelGI is inactive (true for the user's real SDFGI-only project) — Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 don't validate sample-type match at bind time so this was silently tolerated there, WebGPU does and rejected it. **Fix**: swapped to `DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_2D_UINT` — an existing engine default built for exactly this, previously with zero callers anywhere in the codebase. Shared engine code (`gi.cpp:4256`), not WebGPU-specific. Commit `8c36d16ae7`. Verified: all three regression suites green; fresh-build-confirmed 65s live capture shows this error signature completely gone. **SDFGI still does not visibly render** (confirmed via screenshot — UI overlay renders, 3D viewport solid black) — item 1 above is now the sole documented blocker.

4. **[FIXED in Round 24, verified] `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD:1:set0`: `Number of entries (14) did not match the expected number of entries (15) for [BindGroupLayoutInternal]`.** Root cause: the BindGroupLayout itself was always correct (confirmed via targeted diagnostics — exactly 15 entries, matching Dawn's own "expected" count) — the bug was in `_get_compatible_bind_group()`, the function that adapts one `ShaderRD` mode variant's cached `WGUniformSet` to a *different*, layout-compatible mode's BindGroupLayout (Godot's RD layer calls `uniform_set_create()` once and reuses the result across every mode of a `ShaderRD` that shares the same declared uniform interface). Two mode variants can disagree on which bindings are genuinely read_write in their own DCE'd WGSL — it depends on which `#ifdef`-gated code paths that mode's entry point actually reaches, not the binding number alone. `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD:0` never needed a read_write-storage shadow for `lightprobe_texture_data` (binding 16), but `:1` did; the existing filter logic correctly dropped a *stale* shadow entry the target didn't want, but had no matching logic to *add* a shadow entry the target needed that the source never built — silently producing one fewer entry than expected. **Fix**: extracted the shadow-texture/view-creation logic (previously inline in `uniform_set_create()`) into a shared helper, `_create_rw_shadow_bind_entry()`, and call it from `_get_compatible_bind_group()` for any target-required split binding missing from the filtered entry list, sourced from the uniform set's existing `bound_textures` map (the same physical resource is bound at the same GLSL binding number across every mode variant). Commit `f102b9b0fe`. Verified: all three regression suites green; a fresh 65s live capture against the user's real project shows this exact error signature completely gone. **SDFGI still does not visibly render** — items 1-3 above (freshly exposed once this was fixed) are what's standing in the way now.

5. **[FIXED in Round 23, verified] `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD`'s fragment stage (legacy spec-constant path, `specmod#49`), binding 54: `The layout's binding access (StorageTextureAccess::WriteOnly) isn't compatible with the shader's binding access (StorageTextureAccess::ReadWrite)`, plus a follow-on `Entry point's stage (Fragment) is not in the binding visibility in the layout (Compute)`.** Root cause (Round 22): `scene_forward_clustered_inc.glsl:421`'s `geom_facing_grid` (GLSL binding 27, a genuinely read_write `uimage3D` used during SDFGI/VoxelGI voxelization) goes through `_create_module_with_spec_constants()`, which never applied the primary path's read_write-storage-split rewrite to its own WGSL text. **Fix (Round 23)**: `_create_module_with_spec_constants()` now takes `shader->rw_storage_splits` as a parameter and applies the split only to bindings already present in that map (using its exact recorded shadow binding number), rather than re-detecting independently — this avoids Round 22's failure mode, where blind re-detection could discover a different binding set than the primary module if per-specialization DCE diverges. This alone surfaced a second, related bug: the shadow-companion BGL entry's `visibility` was hardcoded to `WGPUShaderStage_Compute` (correct for every prior read_write-split binding, all compute-only; wrong for this fragment-stage one) — fixed by reusing the existing `resolve_stage_visibility()` lookup, keyed on the write-side binding, instead of hardcoding a stage. Both error signatures verified completely gone from a fresh 65s capture. **SDFGI still does not visibly render** — item 1 above (freshly exposed once this was fixed) is now what's standing in the way. See Round 23 below for the full trail, including the export-template gap that nearly hid this fix's own effect.

6. **[FIXED in Round 22, verified] `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD`'s array-layer-count bug (formerly filed as two separate findings, items 1 and 2) — `uniform_set_create()`'s read_write-storage shadow-copy texture used `orig_tex->depth` (always 1 for a 2D-array texture) instead of `orig_tex->layers` when building its `WGPUTextureDescriptor`, silently truncating every such shadow texture to 1 array layer.** Hit directly by SDFGI's `lightprobe_history_tex` (a 30-layer 2D array, read_write via `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`): the shadow got created with 1 layer, its own 30-layer view request failed validation, and the resulting poisoned bind group is what Round 20 had separately filed as a second, seemingly-unrelated `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` binding-18 format mismatch — confirmed the same cascade, not an independent pre-DCE scan gap. Fixed by mirroring `texture_create()`'s own dimension-aware `depthOrArrayLayers` selection. Commit `6509c07338`. Verified live: both error signatures completely gone from a fresh 60s capture; regression suites green.

7. **[FIXED in Round 21, verified] SDFGI's packed-format texture-reinterpretation trick (Godot's `shareable_formats`/`format_override` mechanism) had no working equivalent on WebGPU — this was the original root cause of the "SDFGI black screen" mystery (Rounds 17-20).** Fixed via a new `RD::SUPPORTS_SHAREABLE_TEXTURE_FORMATS` driver trait (true on Vulkan/Metal/D3D12, false on WebGPU) that gates `gi.cpp`'s three affected textures (`lightprobe_data`/`lightprobe_texture`, `cascade.light_data`/`light_tex`, `occlusion_data`/`occlusion_texture`) between the original packed-uint-plus-aliased-view approach and, on WebGPU, storing already-decoded values directly in a natively-supported format (`RGBA16_SFLOAT` for light/lightprobe, `RGBA8_UNORM` for occlusion) with no aliasing at all. See Round 21 below for the full implementation and verification trail. **The scene still does not visibly render on WebGPU** — item 1 above is what's left standing between here and that.

8. **[FIXED in Round 28, verified] `SsaoInterleaveShaderRD`'s `dest_image` binding (set0/binding0) got a real `R8Unorm` texture where the shader layout correctly expects `RGBA8Unorm`, spamming the console — reproduced on the user's real hardware with SSAO enabled alone.** First seen in Round 17 (multi-effect stacking), wrongly believed through Round 27 to never reproduce with any single effect enabled alone — that belief was never actually tested against real hardware in isolation. Root cause was simpler than the "engine picks the wrong texture for some `downsample_mode`" theory every prior round assumed: `ssao_interleave.glsl:27` (upstream Godot, unmodified by this fork) declares `dest_image` as `layout(rgba8, ...)`, but `ss_effects.cpp:1124` has always allocated its real backing texture (`RB_SCOPE_SSAO`/`RB_FINAL`) as single-channel `R8_UNORM` — a genuine, pre-existing upstream inconsistency, not a per-settings texture-selection bug and not a WebGPU-specific gap. Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 tolerate a storage image's declared format not matching its real resource; WebGPU's stricter bind-time validation does not. **Fix**: changed `ss_effects.cpp:1124` to allocate `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_UNORM` instead, matching the shader's own long-standing declaration — safe because every write broadcasts one scalar to all 4 channels and every read only ever uses `.r`. Applied unconditionally (shared code, not gated behind a driver trait), since this makes the real resource correct on every backend, not just WebGPU. Commit `9774f2a1d6`. Verified: all three regression suites green; two fresh 60s live captures — SSAO alone (the user's actual repro) and SSAO+SSR+Glow+SDFGI stacked (the exact combo Round 27 found this bug in) — both zero console errors. See Round 28 below for the full trail.

9. **[LOW, cosmetic/perf] Canvas configured with a non-preferred format.** Startup warning: `WebGPU canvas configured with a different format than is preferred by this device ("rgba8unorm")` — Dawn does an extra copy every frame to compensate. Flagged by the user's own "performance gains to be made" observation (Round 18) but not investigated. **Next step**: compare `navigator.gpu.getPreferredCanvasFormat()` against whatever this driver requests in `context.configure()` (`rendering_context_driver_webgpu.cpp`) and match them if the preferred format is usable.

10. **[RESOLVED as a side effect of Round 21] One-time `WebGPU: Unsupported DataFormat 1` warning** (`DATA_FORMAT_R4G4B4A4_UNORM_PACK16`, fired once during SDFGI startup). `_data_format_to_wgpu()` has no mapping for this obscure packed format — no longer reachable on WebGPU now that `occlusion_texture` no longer requests it. Not independently re-verified as gone from a fresh capture, but no code path can reach it anymore.

11. **[HIGH, was NOT ROOT-CAUSED — now NOT REPRODUCING under the `emsdk-upgrade` toolchain, see Round 40] SDFGI's brightness/exposure climbs continuously over time with SDFGI enabled, until the 3D scene goes solid white — with zero console errors.** Reported directly by the user on their real machine, immediately after Round 27/28's milestone. Resets back to normal whenever any SDFGI field is changed (expected: a field change reallocates `gi.cpp`'s SDFGI cascades and clears the history/light textures — see `SDFGI::create()`), then climbs toward white again. This is a pure visual/algorithmic runaway, not a validation error.

    **Round 40 update (2026-09-18, on the `emsdk-upgrade` branch — emsdk 4.0.11→6.0.9, Dawn/Tint `db49a549...`→`b975919d...`)**: two independent re-checks, both clean. (1) The isolated minimal repro this doc's `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/run_repro.mjs` was purpose-built for (Round 38/39's leading hypothesis — a same-texel cross-submission write-visibility gap, the exact pattern `lightprobe_average_tex` hits) was re-run (`WEBGPU_REAL_GPU=1 node run_repro.mjs`, 500 calls × 7 slack values 0-32, same RTX 4080 SUPER): **zero divergence at every slack value** (`expected == actual` in all 7 rows) — previously this was the mechanism Rounds 33-39 converged on as the most likely root cause. (2) A full, real, un-synthetic re-run of Round 30's own methodology against a fresh scratch copy of the user's real project (`sdfgi_enabled=true` plus `ssr_enabled`/`ssao_enabled`/`ssil_enabled`/`glow_enabled`/`fog_enabled`/`volumetric_fog_enabled` all also stacked on, matching Task 9.5's "everything stacked" combo testing pattern), export freshness checksum-verified, 330-second real-GPU Playwright screenshot capture at 30s intervals (t=30 through t=330, i.e. *longer* than Round 30's own 300s capture and past Round 31's t≈278s divergence-magnitude checkpoint): **pixel-diffed the t=30 and t=330 frames directly (not just eyeballed) — mean channel diff 0.046/255, max 55/255 across only 1256 of 3,515,000 pixels (0.036%), consistent with ordinary temporal-AA/dithering noise, not a systematic brightness climb.** Zero console errors throughout. **Not yet done**: this is strong, but not 100%-conclusive, evidence — it's one scratch project/cascade configuration, not a sweep of every previously-affected setting combination, and "no divergence in the isolated repro + no visible drift in one real capture" is corroborating, not a formal root-cause confirmation that the underlying Dawn/browser-side behavior actually changed (note: `sdfgi_race_repro`'s browser-side WebGPU implementation comes from whatever Chrome is installed on the test machine, not from this repo's own emsdk bump — the vendored Tint/emsdk changes only affect the WASM-side SPIR-V→WGSL translation, not the browser's own Dawn implementation, so this result reflects the current browser version, which may itself have changed independently of this upgrade at some unknown prior point). Recommend the user's own hands-on confirmation over a longer real session before fully closing this item, and re-running this same check is cheap enough to repeat before any future toolchain bump too.

    **Round 37 update**: the cascade-array-binding-collapse bug Round 36 found (below) is now genuinely fixed and WGSL-verified correct — and **the runaway is unaffected, still reproduces identically**. That rules out Round 36's hypothesis as an explanation for *this* item. Rounds 33-35's own findings are the last still-standing, unrefuted leads: a hand-rolled non-atomic SH rolling-average accumulation (`sdfgi_integrate.glsl` ~L330-346) that overshoots its own hard theoretical ceiling by ~481× on WebGPU while staying bit-exact flat on Vulkan (Round 33), confirmed to diverge even with zero cross-frame bounce feedback (Round 34) and with push-constant delivery independently verified correct (Round 35).

    **Round 38 update, read this next — decisive progress, root cause narrowed to one specific texture's cross-frame access pattern**: a targeted diagnostic (forcibly clearing the accumulation's persisted textures every frame via `RD::texture_clear()`, in three different combinations, `gi.cpp`'s `update_probes()`) found that clearing `lightprobe_average_tex` alone (leaving `lightprobe_history_tex` as real, normally-persisting ring-buffer state) makes the runaway disappear — and, unlike the other two clearing configurations tried, this result survives scrutiny of the shader's own accumulator math (it doesn't just mechanically disable the ring-buffer's subtract step, since `lightprobe_history_tex` is left untouched and its reads must still be self-consistent for the flat result to hold — see Round 38 below for the full reasoning). The leading theory is now that `lightprobe_average_tex`'s own cross-frame read-modify-write — read and written at the *same texel every consecutive frame*, unlike `lightprobe_history_tex`'s 30-frame-per-layer ring buffer — is where a WebGPU/Dawn cross-submission write-visibility gap actually surfaces, with `lightprobe_history_tex`'s much wider natural spacing between a layer's write and its next read plausibly absorbing the same latency without incident. **Proposed next step, not yet implemented**: ping-pong/double-buffer `lightprobe_average_tex` itself (2 layers, alternate by frame parity) to give it the same kind of write/read slack `lightprobe_history_tex` already has by construction. This is the leading, but still unconfirmed-by-actual-fix, root cause.

    **Confirmation-test methodology lesson worth internalizing**: a "does the symptom go away" test is only as good as its ability to distinguish *fixed* from *disabled*. Binding the last of N array elements looked like a targeted fix but was actually feeding the shader inert padding data for a scene with fewer real cascades than `MAX_CASCADES` — always sanity-check what a quick experimental patch is *actually* making the shader read before treating a clean capture as proof of a root cause, especially when the patch's effect depends on array/padding sizes that weren't part of the original hypothesis.

    **Round 30 conclusively established scope**: built a fresh SDFGI-only scratch copy of the user's real project (`sdfgi_enabled=true` added to `main.tscn`'s `Environment`, everything else as-shipped) and ran matched 300-second captures — a native Vulkan editor `--write-movie` PNG-sequence capture (`--fixed-fps 60 --disable-vsync`, decoupling sim-time from wall-clock) and a WebGPU Playwright capture (`probe_brightness_trend_r29.mjs`, 15 shots at 20s intervals) — against the *identical* settings and duration. **Native Vulkan is pixel-stable from t=0 to t=300s, zero errors.** **WebGPU is already fully blown to solid white by t=100s and stays there through t=300s, zero console errors** — confirming this is genuinely WebGPU-specific, not an upstream SDFGI algorithm/tuning issue, and squarely in `drivers/webgpu/` scope.

    **The leading hypothesis (missing clamp on the native-storage-format write path) was tested and found insufficient.** Added `clamp(..., 0.0, 65408.0)` — matching the packed RGBE9995 path's own implicit ceiling — to all four native-format `imageStore` write sites: `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s two `dst_light` writes (`positioni` and `neighbour_pos`, both under `#ifdef SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT`) and `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `irradiance`/`radiance` writes to `lightprobe_texture_data`. Verified via checksummed rebuild+re-export that this fix genuinely reached the runtime (see build-trap notes below) — **the scene still went solid white by t=100s, identical to before.** A follow-up diagnostic (temporarily lowering the clamp ceiling to `4.0` instead of `65408.0`, same rebuild/verify/capture cycle) showed the scene progressively brightens and pins at whatever ceiling is chosen rather than converging to a stable value below it — proving the underlying per-frame energy growth is **genuinely unbounded**, not merely missing an overflow guard. 65408.0 is astronomically bright (it's RGBE9995's encoding-overflow limit, not a visually-meaningful bound), so a clamp at that value prevents float overflow/UB but is still far too high to prevent visual whiteout once the loop climbs toward it. **The clamp was kept at 65408.0 as a legitimate defensive fix (real values should never need to exceed the packed format's own representable range, and clamping prevents undefined behavior if they ever do) but is explicitly not a fix for the reported bug** — documenting this to save the next round from re-treading the same hypothesis.

    **Round 31 instrumented and disproved the quantization-damping hypothesis too — the actual divergence is far starker than "less damping."** Added temporary `print_line` instrumentation to `GI::SDFGI::store_probes()` (`gi.cpp`, reverted after use — not committed): every ~60 frames, reads back cascade 0's `lightprobe_data` (layer 0, irradiance plane) via `RD::texture_get_data()`, decodes every texel (native RGBA16F via `Math::half_to_float`, packed via a standard RGB9E5 shared-exponent decode — verified bit-for-bit against `rgbe_encode()`'s own encode formula in `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`), and prints the max magnitude found. Ran matched ~300-real-second captures on both backends against the same SDFGI-only scratch project (`sdfgi_bounce_feedback` at its Environment-class default of `0.5`, so the self-illumination feedback path is active): **native Vulkan's max irradiance is bit-exact flat at `0.79296875` across all 855 samples spanning ~51,300 frames — it converges within the first ~60 frames and never moves again, not even by one quantization step.** **WebGPU's climbs continuously the entire capture**: `1.51` (frame 120) → roughly-multiplicative growth through frame ~2000 → transitions to **linear** growth (~+2.97/frame) from roughly frame 3000 onward, reaching `42080` by frame 16680 (~t=278s) with no sign of leveling off.

    This flat-vs-unbounded dichotomy — not merely "faster" or "slower" growth — rules out the quantization-damping theory outright: a system that's simply *less damped* would still show some slow drift over 51,300 frames, not perfect bit-for-bit stasis. It also weakens the storage-precision angle generally: WebGPU's native RGBA16F actually has independently-normalized 10-bit mantissas per channel (arguably *more* precision than the packed path's single 9-bit shared-exponent mantissa across all three channels), so "more precision = more divergence" is backwards from what imprecision-as-damping would predict.

    **Refined, much more specific next hypothesis**: `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s `MODE_PROCESS_DYNAMIC` block (`params.bounce_feedback > 0.001`, lines ~223-296) samples back the *previous frame's* stored `lightprobe_texture` (a `sampler2DArray` bilinear read, `textureLod(sampler2DArray(lightprobe_texture, linear_sampler), pos_uvw, 0.0).rgb`) and mixes a fraction of it into this frame's `light_total` before storing to `dst_light` — a genuine closed-loop, multi-frame self-illumination feedback path. `dst_light` (direct_light's output) feeds `lightprobe_average_texture`'s SH accumulation in `update_probes()`'s `MODE_PROCESS` pass (format `R32G32B32A32_SINT`, identical on both backends, no `SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` branching there), which `store_probes()`'s `MODE_STORE` then reads back to recompute `irradiance` fresh each call (also backend-agnostic) before writing to `lightprobe_data` — meaning **the only backend-diverging step in this entire loop is the packed-vs-native format of `dst_light`/`lightprobe_texture` itself**, exactly where Round 21's storage-format substitution landed. A scale or read/write mismatch specific to that one substitution, compounding through this feedback loop frame over frame, would produce exactly the observed pattern (early roughly-multiplicative growth consistent with a per-cycle gain slightly > 1, later flattening toward linear as some other term saturates) — while Vulkan's identical loop, going through the same code paths but via the packed format's `texture_create_shared()` reinterpreted view, stays a stable fixed point. **Concrete next step**: instrument (or re-add the same temporary readback pattern to) `cascade.light_data` itself — not just the final `lightprobe_data` output — immediately after `update_light()`'s dispatch on both backends, to see whether the *per-voxel* `dst_light` value is already diverging before it ever reaches the probe/SH accumulation stage, which would confirm the bounce-feedback read/write cycle in `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl` as the exact site rather than anything downstream in `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`.

    **Two independent SCons build-staleness traps found and worth flagging for any future shader-source-only change**: (1) `drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompiled.gen.h` (the ahead-of-time SPIR-V→WGSL precompile cache, built via `env.Run(wgsl_precompile.build_wgsl_precompiled)` in `drivers/webgpu/SCsub`) declares only `wgsl_precompile.py` itself as a dependency, not the actual `.glsl` shader sources it precompiles — editing a `.glsl` file never triggers its regeneration; delete it manually to force a rebuild when testing a shader change that might be in its `SHADER_REGISTRY` (though for *this* bug it turned out to be moot: `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s registry entry never defines `MODE_PROCESS_DYNAMIC`, so the exact runtime variant isn't in the precompiled table at all and always falls back to live Tint translation — the fix reached the runtime correctly via `gi.cpp`'s normal recompile in every build here). (2) Deleting/forcing a regenerate of a generated header does not reliably cascade to recompiling+relinking everything that includes it — `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` (`#include "wgsl_precompiled.gen.h"`) needed its `.o` deleted by hand, and even after that recompiled cleanly, `bin/obj/drivers/libdrivers.*.a` and the final `.wasm`/`.zip` outputs stayed stale until *those* were also deleted by hand and a full relink forced — the same "stale static library/output never re-archived" class of bug already documented elsewhere in this doc (search "three-level cascading SCons staleness"). **Always checksum-verify (`md5sum`) the exported `.wasm` against the freshly-built `bin/` output before trusting any capture result** — this cost significant time to catch (a "fixed" build silently kept testing pre-fix behavior three times over before the pattern was found).

**Suggested next round priorities, in order**: (1) item 11 — decide on and implement a fix for the cascade-array-texture binding collapse (Round 36) — this is the current leading root-cause candidate for the SDFGI brightness runaway; see Round 36 for two candidate approaches (per-cascade named bindings with shader-side branching, vs. teaching `flatten_binding_arrays`/the driver to genuinely split N-element binding arrays) and their tradeoffs, (2) get the user's own real hands-on confirmation that SDFGI's *rendering* (independent of the brightness bug) and SSAO's fix both hold on their actual machine (Round 27/28/30/31/32/33/34/35/36's verification are all sandbox-only), (3) decide whether to formalize the Rounds 15-19 scratch-copy-and-probe workflow into a proper `webgpu_tests/` regression suite — and while doing so, audit/fix the `extensions_support` export-preset gap Round 23 found so future scratch projects don't silently test a stale template, (4) item 9 (canvas preferred-format warning) as time allows.

**Below**: the full round-by-round investigation log, in chronological order, starting from Task 9.5's original "first live run" (effectively Round 1) through Round 19. Kept in full for the detailed root-cause trail behind each fix — the summary above is the "what do I do next" version, this is the "how did we get here / why is the code the way it is" version.

This is the "actual live repro" Task 9.1/9.2's closing notes called for — exported `webgpu_tests/test_project` (the `ShaderCoverage` scene: lights, decals, reflection probes, VoxelGI, fog, particles, etc.) with a real web debug template build, served it locally, and drove it with headless Chrome over raw CDP (`Runtime.consoleAPICalled`/`Log.entryAdded`/`Runtime.exceptionThrown`, `Page.navigate` issued only after the CDP session is attached and `Runtime.enable`/`Log.enable` are sent — navigating via a chrome command-line URL argument instead loses the first several console lines to a race, a real mistake made and caught partway through this task).

**Getting Forward+ to actually engage at all took two fixes, neither of them WebGPU-driver bugs**:
1. `webgpu_tests/test_project/project.godot` only set the base `renderer/rendering_method="forward_plus"` — Godot's web export resolves `renderer/rendering_method.web` specifically (`main.cpp:2653`, defaults to `"gl_compatibility"` for backward compat), which the project didn't set, so the export silently ran Mobile regardless of the base key. Added `renderer/rendering_method.web="forward_plus"` to the test project (kept — this is a real project-configuration fix for the test project itself, not a temporary hack).
2. Even with that set, the engine **still** auto-downgraded to Mobile: `RendererCompositorRD`'s constructor (`renderer_compositor_rd.cpp:375`, shared engine code, not WebGPU-specific at all) requires `RD::LIMIT_MAX_TEXTURES_PER_SHADER_STAGE >= 48` for Clustered, silently substituting Mobile otherwise (`WARN_PRINT_ONCE("Platform supports less than 48 textures per stage...")`). This environment's adapter (`--use-angle=vulkan`, headless Chrome) reports `maxSampledTexturesPerShaderStage: 16` — the WebGPU spec's guaranteed *minimum*, not a realistic value for actual hardware — confirmed via a direct `adapter.limits` query. **This is a genuine, correct hardware-capability gate, identical for Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 on equally limited hardware — not a bug, and not fixed.** To get past it for testing purposes *only*, temporarily short-circuited the check (`textures_per_stage < 48 && false`, tagged `T9LIVE1`), rebuilt, tested, then **reverted before doing anything else** (confirmed via `git diff` showing zero changes to this file at the end of the task). A real deployment on this same hardware would correctly and silently get Mobile, which is the right behavior — this bypass exists only so this session could see further into the Clustered code path than this specific sandboxed environment's virtual GPU would otherwise allow.

**Bug found and fixed**: `cluster_render.glsl`'s fragment stage failed with `Error while parsing WGSL: atomic variables in 'storage' address space must have 'read_write' access mode` on `@group(0u) @binding(6u) var<storage, read> cluster_render`. Root-caused by dumping the actual live WGSL text right before it's handed to Dawn (temporary `T9LIVE2` instrumentation in `_translate_spirv_to_wgsl()`, removed after) and confirming Tint's own raw conversion output was correct (`read_write`) — meaning something *downstream* of Tint was corrupting it. Found it: `shader_create_from_container()` (and its duplicate in the legacy spec-constant re-conversion path) has an existing rule — *"WebGPU restriction: Storage buffers with read_write access cannot be used in vertex shaders"* — that blanket-demotes every `var<storage, read_write>` to `var<storage, read>` for **both** vertex *and* fragment stages. The restriction is real, but it's vertex-only: WebGPU forbids a writable storage buffer's visibility from including the vertex stage at all (per-invocation write-ordering/duplication hazards), but fragment-stage read_write storage (order-independent transparency, atomics-based light clustering — exactly this shader) is completely standard and always has been. No prior shader in this driver's history had ever exercised genuine read_write storage access from a fragment stage (only compute), so this over-broad demotion was silently wrong from the day it was written and simply never had a test case to catch it. **Fix**: narrowed both occurrences (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`, `shader_create_from_container()` and `_create_module_with_spec_constants()`) from `(VERTEX || FRAGMENT)` to `VERTEX` only. Confirmed via a second live run: this specific error is completely gone from the console.

**Three more real issues surfaced, not yet investigated or fixed** (the 12-second capture window this task used ran out before the scene finished loading, so this list is not necessarily complete):
1. **`volumetric_fog.glsl`'s known Tint crash (Task 8.3) is now confirmed to be a live, engine-halting abort, not just a precompile-time failure count.** `thirdparty/tint/.../parser.cc:4467 internal compiler error: TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED unhandled decoration 21` (SPIR-V `Decoration Volatile`) — `volumetric_fog.glsl` is the only shader in the whole `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/` tree using GLSL's `volatile` qualifier (confirmed via `grep -rl '\bvolatile\b'`), and per this same live run's own console output, "Volumetric fog is only available when using the Forward+ renderer" — so this specific crash was structurally unreachable under Mobile and has never been live-triggered before now. Interestingly the WASM instance did **not** fully die from this `RuntimeError: unreachable` — the console log continues afterward with many more shader compiles — worth understanding why (some Promise-boundary swallowing the trap?) before assuming every `TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED` abort in this driver is equally survivable. Task 8.3 explicitly left this shader's root cause uninvestigated; this session didn't change that, just confirmed real-world impact.
2. **An early compute shader (identity not yet determined — occurs before any named `ShaderModule` appears in the console log) fails WGSL conversion**: `textureStore: no matching call to 'textureStore(texture_storage_2d<undefined, write>, vec2<i32>, vec4<f32>)'` — a storage texture whose texel format resolved to `undefined` in the generated WGSL. Not yet identified which shader this is or why the format is missing; needs its own investigation (likely a storage-image format-mapping gap somewhere in `shader_create_from_container()`'s format-remapping logic, possibly for a format this driver hasn't had to remap before).
3. **`GiShaderRD` (VoxelGI/SDFGI compute shader) fails pipeline validation repeatedly**, across many specialized-module variants (`specmod#15` through `specmod#19` — the legacy spec-constant re-conversion path, per Task 8.10): `The shader's texture sample type (TextureSampleType::Uint) isn't compatible with the layout's texture sample type (TextureSampleType::Float)` at `@group(0) @binding(28)`. A binding whose actual texture format is integer (Uint) but whose bind group layout was built expecting Float — the same general class of bug as Tasks 8.7-8.9 (BGL/actual-resource mismatches), but for a texture sample-type instead of a sampler filter-type or stage-visibility mismatch. Not investigated further.

**Not yet done**: identifying and fixing findings 2 and 3 above, re-running with a longer capture window to see if the `ShaderCoverage` scene ever reaches "Rendered N frames successfully" under Forward+ (it did not within this task's 12-second window), and — separately — real screenshot/visual confirmation once nothing is left erroring in the console (this environment's headless-Chrome screenshot capture of the WebGPU canvas has previously been noted as returning blank, per Task 7.13 — untested again this session).

### Round 2 — three more fixed, several more found (same session, continued)

Picked back up immediately to chase down Task 9.5's three open findings (the `volumetric_fog.glsl` crash, the "undefined" storage format, and `GiShaderRD`'s Uint/Float mismatch). Two are now genuinely fixed and verified; one turned out to need bigger, not-yet-attempted work; and continuing to peel back layers surfaced several *more* real, distinct issues — expected, per this whole task chain's own pattern, but worth being honest that "the other three issues" turned into a longer list.

**Fixed and verified: `volumetric_fog.glsl`'s `Volatile` decoration crash (Task 8.3, now closed for the reachable case).** Extended the existing `strip_restrict_decoration` pass (renamed `strip_unsupported_decorations` — updated both call sites, `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` and `tint_cli/main.cpp`, plus the two fuzz harnesses in `tint_cli/fuzz/` which had also silently drifted out of sync with Task 9.1's new passes and got the same update) to also strip SPIR-V `Decoration Volatile` (21), matching GLSL's `volatile buffer`/`volatile image` qualifier on `volumetric_fog.glsl`'s froxel accumulation buffers/images — WGSL has no equivalent concept at all, and like `Restrict`, Tint's own codegen never performs the caching/reordering optimization this decoration exists to suppress, so dropping it is behaviorally safe. **Verified precisely, not just assumed**: the registry's own "default" precompiled variant (`GENERAL_DEFINES_FOG`, no extra defines) never actually reaches the volatile-decorated resources at all (confirmed via `TINT_DEBUG_DUMP_PREPROCESSED` — `eliminate_dead_resources` DCEs them away before Tint ever sees them for that variant, so that variant was never actually exercising this bug despite Task 8.3 listing it). Reconstructed the *real* runtime variant from `fog.cpp`'s actual shader-variant setup (`MODE_DENSITY` plus `DENSITY_USED`/`EMISSION_USED`/`ALBEDO_USED`, the material-feature defines that gate the atomic-accumulation code) and found **two** sub-variants: image-based atomics (`imageAtomicAdd` on `volatile uimage3D`) hits a *different*, unrelated, unfixed Tint gap (`TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED unhandled SPIR-V instruction: OpImageTexelPointer` — WGSL has no image-atomics concept at all, matching the driver's own `has_feature(SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT) → false`), while buffer-based atomics (`atomicAdd` on `volatile buffer`, selected via `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS`) converts cleanly with this fix. Confirmed in `fog.cpp` (`no_atomics` driven by `!has_feature(SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT)`) that this driver **always** selects the buffer-based path — so the image-atomics gap is real but permanently unreachable here, and this fix fully closes the crash for the path that actually runs.

**Fixed and verified: negative sampler LOD clamp validation error.** `[Godot] WebGPU uncaptured error: ... LOD clamp bounds [-1000.000000, 1000.000000] contain a negative number` — traced to `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr2.cpp:782-783` (`state.min_lod = -1000.0f; state.max_lod = 1000.0f;`, the "-1000/1000 means unclamped" idiom from AMD's reference FSR2 Vulkan/D3D12 integration, both of which tolerate negative LOD clamps even though it's meaningless — there's no negative mip level to clamp away, so -1000 and 0 are behaviorally identical everywhere). WebGPU requires `0 <= lodMinClamp <= lodMaxClamp`. Fixed defensively at the WebGPU boundary rather than touching the shared FSR2 code every backend uses: `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`'s `sampler_create()` now clamps `lodMinClamp = MAX(p_state.min_lod, 0.0f)` and `lodMaxClamp = MAX(p_state.max_lod, lodMinClamp)`.

**Fixed and verified: `GiShaderRD`'s texture sample-type mismatch (Uint vs Float).** `The shader's texture sample type (TextureSampleType::Uint) isn't compatible with the layout's texture sample type (TextureSampleType::Float)` at `@group(0) @binding(28)` — root-caused to `gi.glsl`'s `voxel_gi_buffer` (`uniform utexture2D`, a genuinely *unsigned-integer*-sampled texture) going through `shader_create_from_container()`'s `RDD::UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE` bind-group-layout case, which **unconditionally** set `entry.texture.sampleType` to `Float`/`UnfilterableFloat` based only on `is_depth`/`is_ms` — never checking the WGSL-declared component type at all, so every non-depth `UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE` binding in this driver's entire history had silently been assuming Float regardless of whether the GLSL source said `texture2D`, `itexture2D`, or `utexture2D`. Added a new WGSL-text scan (`wgsl_tex_sample_type`, populated alongside the existing `wgsl_tex_dims`/`wgsl_is_depth_texture` scan already in `shader_create_from_container()`) that reads the `texture_2d<T>`/`texture_3d<T>`/etc. component type directly (`u32`→Uint, `i32`→Sint, else Float) and threads it through all three BGL-construction sites that previously hardcoded Float: the main `UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE` case, the `UNIFORM_TYPE_SAMPLER_WITH_TEXTURE` case, and the merged push-constant-group rebuild's own copy of the latter (Task 8.8's "second, separate BGL-entry-construction path"). Confirmed via a second live run: this specific error is completely gone.

**Investigated but not fixed — needs real, separate work**: `ScreenSpaceReflectionFilterShaderRD`'s "undefined" storage format (Task 8.3's already-tracked `screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl:default:comp`, now confirmed live via a targeted fix to `_translate_spirv_to_wgsl`'s call site that logs the shader name on conversion failure — the earlier assumption that this was a successfully-converted-but-wrong WGSL text was wrong; it's actually a Tint-internal WGSL-validation failure inside `tint_wrapper_spirv_to_wgsl` itself). Root cause: `screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl`'s `dest` storage image (`layout(set = 0, binding = 1) uniform restrict writeonly image2D dest;`) has **no explicit format qualifier** — legal GLSL/SPIR-V for a write-only image (`OpTypeImage`'s format is `Unknown`, under the `StorageImageWriteWithoutFormat` capability), but WGSL's `texture_storage_2d<format, write>` has no equivalent — a concrete format is mandatory. Unlike the earlier decoration-stripping fixes, this can't be solved by dropping something: the *real* runtime-bound texture's format has to be known and injected at shader-conversion time, which needs tracing how this specific effect binds `dest` at runtime (likely always one specific format, but not yet confirmed) — genuine, scoped follow-up work, not attempted here.

**Still open, not investigated**: `Texture binding (group:0, binding:1) is TextureSampleType::Depth but used statically with a sampler (group:0, binding:0) that's SamplerBindingType::Filtering` (a depth/sampler-filtering-type mismatch reminiscent of Tasks 8.8/8.9/7.13/7.22's dummy-sampler-substitution work, but for a binding/shader combination those tasks' fixes apparently don't cover), and `The number of storage textures (5) in the Compute stage exceeds the maximum per-stage limit (4)` / `No attachment was specified` (likely downstream symptoms of the fundamental texture-count wall below rather than independent bugs, but not confirmed either way).

**On the user's question — could a ring-buffer/indirection trick work around `maxSampledTexturesPerShaderStage`?** No, not the way a push-constant ring buffer works around limited *data* space. A ring buffer helps because many draws can share one buffer *binding slot* by changing a byte *offset* into it — but WebGPU's texture/sampler binding model has no equivalent "offset into a resource" concept; each distinct texture needs its own bind-group-layout *slot*, full stop. The two real techniques that reduce slot count are (a) **texture arrays** — combine same-size/-format textures into one `texture_2d_array` binding, one slot, indexed at sample time — and (b) genuine **bindless** binding-array support, which isn't reliably available in shipping WebGPU implementations yet. Both require real shader-code changes (indexing by array layer instead of separate sampler variables), not a driver-level shim, and would need auditing every affected shared shader (SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD alone needs 20-21 sampled textures in one fragment stage in this exact scene).

**Empirically checked in a second browser, per the user's ask — and the answer changes the picture**: queried `adapter.limits` directly in Firefox 154 (wgpu, an independent Rust implementation from Dawn) in this exact sandboxed environment (no CDP available for Firefox, so this used a raw `xdotool`-read-window-title probe rather than the Chrome CDP harness — snap-confined Firefox couldn't reach `localhost` for a fetch-based report, and its own `--headless` mode failed to initialize a compositor here at all, `RenderCompositorSWGL failed mapping default framebuffer`, so this ran under Xvfb with a normal window instead). Result: **`maxSampledTexturesPerShaderStage: 64`, `maxSamplersPerShaderStage: 64`, `maxStorageTexturesPerShaderStage: 64`** — comfortably clears everything `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD` needs (20-21) and the storage-texture case above (5 vs. 4). This confirms the 16 seen in Chrome here is specifically a ANGLE/Vulkan-software-backend artifact, not a property of the sandbox itself: the *same* hardware, through a different WebGPU implementation, reports 4x higher. Two implications: (1) the "16 is unrealistic, real hardware reports far more" reasoning above holds, now with direct cross-browser evidence rather than just prior knowledge; (2) **Forward+ might be further live-testable via Firefox in this very sandbox**, without needing different hardware at all — not attempted this session (no Firefox equivalent of the Chrome CDP console-capture harness built yet; would need either Firefox's WebDriver BiDi or another mechanism to read `console.log`/errors, not just a window title).

**Follow-up requested, and real-hardware numbers came back**: handed the user a small read-only diagnostic script (`adapter.info` + `adapter.features` + the same per-stage/binding limits checked above) to paste into DevTools console on their own real machine, in both Chrome and Firefox. Real machine: NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4080 SUPER (Lovelace).

- **Chrome 153, real RTX 4080 SUPER**: `maxSampledTexturesPerShaderStage: 48` — sits *exactly* on Godot's own Clustered-vs-Mobile threshold (`renderer_compositor_rd.cpp:375` requires `>= 48`; this number is stock upstream Godot, not something this fork controls) — so Forward+ **would** actually get selected on this real machine's Chrome, unlike in this sandbox. But `maxSamplersPerShaderStage: 16` is strikingly low for a high-end desktop GPU — the same 16-per-stage floor this sandbox's software Vulkan backend reported for *textures* — worth real concern, since Task 8.7 already found the Mobile ubershader needing up to 18 samplers in one stage before a per-stage-visibility fix reduced it to ~9, and Forward+'s shader is more complex (more lighting features, LTC area lights, etc.) — a real risk that some real Forward+ shader variant could exceed 16 samplers in one stage even on capable hardware. `maxStorageTexturesPerShaderStage: 8` clears the compute-shader storage-texture failure seen in this sandbox (needed 5, sandbox Chrome only offered 4). Feature list does **not** include `shader-f16` — confirming the FSR-variant fix above (Round 3) matters on real hardware too, not just this sandbox: this driver's `has_feature(SUPPORTS_HALF_FLOAT)` is hardcoded `false` regardless of what the browser/hardware can do, so the 16-bit-int FSR1 path was always going to be dead-but-still-compiled on every real deployment, not just here. Does report `subgroups` as a real, usable feature, matching this session's earlier subgroups work.
- **Firefox 154, same real machine**: a flat `64` across almost every per-stage limit (sampled textures, samplers, storage textures, storage buffers, uniform buffers) — identical to what this sandbox's Firefox reported on completely different (virtualized) hardware. This strongly suggests Firefox's `wgpu` backend reports a fixed, conservative baseline tier rather than a real per-GPU query, at least in this configuration — useful to know when reasoning about "what a Firefox user's numbers mean" versus Chrome's, which does appear to reflect the real adapter. Firefox's list *does* include `shader-f16` (unlike Chrome here) — another reason the FSR-variant fix is a real, not hypothetical, fix: different browsers disagree on half-float support even on identical hardware, so a driver-wide hardcoded `false` (this driver's current, deliberate choice, presumably for broad compatibility) means this codepath is always dead weight needing to stay merely *compilable*, regardless of which real browser a player uses.

**Practical implication for this fork's actual audience**: the 48-sampled-texture threshold is right at the edge even for a very capable, current-generation desktop GPU in Chrome — plausibly many real users (especially anyone on integrated graphics, older GPUs, or non-Chrome/non-Firefox WebGPU implementations) will fall back to Mobile the same way this sandbox does, for the same reason (a real, not sandbox-specific, hardware/driver limit). Whether Forward+ is worth shipping at all given how close to the edge this threshold sits, versus focusing further effort on Mobile (which is what this whole fork has targeted and hardened up to this point), is a real product question worth the user's own judgment — not something this investigation can settle on its own.

### Round 3 — two more of the user's specifically-requested fixes, verified; another new crash surfaces

**Fixed and verified: the `OpIsNan`/`OpIsInf` crash.** `TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED unhandled SPIR-V instruction: OpIsNan (val = 156)` — traced to `volumetric_fog_process.glsl`'s `any(isnan(v)) || any(isinf(v))` validity checks (3 call sites: `reprojected_density`, `final_density`, `final_fog`, all `vec4`). Tint's SPIR-V reader has zero support for either `OpIsNan` or `OpIsInf` (confirmed via `grep` — neither appears anywhere in `thirdparty/tint/.../spirv/reader/parser/parser.cc`), and WGSL itself has no `isNan`/`isInf` builtins at all (removed from the spec). Unlike the `Volatile`-decoration fixes, this can't be solved by dropping something at the SPIR-V level — so, uniquely among this session's fixes, **this one edits the shared GLSL source directly** rather than staying WebGPU-only: added `any_nan_or_inf(vec4 v)` (`volumetric_fog_process.glsl`) using `any(notEqual(v, v))` for the NaN check (`v != v` is an exact, IEEE-754-guaranteed test for NaN, not an approximation — GLSL requires `notEqual()` rather than `!=` for a component-wise vector result) and `any(greaterThan(abs(v), vec4(FLT_MAX)))` for the infinity check (exact too: FLT_MAX is the largest finite float32 value by definition, so anything strictly greater must be ±infinity, and NaN comparisons are always false so this can't misfire on the case the first check already covers). This is safe to do in shared code for the same reason Task 9.1's ballot-election simplification was: it's a provably identical computation on every backend, not a behavior change — unlike e.g. the Volatile-decoration fixes, which specifically avoid touching GLSL because dropping `volatile` *would* be a (harmless but real) behavior change on native backends. Verified via the real registry variants (`MODE_DENSITY`, `MODE_FOG`) converting cleanly through `tint_convert_cli` and compiling with zero errors in real Chrome, and via a live re-test showing the crash is completely gone.

**Fixed and verified: the `textureLoad` argument-count mismatch.** Root-caused via live instrumentation (dumping the exact assembled WGSL right before `wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModule`, matched by content rather than by shader label — Task 8.10's "labels aren't unique across contexts" warning bit again: proximity-based log reading pointed at the wrong shader entirely before switching to grepping every dumped block for the literal broken call shape) to `SdfgiPreprocessShaderRD`'s variant 8: `textureLoad(src_light, pos, 0).x` (correct, 3 args) right next to `textureLoad(src_light_aniso, pos, 0, 0).x` (broken, 4 args) two lines later. Root cause: `shader_create_from_container()`'s "convert read-only storage texture to sampled + append a mip-level argument" post-processing (the mechanism from the "undefined format" investigation's sibling code path) searches for `"textureLoad(" + info.var_name` as a plain substring — and `src_light` is a literal *prefix* of `src_light_aniso`, so processing `src_light`'s own registry entry also matches inside `src_light_aniso`'s call, appending a first (spurious) mip-level argument; processing `src_light_aniso`'s own entry immediately after then correctly appends a second one, landing at 4 total. Classic prefix/substring identifier bug. **Fix**: added an identifier-boundary check right after the match (reject if the next character is alphanumeric or `_`), matching the same `_is_ident_char`-style guard already used throughout the SPIR-V preprocessing passes, just expressed against Godot's `String` here instead of raw bytes. Verified: the specific error is completely gone from a live re-test (0 occurrences, down from present in every prior capture), `shader_corpus`/`preprocessing_tests` unaffected.

**Yet another crash surfaced immediately after, not investigated**: `thirdparty/tint/.../parser.cc:745 internal compiler error: TINT_ASSERT(int_ty->width() == 32)` — a Tint-reader assertion about integer bit-width, distinct from everything else found this session. Shader identity not yet determined (would need the same live-dump-and-grep technique used for the `textureLoad` bug). Not chased further this round — this is now the fourth consecutive "fix one, reveal the next" cycle in this single session, and each one has taken a full build/export/capture round-trip to nail down; a good stopping point to consolidate rather than keep going indefinitely in one sitting.

### Round 4 — three more fixed and verified; then a genuine full-browser hang, unresolved

Continued the same cycle to chase the `TINT_ASSERT(int_ty->width() == 32)` crash from Round 3's end. Found and fixed it plus two more real issues the same way — but this round ended differently: after all of them, the live repro stopped producing *any* console output at all, and a follow-up diagnostic found the whole Chrome renderer process itself had frozen solid, not just the page.

**Fixed and verified: the `int_ty->width() == 32` crash (16-bit integers in the "dead" FSR1 high-precision variant).** Root-caused via the same "print shader name right before the call that might abort" technique (this crash aborts *inside* Tint, before any error is ever returned to check — a nullptr-check-based print like Task 9.5's earlier `textureLoad` investigation wouldn't have fired) to `FsrUpscaleShaderRD:0`. `fsr_upscale.glsl` (FSR1, `thirdparty/amd-fsr/`) sets `#define A_HALF` for its `MODE_FSR_UPSCALE_NORMAL` variant, which switches AMD's reference header (`ffx_a.h`) to 16-bit integer packing (`int16_t`/`uint16_t`) for a performance optimization — WGSL only has 32-bit integers, so Tint's SPIR-V reader's core type-conversion function hard-asserts on any other width. **This is the exact same bug shape as `cluster_render.glsl`'s ballot-election optimization from Task 9.1 and `volumetric_fog.glsl`'s image-atomics path from Round 3: a variant that is never actually selected at runtime on this driver (confirmed: `fsr.cpp` already selects `FSR_SHADER_VARIANT_FALLBACK` whenever `!has_feature(SUPPORTS_HALF_FLOAT)`, which this driver hardcodes to `false`) but still needs to *compile* without crashing, because `ShaderRD` compiles every registered mode, not just the one actually selected** — the key generalizable lesson from this whole round: "provably unreachable at runtime" is not the same as "never gets compiled," for any shader using this simpler `ShaderRD::initialize()` + `version_get_shader()` pattern without an accompanying `enable_group()`/group-gating
