mechanism (see the third fix below for the contrast). **Fix**: `fsr.cpp` now registers the FALLBACK defines under *both* the NORMAL and FALLBACK slots whenever half-float support is absent, rather than ever asking this driver to compile the 16-bit-int path at all — zero behavior change on backends that do support half-float (unchanged registration, unchanged selection), and the on-real-hardware Chrome/RTX-4080-SUPER data gathered this round (see below) confirms this matters for *every* real deployment of this driver, not just this sandbox: that machine's Chrome doesn't report `shader-f16` either, so `SUPPORTS_HALF_FLOAT` would be `false` there too, on real hardware, always.

**Fixed and verified: `scene_forward_clustered.glsl`'s missing `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` wiring — the real, live counterpart to Round 3's `volumetric_fog.glsl` finding.** Same underlying Tint gap (`TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED unhandled SPIR-V instruction: OpImageTexelPointer` — WGSL has no image-atomics concept at all), but a materially different situation: `volumetric_fog.glsl`'s `fog.cpp` already had the correct fix in place (`_get_fog_shader_group()` + `ShaderRD::enable_group()`, which — *unlike* `fsr.cpp`'s simpler pattern — genuinely does gate compilation to only the selected group; confirmed by reading `ShaderRD::enable_group()`'s implementation, which calls `_compile_version_start()` only for enabled groups), so Round 3's conclusion that the image-atomics path there is "real but permanently unreachable" holds. `scene_forward_clustered.glsl`'s `SHADER_VERSION_DEPTH_PASS_WITH_SDF` variant (`MODE_RENDER_SDF`, used for real-time SDF generation feeding SDFGI) has the identical `#ifdef NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` fallback already written in the shared GLSL (`imageStore(imageLoad(...) | bits)` instead of `imageAtomicOr(...)`) — **but `scene_shader_forward_clustered.cpp` never defines `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` at all**, unconditionally taking the image-atomics branch regardless of driver support. Unlike the FSR/fog cases, this is not dead code: `MODE_RENDER_SDF` is a real, reachable variant this driver would actually need for SDFGI to work under Forward+. **Fix**: added the same `has_feature(RD::SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT)`-conditional `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` define `fog.cpp` already uses, applied to this one variant. Verified: the crash is completely gone from a live re-test.

**Fixed and verified: missing `depth32float-stencil8` WebGPU feature request.** A plain, non-crashing `TypeError: Failed to execute 'createTexture' ... requires the 'depth32float-stencil8' feature to be enabled` — once the two crashes above stopped blocking startup, engine initialization got far enough to actually attempt creating a combined depth+stencil render target using this format, which `engine.js`'s device-creation code simply never requested as an optional feature (unlike `'subgroups'`, added in Task 9.1, following the exact same "request if the adapter supports it" pattern already used there). **Fix**: added `'depth32float-stencil8'` to the `optionalFeatures` list. Verified: the error is gone from a live re-test.

**Real hardware data arrived this round, from the user's own machine (NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4080 SUPER)**, via a small read-only diagnostic script handed to them to run in DevTools: Chrome reports `maxSampledTexturesPerShaderStage: 48` (exactly Godot's own Clustered-vs-Mobile threshold — Forward+ *would* get selected on this real machine, unlike this sandbox) but a surprisingly low `maxSamplersPerShaderStage: 16` for a current-generation desktop GPU, and no `shader-f16` feature (confirming the FSR fix above matters on real hardware, not just here). Firefox on the *same* real machine reports a flat `64` across almost every per-stage limit — identical to what this sandbox's (virtualized) Firefox reported, suggesting Firefox's `wgpu` backend reports a fixed baseline tier rather than querying real hardware, at least in this configuration. Practical implication: the 48-texture threshold is right at the edge even for capable current hardware in Chrome, and the low real sampler count is a genuine, not merely theoretical, risk for whether Forward+'s more complex shaders fit within it. Full detail above, before this Round 4 section.

**Then: a genuine hang, not yet diagnosed.** With all three fixes above in place, a live re-test produced its usual output up through `"[ShaderCoverage] Scene built. Rendering 10 frames..."` — then nothing. No error, no exception, no completion message, across repeated tries with progressively longer capture windows (25s, 60s, 90s — no change, ruling out "just slow"). A follow-up diagnostic tried to evaluate trivial JavaScript (`1+1`) over the same CDP connection after the apparent hang and the **entire WebSocket connection to Chrome's DevTools protocol died with a keepalive ping timeout** — meaning Chrome's own internal protocol-handling machinery, which normally runs independently of whatever the page's JavaScript/WASM is doing, stopped responding entirely. This points to something more severe than an async wait or a stuck promise: a synchronous computation (almost certainly inside the single-threaded WASM instance, given "single-threaded, GDExtension support" in this build's own startup banner) that is blocking the browser's main thread so completely that even Chrome's out-of-process DevTools IPC can't get scheduled — consistent with either a genuine infinite loop somewhere in a code path that only became reachable once the three crashes above stopped short-circuiting it, or catastrophically bad performance in this sandbox's software-rendered GPU path for some newly-reached operation (e.g. a large SDF/voxel-GI computation that would be fast on real hardware but pathological on a software Vulkan implementation). **Not diagnosed further this round** — this needs different tooling than console-log capture (which produces nothing during a true hang): likely bisecting by disabling pieces of the `ShaderCoverage` scene to narrow down which feature triggers it, or testing on real hardware (where the same code, if it's a performance problem rather than a true infinite loop, might simply complete in a reasonable time instead of hanging).

### Round 5 — real Chrome/RTX 4080 SUPER run resolves the "hang" mystery; four more real bugs found and fixed

The user built and ran the Forward+ template themselves, in their own real Chrome (not this sandbox), and supplied the raw DevTools console log (`errors-chrome.txt`, 2.7MB / ~40,600 lines). This immediately resolved Round 4's biggest open question: **there was no true browser freeze.** On real hardware the scene renders all 10 frames; what looked like a silent hang in this sandbox was actually a firehose of WebGPU validation errors — many per frame, for 10 frames — that this sandbox's headless/software-rendered Chrome was apparently too slow to surface over the CDP console feed before the capture window or the CDP connection itself gave out. Real hardware just powers through it (still visibly broken output, since these are real validation errors, but no freeze). Lesson: a "hang" diagnosed purely from a starved diagnostic channel (CDP over a loaded software renderer) needs corroboration from a second signal before being treated as a true freeze — this one wasn't.

Deduplicating the ~40,600 lines down to unique error *shapes* surfaced about a dozen distinct issues. Fixed four this round, prioritizing the one that looked most responsible for cascading failures:

**Fixed: render pipelines' unused color-target slots used a real format instead of a genuine "no attachment" hole — the likely single biggest contributor to broken/absent output.** Symptom: `GPUValidationError: Attachment state of [RenderPipeline "pipe#N:SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD:N"] is not compatible with [RenderPassEncoder]` — the pipeline declared 3 color targets (`RGBA16Float, RGBA8Unorm, RGBA8Unorm`), the actual render pass only had 1 (`RGBA16Float`), repeating for essentially every opaque-pass draw call. Root-caused with a background research pass through `render_forward_clustered.cpp`: Godot's async pipeline-precompilation path always reserves 3 color-attachment slots per pipeline (color, specular-or-unused, velocity-or-unused) via `_get_color_framebuffer_format_for_pipeline()`, using a default-constructed `RD::AttachmentFormat` (format `RGBA8Unorm`) for whichever slots aren't needed by a given scene — this is normal, backend-agnostic Godot behavior, not a bug. The actual render pass for a plain opaque draw (no separate-specular, no motion vectors) legitimately only has 1 real attachment; `command_begin_render_pass` (`drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp:7337`) correctly represents an unused slot as a genuine hole (`att.view = nullptr`). But `render_pipeline_create`'s color-target loop (`:8846`) only masked off writes for an unused slot (`writeMask = None`) while still giving it a concrete `RGBA8Unorm` format — Dawn's validator requires a pipeline's color-target slot presence/absence to match the render pass's attachment slot presence/absence *exactly*; a masked-but-formatted target is not the same as an absent one. Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 don't hit this because their `ATTACHMENT_UNUSED`-style references are pass-compatible regardless of what the pipeline declares there — there's no such strict rule outside WebGPU. **Fix**: for `ATTACHMENT_UNUSED` slots, set `color_targets[i].format = WGPUTextureFormat_Undefined` (Dawn's C-API sentinel for the JS API's `null` target-array entry) instead of a real format. Not yet re-verified live (needs the user's next run) but the fix directly targets the exact validation message and mechanism.

**Fixed: missing device-limit requests for storage textures and compute workgroup size.** Two more separate, non-cascading validation errors: `The number of storage textures (5) in the Compute stage exceeds the maximum per-stage limit (4)` (`SsEffectsDownsampleShaderRD`) and `The total number of workgroup invocations (512) exceeds the maximum allowed (256)` (an unlabeled compute pipeline) — both explicitly said "this adapter supports a higher limit... which can be specified in requiredLimits". `engine.js`'s device-request code already has a `limitsToMax` mechanism that requests the adapter's actual max for several limits (texture/sampler/buffer counts, etc., added across earlier tasks) but was simply missing `maxStorageTexturesPerShaderStage` and the compute-workgroup-size family (`maxComputeInvocationsPerWorkgroup`, `maxComputeWorkgroupSizeX/Y/Z`, `maxComputeWorkgroupStorageSize`). **Fix**: added all five to the existing list — this hardware already supports the higher values (1024 invocations, 8 storage textures) per the error text itself, so this is a pure device-request omission, not a hardware ceiling.

**Fixed: a standalone (non-combined) depth+stencil texture binding could select both aspects, which WebGPU forbids.** `GPUValidationError: Multiple aspects (Depth|Stencil) selected in [TextureView of Texture (..., TextureFormat::Depth32FloatStencil8)]` for `ClusterDebugShaderRD`'s binding 6. Task 7.13/8.8's existing fix for this exact problem (create an on-demand depth-only view, since a combined format's default view selects both aspects but a `texture_depth_2d` binding requires exactly one) was only ever implemented in the `UNIFORM_TYPE_SAMPLER_WITH_TEXTURE` case (combined sampler+texture pairs) — there was no equivalent in the plain standalone `UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE`/`UNIFORM_TYPE_INPUT_ATTACHMENT` case, used when a shader does `textureLoad` on a depth texture directly with no paired sampler (as `ClusterDebugShaderRD`, a compute shader, does). This gap was latent all along but only became reachable once Round 4's `depth32float-stencil8` feature request let this format actually get created and bound at all. **Fix**: mirrored the identical depth-only-view-creation branch into the standalone case.

**Fixed: a false-positive in the Task 7.13 depth-texture-reclassification heuristic.** `GPUValidationError: The shader's texture sample type (Depth) isn't compatible with the layout's texture sample type (UnfilterableFloat)` for `VoxelGiShaderRD:4` (the `MODE_DYNAMIC_LIGHTING` variant, confirmed by matching `gi.cpp`'s variant list index against `voxel_gi.glsl`). Root cause: `voxel_gi.glsl`'s `MODE_DYNAMIC_LIGHTING` block declares `layout(r32f, ...) uniform restrict image2D depth;` — an ordinary r32f *storage image* used by dynamic-GI light injection to store a plain distance/height value, not a real hardware depth attachment — which gets converted from a storage texture to a sampled `texture_2d<f32>` by the read-only-storage-to-sampled pass (`wgsl_read_storage_to_sampled`, Task 8.x), and *then* independently caught by `_reclassify_single_component_depth_textures()`'s heuristic (Task 7.13: rewrite `texture_2d<f32>` to `texture_depth_2d` when the variable name contains "depth" and it's only ever accessed one component at a time), since the heuristic had no way to know this particular `texture_2d<f32>` didn't originate from a real depth-format GLSL binding. The result: Tint declares the module's binding as `texture_depth_2d` (Depth sampleType) while the BindGroupLayout — built from the *original* r32f storage format via `_texture_sample_type_for_format()` — says UnfilterableFloat, a hard mismatch neither side can self-correct. This is a genuinely different bug shape from anything in Tasks 7.13–7.22: the naming heuristic's two independent signals (name contains "depth", single-component access) can both be satisfied by an ordinary color-format buffer that merely happens to be named that way for domain reasons. **Fix**: `_reclassify_single_component_depth_textures()` now takes the `wgsl_read_storage_to_sampled` key set (already computed earlier in the same function, before the reclassification call) and skips any candidate whose `@group/@binding` — parsed backward from its declaration via a new `_find_preceding_group_binding()` helper — is in that set, since a binding that went through the storage→sampled conversion is by construction never a real depth-format texture. The sibling call site in `_create_module_with_spec_constants()` doesn't perform this conversion itself, so it passes an empty exclusion set (no behavior change there). Not yet re-verified live against the exact `VoxelGiShaderRD:4` binding number from a from-scratch standalone SPIR-V compile (a manual `glslangValidator`-based reconstruction of this specific variant didn't reproduce binding 25 exactly — likely because it's missing some real-engine-only compilation detail not modeled in the standalone extraction script), but the fix targets a structurally real, reproducible false-positive (independently confirmed via the `MODE_DYNAMIC_LIGHTING` reconstruction: `depth` does exist, is r32f, and is genuinely at risk of this exact collision) and is safe by construction — it can only suppress a reclassification that was already wrong, never introduce a new one.

**Not yet root-caused, lower priority (don't obviously cascade into full breakage, unlike the attachment fix above)**: `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD:0`'s "shader's texture sample type (Sint) isn't compatible with the layout's texture sample type (UnfilterableFloat)" at binding 19 — investigated at length (checked `MODE_PROCESS` and `MODE_SCROLL` variants' real compiled WGSL directly; `lightprobe_history_texture`, the natural suspect, stays a proper `read_write` storage texture in both, never converted to sampled, so this isn't the same bug as the VoxelGi case above) but the exact binding/variant combination that produces a Sint-sampled (not storage) texture at binding 19 wasn't found before time ran out this round; `ResolveShaderRD`'s "Binding type in the shader (texture) doesn't match the type in the layout (sampler)"; `SsaoInterleaveShaderRD`'s storage-format mismatch (`RGBA8Unorm` layout vs `RGB10A2Unorm` shader); a 3D-texture-treated-as-2D-view dimension mismatch on a `4x4x1` texture (likely a fallback/dummy texture substitution picking the wrong dimension). None of these were investigated deeply enough this round to have confident root causes yet.

### Round 6 — Round 5 re-verified against a fresh real Chrome run; VoxelGi fix's premise corrected; two more fixed

The user rebuilt and re-ran on the same real hardware (RTX 4080 SUPER), producing a fresh `errors-chrome.txt` (~49,900 lines). Diffing unique error shapes against Round 5's log confirmed **all four Round 5 fixes hold**: the attachment-state mismatch, the storage-texture/workgroup limit errors, the "Multiple aspects" depth-stencil view error, and the `depth32float-stencil8` feature error are all completely gone, with no new error shapes introduced and both regression suites still green. Total unique error count dropped from 19 to 15. The pre-existing, out-of-scope `screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl` formatless-storage crash (Task 8.3) is unchanged, as expected.

**However, the VoxelGi fix from Round 5 did not actually work** — `VoxelGiShaderRD:4`'s "shader's texture sample type (Depth) isn't compatible with the layout's texture sample type (UnfilterableFloat)" at `@binding(25)` was still present, unchanged, in the fresh log. Root-caused properly this time by rebuilding locally with a temporary print of the exact post-processing WGSL for every `VoxelGiShaderRD` variant (removed before commit) and reproducing the live pipeline-creation error in this sandbox with the Task 9's `RendererCompositorRD` 48-texture gate temporarily bypassed (same T9LIVE1 pattern as Round 3-5, reverted after). **The real cause was different from Round 5's diagnosis**: `depth` (the r32f storage image at binding 24) is `restrict` (read_write) in the GLSL, not `readonly`, and Tint declares it `texture_storage_2d<r32float, write>` — it was never going through the `wgsl_read_storage_to_sampled` conversion Round 5's fix excluded at all. The *actual* mechanism is a completely separate pass: when the device lacks the `readonly-and-readwrite-storage-textures` feature (true for both this sandbox and, per the Round 4 real-hardware data, likely Chrome in general), a read_write storage texture gets split into the write-only texture itself plus a synthetic **shadow read companion** at `binding+1`, named `<var>_rw_in` and declared as a plain sampled texture (`texture_2d<f32>`) — so `depth` (binding 24) gained a `depth_rw_in` companion at binding 25, tracked in a *different* map (`wgsl_rw_storage_splits`, keyed by the *original* binding with the *shadow* binding as the value) that Round 5's fix never consulted. `depth_rw_in` independently satisfies both of `_reclassify_single_component_depth_textures()`'s signals (name contains "depth", read one component at a time) and got wrongly reclassified to `texture_depth_2d`, exactly reproducing the same failure shape as Round 5's (wrong) diagnosis, just via a different synthetic-binding source. **Fix**: at the reclassification call site, build a combined exclusion set from both `wgsl_read_storage_to_sampled` *and* every shadow-companion binding recorded in `wgsl_rw_storage_splits` (computed as `(original_key & group_mask) | shadow_binding`). Verified live in this sandbox (with the temporary Forward+ bypass): the `depth_rw_in`/binding-25 mismatch is completely gone from a fresh capture. This is a good general lesson for this driver: **any WGSL-text-level post-processing pass that synthesizes a new binding out of a real one (storage→sampled conversion, read_write→write+shadow-read split, and potentially others not yet audited) is a source of false positives for the name-based depth-reclassification heuristic**, and each needs its own exclusion-set entry — the fix here only covers the two known synthesis mechanisms; a third one, if one exists, would reproduce this same failure shape again.

**Fixed and verified: a 3D-dimension Godot texture bound to a shader slot expecting 2D produced an invalid view-creation call, not a graceful fallback.** `GPUValidationError: The dimension (TextureViewDimension::e2D) ... is not compatible with the dimension (TextureDimension::e3D) of [Texture ...]` — by far the most frequent single error shape in the Round 5 log (81 occurrences). The existing "fix dimension mismatch" logic (used when a shader's declared texture dimension doesn't match the real bound texture's default view dimension, e.g. Cube vs 2D) unconditionally tried to create a *new* view with the shader's expected dimension against the real texture — but WebGPU's view/texture dimension compatibility is not just "does the layout say something different," it's structurally fixed by the texture's own underlying `WGPUTextureDimension`: a 3D-dimension texture object can only ever have a 3D view, and a 2D-dimension texture object can never have a 3D view, full stop — no view-descriptor trick bridges this, unlike the existing Cube-from-insufficient-layers case which the code already handled by falling back to a dummy texture instead of attempting an impossible view. **Fix**: added the identical structural-compatibility check (mirrored in both the standalone `UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE` and combined `UNIFORM_TYPE_SAMPLER_WITH_TEXTURE` cases) — when the expected view dimension and the real texture's `WGPUTextureDimension` are in incompatible classes (3D vs non-3D), substitute `fallback_float_texture_view` (or `fallback_cube_texture_view` if Cube was expected) instead of attempting the invalid `wgpuTextureCreateView` call, exactly as already done for the Cube/insufficient-layers case. Verified live in this sandbox: the error is completely gone from a fresh capture with the fix in place. Not yet identified which specific shader/uniform triggers this (the error message alone doesn't name one, unlike the BGL-mismatch errors), but the fix is general and safe regardless — any future occurrence of a genuinely-incompatible dimension pairing (not just this specific 4x4x1 case) gets the same safe substitution instead of a hard validation error.

Rounds 1-6's accumulated fixes were committed locally (commit `27bb9dd8a3`, not pushed — see repo convention).

### Round 7 — SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD's Sint mismatch, same root cause family as Round 6's VoxelGi fix

Continued straight from Round 6's note that this was worth revisiting with the "temporary print + local live-repro" technique rather than more standalone `glslangValidator` reconstruction. That approach worked immediately, and the root cause turned out to be closely related to (but distinct from) Round 6's VoxelGi finding: the *same* underlying mechanism, missing a piece of bookkeeping the VoxelGi fix didn't need to touch.

**Fixed and verified: the read_write-storage-texture shadow-read split never recorded the shadow binding's real format, so its BGL entry always fell back to a wrong default.** `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD:0` (`MODE_PROCESS`) declares `lightprobe_history_texture` as `restrict iimage2DArray` (rgba16sint, read_write, no `readonly` qualifier) at binding 18. On a device lacking `readonly-and-readwrite-storage-textures` (this sandbox, and per Round 4's real-hardware data, Chrome in general), the read_write-storage-texture split (the same mechanism Round 6 found for VoxelGi's `depth`) synthesizes a shadow-read companion `lightprobe_history_texture_rw_in` at binding 19, correctly declared as `texture_2d_array<i32>` (Sint, matching the source format) by the split's own text-rewriting logic. The mismatch: the BGL-construction code for this shadow entry (two call sites, both already existing) looks up the shadow's format via `wgsl_storage_tex_format.has(shadow_key)` to compute its `sampleType` — but the split code that creates the shadow binding **never wrote anything into that map for the shadow's own key**, only for the write-side original binding (which isn't even the relevant one here, and separately keeps its real format correctly, since the write-only side's declaration is unmodified in that regard). The lookup silently missed, fell through to the `RGBA8Unorm` default, and produced `UnfilterableFloat` — a hard, structural mismatch against the shadow's real `Sint` declaration that no amount of luck could avoid: *every* read_write storage texture whose format isn't a 32-bit float/generic type (i.e. every uint/sint one) hitting this split would produce this same error, not just this one shader. This is why it surfaced as a completely different error (`Sint` vs `UnfilterableFloat`, at a numerically-coincidental binding) from Round 6's VoxelGi case (`Depth` vs `UnfilterableFloat`) despite sharing the same split mechanism: Round 6's `depth` variable is r32float, whose default-fallback format (`RGBA8Unorm`, an unfiltered-float type) happens to *also* size right for the depth-reclassification heuristic's separate misfire, while `lightprobe_history_texture`'s int format has no relationship to that heuristic at all — this is a plain missing-bookkeeping bug in the split itself, not a reclassification false-positive. **Fix**: extracted the format-string-to-`WGPUTextureFormat` mapping (previously duplicated inline at the read-only-storage-to-sampled conversion's call site) into a shared `_wgsl_storage_format_string_to_wgpu()` helper, and call it at the read_write-split's own call site to populate `wgsl_storage_tex_format[shadow_key]` at the same point the split records `wgsl_rw_storage_splits[key] = shadow_bnd`, right when `info.fmt` (the real GLSL/WGSL format string, e.g. "rgba16sint") is still available — mirroring the comment already on the original conversion's call site about needing to record this before the declaration is rewritten away. Verified live in this sandbox (temporary Forward+ bypass, reverted after): the `Sint`/`UnfilterableFloat` mismatch at binding 19 is completely gone from a fresh capture, alongside re-confirmation that Round 6's VoxelGi and dimension fixes still hold. Both regression suites remain green (13/13, 192/192+1 skip).

**General lesson, extending Round 6's**: the read_write-storage-texture-split's shadow-read companions are a *second* class of synthetic binding (distinct from the read-only-storage-to-sampled conversion) that needs its own care wherever downstream code assumes a binding's format/metadata came from the original WGSL declaration scan — this format-lookup gap and the depth-reclassification false-positive gap were two independent omissions in the same underlying mechanism, found one round apart. Worth a proactive audit of every other place `wgsl_rw_storage_splits`/`wgsl_read_storage_to_sampled` shadow/converted keys get consulted, in case a third such gap exists (e.g. dimension lookups, multisample flags) rather than waiting for another shader to surface one.

Rounds 1-7's accumulated fixes were committed locally (commits `27bb9dd8a3`, `c479405ea4` — not pushed, see repo convention).

### Round 8 — the 3D/2D dimension error only partly went away; found and fixed a second, distinct source of it

The user rebuilt and re-ran again on the same real hardware, producing a fresh `errors-chrome.txt` (~34,000 lines, down from ~49,900). Diffing confirmed Rounds 6 and 7's fixes hold: neither the VoxelGi `Depth`/`UnfilterableFloat` mismatch nor the `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` `Sint`/`UnfilterableFloat` mismatch appear anywhere in the fresh log.

**However, the 3D/2D view-dimension error (`"unnamed#15 4x4x1 mip1 fmt18 usage0x6"`) was still present** — down from 81 occurrences (Round 5's log) to 72 (this log), a real but partial reduction, meaning Round 6's fix addressed *some* but not all of its occurrences. Given the identical texture signature reappearing in both logs, this pointed at a second, independent call site producing the same class of error against the same (or an identically-shaped) texture, rather than Round 6's fix being wrong for what it did cover.

Investigated via static analysis of every `wgpuTextureCreateView` call site in the driver (about 25 in total) cross-referenced against every engine-side caller of `texture_create_shared_from_slice()` (the RD-level API for getting a single mip/layer/slice of an existing texture, used pervasively for mipmap chains, cubemap faces, atlas entries, etc.). Found: `RenderingDeviceDriverWebGPU::texture_create_shared_from_slice()`'s `switch (p_slice_type)` unconditionally sets `view_desc.dimension = WGPUTextureViewDimension_2D` for the default `TEXTURE_SLICE_2D` case, regardless of whether the *source* texture (`orig`) is actually 2D or 3D-dimension. Several engine call sites (e.g. `texture_storage.cpp`'s generic mipmap-slicing helpers) request `TEXTURE_SLICE_2D` generically without knowing ahead of time whether the texture they were handed is 2D or 3D — this works fine on Vulkan/Metal (which support viewing a single Z-slice of a 3D texture as a 2D view via `VK_IMAGE_VIEW_TYPE_2D` + `2D_ARRAY_COMPATIBLE`), but **WebGPU has no equivalent at all** — a texture view's dimension must structurally match the real texture's own dimension, with a 3D-dimension texture only ever able to produce a 3D view (covering its whole depth range, no per-slice isolation possible via a view). This is the same class of structural incompatibility Round 6 fixed for uniform-binding fallback logic, just surfacing at a completely different point in the driver (texture-slice creation, not bind-group creation) — confirming Round 6's own prediction that a second such gap might exist.

Attempted to pin down the exact Godot-side call site responsible via a temporary diagnostic print (removed before commit) gated on `orig->dimension == WGPUTextureDimension_3D`, rebuilt, and tried to reproduce live — **this diagnostic never fired in this sandbox's capture**, consistent with the now-established pattern that this sandbox's CDP console capture reliably dies (from log-volume/diagnostic-channel exhaustion, not a real hang — see Round 4/5) well before the point in the render sequence where this specific error manifests on real hardware. This sandbox cannot currently verify or refute fixes to errors that only occur deep into the frame sequence; only the user's real-hardware testing can close the loop on this one.

**Fix (structural, not yet live-verified)**: when `orig->dimension == WGPUTextureDimension_3D` and the requested slice type would produce anything other than a 3D view, force a full 3D view instead of the invalid one — safe by construction (avoids the hard validation error unconditionally) but not a complete fix: a full 3D view has no per-Z-slice isolation, so any caller genuinely relying on `texture_create_shared_from_slice(..., TEXTURE_SLICE_2D, ...)` to target one specific Z-layer of a 3D texture (e.g. for per-slice compute writes or an eventual render-target use) will not get correct isolated-slice behavior from the returned texture — it gets the *whole* volume instead. A fully correct fix would need WebGPU's `depthSlice` field on `GPURenderPassColorAttachment` (which *does* let a render pass target one Z-slice of a 3D texture directly, without going through a view at all) wired into whichever call site actually needs per-slice writes — that's caller-side work, out of scope for this round, and the exact caller wasn't identified (the diagnostic print never fired). Both regression suites remain green (13/13, 192/192+1 skip); this fix should be treated as **unverified against the actual failure** until the user's next real-hardware log confirms the occurrence count for this error shape.

Rounds 1-8's accumulated fixes were committed locally (commits `27bb9dd8a3`, `c479405ea4`, `5fc840cb8f` — not pushed, see repo convention).

### Round 9 — Resolve and SsaoInterleave/Octmap root-caused and fixed; Round 8's fix confirmed ineffective; the 3D/2D texture still unidentified

The user rebuilt and re-ran again (RTX 4080 SUPER), producing a fresh `errors-chrome.txt` (~22,500 lines, down from ~34,000) and a new `errors-godot-editor.txt` (the editor's own console — only the pre-existing, unrelated Task 8.3 issue). Good news first: both `ResolveShaderRD`'s sampler/texture mismatch and the `SsaoInterleaveShaderRD`-labeled format mismatch (see Round 8's note on misleading BGL labels — this one turned out to actually be `octmap_downsampler.glsl`/`octmap_filter.glsl`, not SSAO at all) are now root-caused and fixed.

**Bad news: Round 8's `texture_create_shared_from_slice` fix made zero measurable difference** — the 3D/2D dimension error's count was exactly 72 in both the Round 8 log and this one. Confirmed via extensive live-repro effort this round (see below) that this fix's target genuinely doesn't fire in this sandbox's scene at all, in either Mobile or (T9LIVE1-bypassed) Forward+ configurations — the fix likely just isn't the actual site producing this specific error on real hardware, though it remains a real, safe, harmless fix for a genuine WebGPU limitation regardless.

**Fixed and verified: `octmap_downsampler.glsl`/`octmap_filter.glsl`'s `rgb10a2unorm` storage format fell through to a hardcoded `RGBA8Unorm` default.** `copy_effects.cpp` eagerly compiles both an `OCTMAP_FORMAT rgb10_a2` and an `OCTMAP_FORMAT rgba16f` variant of these shaders (same "`ShaderRD` compiles every registered mode" pattern as Task 9.5's very first FSR1 fix), so the `rgb10_a2` variant — GLSL `layout(rgb10_a2, ...) uniform image2D` → WGSL `texture_storage_2d<rgb10a2unorm, ...>` — genuinely gets compiled and its bind-group layout built regardless of which variant a given device ends up selecting at runtime. Root cause: the WGSL text scan that reads a storage texture's declared texel format (`texture_storage_2d<FORMAT, access>`) to populate its BindGroupLayout entry is a hand-written exhaustive `if/else strncmp` chain over every WGSL storage format string — and it was simply missing `rgb10a2unorm`, `rgb10a2uint`, and `rg11b10ufloat` entirely (these are more recently standardized WGSL storage formats than the rest of the list). Any format not recognized silently fell through to the function's `RGBA8Unorm` fallback, producing a BindGroupLayout that disagreed with what Tint's WGSL module actually declared — "layout's binding format (RGBA8Unorm) doesn't match shader's binding format (RGB10A2Unorm)". **Fix**: added the three missing format strings to this scan, and to the separate (Round 7-introduced) `_wgsl_storage_format_string_to_wgpu()` helper used by the read-only-storage-conversion and read_write-split shadow-binding paths, for consistency/future-proofing against the same gap resurfacing there. Verified live in this sandbox (temporary Forward+ bypass, reverted after): the RGB10A2 mismatch is completely gone from a fresh capture.

**Fixed and verified: `resolve.glsl`'s multisampled combined sampler (`sampler2DMS`) was assumed to always have a paired sampler variable, but WGSL has none for it.** `The shader's texture sample type... doesn't match... Binding type in the shader (texture) doesn't match the type in the layout (sampler)` at `ResolveShaderRD:0` (confirmed, this time, to genuinely be `ResolveShaderRD` — module and BGL label agreed) binding 2, which is `resolve.glsl`'s `MODE_RESOLVE_GI` variant's `source_normal_roughness` (the second of two `sampler2DMS` uniforms in that mode). Root cause: GLSL's `sampler2DMS` (multisampled combined sampler) only ever needs `texelFetch`-style access, which WGSL's `texture_multisampled_2d` type supports directly via `textureLoad` with no sampler object involved at all — so the SPIR-V `split_combined_samplers` preprocessing pass (which handles the general combined-sampler split into separate sampler+texture bindings) already special-cases multisampled combined types: no sampler variable is emitted, and the split-out texture is placed at `binding*2` instead of the usual `binding*2+1` (since there's no sampler occupying the `*2+0` slot). The BGL-construction and bind-group-entry-construction code for `UNIFORM_TYPE_SAMPLER_WITH_TEXTURE`, however, unconditionally assumed the general (non-multisampled) layout everywhere — always emitting a sampler entry at `binding*2+0` and a texture entry at `binding*2+1`, regardless of multisampling. For `source_normal_roughness` (original binding 1), this produced a BGL with a *sampler* at binding 2 — but Tint's real WGSL module declares a `texture_multisampled_2d` *there instead* (no separate sampler exists at all), a structural mismatch neither Dawn nor Godot could paper over. **Fix**: both the BGL-construction copy and the bind-group-entry-construction copy of this case now detect the multisampled-no-sampler condition (via the existing `wgsl_is_multisampled_texture` map, checked at the `binding*2+0` key specifically) and, when true, skip the sampler entry/binding entirely and place the texture at `binding*2+0` instead of `+1` — mirroring exactly what the SPIR-V preprocessing pass already does. Also had to fix the BGL entries array's pre-sizing pass (previously always counted 2 entries per `SAMPLER_WITH_TEXTURE` uniform unconditionally), since undercounting would have left a bogus zeroed entry in the array. Verified live in this sandbox (same session as the RGB10A2 fix, both together): the mismatch is completely gone, no `[BGL-DUP]` duplicate-binding warnings appeared (confirming the entries-array resize fix is correct), and the full `driver_unit_tests` suite (327 tests, more thorough than the usual two-suite check given how much this touches core BGL-construction logic) passes alongside the usual shader corpus and preprocessing suites.

**On the still-unidentified 3D/2D dimension error**: spent significant effort this round trying to pin down the actual source via a temporary `texture_create()`-level diagnostic print keyed on the exact observed texture profile (3D-dimension, depth=1, width=4, height=4) — this print is unconditional and fires synchronously at texture-creation time, so it should have been captured reliably regardless of this sandbox's CDP-capture-dies-early limitation (since texture creation happens during the fully-captured scene-build phase, well before the point where captures usually die). **It never fired, in either Mobile or Forward+-bypassed configurations** — meaning this exact texture profile is never created anywhere in this sandbox's run of the ShaderCoverage scene at all, under any configuration tried. Static analysis of every `TEXTURE_TYPE_3D` allocation site in the engine (VoxelGI's octree texture, SDFGI's cascades, volumetric fog's froxel buffer, the built-in default-3D-texture placeholders) found candidates that were structurally close (3D, sometimes RGBA8Unorm) but none matching the exact `4x4x1`/single-mip signature. This suggests the real trigger may depend on real-hardware-only behavior (an optional WebGPU feature or capability query that resolves differently on real Chrome than in this sandbox's software-rendered environment, causing a different code path to run) rather than being reachable through this sandbox at all — a similar shape to Task 8.3's own cross-environment gap. This is now the sole remaining unidentified item from the original three (Resolve, SsaoInterleave/Octmap, and this one); further progress likely needs either a different diagnostic approach entirely (e.g. a small standalone JS page that monkey-patches `GPUDevice.createTexture`/`createView` to log real call arguments directly in the user's own browser) or continued deprioritization in favor of lower-hanging remaining work.

Round 9's fixes were committed locally (commit `e60aa6a770` — not pushed, see repo convention).

### Round 10 — Resolve and Octmap fixes confirmed on fresh real hardware; the last known sampler-type gap found and fixed; the 3D/2D texture remains the sole unidentified item

The user rebuilt and re-ran again (RTX 4080 SUPER), producing a fresh `errors-chrome.txt` (~25,600 lines, down from ~22,500 — within normal run-to-run noise, not a regression signal by itself). Confirmed both Round 9 fixes hold: neither the Resolve sampler/texture mismatch nor the RGB10A2 format mismatch appear anywhere in the fresh log.

**Fixed and verified: `ss_effects_downsample.glsl`'s depth texture, read via `textureGather`, kept its paired sampler at the default Filtering type.** `GPUValidationError: Texture binding (...) is TextureSampleType::Depth but used statically with a sampler (...) that's SamplerBindingType::Filtering` at `SsEffectsDownsampleShaderRD` — this is the same "Depth/Filtering sampler mismatch" flagged as a known-but-uninvestigated item all the way back near the start of this session's live-repro work (Task 9.5's very first status notes). Root cause: this driver already has a working mechanism for exactly this problem — a WGSL-text scan (populating `wgsl_sampler_needs_nonfiltering`) that finds samplers statically passed alongside a depth-reclassified texture (Task 7.13) to a non-comparison sample call, and forces them to `WGPUSamplerBindingType_NonFiltering` instead of the invalid default `Filtering` — but the scan's function-name list (`textureSample`, `textureSampleLevel`, `textureSampleBias`, `textureSampleGrad`) never included `textureGather`. `ss_effects_downsample.glsl`'s `source_depth` (a GLSL `sampler2D`, reclassified to `texture_depth_2d` per Task 7.13's heuristic) is read via a mix of `texelFetch` (compiles to WGSL `textureLoad`, which for any texture type takes no sampler parameter at all — nothing to detect, and correctly so, since WebGPU can't complain about a sampler that's never referenced) and `textureGather` (Task 7.22's `texture_depth_2d` overload *does* take `(texture, sampler, coords)`, explicitly pairing the two) — the second usage is exactly the shape the scan was designed to catch, just for a function name that was missing from its list. **Fix**: added `"textureGather("` to the scanned function list — the depth-texture overload's argument order matches the existing scan logic exactly (texture first, sampler second), so no other changes were needed. Verified live in this sandbox (temporary Forward+ bypass, reverted after): the error is completely gone from a fresh capture, and both regression suites remain green.

**The 3D/2D dimension-view error remains the sole unidentified item.** Count went from 72 (Round 8/9's logs) to 81 in this fresh log — within the same range as Round 5's original 81, and not obviously tied to anything changed this round (nothing touching texture-view or dimension code was modified between Round 9 and this one) — likely just run-to-run noise (scene timing, frame-to-frame culling differences, etc.) rather than a signal. Still not reproduced in this sandbox under any configuration tried across Rounds 8 and 9; no further diagnostic attempt was made this round given the prior round's exhaustive (and unsuccessful) effort. This is now genuinely the only unresolved item from the original error list, aside from the long-known, out-of-scope Task 8.3 gap.

Round 10's fix was committed locally (commit `f1ef474324` — not pushed, see repo convention), alongside a new standalone diagnostic tool (`webgpu_tests/wgpu_debug_probe/`, commit `5ed519db58`) built specifically to crack the one remaining item below.

### Round 11 — the 3D/2D texture mystery finally solved, via a real-hardware-only JS-level probe

`wgpu_debug_probe.js` (see `webgpu_tests/wgpu_debug_probe/README.md`) wraps `GPUDevice.createTexture`/`GPUTexture.createView` in the exported page itself, logging real call arguments and a JS stack trace at the moment of each call — ground truth that survives even when Dawn's own error text is confusing. The user ran it on real hardware (RTX 4080 SUPER) and it immediately caught the culprit: texture #19, a 4x4x4 RGBA8Unorm 3D texture created at startup (alongside two siblings, #17/#18, all within 1.4ms of each other and of "Using Device #0"), repeatedly asked for a `"2d"`-dimension view through the whole render loop.

**Important correction to five rounds of prior assumptions**: the "4x4x1" seen in every Dawn error message this whole investigation (`[Texture "unnamed#15 4x4x1 mip1 fmt18 usage0x6"]`) was never the texture's real size — it's a **label string**, verbatim, that this driver itself synthesizes and assigns to `WGPUTextureDescriptor.label` for any texture Godot doesn't explicitly name via `set_resource_name()`. It's formatted to *look like* Dawn's own auto-generated `"unnamed#N"` placeholder (for consistency when it does get auto-generated), but the specific text baked into it is unrelated to the texture's actual live dimensions. Every earlier static-analysis attempt to find "a 3D texture sized exactly 4x4x1" (Rounds 5, 8, 9) was chasing a profile that never existed — the real texture is 4x4x4, exactly matching Godot's built-in `DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_3D_WHITE`/`_BLACK`/`_TRANSPARENT` trio (`texture_storage.cpp`, created as a group of three at startup, format RGBA8Unorm, usage `SAMPLING_BIT | CAN_UPDATE_BIT` → exactly `TextureBinding | CopyDst` = `0x6`, matching every field of the error down to the byte). This is a good general lesson for reading this driver's error output going forward: **don't trust the label text's apparent size/format fields as ground truth — they're this driver's own synthesized string, not Dawn's.**

Root-caused via a second, more targeted probe run plus a C++-side diagnostic confirming that every `texture_3d<f32>` binding in `scene_forward_clustered.glsl` (`voxel_gi_textures`, `sdfgi_occlusion_cascades`, `volumetric_fog_texture`, and a read_write-split shadow companion) is correctly detected as 3D-dimension by this driver's own WGSL scan, across every shader variant checked (17 through 37) — ruling out both of the two previously-fixed dimension-mismatch code paths (the general uniform-binding fixup from Round 6, and `texture_create_shared_from_slice()` from Round 8) as the source, since neither would even attempt a mismatched view when both sides agree on 3D. That left a **third, previously-unaudited call site**: `RenderingDeviceDriverWebGPU::_get_compatible_bind_group()` — a cache-reuse optimization that adapts an already-built `WGUniformSet` (created for one shader/pipeline variant) to a *different*, layout-compatible target shader variant, by comparing each binding's declared type/dimension between the two variants and creating a new view if they disagree. This function already had bespoke Depth/Float sample-type adaptation and sampler-type adaptation logic, and a dimension-adaptation branch alongside them — but unlike the two already-fixed call sites, this one's dimension branch had **no structural-compatibility guard at all**, unconditionally requesting `wgpuTextureCreateView` with whatever the target shader declared, even when that's a fundamentally different dimension class (2D vs 3D) than the real bound texture. Exactly the same class of bug Round 6 and Round 8 each fixed once already, found a third time in a location neither of those rounds' audits reached (it's not part of the general per-uniform bind-group-entry construction those rounds focused on — it's a separate, later adaptation pass that only runs when a cached uniform set gets reused against a different pipeline).

**Fix**: added the identical `dim_class_incompatible` check (3D texture → only a 3D view legal; 2D-family texture → never a 3D view) already used in the other two locations, substituting `fallback_float_texture_view`/`fallback_cube_texture_view` instead of attempting the invalid view when the target's requested dimension is structurally incompatible with the real texture. Verified live using the probe itself (temporary Forward+ bypass in this sandbox, reverted after): the same three startup textures (#17/#18/#19) are still created identically, but **zero** incompatible `createView()` calls occur anywhere through the full 10-frame render loop — a complete, direct fix, not a partial mitigation. All three regression suites remain green (13/13 shader corpus, 192/192+1 skip preprocessing, 327/327 driver unit tests).

This closes out the *entire* original Forward+ live-repro error list from this session, except the long-known, separately-scoped Task 8.3 formatless-storage-image gap (`screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl`), which was never part of this specific investigation and needs its own dedicated runtime-format-injection work.

Round 11's fix and the new `wgpu_debug_probe.js` tool were committed locally (commits `f1ef474324` through `53e6cb8744` — not pushed, see repo convention).

### Round 12 — a genuinely different real project (not the ShaderCoverage scene), and a fundamental cluster-building bug this whole session never actually exercised

The user clarified they've actually been testing against their own project (a "cameraSim" scene, not the repo's `webgpu_tests/test_project` ShaderCoverage scene) all along, and sent a fresh `errors-chrome.txt` + `errors-godot-editor.txt` from it. This is the first log this session from genuinely different project content, and it immediately surfaced something new: `No attachment was specified` (at `CreateRenderPipeline`, for `ClusterRenderShaderRD`) and `Render pass has no attachments` (at `BeginRenderPass`), both firing on essentially every frame, cascading into `[Invalid CommandBuffer]` each time.

**Fixed and verified: `SUPPORTS_FRAGMENT_SHADER_WITH_ONLY_SIDE_EFFECTS` was wrongly reported as supported.** Root cause, found directly by reading `has_feature()`: this driver claims `true` for this capability with the comment "WebGPU render passes work with no color attachments" — true, but incomplete: a WebGPU render pass *can* have zero color attachments as long as a depth-stencil attachment exists, but "fragment shader with only side effects" specifically means a pipeline with **no attachments at all** (neither color nor depth), which WebGPU always rejects outright, unlike Vulkan/Metal/D3D12. `cluster_builder_rd.cpp` (the code that builds Forward+'s light/decal/reflection-probe cluster grid — used by every Forward+ frame, completely independent of the `ShaderCoverage` scene's specific content) already has a complete, working fallback for exactly this case: when the feature is reported unsupported, it uses a real color attachment (`SHADER_USE_ATTACHMENT` shader variant + an actual `AttachmentFormat`) instead of `framebuffer_format_create_empty()`. This driver's wrong `true` meant that fallback path was never taken, and every cluster-render pipeline/pass was built with a genuinely empty framebuffer format — invalid in WebGPU regardless of anything else being correct. **Fix**: changed the flag to `false`, letting Godot's own existing, already-correct fallback take over. Verified live (temporary Forward+ bypass in this sandbox, reverted after): both error shapes are completely gone, and all three regression suites remain green (13/13, 192/192+1 skip, 327/327).

**Why eleven rounds of testing against the ShaderCoverage scene never caught this**: cluster building is fundamental to every Forward+ frame, so this bug should have fired in every prior live-repro round too — but this sandbox's Chrome instance falls back to the 48-texture-limited Mobile renderer by default (real, hardware-driven, see Round 4), and every earlier round either tested under that fallback (never reaching Forward+/cluster code at all) or used the temporary `T9LIVE1` Forward+ bypass for a narrow, single-purpose verification capture that happened to complete before or without this error surfacing in the captured window. It took the user's own real hardware (where Forward+ is the *actual* selected renderer, not bypassed) running a real, independent project to expose it. This is a good reminder that this sandbox's live-repro loop, however useful it's been, is not a substitute for testing on real hardware against real content — it can only prove the absence of what it happens to reach.

Round 12's fix was committed locally (commit `fc3b72b1a6` — not pushed, see repo convention).

### Round 13 — Round 12's fix was incomplete: the draw-time framebuffer was never updated to match

The user rebuilt and re-tested against their real project immediately. Good news: `No attachment was specified` (the pipeline-creation-time error) was gone, confirming that half of Round 12's fix (the constructor's pipeline setup) was correct. Bad news: `Render pass has no attachments` was still firing, now on *every* draw (2254 occurrences in this log, versus 886 in Round 12's — worse, not better, since more of the frame now gets further before failing).

**Root cause**: `ClusterBuilderRD::setup()` (a *different* function from the constructor Round 12 fixed) builds the actual per-instance framebuffer used at draw time, and it was **unconditionally** calling `RD::framebuffer_create_empty()` — with no check of `SUPPORTS_FRAGMENT_SHADER_WITH_ONLY_SIDE_EFFECTS` at all, unlike the constructor. `RD::framebuffer_create_empty()` itself (`rendering_device.cpp`) always builds a zero-attachment format internally, regardless of driver capability — there's no automatic capability-aware behavior hidden inside it. So even after Round 12 correctly made the *pipeline* expect a real color attachment (`SHADER_USE_ATTACHMENT` variant) on platforms lacking the feature, the actual *framebuffer* passed to `draw_list_begin()` in `render()` still had zero attachments — a pipeline/framebuffer mismatch that, in WebGPU's case, manifests as the render pass itself (built from the framebuffer, independent of which pipeline gets bound into it) having no attachments at all. This is a straightforward oversight in `cluster_builder_rd.cpp`: whoever added the `SHADER_USE_ATTACHMENT` fallback for the pipeline never updated the sibling framebuffer-creation code to match — grep confirms `has_feature(SUPPORTS_FRAGMENT_SHADER_WITH_ONLY_SIDE_EFFECTS)` appears exactly once in the whole file (the constructor), not twice.

**Fix**: added the identical capability check to `setup()`. When the feature is available, behavior is unchanged (`framebuffer_create_empty()`, matching the `SHADER_NORMAL` pipeline). When it's not, create a real `RD::TEXTURE_TYPE_2D` color texture (format `RGBA8Unorm`, matching the pipeline's `AttachmentFormat()` default, sized to the cluster-grid resolution, with the same sample count already computed for the MSAA/non-MSAA split) and build the framebuffer from it via `framebuffer_create()` instead — the texture's contents are never read; the pipeline only writes to it as required to satisfy WebGPU's "needs one real attachment" rule. Added a new member (`framebuffer_color_attachment`) to own this texture's lifetime, freed alongside the existing `framebuffer` member in `_clear()` (which `setup()` already calls on every invocation, including resizes). This is a change to **shared, cross-backend engine code**, not driver-only code — but it's fully backward-compatible: `has_feature()` returns `true` on Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 (they genuinely support side-effects-only fragment shaders), so the new branch is dead code there, identical to how the constructor's pre-existing fallback already worked.

Verified live (temporary Forward+ bypass in this sandbox, reverted after): both `Render pass has no attachments` and `No attachment was specified` are completely gone from a fresh capture; console output is clean apart from the pre-existing, unrelated Task 8.3 issue. Confirmed via a full native-editor build (`scons platform=linuxbsd target=editor dev_build=yes`) before the web rebuild, to catch any C++ errors quickly given this touches shared code. All three regression suites remain green (13/13, 192/192+1 skip, 327/327).

### Round 14 — zero validation errors but "UI/2D visible, no 3D (solid black)"; a silent WASM crash, invisible to console-only capture, root-caused via scene bisection + an injected error-stack probe

After Round 13, the user rebuilt and reported a *different kind* of symptom for the first time this task: no WebGPU validation errors at all, but "I see the UI/2D but no 3D" — clarified (via `AskUserQuestion`) as solid black where 3D content should render. This broke the established "read errors-chrome.txt" workflow, since there were no errors to read.

**New tooling**: extended the CDP live-repro harness with `Page.captureScreenshot` and a `Runtime.evaluate` DOM check (for the exported `index.html`'s own `#status`/`#status-notice` loading-overlay elements), since a blank/black canvas produces no console output at all. The very first screenshot revealed Godot's own branded loading splash stuck permanently visible — never hidden via `setStatusMode('hidden')` — which plausibly *is* what the user was describing as "UI" (their own splash, not the intended 3D scene). Critically, the underlying cause — a genuine native WASM crash, `RuntimeError: table index is out of bounds` (an invalid `call_indirect`) — **never appeared via `Runtime.consoleAPICalled` or `Runtime.exceptionThrown`** despite the exported page's own failure handler (`displayFailureNotice`) calling `console.error(err)` on it. Only screenshot + direct DOM inspection surfaced it initially. A second probe script (`Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` injecting a `console.error`/`unhandledrejection`/`window.onerror` monkey-patch that stashes full `Error.stack` text into a page-global array, read back afterward via `Runtime.evaluate`) finally captured the actual stack — confirming CDP's console-event delivery for this specific error class is simply unreliable in this setup; capturing it required intercepting at the JS layer directly rather than trusting the DevTools protocol events.

**Bisection**: systematically disabled scene features in `shader_coverage.gd` (particles/GI/fog/decals/reflection-probes/skeleton/multimesh, then SDFGI, then SSAO/SSIL/SSR/glow/volumetric-fog) — crash persisted through all of them. Disabling TAA+FSR2+DOF together made the crash stop. Re-enabling DOF alone: no crash. Re-enabling TAA+FSR2 together: crash reproduced. Isolating further — TAA alone (FSR2 disabled): no crash over a full 35s capture. FSR2 alone (TAA disabled): crash reproduced. This isolated **FSR2 specifically** (not TAA, not their combination) as the trigger.

**Root cause, found via the injected error-stack probe**: immediately before the WASM trap, the console shows:
```
ERROR: Format 'B10G11R11_Ufloat_Pack32' does not support usage as storage image.
   at: texture_create (servers/rendering/rendering_device.cpp:1708)
ERROR: Condition "texture.is_null()" is true. Returning: FFX_ERROR_BACKEND_API_ERROR
   at: create_resource_rd (servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr2.cpp:241)
```
`RenderingDeviceDriverWebGPU::texture_get_usages_supported_by_format()`'s storage-capability switch (added for a different purpose back in the original driver bring-up) never listed `DATA_FORMAT_B10G11R11_UFLOAT_PACK32` (RG11B10_UFLOAT) — nor the two `RGB10A2` formats — as storage-capable, even though they demonstrably are on this build: the very same session's Tint/Dawn `textureStore` overload-resolution error dumps (visible in every capture, unrelated shader) list `rgb10a2unorm` and `rg11b10ufloat` as valid write-storage formats, and Round 9 already taught the WGSL-text layer (`_wgsl_storage_format_string_to_wgpu()`) to handle exactly these three formats — but that fix never touched this separate, RenderingDevice-level capability-advertisement switch. FSR2's `create_resource_rd` requests a storage-usable RG11B10_UFLOAT texture (for its luminance/exposure accumulation buffer) and gets a hard `texture_create()` failure back as a null RID. The AMD FSR2 SDK (`thirdparty/amd-fsr2`) does not handle that error gracefully — it appears to proceed with the invalid/null resource, and several GPU-job-scheduling steps later this manifests as the unrelated-looking `table index is out of bounds` trap (a corrupted or out-of-range indirect call, not a WebGPU-level validation error at all, hence total invisibility to normal error-log analysis).

**Fix**: added `DATA_FORMAT_A2B10G10R10_UNORM_PACK32`, `DATA_FORMAT_A2B10G10R10_UINT_PACK32`, and `DATA_FORMAT_B10G11R11_UFLOAT_PACK32` to the storage-capability switch in `texture_get_usages_supported_by_format()`, matching what Round 9 already established these formats support at the WGSL level.

Rebuilt (web template_debug) and live-verified: the RG11B10_UFLOAT storage-format rejection and the original `table index is out of bounds` trap are both **completely gone**. But FSR2 immediately hit a **second, distinct** crash further into its own pipeline, caught via the same error-stack probe technique:
```
thirdparty/tint/src/tint/lang/spirv/reader/parser/parser.cc:2493 internal compiler error: TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED unhandled SPIR-V instruction: OpImageTexelPointer (val = 60)
...
RuntimeError: unreachable
...
ERROR: Condition "effect_pass.root_signature.shader_rid.is_null()" is true. Returning: FFX_ERROR_BACKEND_API_ERROR
   at: create_pipeline_rd (servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr2.cpp:307)
RuntimeError: table index is out of bounds  (a second, cascading trap)
```

**Root cause #2, confirmed at the Tint source level (not a driver bug, a hard capability gap)**: `thirdparty/tint/src/tint/lang/spirv/reader/parser/parser.cc` has a complete `case` for every other `OpImage*` instruction but **none at all** for `OpImageTexelPointer` — the SPIR-V instruction glslang always emits for any `imageAtomicAdd`/`imageAtomicOr`/etc. call on a texture. This isn't an oversight Tint could trivially add: WGSL itself has no atomic-on-texture concept whatsoever (atomics only exist on `atomic<T>` in the `storage`/`workgroup` address space, i.e. buffers) — there is no valid WGSL translation target for this opcode, full stop. FSR2's `compute_luminance_pyramid_pass` (its SPD-based auto-exposure downsample) uses exactly this pattern via `rw_spd_global_atomic`, a 1×1 `r32ui` image used purely as a cross-workgroup atomic counter (`SPD_IncreaseAtomicCounter`/`SPD_ResetAtomicCounter` in `ffx_fsr2_compute_luminance_pyramid_pass.glsl`) — and this dispatch is **unconditional** on every FSR2 frame (confirmed by reading `ffx_fsr2.cpp:987-989`, whose own comment says "Auto exposure always used to track luma changes in locking logic"; not gated by the public `FFX_FSR2_ENABLE_AUTO_EXPOSURE` flag at all). So any use of FSR2 upscaling was guaranteed to hit this, unconditionally.

**Not actually novel** — this project already has an established fix for precisely this class of gap: `RD::SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT` (an existing `Features` enum value, already correctly reported `false` by this driver: `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp:9857-9858`, "WebGPU has no image atomics support") plus the `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` shader-variant-define pattern, already fully implemented and working in `fog.cpp`/`volumetric_fog.glsl` (density/emissive/light compositing) and `scene_shader_forward_clustered.cpp` (SDFGI's `geom_facing_grid`) — both already correctly avoid texture atomics on this driver via a buffer-backed fallback variant (`#ifdef NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` → declare a `buffer` block + `atomicAdd()` instead of a `uimage2D` + `imageAtomicAdd()`). FSR2 (`thirdparty/amd-fsr2` + `effects/fsr2.cpp`) simply never checked this feature at all — a real, pre-existing gap in the FSR2 integration, not something specific to this session's driver work.

**Fix**: applied the identical `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` pattern to FSR2's SPD atomic counter specifically (its only texture-atomic usage in the whole FSR2 integration):
- `thirdparty/amd-fsr2/shaders/ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h`: `rw_spd_global_atomic`'s declaration now branches — a `coherent buffer` block with one `uint` under `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS`, the original `uimage2D` otherwise.
- `thirdparty/amd-fsr2/shaders/ffx_fsr2_compute_luminance_pyramid_pass.glsl`: `SPD_IncreaseAtomicCounter`/`SPD_ResetAtomicCounter` branch the same way (`atomicAdd()`/plain assignment on the buffer vs. `imageAtomicAdd()`/`imageStore()` on the image).
- `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr2.cpp`: three engine-glue changes. (1) The `FFX_FSR2_PASS_COMPUTE_LUMINANCE_PYRAMID` pass now initializes its shader with two variants (plain / `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS`) and picks one via `RD::has_feature(SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT)`, mirroring the existing fp16-variant pattern used by the depth-clip pass just above it. (2) `create_resource_rd()` special-cases `FFX_FSR2_RESOURCE_IDENTIFIER_SPD_ATOMIC_COUNT` (matched via `p_create_resource_description->id`, the same numeric identifier FSR2's own resource table uses) to create a real 4-byte storage buffer via `storage_buffer_create()` instead of a 1×1 texture, when the feature is unsupported. (3) `execute_gpu_job_compute_rd()`'s UAV-binding loop checks the same identifier (via `p_scratch.resources.ids[internal_index]`, which `Resources::add()` already tags every resource with) and binds it as `RD::UNIFORM_TYPE_STORAGE_BUFFER` instead of `RD::UNIFORM_TYPE_IMAGE` to match.

**Not yet done as of this writing**: this fix is applied but not yet build/live-verified (a native editor build was kicked off to catch compile errors in the shared `fsr2.cpp` glue code, since it compiles on every RD backend, not just WebGPU); still need a web rebuild + fresh live-repro capture to confirm both FSR2 crashes are actually gone and 3D content renders. The "solid black" screenshot seen earlier with TAA/FSR2/DOF all disabled (this sandbox's software/slow rendering not completing 10 frames within the capture window, vs. a second real bug) also remains unexplained and should be re-checked once this fix is confirmed. Also still pending: revert the bisection state in `webgpu_tests/test_project/scripts/shader_coverage.gd` back to all-features-enabled before committing; revert the `T9LIVE1` Forward+ bypass and `export_presets.cfg` debug-template override used for local verification.

**Extensive isolation of the buffer-atomic ICE (native `tint_convert_cli`, bypassing the browser entirely)**: switched to a much faster, more precise iteration loop — dump the actual compiled SPIR-V via `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` (native editor under `xvfb-run`, since headless mode skips shader compilation), then feed it straight to `bin/tint_convert_cli` for the full, un-forked Tint diagnostic and a `spirv-dis` disassembly, with zero browser/network/caching variables involved. This is what should have been reached for immediately instead of the earlier browser-rebuild-recapture cycles; noted here for future reference on this driver's debugging workflow (see also CLAUDE.md's "Debugging a Tint conversion failure" section).

Roughly a dozen GLSL-level variants were tried and individually verified via this loop: capturing the atomic result into an inout parameter vs. a local vs. a return value; `coherent` vs `volatile` vs no buffer qualifier; a scalar buffer member vs. a length-1 array member (matching `fog.cpp`'s exact proven-working shape); removing the second (reset) atomic access entirely; `atomicExchange` vs `atomicAnd` vs a second `atomicAdd`; inlining away an extra function-call hop. **Every variant containing any real atomic access to the buffer hit the identical ICE**; a minimal, fully standalone reproduction of the same pattern (a bare `buffer { uint data[]; }` with `atomicAdd` and a discarded `atomicExchange`) compiled through the exact same `tint_convert_cli` **cleanly** — proving Tint's buffer-atomics support is fundamentally sound in isolation, and that this is a narrow bug specific to something else present in FSR2's very large (~12,000-line preprocessed) `compute_luminance_pyramid` shader that only gets reached once the atomics-lowering pass's type-propagation walk is triggered by a real atomic being present at all. Root-causing the exact unrelated trigger construct inside that shader was judged out of reasonable scope for this session (it would need either a full bisection of the preprocessed shader or a proper upstream Tint investigation).

**Final fix for Task 9.5 Round 14**: rather than leave FSR2 crashing, disabled FSR2 upscaling entirely on backends lacking `SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT` (i.e., WebGPU) at the point `VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_FSR2` is selected in `render_forward_clustered.cpp`, falling back to no upscaling — exactly the same pattern already used for `VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_METALFX_TEMPORAL` when `METAL_MFXTEMPORAL_ENABLED` isn't defined, immediately above it in the same switch. This is a one-line, low-risk, easily-reversible gate: when the underlying Tint bug is eventually found and fixed (or FSR2's shader reworked to avoid whatever it's tripping), removing this gate immediately re-enables FSR2 with the (independently correct, and left in place) buffer-atomic-fallback work already done in `fsr2.cpp`/the vendored FSR2 shaders. Verified live: with FSR2 forced on in the test scene, the earlier crash (`table index is out of bounds` / `unreachable`) is completely gone — confirmed via a 100-second capture with zero crash-related console output, versus the crash reliably reproducing within 35 seconds in every prior round.

**The RG11B10_UFLOAT storage-capability fix (the first, independent bug found this round) is real, live-verified, and stays in regardless of the FSR2 gate above** — it fixed a genuine driver capability-table gap unrelated to the Tint atomics issue (confirmed: with only that fix applied and FSR2's atomics-blocking removed via the shader_variant force, the original crash was gone and a *different*, later crash appeared — proving the RG11B10_UFLOAT fix was correct and not merely masking the same bug).

**A further, distinct finding while chasing "solid black" even after the crash was fixed**: with a 100-second capture (vs. the previous 35s), the screen was still black, but this time for a clearly identifiable and expected reason — `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD:20`'s pipeline layout fails validation ("The number of sampled textures (21) in the Fragment stage exceeds the maximum per-stage limit (16)"), cascading into a full WebGPU device loss and a stuck error-retry loop. This is **not a real driver bug** — it's this local sandbox's own (likely software/fallback) WebGPU adapter genuinely only supporting 16 sampled textures per fragment stage, and the session-long `T9LIVE1` temporary bypass (which forces Forward+ regardless of the engine's own `textures_per_stage < 48` capability check, specifically to make Forward+/FSR2/cluster-building testable in this weak sandbox at all) was overriding the exact fallback-to-Mobile logic that exists to catch precisely this case. The user's real hardware (RTX 4080 SUPER) supports far more than 16 per-stage textures and was never going to hit this; it's a sandbox-only artifact of deliberately bypassing a real, working hardware-capability gate for testing purposes, not a new bug to fix.

**Current standing (2026-09-10, end of this session)**: Tasks 9.1–9.3 are done at the shader-conversion level. Task 9.5 has now made **twenty-six** fix attempts across fourteen rounds; twenty-three are live-verified as genuinely fixing their target error. The cluster-building bug (Round 12/13) is fully fixed. Round 14 is the deepest single investigation this task has seen, surfacing three distinct, real findings: (1) a driver storage-capability-table gap (RG11B10_UFLOAT/RGB10A2, fixed), (2) a hard architectural gap between GLSL/SPIR-V image atomics and WGSL (which has none), for which the correct buffer-based fallback is implemented in `fsr2.cpp`/vendored FSR2 shaders but is currently gated off pending a deeper, still-unresolved Tint compiler bug specific to FSR2's own shader, and (3) confirmation that this driver's own hardware-capability-based Forward+ fallback (the thing `T9LIVE1` exists to bypass for testing) is itself working correctly — the "solid black" symptom in this specific sandbox, once the crash was fixed, turned out to be exactly that fallback's absence (due to the deliberate bypass), not a new engine bug. The accompanying methodological lessons — CDP console-event capture alone is unreliable near a WASM crash; a JS-level error-stack probe and screenshot capture are both now standard tooling; `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` + `tint_convert_cli` + `spirv-dis`, run natively under `xvfb-run`, is a far faster and more precise Tint-issue isolation loop than round-tripping through a browser rebuild — are likely to matter for future rounds more than this specific bug's resolution. `screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl`'s formatless storage image (Task 8.3) remains the one identified-but-unfixed, separately-scoped gap. Next step: get a fresh log from the user's real project (real hardware, FSR2 now safely disabled under WebGPU rather than crashing) to confirm the original "UI but no 3D" symptom is resolved; separately, the buffer-atomics-in-FSR2 Tint ICE remains open for whoever wants to pursue re-enabling FSR2 on WebGPU properly.

### Round 15 (2026-09-11): Task 8.3 (SSR formatless storage image) fixed and live-verified; a major infrastructure discovery — this sandbox has real GPU/Vulkan access, and a cascading SCons staleness bug was silently breaking every automated web export

**Task 8.3 fixed**: `screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl`'s `dest` storage image had no texel format qualifier (`layout(set = 0, binding = 1) uniform restrict writeonly image2D dest;`), so Tint couldn't infer a WGSL `texture_storage_2d<FORMAT, write>` type and rejected every `textureStore` against it. Root cause traced via `ss_effects.cpp`'s `ssr_allocate_buffers()`: `dest` is a mip slice of `RB_SCOPE_SSR/RB_SSR`, always allocated at the render buffers' base HDR format (`RGBA16_SFLOAT` in every configuration this fork supports) — and the sibling shader writing mip 0 of the exact same texture, `screen_space_reflection.glsl`, already hardcodes `layout(rgba16f, ...)` for its own output image. Added the identical qualifier to `dest`. A repo-wide grep for any `uniform image(2D|3D|2DArray|Cube|1D)` declaration lacking a `layout(FORMAT, ...)` qualifier turned up zero other instances — this was the last one. Verified directly against the real pipeline (`wgsl_precompile.py` + `tint_convert_cli`): no longer among the Tint conversion failures. Since fixed, verified live in-browser too (see below): zero SSR-related errors in a real WebGPU run.

**This sandbox has a real GPU** (`nvidia-smi` confirms an RTX 4080 SUPER, `/dev/dri` present) — the same hardware class the user's own reports have come from. Headless Chromium **can** get real Vulkan-backed WebGPU here (not the `--use-angle=swiftshader` software fallback this whole task's sandbox-limitation narrative has assumed throughout): `chromium.launch({ headless: true, args: ['--enable-unsafe-webgpu', '--enable-features=Vulkan', '--use-angle=vulkan', '--use-vulkan=native', '--ignore-gpu-blocklist', '--no-sandbox'] })` under `xvfb-run` gets a real `WebGPU 1.0 - Forward+` device and real draws — this is a materially more representative automated-testing setup than anything used in this task's fourteen prior rounds, and should replace `--use-angle=swiftshader` wherever this sandbox (or any dev machine with a real GPU, e.g. the user's own Linux desktop) is doing the testing. The user's own real Chrome uses `--use-vulkan --enable-features=Vulkan --ignore-gpu-blocklist` on top of the ordinary WebGPU-enablement flags; the extra `--use-angle=vulkan --use-vulkan=native --no-sandbox` above are needed specifically for a *headless*, sandboxed automation context.

**A three-level cascading SCons staleness bug was breaking every fresh `dlink`-enabled web export in this sandbox**, unrelated to any Godot/WebGPU source change — this is why `webgpu_tests/scene_smoketest`'s browser stage failed 19/19 with `TypeError: resolved is not a function` (an Emscripten dynamic-linking symbol-resolution failure) regardless of which browser, which flags, or whether the SSR fix was applied (confirmed via a control build from unmodified HEAD with the fix `git stash`ed out — identical crash). Root-caused by instrumenting a local copy of the exported `index.js`'s dlink symbol-resolution proxy (`stubs[prop]=(...args)=>{resolved||=resolveSymbol(prop); ...}`) to log the unresolved symbol name before it throws: `_Z35initialize_objectdb_profiler_module25ModuleInitializationLevel` — the registration entry point for the `objectdb_profiler` module (`modules/objectdb_profiler/`), which is correctly gated behind `env.debug_features` in both its `config.py`'s `can_build()` and its own `SCsub`, so it's correctly *excluded* from `template_release` builds. The bug: three build artifacts, each one level further from the actual source, were stale from an earlier `target=template_debug` build (`debug_features=True`, module included) and never got invalidated when subsequently building `target=template_release` (`debug_features=False`, module excluded) in the same tree:
1. `modules/register_module_types.gen.cpp` / `modules/modules_enabled.gen.h` — generated by `modules/SCsub`'s `env.CommandNoCache(...)` calls, whose declared sources (`env.modules_detected`, `env.Value(env.module_list)`) don't capture `env.debug_features` or any other per-target flag that individual modules' `can_build()` checks actually depend on — the exact same "incomplete declared-dependency list" bug class as this driver's own already-documented `wgsl_precompiled.gen.h` issue (Task 8.7's closing note), just in stock Godot module-registration infrastructure rather than `drivers/webgpu/`.
2. The compiled object `bin/obj/modules/register_module_types.gen.web.template_release.wasm32.nothreads.dlink.o` — stayed stale (still referencing the old, debug-build-generated `.cpp`'s content) even *after* deleting and regenerating the `.gen.cpp` source twice; SCons never recompiled it until the `.o` itself was deleted directly.
3. `bin/obj/modules/libmodules.web.template_release.wasm32.nothreads.dlink.a` — the static library archiving that object was found dated **2026-09-09**, two days stale, and never re-archived even after the `.o` was correctly recompiled; deleting it directly was what finally forced a correct re-link.

**Fix applied this session (build-state only, not a source change)**: deleted all three stale artifacts and rebuilt; the exported `template_release` web build now boots correctly (`Godot Engine v4.7.2.stable.custom_build.44f012f96`, `WebGPU 1.0 - Forward+`, real per-frame draws) with zero console errors. **Not yet fixed at the SCons-source level** — the underlying incomplete-dependency-list bug (level 1 above) remains and will resurface for anyone switching between `debug_features`-differing targets (`editor`/`template_debug` vs `template_release`) in the same build tree without a clean; a real fix would need `modules/SCsub`'s two `CommandNoCache` calls to include `env.debug_features` (and whatever else per-module `can_build()` implementations key off) in their declared sources, which is a change to stock Godot module-registration code, not fork-specific — out of scope for this session but worth fixing (or at minimum, worth remembering: *always delete `modules/modules_enabled.gen.h`, `modules/register_module_types.gen.cpp`, and their compiled `.o`/the `libmodules.a` archiving it, before building a different target in an existing tree*).

**With both fixed, live real-Vulkan verification reproduces the user's original symptom directly and automatably**: UI/2D renders correctly (confirmed via screenshot — the camera-settings panel and diagram overlays are fully visible and correct), the 3D viewport is genuinely solid black, and **zero console/GPU errors are logged** at 59-60fps with real per-frame draw counts (`draws/f=134`, `RP/f=13`). This is now a repeatable, fully-automated repro — no round-trip through the user's own machine needed.

### Round 16 (2026-09-11, same session): root cause of the "solid black, zero errors" symptom found and fixed — a genuinely new class of bug (read_write-storage shadow companions never refresh for GPU-authored textures), specific to DOF

**Bisection**: rendering the exact same project through native Vulkan (`--write-movie`, bypassing WebGPU entirely) showed a perfectly correct, fully lit scene — proving this was a real WebGPU-only bug, not a content/exposure/camera authoring issue.
