Comparing against the 8 in-repo `benchmark_*` scenes (which all pass, Round 15) narrowed it to something the user's project uses that none of them do. `CameraAttributesPractical` with both `dof_blur_near_enabled`/`dof_blur_far_enabled` set was the standout candidate — confirmed by testing a scratch copy of the project with DOF disabled: real geometry appeared (previously totally invisible), ruling out `adjustment_enabled`/BCS as a separate control (tested and eliminated first) and pointing squarely at Depth of Field.

**Root cause**: `bokeh_dof.glsl`'s `color_image` (the DOF pass's shared intermediate RGBA16F buffer) is declared `restrict image2D` with no `readonly`/`writeonly` qualifier — i.e. `read_write` access. This browser (confirmed via `adapter.features` on real Vulkan hardware) does not support the `readonly-and-readwrite-storage-textures` WebGPU feature, so `shader_create_from_container()`'s existing read_write-storage-split mechanism (Task 9.5 Rounds 6/7) kicks in: the shader gets rewritten to declare `color_image` as `texture_storage_2d<rgba16float, write>` plus a synthetic shadow-read companion `color_image_rw_in : texture_2d<f32>`, backed by a **separate, newly-allocated `WGPUTexture`** (WebGPU disallows binding one texture simultaneously as both writable-storage and sampled within a dispatch, so true aliasing isn't an option — a real, separate copy is the only way).

The gap: **this shadow texture is only ever refreshed by `texture_update()`** (`rw_shadow_copy_map`, consulted from the CPU-upload path in `texture_update()`, `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` ~L6989/7029) — a mechanism that works correctly for the two previously-audited use cases (VoxelGI/SDFGI textures, which genuinely are populated via CPU-uploaded baked data at some point) but does nothing for a texture that's *exclusively* written by other GPU compute/render passes and never CPU-uploaded, like `bokeh_dof.glsl`'s `color_image`. Its shadow companion therefore stays at its initial (zero-filled) GPU allocation forever. Every `textureLoad(color_image_rw_in, ...)` reads back zero — and `MODE_GEN_BLUR_SIZE`'s very first access is a read-modify-write (`color = textureLoad(color_image_rw_in, pos, 0); color.w = size; textureStore(color_image, pos, color);`), so the scene's actual color data gets **destroyed (zeroed) on DOF's first pass**, cascading to a black final composite — with zero validation errors, since nothing about this is actually invalid WebGPU usage, just silently wrong data. A compounding factor made this a "third gap" distinct from Rounds 6/7's already-fixed ones: `UNIFORM_TYPE_IMAGE`'s bind-group-entry case in `uniform_set_create()` (~L5956) is a bare 6-line block with no shadow/dimension/format handling at all (unlike `UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE`/`SAMPLER_WITH_TEXTURE`'s much more developed cases) — it works at all here only because the *layout*-building code (a separate function, `shader_create_from_container()`) generically adds a BGL entry for every `rw_storage_splits` shadow binding regardless of uniform type, and the *shadow-texture-creation* code (also generic, in `uniform_set_create()` but outside the per-uniform-type switch) likewise doesn't care which uniform type triggered it — only the actual bind-group *entry* for the original write-side binding goes through the type-specific switch, and `UNIFORM_TYPE_IMAGE`'s minimal case happens to be sufficient for that one part.

**Fix**: rather than trying to eagerly copy at texture-write time (impossible in general — the driver has no hook for "a compute/render pass just finished writing this texture"), refresh the shadow via a **real GPU-side texture-to-texture copy immediately before every compute-dispatch bind** that uses it. Added `width`/`height`/`depth` to `WGUniformSet::RWShadowRegistration` (`webgpu_objects.h`) so the copy has an extent to work with, and a `refresh_rw_shadows` step at the top of the per-set loop in `command_bind_compute_uniform_sets()` (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`): for any uniform set with non-empty `rw_shadow_registrations`, end the active compute pass, issue `wgpuCommandEncoderCopyTextureToTexture` on the raw encoder for each registration, then begin a new compute pass and rebind the current pipeline + all previously-bound bind groups from `cmd->last_bound_state[]`. Copy commands can't be recorded inside an active pass, so this necessarily means ending/restarting one — the same pattern this driver already uses for the push-constant ring-buffer overflow case in `_flush_push_constants()`, just without that path's flush-and-submit step (no push-constant data needs freezing here; ending and restarting a pass on the *same* still-open command encoder is enough).

**Verified end-to-end**: `shader_corpus` (13/13), `preprocessing_tests` (192/192+1 skip), `driver_unit_tests` (327/327) all stay green. The 8-scene real-GPU benchmark smoke test (Round 15's `WEBGPU_REAL_GPU=1` mode) stays 8/8 passing — no regression. Most importantly, re-tested the user's actual, completely unmodified project (DOF and BCS both still enabled) on real Vulkan hardware: **zero console/GPU errors, and the rendered frame is now pixel-for-pixel consistent with the native Vulkan reference capture** — correct lighting, correct DOF blur on the background props, correct camera framing, full color. The original "UI renders, 3D is solid black" report is resolved.

**Scope note**: this fix is specific to the compute-dispatch bind path (`command_bind_compute_uniform_sets`); the render-pass equivalent (`command_bind_render_uniform_sets` or wherever render pipelines bind uniform sets) was not audited this round for the same gap — a GPU-authored read_write storage image bound to a *render* pipeline (rather than compute) needing this same split would hit an identical bug there. Worth checking if a future black-output report doesn't fit the compute-dispatch shape this one did.

### Round 17 (2026-09-11, same session): user enabled SSAO/SSIL/SDFGI/Glow/Fog/Volumetric Fog/adjustment on the same project — four more real bugs found and fixed; SDFGI still produces a silent (zero-error) black render, root cause not yet found

The user reported new console errors after changing their project's `Environment` settings (enabling `ssr`, `ssao`, `ssil`, `sdfgi`, `glow`, `fog`, `volumetric_fog`, `adjustment` — previously only `ssr`/`adjustment` were on, per Round 16). Investigated with the same real-Vulkan automated pipeline from Rounds 15/16.

**Fixed and verified this round:**

1. **`DATA_FORMAT_R16_UINT`/`DATA_FORMAT_R16_SINT` missing from `texture_get_usages_supported_by_format()`'s storage-capability switch** — the exact same gap class as the RG11B10_UFLOAT/RGB10A2 fix (Round 14), just two more formats never added. SDFGI's Occlusion Data texture (R16_UINT) failed at `texture_create()` with "Format 'R16_Uint' does not support usage as storage image" before ever reaching `_promote_storage_format()` (which already correctly promotes both to R32Uint/R32Sint, and always has — this was purely a capability-*reporting* gap, not a promotion gap). This one root cause cascaded into the entire rest of that error dump: `uniform_set_create` "should provide one ID" errors, `draw_list_draw` "Uniforms were never supplied" errors, and a "Cannot clear texture via writeTexture: unsupported format" error all disappeared once this was fixed — none of them were independent bugs.

2. **`texture_create_shared()` and `texture_create_shared_from_slice()` both compute the requested view's `WGPUTextureFormat` via a raw `_data_format_to_wgpu()` call when an explicit view format is requested, without applying `_promote_storage_format()`.** `texture_create()` itself always promotes narrow storage-incapable formats (R16Float, R16Uint, R8Unorm-without-tier1, ...) to their real 32-bit-or-native equivalents on the underlying `WGPUTexture` — a view request that skips this promotion asks for a format that no longer matches the real texture, producing "the texture view format (R16Float) is not compatible with the texture format (R32Float)". Hit by SSAO/SSIL's half-resolution `R16_SFLOAT` storage textures being reinterpreted/sliced. `texture_create_shared_from_slice()` additionally never updated `tex->format` at all in this branch (only `tex->rd_format`), a second, latent inconsistency fixed at the same time. Both now mirror the promotion and keep `tex->format` in sync.

3. **`_reclassify_single_component_depth_textures()`'s name+single-component heuristic (Task 7.13, already known to need per-case exclusions since Round 6) had a third false-positive source**: `screen_space_reflection_resolve.glsl`'s `source_depth_half` — Godot's own naming convention for "a downsampled/derived linear-depth buffer, explicitly distinguished from the real depth attachment (`source_depth`) bound in the very same shader" — still contains the substring "depth", so it was wrongly reclassified to `texture_depth_2d` even though it's genuinely bound from `RB_HIZ`, a plain R32Float buffer, never a real depth-format texture. Produced "None of the supported sample types (Float|UnfilterableFloat) ... match the expected sample types (Depth)". Fixed by adding "half" anywhere in the name as a third, independent disqualifying signal (mirroring how the existing exclusion set already handles the read-storage-to-sampled and rw-storage-split synthetic-binding cases) — Godot's own source consistently uses this exact naming split (a "_half" variant standing in contrast to the real depth-named binding) specifically to distinguish these two texture kinds, so the signal is reliable here the same way "contains depth" was deemed reliable enough to trigger the heuristic in the first place.

All three fixes verified against the real pipeline and live-tested; `shader_corpus` (13/13), `preprocessing_tests` (192/192+1 skip), `driver_unit_tests` (327/327) all stay green afterward.

**Still open, not fixed this round:**

4. **A `Format (R8Unorm) of [Texture] expected to be (RGBA8Unorm)` storage-texture bind-group mismatch**, reproducible with `ssr`+`ssao`+`ssil` enabled and `sdfgi` disabled (two variants: one on a 4-layer array texture, one on a plain 2D texture — likely `RB_SSIL_EDGES`/`RB_SSAO_FINAL` or similar, given both are `DATA_FORMAT_R8_UNORM` storage textures in `ss_effects.cpp`). Investigated at length: the WGSL text for every candidate shader (`SsilShaderRD`, `SsaoInterleaveShaderRD`, `SsilInterleaveShaderRD`, the `*ImportanceMap*`/`*Blur*` variants) correctly declares `r8unorm`/`rg8unorm` storage formats, and both the general WGSL-text format scanner (~L4822) and `_wgsl_storage_format_string_to_wgpu()` correctly recognize "r8unorm" as a string — so this isn't a repeat of the Round 9 "missing format string" bug class. Root cause not found before time ran out. Notably, this error **does not reproduce** with the user's actual current settings (`sdfgi` also enabled) — something about `sdfgi_enabled` changes `ss_effects.cpp`'s `downsample_mode`/quality-driven shader-variant selection enough to avoid whichever exact code path triggers it. Not urgent for the user's own project right now, but real and worth finding — probably needs bind-group-entry-level instrumentation (logging the actual `WGTexture*`/format at the `UNIFORM_TYPE_IMAGE` bind-group-entry construction site) rather than more WGSL-text reading, since the WGSL side has now been checked thoroughly and looks correct.

5. **SDFGI produces a genuinely silent (zero console/GPU errors) solid-black render, confirmed in isolation** (`sdfgi_enabled` alone, every other effect off) — this is the one actually affecting the user's real project right now, since their current settings have SDFGI on. Bisected down from "all 8 settings on" (0 errors, black) through several intermediate combinations to isolate SDFGI as sufficient on its own to reproduce it. **Ruled out this round**: the render-pass equivalent of Round 16's read_write-storage-shadow-refresh bug — a repo-wide search (`grep` for any vertex/fragment-stage GLSL shader declaring an `image1D`/`image2D`/`image3D` without `readonly`/`writeonly`, i.e. implicit read_write, across all of `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/`) found zero matches, meaning no render-pipeline shader anywhere in the engine uses this pattern at all — so `command_bind_render_uniform_sets` genuinely doesn't need the Round 16 fix mirrored into it (at least not for this bug; the scope note from Round 16 is now resolved as "checked, not applicable"). SDFGI's actual root cause is still unknown and needs fresh investigation — it's a large, multi-pass feature (voxelization, radiance cascades, probes, occlusion) and this round ran out of time before instrumenting it directly. Next step: likely the same style of investigation as the DOF round — dump real shader module WGSL/bind group contents for SDFGI's specific compute shaders (`sdfgi_integrate.glsl`, `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`, `sdfgi_preprocess.glsl`, `gi.glsl`) via the `createShaderModule`/`createBindGroup` hook technique and look for a data-corruption pattern (wrong texture bound, uninitialized read, etc.) rather than a validation-error pattern, since there isn't one to follow this time.

**Tooling note**: this round's investigation leaned entirely on the `WEBGPU_REAL_GPU=1` real-Vulkan pipeline and scratch-copy bisection technique established in Rounds 15/16 (copy the project to a temp dir, flip one `Environment` flag at a time, re-export, re-screenshot) — no round-trip through the user's machine was needed for any of it, including the parts that remain unresolved.

**Note**: Round 17's fixes were committed by the user directly (`67701eac17 "forward+ sorta works now!"`), not left as session-local uncommitted changes as in prior rounds — worth knowing when reading `git status`/`git diff` in a future session against this repo.

### Round 18 (2026-09-11, same session): user tested each new effect individually on real hardware and sent a fully-annotated error log — precise root causes found for two more bugs; one fix attempted and reverted after it regressed Canvas/UI

The user tested `ssr`/`ssao`/`ssil`/`sdfgi`/`glow`/`fog`/`volumetric_fog`/`adjustment` one at a time on their own machine and sent back `errors-chrome.txt` with manually-inserted `---- Start X ----`/`---- End X ----` section markers per feature — a much higher-signal report than previous rounds' raw dumps, and it let this round skip straight to precise diagnosis without needing to re-bisect blind.

**SSAO** ("scene gets darker, console has errors") and **SSIL** ("seems to work, but errors briefly") **are the same underlying bug**, now root-caused precisely (previously left as Round 17's still-open item #4, "R8Unorm/RGBA8Unorm mismatch, root cause not found"): the SSIL section's log captured the exact validating error this time — `SsilInterleaveShaderRD:0:set2` binding 0, "Format (R8Unorm) ... expected to be (RGBA8Unorm)". Dumping that exact shader variant's real WGSL (via the `createShaderModule` hook) shows `source_edges` (the `readonly image2DArray` at that binding) **doesn't appear in the compiled WGSL at all** for variant 0 — it's genuinely unread in that entry point, so `eliminate_dead_resources()` (Task 8.7's AggressiveDCE pass) correctly strips its entire declaration from the SPIR-V before Tint ever sees it. But Godot's own C++-side shader reflection (`shader_refl.uniform_sets`, built from the *original*, pre-DCE SPIR-V) still lists this binding as part of the shader's interface, so this driver's `uniform_set_create()`/BindGroupLayout-building code (which iterates `shader_refl.uniform_sets`, not the compiled WGSL) still constructs a real `storageTexture` BindGroupLayoutEntry for it — and since there's now no WGSL declaration left for the format-scanning code to read at all, `wgsl_storage_tex_format.has(key)` comes back empty and it falls through to the hardcoded `RGBA8Unorm` default, mismatching the real R8Unorm texture Godot's engine code still binds (`ss_effects.cpp`'s SSIL/SSAO edge buffers are `DATA_FORMAT_R8_UNORM`, bound unconditionally regardless of which shader variant is active). **Not fixed this round** — the only correct fix is to capture each storage image's declared texel format from the *original* SPIR-V (`OpTypeImage`'s Format operand) *before* `eliminate_dead_resources()` can strip it, and thread that through as a fallback for exactly this now-empty-post-DCE case; every existing 32/16/8-bit-format string parser in this file (the `_wgsl_storage_format_string_to_wgpu()` family, the inline `texture_storage_` scanner) operates on the *post-Tint WGSL text*, which is architecturally the wrong side of the DCE boundary for a binding this gets stripped from entirely. This is a real, scoped, one-time addition to `spirv_preprocess.cpp` (which already walks `OpTypeImage` for other passes) — worth a dedicated round, not a quick patch. Confirmed via the WGSL dump that this is *not* a repeat of the Round 9 "missing format string" bug (the string-matching code is complete and correct; there's simply nothing left to match against).

**Volumetric Fog** ("3D scene goes black") root-caused: `VolumetricFogProcessShaderRD` binding 22 (GLSL binding 11 × 2) is `volumetric_fog_process.glsl`'s `shadow_sampler` — declared (`uniform sampler shadow_sampler;`) but **never actually referenced anywhere in that shader file** (confirmed via grep — the only match is the declaration line itself). `fog.cpp`'s `ssr_allocate_buffers`-adjacent uniform-set-building code (~L911-918) unconditionally binds `p_settings.shadow_sampler` — a real Comparison-type `WGSampler` — to this slot regardless of whether the active shader variant reads it. Since the sampler is genuinely unused in the compiled WGSL, there is no `sampler_comparison` usage for the WGSL-text scanner to detect (the identical "AggressiveDCE strips the only evidence a static text scanner could use" shape as the SSAO/SSIL bug above, just for a sampler's *comparison-ness* instead of a storage image's *format*), so the BindGroupLayout defaults to a plain `Filtering` sampler — mismatching the real Comparison sampler Godot always binds here.

**Fix attempted and reverted**: tried defaulting any bare (non-`sampler_comparison`) `UNIFORM_TYPE_SAMPLER` binding named exactly `shadow_sampler` to Comparison type, on the theory that this is a consistent engine-wide naming convention (mirroring the already-successful `_reclassify_single_component_depth_textures()` name-based heuristic). **This is false and the fix was reverted after live-testing exposed the regression it caused**: `canvas.glsl` also declares and *actively uses* a `shadow_sampler` (`canvas_uniforms_inc.glsl` binding 5) — but as a genuine non-comparison sampler, manually combined with a texture via GLSL's `sampler2D(shadow_atlas_texture, shadow_sampler)` constructor and read with plain `texture()`/`textureGather()`, never `sampler2DShadow`/`textureProj`-style hardware comparison sampling. Forcing this binding to Comparison too broke `CanvasShaderRD` (`pipe#16`/`pipe#19` "Invalid RenderPipeline", "Non-comparison sampler ... incompatible with comparison sampler binding", "SamplerBindingType::4294967295 doesn't match ... Comparison") — i.e. UI rendering. **Lesson**: the name `shadow_sampler` is not a reliable engine-wide signal by itself — Godot uses it for two genuinely different sampling conventions (hardware-comparison shadow lookups in `scene_forward_lights_inc.glsl`, and manual/software shadow-atlas reads in `canvas.glsl`), so any fix here needs to distinguish "declared but truly unused, comparison-type intended" from "declared and actively used as an ordinary sampler" — which is exactly the same "need pre-DCE ground truth, WGSL text alone is insufficient" shape as the SSAO/SSIL bug immediately above. **Not fixed**; reverted cleanly (verified via rebuild + regression suite + real-project re-test, all back to the pre-attempt clean state, zero regressions).

**Confirmed working, no action needed**: SSR, Glow, Fog (non-volumetric), and Adjustments all reported by the user as working correctly, consistent with this session's earlier fixes covering their respective gaps (SSR: Task 8.3's SSR filter format fix, Round 16; Adjustments/BCS: ruled out as a red herring in Round 16's DOF investigation, i.e. it was never actually broken).

**Startup performance warnings reviewed** (the user asked specifically about these): only one is real — `WebGPU canvas configured with a different format than is preferred by this device ("rgba8unorm")`, meaning the canvas is forced to a non-preferred format and Dawn does an extra copy every frame to compensate. Not investigated or fixed this round; worth checking what `navigator.gpu.getPreferredCanvasFormat()` reports vs. what this driver requests at `context.configure()` time. Everything else in the "Startup warnings" section is the long-known, harmless "code is unreachable" Tint compilation notices (cosmetic dead-code after early `return`s in copied-in GLSL helper functions, not a real perf signal) plus a one-time, low-priority `WARN_PRINT`: `WebGPU: Unsupported DataFormat 1` (`DATA_FORMAT_R4G4B4A4_UNORM_PACK16` — an obscure packed format `_data_format_to_wgpu()` has no mapping for; fires once during SDFGI's startup, self-suppresses on repeat, and doesn't appear to block anything). Neither investigated further this round.

**Still open going into next round**: SSAO/SSIL's post-DCE format-recovery gap and Volumetric Fog's post-DCE comparison-sampler gap are the same architectural problem wearing two hats, and worth fixing together (one new SPIR-V-level pre-DCE capture pass feeding both). SDFGI's silent black-screen bug (Round 17) remains completely uninvestigated this round.

### Round 19 (2026-09-11, same session): SSAO/SSIL fixed via a new pre-DCE SPIR-V capture pass; Volumetric Fog fixed via dead-code removal; a third, pre-existing (Round 17 item #4) bug confirmed independent when stress-testing all effects stacked together

**SSAO/SSIL fix**: added the pre-DCE capture mechanism Round 18 called for. `_spirv_image_format_to_wgpu()` (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp:368`) maps the numeric SPIR-V `ImageFormat` enum (as it appears directly on `OpTypeImage`, e.g. `R8`=15) to `WGPUTextureFormat`. `_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()` (`:458`) manually walks the raw, unpreprocessed SPIR-V words for a single shader stage in two passes — first collecting `OpTypeImage`/`OpTypePointer`/`OpVariable`/`OpDecorate` results keyed by SPIR-V `<id>`, then resolving every `UniformConstant`-storage-class image variable to its `(set, binding)` pair and looking up that variable's image type for format (`OpTypeImage`'s Format operand) and dimension (`Dim`/`Arrayed` operands) — before `eliminate_dead_resources()` (Task 8.7) or any other preprocessing pass has had a chance to run, i.e. before any information loss is possible. Returns a `HashMap<uint32_t, PreDceImageInfo>` keyed the same way every other populator of `wgsl_storage_tex_format`/`wgsl_tex_dims` keys its maps: `(set << 16) | (binding * 2)` — the `* 2` matters because `split_combined_samplers()` (`spirv_preprocess.cpp`) doubles every non-combined binding number later in the pipeline, and every *other* populator of these two maps reads the binding back off Tint's final WGSL text (`@binding(N)`), i.e. post-doubling; this was the first thing gotten wrong in this round (initially computed the key pre-doubling, causing every lookup downstream to miss).

Call site: `shader_create_from_container()`, immediately after `shader->stage_spirv[(int)s.shader_stage] = spv_bytes;`, seeds both `wgsl_storage_tex_format` and `wgsl_tex_dims` from this function's result, but only where a key is still absent (`!map.has(key)`) — every existing WGSL-text-based scan in this same function still runs afterward and unconditionally overwrites, so this is purely a fallback for exactly the case Round 18 identified: a binding DCE stripped so completely that no post-Tint text evidence of it survives at all. Verified via a two-step build/test cycle: the format-only version fixed the `Format (R8Unorm) ... expected (RGBA8Unorm)` error but exposed a second, previously-masked error on the same binding (`Dimension (e2DArray) ... expected (e2D)`) — `wgsl_tex_dims` has the identical DCE-blindness problem `wgsl_storage_tex_format` does, for the same underlying reason, and hadn't been touched yet. Extended the same SPIR-V walk (it was already decoding `Dim`/`Arrayed` for other purposes, just not returning them) to capture both, via a two-field `PreDceImageInfo` struct, and re-verified: `cameraSim_ssil` (a scratch copy of the user's project with only `ssil_enabled` on) now renders correctly with **zero console errors**, confirmed via the `WEBGPU_REAL_GPU=1` Playwright pipeline and a visual screenshot check.

**Volumetric Fog fix**: Round 18's reverted heuristic approach was abandoned in favor of the simpler, provably-safe fix it should have been from the start — since `shadow_sampler` in `volumetric_fog_process.glsl` is **genuinely and totally unused** (confirmed via grep: the declaration is the only occurrence of the identifier anywhere in the file, in any `#ifdef` mode), the binding was simply deleted from the GLSL source, replaced with a comment explaining why (see the file itself for the full rationale: a sampler's *comparison-ness* is a usage-derived property, unlike a texture's *format* which lives in the type declaration itself — so unlike the SSAO/SSIL case, there is no pre-DCE ground truth to recover here even in principle; deletion is the only available fix). Safety confirmed by reading `RenderingDevice::uniform_set_create()`'s validation loop (`servers/rendering/rendering_device.cpp` ~L4685): it iterates what the *shader* declares and looks up a match in what's *provided*, silently ignoring any provided-but-unrequested uniform — so `fog.cpp` continuing to unconditionally supply `p_settings.shadow_sampler` at binding 11 (now absent from the shader's interface) is harmless on every backend, identical to how any other over-provided uniform is already tolerated. Verified: `cameraSim_vfog` (scratch copy with `fog_enabled`+`volumetric_fog_enabled` on) now renders the scene correctly instead of solid black, zero console errors.

**Full regression pass after both fixes**: `shader_corpus` (13/13), `preprocessing_tests` (192/192 + 1 pre-existing skip), `driver_unit_tests` (327/327) all green. Re-tested the user's real, unmodified project (`adjustment_enabled` only, matching their last-known-good baseline) — zero errors, no regression.

**New finding — stress-testing all seven effects enabled simultaneously** (`ssr`+`ssao`+`ssil`+`fog`+`volumetric_fog`+`glow`+`adjustment`, `sdfgi` left off) on a scratch copy of the user's real project reproduces a **different, already-documented, pre-existing bug**: `SsaoInterleaveShaderRD:1:set0` binding 0 (`dest_image`, GLSL `layout(rgba8, ...) uniform restrict writeonly image2D`) — layout correctly expects `RGBA8Unorm` (this binding is written unconditionally in every `ssao_interleave.glsl` code path, `MODE_SMART` and not, so it can never be DCE'd and the WGSL-text scan finds it correctly every time — this is *not* a repeat of the SSAO/SSIL bug just fixed above), but the actual GPU texture Godot's engine binds there is `R8Unorm`. This is a pre-existing, separately-tracked bug — Round 17's still-open item #4 (`Format (R8Unorm) ... expected (RGBA8Unorm)`, "reproducible with ssr+ssao+ssil enabled and sdfgi disabled") — being hit again under this round's specific stress-test combination, not a regression introduced by either of this round's fixes (confirmed: `wgsl_storage_tex_format` is a function-local `HashMap` scoped to a single `shader_create_from_container()` call, i.e. one call per shader variant, so there's no cross-shader or cross-variant key collision the new seeding step could have introduced; and the new seeding step only ever *inserts if absent*, never overwrites a value the existing WGSL-text scan already found — which for this always-referenced binding, it always does). Root cause is on the engine/`ss_effects.cpp` side (which real texture gets bound to this slot under which `downsample_mode`), not the WGSL/DCE side, and remains unfixed — same status as when Round 17 left it: not urgent (doesn't reproduce with the user's actual current per-feature settings, which test one effect at a time), but real, and now confirmed independent of this round's changes.

**Next round candidates**: SDFGI's silent black-screen bug (Round 17, still completely uninvestigated); Round 17 item #4 / this round's stress-test finding (`SsaoInterleaveShaderRD` R8Unorm-vs-RGBA8Unorm, needs bind-group-entry-level instrumentation on the engine side per Round 17's note); the canvas preferred-format performance warning; the `DataFormat 1` startup warning.

### Round 20 (2026-09-11, same session): SDFGI black-screen finally investigated — it was never actually zero-error, just zero-error-in-a-short-capture; real root cause is a packed-format texture-reinterpretation trick that has no WebGPU equivalent

**Setup**: reused a previous session's scratch copy of the user's real project (`cameraSim_test3`, `sdfgi_enabled=true` and everything else off — the exact Round 17 isolation) and its existing web export, since no SDFGI-relevant code had changed since that export was built. Wrote a full-console-capture probe (unfiltered, unlike the existing error-only `probe_check.mjs`) to see everything, not just what a filter thought mattered.

**False start — the "hang" theory**: the existing `[PERF]` frame counter (an always-on, once-per-second `EM_ASM` log in `begin_segment()`, `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) printed exactly once in both a 12s and a 20s capture, which looked like the whole engine tick loop had frozen after ~1 second (consistent with a genuine JS-main-thread deadlock, e.g. a blocking synchronous GPU-readback wait in a single-threaded Emscripten build — `gi.cpp`'s two `texture_get_data()` calls in SDFGI's cascade-update path were checked as a candidate but are dead code, `#if 0`'d out). Extending the capture to 60 seconds disproved this: `[PERF]` resumed logging normally every second from ~frame 2 onward, settling at a stable ~60fps with real per-frame draw/bind-group/pipeline counts. **The engine was never hung — it just took a bit under 2 seconds for whatever `[PERF]`'s timer got wedged behind at the very start (almost certainly the very GPU errors below, given Dawn's error-callback delivery timing) to resolve.**

**The real finding — SDFGI is not actually zero-error, it just takes longer than any prior capture window to show its errors**: the 60-second capture surfaced 7113 `[Invalid CommandBuffer]`, 7112 `[Invalid BindGroup]`, and a handful (1-19 each) of distinct, specific `GPUValidationError`s that only fire once (or once per cascade) near startup, all tracing back to SDFGI's resource creation. Every one of the thousands of "Invalid ... due to a previous error" messages is a downstream consequence of a handful of *root* errors poisoning a BindGroup/CommandBuffer that then gets reused (in some form) every frame for the rest of the session — this is the actual mechanism behind the "solid black" symptom: once SDFGI's cascades finish initializing (which takes several real frames) and hit one of the errors below, every frame thereafter submits doomed work, silently. **Root causes, in order of first occurrence**:

1. **The big one, and the architectural root cause**: `texture_create_shared()` (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) fails to create a view of `lightprobe_data` (a `2312×136`, 8-layer, `R32Uint` texture — `gi.cpp`'s `tf_octprobes`, storing packed RGBE probe data via `imageStore` as raw uint32) in the reinterpreted format `RGB9E5Ufloat` that `lightprobe_texture` (`gi.cpp:525`) requests via `TextureView.format_override = DATA_FORMAT_E5B9G9R9_UFLOAT_PACK32`: `GPUValidationError: The texture usage (...StorageBinding...) includes TextureUsage::StorageBinding, which is incompatible with the format (TextureFormat::RGB9E5Ufloat)`. Traced this all the way down: Godot's `shareable_formats` mechanism (which on Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 declares that a texture may be *viewed* in multiple bit-compatible formats — exactly this RGBE-packing-then-reinterpret-as-float trick) is **not implemented at all** by this driver beyond the one automatic sRGB pair (grep confirms `viewFormatCount`/`viewFormats` only ever gets set for that one case, `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` ~L1946) — so the real `WGPUTexture` is created with only its native format in its `viewFormats` list, and even if that were fixed, **RGB9E5Ufloat is not a WebGPU storage-texture format at all** (it can be sampled, never written via `textureStore`), so a texture carrying `StorageBinding` usage (needed for the *write* pass, which stores raw uint32 bits) can never legally have a view in this format, full stop — there is no `viewFormats` fix that helps. Checked whether a *narrower-usage* view (dropping `StorageBinding` just for this one view) could route around it: `WGPUTextureViewDescriptor` in this project's vendored `emsdk/.../webgpu.h` has no `usage` field at all (that's a newer WebGPU API surface this emdawnwebgpu version predates) — so there is no way to ask for a storage-free view either. **The exact same shape hits `cascade.light_tex`** (`gi.cpp:560`, `cascade.light_data`'s R32Uint→RGB9E5Ufloat reinterpretation, per-cascade 3D version of the same trick, 4 occurrences matching the test scene's 4 cascades) and, doubly, **`occlusion_texture`** (`gi.cpp:544`, `occlusion_data`'s R16Uint→`DATA_FORMAT_R4G4B4A4_UNORM_PACK16` reinterpretation) — which fails even earlier and more fundamentally, since `_data_format_to_wgpu()` has *no mapping at all* for `R4G4B4A4_UNORM_PACK16` (logged separately as `WGSL: Unsupported DataFormat 1`, previously filed as a standalone "very low, cosmetic" item — it is not independently fixable via a format-table addition either, since WebGPU has no native 4-bit-per-channel packed format whatsoever; it's the same architectural gap as the other two, just with no partial format match to begin with). **This is the actual root cause of the SDFGI black screen**, and it's the same general class of problem as the FSR2 image-atomics gap (Round 14) — a GLSL/SPIR-V-level trick with no direct WebGPU translation, needing either a real second-texture-plus-decode-pass architecture or a shader-level rewrite to a natively-WebGPU-compatible format, not a driver-only patch. Not attempted this round — see CURRENT STATUS item 1 for the two candidate real-fix strategies.

2. **A previously-unknown gap in Round 19's own pre-DCE storage-format fix**: `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s `dst_light` (`layout(r32ui, ...) uniform restrict uimage3D dst_light;`, GLSL binding 6 → WGSL binding 12 post-doubling) still falls back to the `RGBA8Unorm` default (`Format (TextureFormat::R32Uint) ... expected to be (TextureFormat::RGBA8Unorm)`), despite being structurally identical to the SSAO/SSIL case `_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()` was built to catch (a plain, non-array, non-combined storage image). Root cause not fully chased down this round (would need an instrumented build to see whether the SPIR-V walk finds-but-mis-keys it, or never finds it) — flagged as CURRENT STATUS item 2, along with a same-shaped `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` failure on a `RGBA16Sint`/30-layer texture (likely `lightprobe_history_scroll`).

3. Two more distinct, unrelated errors surfaced and are logged as CURRENT STATUS items 3-4 but not chased further this round: a `StorageTextureAccess::WriteOnly` vs `ReadWrite` mismatch in `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD`'s legacy spec-constant path (same bug *shape* as Round 16's DOF fix, different texture), and a `Texture view array layer range (...30...) exceeds the texture's array layer count (1)` on what's almost certainly `lightprobe_history_scroll` (a `texture_create()`-side construction bug, not a view-creation bug — the real texture apparently got built with the wrong layer count).

**No code changes this round.** All four findings above are real and reproducible, but item 1 (the actual root cause) needs an architectural decision before writing code, and items 2-4 need more targeted instrumentation than this round's time allowed to safely fix without risking a rushed, unverified patch. This is a documentation-only round: `TASKS.md`'s CURRENT STATUS block above has been restructured with these four items in priority order, replacing the old single "SDFGI: uninvestigated" entry.

**Next round candidates**: pick a fix strategy for item 1 and implement it (this is the one actually blocking the user's current settings); separately, instrument `_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()`'s call sites to close item 2's gap (likely relevant beyond just SDFGI); items 3-4 as time allows.

### Round 21 (2026-09-11, same session): the SDFGI packed-format root cause (Round 20 item 1) fixed and verified — scene still doesn't render, due to separate, already-catalogued bugs (items 2-4) surfacing next

**Chosen fix, per the user's explicit ask for "the most performant and least trouble" option**: skip the packed-uint-plus-aliased-view trick entirely on WebGPU rather than trying to build a decode-pass workaround. A decode pass was ruled out up front: neither `RGB9E5Ufloat` nor `R4G4B4A4`-shaped formats are storage-writable in WebGPU at all (checked directly against the spec's format-capability table), so a decode pass would *still* need to land on some other, actually-supported format as its target — meaning it's strictly more total work (a whole extra compute pass plus new per-update synchronization, since SDFGI's light data is recomputed continuously, not once) than just writing the decoded value directly to that same target format to begin with. Native-format storage is therefore both less code and cheaper at runtime.

**Mechanism**: added `RD::SUPPORTS_SHAREABLE_TEXTURE_FORMATS` to `rendering_device_commons.h`'s `Features` enum (the existing project-wide "how do backends differ" mechanism, per CLAUDE.md's driver-trait convention) — `true` on Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 (`has_feature()` in each of those three drivers), `false` on WebGPU. `gi.cpp`'s `SDFGI::create()` now branches on this trait for the three affected texture pairs: when unsupported, `tf_light`/`tf_octprobes` use `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT` and `tf_occlusion` uses `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_UNORM` (no `shareable_formats` entries at all), and instead of calling `texture_create_shared()` with a `format_override`, `lightprobe_texture`/`occlusion_texture`/`cascade.light_tex` are just assigned the same RID as the real underlying texture (`lightprobe_data`/`occlusion_data`/`cascade.light_data`) — a single real texture, no aliasing, already `SAMPLING_BIT | STORAGE_BIT` from `tf_sdf`'s inherited `usage_bits`, so no other C++ changes were needed for the dual storage+sampled usage.

**Why the shader-side change turned out smaller than expected**: grepped every consumer of `lightprobe_texture`/`occlusion_texture`/`cascade.light_tex` across `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`, `sdfgi_debug.glsl`, `sdfgi_debug_probes.glsl`, `gi.glsl`, and `volumetric_fog_process.glsl` — **every read site already does a plain `textureLod`/`texelFetch` sample with no manual RGBE/nibble decode anywhere**, because the whole point of the original trick was to make the *hardware* do the decode via the reinterpreted view. Since the new native-format texture is real (not aliased) but still a plain float-sampled texture from the shader's perspective, every read site needed **zero changes**. Only the *write* sites needed a new `#ifdef SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` branch (a new define, threaded through `gi.cpp`'s `GI::init()` into the `preprocess`/`direct_light`/`integrate` `ShaderRD`s' `defines` string, mirroring `fsr.cpp`'s existing `has_feature()`-gated variant-define pattern): `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `lightprobe_texture_data` (octahedral probe write, was `r32ui`), `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s `dst_light` (was `r32ui`), and `sdfgi_preprocess.glsl`'s two occlusion-packing sites — the final `dst_occlusion` store (`MODE_STORE`, was `r16ui`) and the scroll-time `src_occlusion` read-back (`MODE_SCROLL_OCCLUSION`, also reads the same packed texture, a site not obvious from `gi.cpp` alone and only found by grepping the shader itself for every storage-image declaration touching these bindings). Each native branch declares the image as `rgba16f`/`rgba8` and stores/loads a plain `vec4` instead of packing/unpacking bits — for occlusion specifically, storing the *unquantized* float directly (previously quantized to 4-bit-per-channel precision to fit the packed format) is a small free precision improvement.

**A real near-miss, corrected before committing**: initially assumed `sdfgi_preprocess.glsl`'s `MODE_SCROLL` mode's `dst_light`/`dst_light_aniso` bindings (which read a pre-packed RGBE value out of the `ProcessVoxel` SSBO and store it into a texture) also needed a native-format branch, and wrote one (including a hand-derived `rgbe8985_decode()` inverse of the packing formula). Actually verifying against `gi.cpp`'s `scroll_uniform_set` construction (`PRE_PROCESS_SCROLL`) showed this binds to `render_emission`/`render_emission_aniso` — plain, always-`R32_UINT`, never-aliased SDF-voxelization scratch buffers, completely unrelated to `cascade.light_data`. Reverted both the declaration branch and the decode function before building. (Separately: `cascade.light_data`/`light_tex` turns out to never need explicit "scrolling" at all — unlike static-geometry-derived data like albedo/occlusion, light is recomputed fully every frame by the dynamic direct-light pass regardless of cascade movement, so there's nothing to preserve.) This is a reminder that "same-named binding, similar-looking packed value" isn't sufic — the actual C++ uniform-set-construction code is the only reliable source of truth for what a binding is really wired to.

**Verification**:
- Native editor (Vulkan) rebuilt twice (before and after the correction above) and run headlessly against `cameraSim_test3` (the Round 17/20 SDFGI-isolated scratch project) — zero shader/GLSL errors both times, confirming the unchanged `#else` branch (which is everything Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 still compile) is untouched and correct.
- **Learned mid-round**: `wgsl_precompile.py`'s `SHADER_REGISTRY` is a hand-maintained mirror of `gi.cpp`'s actual define construction (documented in its own comments as such) — it does not automatically pick up new C++-computed defines. Updated `GENERAL_DEFINES_SDFGI_PREPROCESS`/`_DIRECT_LIGHT`/`_INTEGRATE` to unconditionally include `#define SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` (this script always precompiles for the WebGPU target, which always has this trait false) — without this the precompile step silently keeps testing the old, unchanged `#else` branch and proves nothing about the new code.
- Web export template rebuilt (`scons ... webgpu=yes`, with `wgsl_precompiled.gen.h` force-regenerated) — `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`/`sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`/`sdfgi_preprocess.glsl` all compile cleanly through glslang → SPIR-V → Tint → WGSL for the new native-format branch across every registered mode; the same 12 GLSL / 4 Tint pre-existing baseline failures as before (`tonemap_mobile.glsl`, `voxel_gi_debug.glsl`, `sdfgi_debug_probes.glsl`'s vertex stage — none in the files touched this round).
- `shader_corpus` (13/13), `preprocessing_tests` (192/192 + 1 pre-existing skip), `driver_unit_tests` (327/327) all green, both before and after the `MODE_SCROLL` correction.
- **Real-GPU live test, 60s capture** (this round's own methodological lesson from Round 20 applied): exported `cameraSim_test3` to a fresh build directory and probed with `WEBGPU_REAL_GPU=1`-equivalent flags. **A genuine build-pipeline trap cost real time here**: the exported `.wasm`/`.side.wasm` didn't actually contain this round's fresh code on the first two attempts, because Godot's headless `--export-release` resolves export templates from the *installed* template directory (`~/.local/share/godot/export_templates/4.7.2.stable/`, itself a symlink into `builds/4.7.2.stable/templates/` in this sandbox) rather than picking up `bin/`'s freshly-built output directly — a step some earlier round in this session must have done once and never needed again until now, since no prior round in the log mentions it. Fixed by explicitly `cp`-ing `bin/godot.web.template_release.wasm32.nothreads.dlink.zip` over the installed template before each export (confirmed via checksum match against the export's actual `.wasm` output before trusting any subsequent test). **Worth flagging for future rounds**: always checksum-verify an export's `.wasm`/`.side.wasm` against the just-built `bin/` output before trusting a "no errors" or "still errors" result — a stale template silently produces a misleading repro either way.
- With the template genuinely fresh: the original `RGB9E5Ufloat`/`R32Uint` view-creation errors and the derived `SdfgiPreprocessShaderRD` Uint/Float sample-type mismatch (a second-order consequence of the occlusion fix landing before the `MODE_SCROLL_OCCLUSION` gap above was found) are **completely gone** from a fresh 60s capture. What remains is exactly the Round 20 items 2-4 error signature (now confirmed: item 2's `RGBA16Sint` texture is `lightprobe_history_scroll`, per the live error's exact format match) — a different, already-catalogued layer of bugs, not a new regression from this round's change (confirmed: identical error text/counts appear with or without a stress-test variation of scene settings). The scene's viewport itself is still black in the screenshot (only the 2D camera-simulator UI overlay renders) — **this round fixes its target bug and is a genuine, verified step forward, but does not by itself make SDFGI visible**; items 1-3 in CURRENT STATUS are what's left standing between here and that.

**Committed**: `servers/rendering/rendering_device_commons.h`, `drivers/{vulkan,metal,d3d12,webgpu}/rendering_device_driver_*.cpp`, `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp`, `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/environment/{sdfgi_integrate,sdfgi_direct_light,sdfgi_preprocess}.glsl`, `drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompile.py`.

### Round 22 (2026-09-11, same session): items 1+2 (the shadow-copy array-layer bug) fixed and verified as one bug, not two; item 3 investigated and root-caused, fix attempted and reverted — needs bind-group-construction work, not just a WGSL-text change

**Items 1 and 2 turned out to be the same bug.** Instrumented `texture_create()` directly (temporary width/height-keyed `WEBGPU_DIAG` prints) expecting to find the cause in `gi.cpp`'s texture setup or the pre-DCE scan — but the prints never fired for the 289×272 `lightprobe_history`-shaped texture at all, meaning the real (correctly-created, 30-layer) texture was never the problem. Traced instead to `uniform_set_create()`'s read_write-storage-split shadow-texture creation (the Round 16 DOF mechanism, `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` ~line 6432): `shadow_desc.size = { orig_tex->width, orig_tex->height, orig_tex->depth }` used `orig_tex->depth` unconditionally — correct for a 3D texture, but `depth` is unused/always 1 for a 2D(-array) texture, where the real layer count lives in `layers`. `cascade.lightprobe_history_tex` (`sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `imageLoad`+`imageStore` on `lightprobe_history_texture`, genuinely read_write, a 30-layer 2D array) goes through exactly this split (this browser lacks `readonly-and-readwrite-storage-textures`), so its shadow got created with only 1 layer; the subsequent view request for all 30 failed (`arrayLayerCount: 30 exceeds ... (1)`), poisoning every `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` bind group — which is also what Round 20 had separately filed as item 1's `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` binding-18 Sint/format mismatch (that "second bug" was this same cascade, not an independent pre-DCE scan gap). **Fix**: mirror `texture_create()`'s own dimension-aware selection — `depthOrArrayLayers` is `orig_tex->depth` only for `TEXTURE_TYPE_3D`, `orig_tex->layers` otherwise. One line. Committed as `6509c07338`. Verified: `shader_corpus`/`preprocessing_tests`/`driver_unit_tests` all green; live 60s capture against the SDFGI-only scratch project shows both the array-layer-range error and the `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` Sint mismatch completely gone.

**Item 3, root-caused but not landed.** `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD`'s fragment stage, `@group(1) @binding(54)`: traced the GLSL source to `scene_forward_clustered_inc.glsl:421`, `layout(r32ui, set = 1, binding = 27) uniform restrict uimage3D geom_facing_grid;` — no `readonly`/`writeonly` qualifier (its siblings `albedo_volume_grid`/`emission_grid`/`emission_aniso_grid` are all `writeonly`; this one is genuinely read_write, used during SDFGI/VoxelGI voxelization to accumulate per-voxel facing data). GLSL binding 27 doubles to WGSL binding 54 (`split_combined_samplers()`), matching the error exactly. Root cause: `_create_module_with_spec_constants()` (the legacy spec-constant re-conversion path used for this module, `specmod#49`) re-runs Tint independently on a patched SPIR-V copy but — unlike `shader_create_from_container()` — never applies the read_write-storage-split rewrite to its own WGSL text; it only demotes vertex-stage `read_write` storage *buffers*. So this specialized module's WGSL still declares `geom_facing_grid` as `read_write`, while the pipeline's `BindGroupLayout` (built once from the *primary* module in `shader_create_from_container()`, which does split it) expects `WriteOnly` — Dawn correctly rejects the mismatch at `CreateRenderPipeline`.

Attempted fix: duplicated the WGSL-text half of the split (steps 1-4: rewrite `read_write`→`write`, insert a synthetic shadow `texture_storage_*`→`texture_*` declaration, redirect `textureLoad` calls, add the mip-level arg) directly into `_create_module_with_spec_constants()`, deliberately skipping the two lines that populate `wgsl_rw_storage_splits`/`wgsl_storage_tex_format` (those are `shader_create_from_container()`-local, already correctly populated once from the primary module, and out of scope here). This **did** eliminate the original WriteOnly/ReadWrite error — but live-testing surfaced a new, different error cluster in its place: `Number of entries (14) did not match the expected number of entries (15) for BindGroupLayoutInternal "bgl:SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD:1:set0"`, `Entry point's stage (Fragment) is not in the binding visibility in the layout (Compute)`, and a sample-type mismatch on the shadow texture (falls through to the `RGBA8Unorm`/`UnfilterableFloat` default, same gap the original split code's own comment already flags for exactly this reason — `wgsl_storage_tex_format[shadow_key]` never gets populated for a shadow discovered outside the primary module). **Conclusion: making the specialized module's WGSL *text* match the split isn't sufficient** — the actual bind-group/BindGroupLayout construction path for the legacy spec-constant pipeline was never built to know a shadow companion binding exists at all when the split is discovered independently per-specialization, so its entries, visibility, and format all come out wrong or missing. This is real plumbing work (touching wherever `uniform_set_create()`/BGL construction reads `shader->rw_storage_splits` and how that interacts with a specialization-only-discovered split), not a one-line text fix — **reverted before committing** (confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` showing zero changes to this file at session end). Left for a future round; not urgent per Round 20's own note (doesn't block the user's current per-feature settings, since VoxelGI/SDFGI voxelization specifically is what's affected).

**Verified**: `shader_corpus`/`preprocessing_tests`/`driver_unit_tests` green throughout (both the landed item 1+2 fix and, separately, before reverting the item 3 attempt). SDFGI still does not visibly render on WebGPU — item 3 is the only remaining known blocker in the render path; once fixed, worth a fresh full capture to check whether anything else was still being masked by it (same pattern as this round's own item 1/2 discovery).

**Committed**: `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` (`6509c07338` only — item 3's attempt was reverted, not committed).

### Round 23 (2026-09-11, same session): item 3's read_write-storage-split gap fixed for the legacy spec-constant path — a second, previously-hidden bug (`SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` BGL entry-count mismatch) is now the new blocker

**Fix**: `_create_module_with_spec_constants()` now takes the shader's already-populated `rw_storage_splits` map (`shader->rw_storage_splits`, built once by the primary `shader_create_from_container()` conversion) as a parameter, threaded through from all three call sites (vertex/fragment/compute). Rather than re-detecting `read_write>` storage-texture declarations independently in this respecialized-module's own WGSL text — which Round 22's reverted attempt did, and which risks discovering a different set of bindings than the primary module found if per-specialization dead-code elimination diverges — the new pass only ever splits a `(set,binding)` that's already a key in the passed-in map, using the exact shadow binding number the BGL was already built with. This guarantees the specialized module's declared bindings always agree with the BindGroupLayout Dawn validates it against, however DCE shakes out. `scene_forward_clustered_inc.glsl`'s `geom_facing_grid` (GLSL binding 27 → WGSL binding 54, genuinely read_write, used during SDFGI/VoxelGI voxelization) is exactly this case for `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD`'s fragment-stage `specmod#49`.

Verifying this took real effort to even observe correctly: the first two rebuild/export/test cycles showed *zero change at all* despite the fix compiling clean, because this scratch project's `export_presets.cfg` has `variant/extensions_support=false`, which makes Godot's web exporter resolve to `web_nothreads_release.zip` (a symlink to a completely separate, non-`dlink_enabled` build under `builds/4.7.2.stable/templates/`) — not the `web_dlink_nothreads_release.zip` this and prior rounds have been rebuilding via CLAUDE.md's documented `dlink_enabled=yes` command. Confirmed via `md5sum` on the exported `index.side.wasm` against the freshly built `bin/` wasm: they never matched, across two full rebuild cycles, until either the preset is flipped to `extensions_support=true` (matching the dlink template actually being built) or the non-dlink template is separately rebuilt. Fixed by flipping this scratch copy's preset to `extensions_support=true` — this is very likely a latent gap in the Rounds 20-22 testing methodology too (worth a future round checking whether any of those "live-verified" claims were actually exercising stale non-dlink templates); flagged here rather than silently worked around.

With that resolved, a debug build (temporary `WEBGPU_VERBOSE`-gated prints, removed before the final/committed build) confirmed the fix engages correctly (`found read_write grp=1 bnd=54 matched=1`) and the original `WriteOnly`/`ReadWrite` mismatch is completely gone from a fresh capture. It immediately exposed a second bug, though — identical in shape to what Round 22's own reverted attempt also hit: `Entry point's stage (Fragment) is not in the binding visibility in the layout (Compute)`. Root cause: the shadow-companion BGL entry's `visibility` field (both the primary construction at `shader_create_from_container()` and its duplicate in the merged-entries/legacy-spec-constant-layout path) was hardcoded to `WGPUShaderStage_Compute` — correct for every read_write-split binding this driver had seen before (all compute-only), wrong for `geom_facing_grid` which is read_write from the *fragment* stage. **Fix**: both sites now call the same `resolve_stage_visibility()` lambda already used for every other BGL entry in this function, keyed on the *write-side* binding (which the WGSL-per-stage scan, `wgsl_binding_stages`, already has correct multi-stage visibility for) rather than hardcoding a single stage. Verified: this exact error is also completely gone from a fresh capture.

**Not yet resolved — SDFGI still does not visibly render.** With both of the above fixed, a third, previously-hidden root cause surfaces: `Number of entries (14) did not match the expected number of entries (15) for [BindGroupLayoutInternal "bgl:SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD:1:set0"]` — the *exact* same error text Round 22's abandoned fix attempt also hit (see Round 22 above), for a *different* shader than the one this round targeted. That two independently-different implementations (Round 22's blind re-detection and this round's key-driven approach) both surface the identical symptom strongly suggests it's a genuine, separate, pre-existing bug in how this shader's BGL entries are constructed (`SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` likely has more than one read_write-split binding in the same set, and something about how they're merged/deduplicated drops one) — not an artifact of either fix's specific implementation. Not investigated further this round; a fresh full capture after any future fix here should check whether anything *else* was still being masked (same pattern as Round 22's own item 1/2 discovery and this round's own item 3 discovery).

**Verified**: `shader_corpus` (13/13), `preprocessing_tests` (192/192 + 1 pre-existing skip), `driver_unit_tests` (327/327) all green. Live 65s capture against the SDFGI-only scratch project (`cameraSim_test3`, `extensions_support=true`): the two targeted error signatures (`WriteOnly`/`ReadWrite` mismatch, stage-visibility mismatch) are completely gone; a screenshot confirms the UI renders correctly but the 3D viewport remains solid black, consistent with the newly-surfaced `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` blocker. Native/Vulkan editor is unaffected by construction — every change this round is confined to `drivers/webgpu/`, which per CLAUDE.md isn't compiled into non-web builds at all.

**Committed**: `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` + `.h`, and this `TASKS.md` update (`559d299b1f`).

### Round 24 (2026-09-11, same session): the `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` BGL entry-count mismatch was never a BGL-construction bug at all — it was a gap in cross-variant bind group reuse

**Investigation**: rather than guess, instrumented three points directly: (1) the `rw_infos` scan in `shader_create_from_container()` (which bindings get detected as read_write per shader stage/variant), (2) the primary BGL-construction loop's final entry list, and (3) `uniform_set_create()`'s shadow-binding loop (whether `us->bound_textures` actually has the write-side texture for each split). All three came back clean: the BGL for `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD:1:set0` was built with exactly 15 entries — matching Dawn's own "expected" count in the error text — and `uniform_set_create()` successfully created every shadow entry it attempted. The mismatch had to be happening somewhere else entirely.

It was. `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD:0`, `:1`, `:2`, `:3` are four `#ifdef`-selected modes of the *same* `ShaderRD`, all sharing one declared uniform interface — Godot's RD layer calls `uniform_set_create()` **once** for the shared resource set and reuses that single `WGUniformSet` across all four modes' pipelines via `_get_compatible_bind_group()`, which adapts the cached bind group to whichever mode's BindGroupLayout is actually being dispatched against. The four modes don't all detect the same bindings as read_write in their own DCE'd WGSL, though — it depends on which `#ifdef` branches that specific mode's entry point actually reaches, not the binding number alone. Mode `0`'s own `rw_infos` scan found bindings 18 and 20 as read_write (never touching binding 16); mode `1`'s found 16 and 20 (never touching 18). So the `WGUniformSet` created against mode `0` has a shadow entry at binding 19 (for its own binding 18) but **no** entry at binding 17 (mode `1`'s shadow for binding 16) — and when `_get_compatible_bind_group()` adapts that cached set for mode `1`'s pipeline, its existing logic correctly *filters out* the now-unwanted binding-19 entry (not in mode `1`'s `target_bindings`) but had **no logic to add** the binding-17 entry mode `1` actually needs, since mode `0`'s cached set never had one to filter *in*. Net effect: 15 (mode 0's real count) − 1 (dropped 19) + 0 (never gained 17) = 14 — exactly the "Number of entries (14) did not match the expected number of entries (15)" observed.

**Fix**: extracted the shadow-texture/view-creation logic (previously inline in `uniform_set_create()`'s own shadow loop) into a new shared helper, `_create_rw_shadow_bind_entry()` (declared in the header, defined immediately before `uniform_set_create()`). `_get_compatible_bind_group()` now walks `p_target_shader->rw_storage_splits` after its existing filter step and, for any `(set,binding)→shadow_binding` the filtered list doesn't already contain, looks up the original texture in `p_us->bound_textures` (present regardless of which mode created the cached set, since it's a plain, always-present primary uniform entry — every mode declares the same texture at the same binding number, it's only the *read_write-ness* that varies) and calls the new helper to build and append the missing shadow entry on the fly.

**Verified**: `shader_corpus` (13/13), `preprocessing_tests` (192/192 + 1 pre-existing skip), `driver_unit_tests` (327/327) all green. Live 65s capture against the SDFGI-only scratch project (`cameraSim_test3`, fresh-build `md5sum`-confirmed against `bin/` throughout every iteration per Round 23's own lesson): the `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` "Number of entries" error is completely gone — zero occurrences across the full capture, down from a steady stream every prior round. Native/Vulkan is unaffected by construction (change confined to `drivers/webgpu/`).

**SDFGI still does not visibly render.** Clearing this exposed three more, previously-masked errors — now items 1-3 in the CURRENT STATUS findings list above: `GiShaderRD:0:set0` binding 28's Uint/Float sample-type mismatch (this task's *original* Round-1 finding, never actually fixed, just buried under louder errors for 23 rounds), `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD`'s "No attachment was specified" on two pipeline variants (now the dominant source of console noise — its cascading "invalid due to previous error" spam accounts for the large majority of every capture's error count), and one single-occurrence texture-view-dimension mismatch. None of these three are caused by or related to this round's fix — confirmed by diffing error signatures before/after; only the `SdfgiIntegrateShaderRD` line disappeared, everything else is identical.

### Round 25 (2026-09-11, same session): `GiShaderRD`'s Uint/Float sample-type mismatch — this task's original Round-1 finding, fixed

**This is the oldest open finding in this entire task chain** — first observed in Round 1/2 (this file's very first Task 9.5 live-run notes), 23 rounds ago, and never actually fixed; it was simply outlasted by louder, earlier-firing errors every round until Round 24 cleared enough noise for it to surface as a real blocker again.

**Investigation**: confirmed via a fresh-build live capture that the error text matches Round 24's filed finding exactly: `GiShaderRD:0:set0`/`:2:set0`, `None of the supported sample types (Float|UnfilterableFloat) of [Texture "unnamed#1 4x4x1 mip1 fmt18 usage0x6"] match the expected sample types (Uint)`. `gi.glsl` binding 14 (WGSL binding 28 after `split_combined_samplers()`'s doubling) is declared `layout(set = 0, binding = 14) uniform utexture2D voxel_gi_buffer` — genuinely unsigned-integer. The bound texture is a tiny 4×4 placeholder, which pointed straight at a default-texture fallback rather than any DCE/WGSL-scan gap (the pattern every prior round in this chain had been finding).

**Root cause**: `GI::process_gi()` (`servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp:4256`) binds `p_voxel_gi_buffer` at binding 14 when valid, else falls back to `RendererRD::TextureStorage::DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_BLACK` — a `Float`/`Unorm`-format placeholder. `p_voxel_gi_buffer` is only valid when VoxelGI is actually active (`render_forward_clustered.cpp:2199`: `rb_data->has_voxelgi() ? rb_data->get_voxelgi() : RID()`); the user's real project uses SDFGI without VoxelGI probes, so this is always the fallback path. Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 don't validate a bound texture's sample type against the shader's declared type at descriptor/bind-group-creation time, so binding a Float-format texture where the shader declares `utexture2D` was silently tolerated there (arguably latent UB, but never observably wrong); WebGPU validates this strictly and rejects it outright. Confirmed this is a **shared-engine bug, not WebGPU-specific**: `texture_storage.h`/`.cpp` already define `DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_2D_UINT` (an `RGBA8_UINT`, all-zero 4×4 default) — grepped the entire `servers/`/`drivers/` tree and found it has **zero callers anywhere** except its own creation. It was added to the engine as infrastructure for exactly this kind of situation and never actually wired up.

**Fix**: one-line swap, `gi.cpp:4256`, `DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_BLACK` → `DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_2D_UINT`. No other call site in `gi.cpp` binds this texture at binding 14 (the only other `binding = 14` in the file is an unrelated `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` storage-image binding in a different uniform set). Commit `8c36d16ae7`.

**Verified**: native editor + web template both rebuild clean. `shader_corpus` (13/13), `preprocessing_tests` (192/192 + 1 skip), `driver_unit_tests` (327/327) all green. Live 65s capture against `cameraSim_test3` (fresh-build `md5sum`-confirmed per Round 23's lesson): the sample-type error signature is completely gone — 0 occurrences, down from firing on every `GiShaderRD` bind group creation in the baseline capture; total error count essentially unchanged (10740 → 10742) since it's now dominated entirely by item 2's unrelated cascading spam. Native/Vulkan unaffected by construction (the fallback texture is all-zero either way; the fix only makes its *declared type* match what the shader already expected, which is strictly more correct on every backend, not a behavior change).

**SDFGI still does not visibly render** (confirmed via screenshot: UI/gizmo overlay renders correctly, 3D viewport is solid black) — Round 24's item 2 (`SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD` "No attachment was specified" on pipeline variants 8 and 17, plus its cascading "invalid due to previous error" spam, now clearly the dominant remaining error source) is the sole documented blocker left. Item 3 (the single-occurrence `TextureViewDimension` mismatch) is also still open and, per this round's fresh capture, is itself `GiShaderRD:2:set0` binding 2 (`sdf_cascades[]`, expects a 3D view, got a 4×4 `RGBA8Unorm` 2D texture) — very likely the *same* default-texture-fallback bug shape as this round's fix, just for dimensionality instead of sample type, on a different binding. Worth checking first when picking up item 3.

**Committed**: `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` + `.h` (`f102b9b0fe`), and this `TASKS.md` update.

### Round 26 (2026-09-11, same session): `SceneForwardClusteredShaderRD`'s SDF-voxelization pipeline had no attachment on either side — same bug class as Round 12/13, fixed in four places

**Root cause**: `SHADER_VERSION_DEPTH_PASS_WITH_SDF`'s fragment stage (`MODE_RENDER_DEPTH` + `MODE_RENDER_SDF`, with neither `MODE_RENDER_MATERIAL` nor `MODE_RENDER_NORMAL_ROUGHNESS`) declares **zero color outputs** — confirmed by reading `scene_forward_clustered.glsl`'s fragment-output section directly: the `#ifdef MODE_RENDER_MATERIAL`/`#ifdef MODE_RENDER_NORMAL_ROUGHNESS` blocks are both skipped, and the `#else // RENDER DEPTH` branch (which declares `frag_color`) never runs either since we're inside `#ifdef MODE_RENDER_DEPTH`. This shader writes exclusively via `imageStore`/`imageAtomicOr` side effects into `geom_facing_grid`/`albedo_volume_grid` during SDFGI's voxelization pass (`RenderForwardClustered::_render_sdfgi()`, which renders the scene from 3 orthogonal axes into these storage images — see Round 1's earliest notes for this pass's origin). `_render_sdfgi()` paired it with a genuinely empty (zero-attachment, zero-depth) framebuffer via `framebuffer_create_empty()`, unconditionally — needing `SUPPORTS_FRAGMENT_SHADER_WITH_ONLY_SIDE_EFFECTS`, the exact same requirement Round 12/13 already found and fixed for `ClusterRenderShaderRD`/`ClusterBuilderRD`, just never applied to this shader. WebGPU rejects zero-attachment render pipelines/passes outright (`"No attachment was specified"` at pipeline creation, `"Render pass has no attachments"` at draw time), and once poisoned, every subsequent `BindGroup`/`CommandBuffer` for the rest of the session fails too (`"is invalid due to a previous error"`) — by Round 24/25, this was confirmed as the single largest source of console noise (thousands of cascading errors per capture).

**Identified via variant-index arithmetic, not guesswork**: `scene_shader_forward_clustered.cpp`'s constructor pushes 9 `ShaderRD::VariantDefine`s per `ubershader` iteration (0 and 1), so `SHADER_VERSION_DEPTH_PASS_WITH_SDF` (the 9th push in each iteration) lands at raw mode index 8 (non-ubershader) and 17 (ubershader) — exactly matching Round 24's filed "pipelines 11, 13 (variant 17) and 14, 15 (variant 8)" (the `pipe#N` numbers are separate, monotonic pipeline-instance ids; `variant N` is this raw `ShaderRD` mode index, confirmed against this driver's own `desc.label` convention and console output elsewhere in this session showing shader modules labeled `ShaderName:variant:stgN`).

**Fix — four places that all have to agree once one changes, mirroring Round 12/13's `ClusterBuilderRD` pattern exactly**:
1. `scene_shader_forward_clustered.cpp`'s constructor: added `needs_dummy_attachment_define` (`"\n#define NEEDS_DUMMY_COLOR_ATTACHMENT\n"` when `!SUPPORTS_FRAGMENT_SHADER_WITH_ONLY_SIDE_EFFECTS`), appended to both `SHADER_VERSION_DEPTH_PASS_WITH_SDF` push_backs.
2. `scene_forward_clustered.glsl`: when `NEEDS_DUMMY_COLOR_ATTACHMENT` is defined (and neither `MODE_RENDER_MATERIAL` nor `MODE_RENDER_NORMAL_ROUGHNESS`), declares `layout(location = 0) out vec4 dummy_color_attachment;` and writes `vec4(0.0)` to it at the top of the `MODE_RENDER_SDF` block — never read, exists purely to give WGSL an entry-point output to satisfy the "one real attachment" requirement.
3. `scene_shader_forward_clustered.cpp`'s `_create_pipeline()`: added a `case PIPELINE_VERSION_DEPTH_PASS_WITH_SDF:` to the blend-state switch, using `RD::PipelineColorBlendState::create_disabled(1)` when the capability is unsupported (falls through to the existing zero-attachment default otherwise, unchanged on Vulkan/Metal/D3D12).
4. `render_forward_clustered.cpp`'s `_render_sdfgi()`: when the capability is unsupported, creates a real `RGBA8Unorm` color texture (`RD::TEXTURE_USAGE_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_BIT`, sized to the pass's `fb_size`) and `framebuffer_create({color_tex})` instead of `framebuffer_create_empty(fb_size)` — added a new `sdfgi_framebuffer_color_cache` member (mirroring the existing `sdfgi_framebuffer_size_cache`) to own the texture's lifetime, freed alongside it in the destructor.

**A fifth site was needed too, found only by testing** — pipeline *pre-warming*, not just the real draw-time path: `_mesh_compile_pipelines_for_surface()` eagerly pre-creates `PIPELINE_VERSION_DEPTH_PASS_WITH_SDF` pipelines whenever `global_pipeline_data_required.use_sdfgi` is set, against two framebuffer shapes — `_get_depth_framebuffer_format_for_pipeline(false, false)` (depth-only, 0 color attachments) and `framebuffer_format_create_empty()` (truly empty) — neither of which is what `_render_sdfgi()` actually draws with once fix #4 above lands. Because `blend_state` is now fixed at `create_disabled(1)` for this `PipelineVersion` unconditionally (fix #3), and a shader's `fragment_output_mask` is fixed by the compiled module (not by which framebuffer a given pipeline-creation call targets), every pre-warm attempt against these two stale shapes produced a **new**, different error: `"Mismatch fragment shader output mask (1) and framebuffer color output mask (0)"` at `rendering_device.cpp:5176`'s `render_pipeline_create()`, caught by `_create_pipeline()`'s own `ERR_FAIL_COND(pipeline.is_null())`. Root-caused via temporary `print_line()` instrumentation (removed before commit) logging `framebuffer_format_id`/`blend_attachments`/`ubershader` right before each `_create_pipeline()` call for this `PipelineVersion` — showed pre-warm pipelines being created against format ids `4`/`8` (the two stale shapes) *before* `_render_sdfgi()`'s own real framebuffer (format id `2`) was ever created. **Fix**: added `_get_sdf_color_framebuffer_format_for_pipeline()`, building the exact same single-`RGBA8Unorm`-attachment `AttachmentFormat` list `_render_sdfgi()`'s real texture produces; `_mesh_compile_pipelines_for_surface()`'s SDFGI pre-warm branch now uses this (instead of the two stale shapes) when the capability is unsupported. Because `framebuffer_format_create()` deduplicates identical attachment lists to the same `FramebufferFormatID`, this pre-warmed pipeline is now genuinely reused at runtime by `_render_sdfgi()` (confirmed via the same instrumentation: pre-warm and real-draw calls both landed on format id `2`) rather than being wasted work — arguably *more* correct than the original two-shape pre-warm even setting WebGPU aside, since one of those two shapes was never actually used by any real call site to begin with.

**Verified**: native editor (`scons platform=linuxbsd target=editor dev_build=yes`) and web template (`scons platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes opengl3=no threads=no`) both rebuild clean. All three regression suites green (13/13, 192/192 + 1 skip, 327/327). Fresh-build `md5sum`-confirmed live capture (65s) against `cameraSim_test3` (the user's real project, SDFGI-only scratch copy): zero occurrences of `"No attachment was specified"`, `"Render pass has no attachments"`, or the fragment-output-mask mismatch — down from being the single largest error source. Native/Vulkan re-confirmed unaffected via a headless native-editor run against the same scratch project with SDFGI enabled (`SUPPORTS_FRAGMENT_SHADER_WITH_ONLY_SIDE_EFFECTS` is `true` there, so all five new branches are dead code, identical in spirit to Round 12/13's own verification) — zero errors in the log.

**SDFGI still does not visibly render** (screenshot: UI/gizmo overlay renders correctly, 3D viewport solid black) — clearing this bug exposed the already-catalogued `GiShaderRD` `TextureViewDimension` mismatch (filed in Round 24, re-confirmed in Round 25 as `GiShaderRD:2:set0` binding 2, `sdf_cascades[]`, a 4×4 `RGBA8Unorm` 2D placeholder bound where a 3D view is expected) as the **sole remaining documented blocker** — now the dominant error source by a wide margin (~10,700 total errors per 65s capture, almost entirely cascading `"is invalid due to a previous error"` spam from this one root cause, versus a handful of distinct root causes in earlier rounds). Round 25 already flagged this as very likely the same "wrong-typed default-texture fallback" bug shape as its own `voxel_gi_buffer` fix (`gi.cpp:4256`) — worth checking `gi.cpp`'s `sdf_cascades[]`/unused-cascade-slot fallback for a 2D default where a 3D one (`DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_3D_WHITE`/`_BLACK`, already used correctly elsewhere in this same file) is needed, before assuming a new investigation is required.

**Committed**: `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/forward_clustered/render_forward_clustered.cpp` + `.h`, `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/forward_clustered/scene_shader_forward_clustered.cpp`, `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/forward_clustered/scene_forward_clustered.glsl` (`02230c1782`).

### Round 27 (2026-09-11, same session): two pre-DCE-scan blind spots (array types, sample type) fixed — **SDFGI genuinely renders end-to-end for the first time in this task's history**

Started from Round 26's `GiShaderRD:2:set0` binding 2 (`sdf_cascades[]`) `TextureViewDimension` mismatch, flagged as the sole remaining blocker and a strong suspected match for Round 25's "wrong-typed default-texture fallback" bug shape. That lead turned out to be a red herring — `gi.cpp`'s cascade-slot fallback textures were already correctly 3D-shaped throughout (verified by reading every `DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_3D_WHITE` call site in the file) — the real bug was one level deeper, in the driver's own WGSL-scan/fallback machinery, and empirical instrumentation (not static reading) is what actually found it.

**Diagnostic approach**: added temporary `print_line()` instrumentation at three points — the live post-Tint WGSL-text dimension scan, the merged-BindGroupLayout-construction loop, and `uniform_set_create()`'s texture-binding path — all gated on `shader->name.contains("Gi")`, then rebuilt the web template and ran it against `cameraSim_test3` (a prior session's SDFGI-only scratch copy of the user's real project, reused from `/tmp/.../780c0d0a.../scratchpad/`) via the existing `probe_alllogs_r26.mjs` tool. First attempt produced garbled output — `String::utf8(tp).substr(0, 24)` on a non-null-terminated pointer into the middle of the WGSL text pulled in an arbitrary tail (including embedded newlines that Chrome's console then split across multiple lines), making the printed `dim=` values impossible to attribute correctly to the right binding. Rewrote the instrumentation to print only bounded, already-owned data (`name`/`stage`/`grp`/`bnd`/`dim` as plain integers, no raw pointer slicing) before trusting any output — a mistake worth remembering for any future instrumentation of this same scan code.

**Bug 1 — `_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()` never unwraps `OpTypeArray`/`OpTypeRuntimeArray`.** The clean instrumentation showed, for `GiShaderRD:2` (confirmed via variant-index arithmetic to be `MODE_SDFGI`): `sdf_cascades`/`light_cascades`/`occlusion_texture` (GLSL bindings 1, 2, 5) all correctly scanned as dimension 3D — but `aniso0_cascades`/`aniso1_cascades` (GLSL bindings 3, 4) were **missing from `wgsl_tex_dims` entirely**, silently defaulting to the wrong `WGPUTextureViewDimension_2D`. Root cause, confirmed by reading `gi.glsl`: `aniso0_cascades[SDFGI_MAX_CASCADES]`/`aniso1_cascades[SDFGI_MAX_CASCADES]` are declared but have **zero reads anywhere in the file, in any mode** (grep-confirmed) — genuinely dead code, always DCE'd, so the live post-Tint WGSL scan can never find them, and this is exactly the shape Round 19's pre-DCE fallback was built to catch. But `_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()` resolves a `UniformConstant` variable's type by looking up its pointer's pointee directly in `image_types` (a map of `OpTypeImage` result-ids) — for a GLSL *array* of textures, the pointee is an `OpTypeArray` wrapping the real `OpTypeImage`, not the image type itself, so the lookup always missed and the function silently skipped every array-of-textures binding, not just this one (Round 19's own fix never hit this because every binding it targeted that round happened to be a scalar, non-array storage image). **Fix**: added `OpTypeArray`/`OpTypeRuntimeArray` (opcodes 28/29) to the type-collection pass, building an `array_to_elem` map, and unwrap one level through it before the `image_types` lookup (SPIR-V/GLSL never nest arrays of opaque handle types, so one level is sufficient).
