This alone fixed `aniso0_cascades`/`aniso1_cascades` and, as a bonus, another previously-missing binding at WGSL bnd 34 (`GiShaderRD`'s `voxel_gi_octree`-family array binding) that turned out to need the exact same fix.

**Bug 2 — the pre-DCE fallback only ever recovered storage-image *format*/*dimension*, never a plain sampled texture's *sample type*.** Fixing Bug 1 and re-testing (60s+ capture, per Round 20's own established lesson) eliminated all dimension-mismatch errors but surfaced a new one: `None of the supported sample types (Uint) of [Texture "...fmt21..."] match the expected sample types (Float)`, ~10,700 cascading errors per capture — a different bug the dimension mismatch had been masking. Traced (same instrumentation technique, now also logging in `_get_compatible_bind_group()`'s texture-sample-type-adaptation branch) to `voxel_gi_buffer` — Round 25's own fix target (`utexture2D`, i.e. Uint sample type) — being DCE'd out of `MODE_SDFGI`'s WGSL too (SDFGI-only scenes never read it), exactly like Bug 1's textures. Unlike dimension/format, `wgsl_tex_sample_type` had no pre-DCE fallback recovery mechanism at all — Round 19 never extended it that far — so `MODE_SDFGI`'s own BindGroupLayout defaulted this binding's sample type to `Float` (the code's existing default for "no evidence found," correct for the common case, wrong here). `_get_compatible_bind_group()`'s cross-mode rebind logic (Round 24's domain) then tried to reuse the correctly-Uint cached texture from `MODE_VOXEL_GI`'s bind group against `MODE_SDFGI`'s wrongly-Float target layout — and its texture-sample-type-adaptation code only ever handled the specific `Depth→Float` case (an older, narrower fix), with no generic Uint/Sint-vs-Float fallback. **Fix**: added a `sample_type` field to `PreDceImageInfo`, resolved via a new `OpTypeInt` (opcode 21) signedness table cross-referenced against `OpTypeImage`'s `SampledType` operand (deliberately left `Undefined` — not asserted to `Float` — for anything that doesn't resolve to an integer type, since `Float` is already the correct existing default for a real float image), and seeded into `wgsl_tex_sample_type` the same insert-if-absent way the other two fields already are.

**Both fixes are in the same function/call site Round 19 established, extending it rather than adding new machinery** — `_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()` (declaration/type-collection pass) and its single seeding call site in `shader_create_from_container()`. All temporary `print_line()` instrumentation removed before the final build/commit.

**Verified**: native editor and web template (`scons platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes opengl3=no threads=no`) both rebuild clean (also rebuilt `tint_convert_cli`). All three regression suites green (13/13, 192/192 + 1 skip, 327/327). Export-template freshness confirmed via `md5sum` on `index.side.wasm` against the freshly-built `bin/` wasm before every test (per Round 23's lesson) — this scratch project's `export_presets.cfg` already has `extensions_support=true` (the correct/dlink preset), so no repeat of Round 23's stale-template trap. Fresh 65s live capture against `cameraSim_test3` (SDFGI-only): **zero console errors**, and a screenshot confirms a genuinely rendered, lit 3D scene (a character model with visible shading detail) — not the solid-black viewport every prior round left behind. Broader sanity pass: a second scratch copy with SDFGI+Glow+Fog+Adjustment all enabled together also came back at zero errors (screenshot shows a heavily over-exposed but non-erroring render — plausibly a legitimate scene/tonemap interaction for this particular settings combination, not a validation error, and out of scope for this round). A third combined test, SDFGI+SSR+SSAO+Glow, reproduced ~10,500 cascading errors — but the sole distinct root error is the **already-documented, pre-existing** `SsaoInterleaveShaderRD` R8Unorm-vs-RGBA8Unorm bug (item 8 in the CURRENT STATUS list, known since Round 17/19) — confirmed independent of this round's changes (no `GiShaderRD`/SDFGI-related error appears anywhere in that capture) and not a new regression.

**Not yet done**: the user's own hands-on confirmation on their real machine/project — everything above is sandbox-verified only, and per this task's own repeated history (Task 9.5's very first sandbox-vs-real-hardware gap), that's the step that actually closes this out. The `SsaoInterleaveShaderRD` bug (item 8) remains open, low-priority, unrelated to SDFGI.

**Committed**: `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` (`_extract_pre_dce_storage_image_info()` array-unwrap + sample-type extensions) — `fca56f2adf`.

### Round 28 (2026-09-11, same session): `SsaoInterleaveShaderRD` R8Unorm-vs-RGBA8Unorm bug fixed — root cause was a genuine, pre-existing upstream inconsistency, not a WebGPU driver gap

Triggered by a corrected priority assessment (see the docs-only commit `979f46537c` just before this round): the user reported the console gets spammed by this error with **SSAO enabled alone** on their real machine, not just under multi-effect stacking as every prior round (17/19/27) had assumed. The repo's own `errors-chrome.txt` scratch capture (`grep -n "SsaoInterleaveShaderRD" errors-chrome.txt`) confirms the exact signature: `Format (TextureFormat::R8Unorm) of [Texture ...] expected to be (TextureFormat::RGBA8Unorm)` against `bgl:SsaoInterleaveShaderRD:1:set0`.

**Root cause, found without needing bind-group-entry instrumentation this time** (the TASKS.md-suggested next step from Round 19) — reading the actual GLSL and its real C++ texture allocation side by side was enough: `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/effects/ssao_interleave.glsl:27` declares `dest_image` as `layout(rgba8, ...) uniform restrict writeonly image2D` (upstream Godot source, confirmed unmodified by this fork via `git log` on the file), but `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/ss_effects.cpp:1124` allocates the real backing texture (`RB_SCOPE_SSAO`/`RB_FINAL`, GLSL's `ao_final`) as single-channel `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R8_UNORM`. This is a genuine, pre-existing format inconsistency in stock/upstream Godot — not something this fork introduced, and not a WebGPU-specific behavioral gap. Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 tolerate a storage image's declared SPIR-V format not exactly matching its real bound resource's format (looser validation than WebGPU's spec-mandated strict bind-time check), so this has silently "worked" everywhere except WebGPU. Confirmed safe to fix by checking every read/write site: `imageStore(dest_image, ..., vec4(ao))`/`vec4(avg)` always broadcasts one scalar to all 4 components (both `MODE_SMART` and non-smart paths), and every downstream read (`scene_forward_clustered.glsl`'s `ao_buffer` sampling, both the multiview and non-multiview branches) only ever reads `.r` — so the real data has always been single-channel in practice; only the *declared* storage format was wrong. Also checked the sibling `ssao_importance_map.glsl`/`ssil_importance_map.glsl` (writing `RB_IMPORTANCE_MAP`/`RB_IMPORTANCE_PONG`, also real `R8_UNORM`) — both correctly declare `layout(r8, ...)`, confirming this mismatch is specific to `ssao_interleave.glsl`/`RB_FINAL`, not a broader pattern across `ss_effects.cpp`.

**Fix**: changed `ss_effects.cpp:1124`'s `create_texture(RB_SCOPE_SSAO, RB_FINAL, ...)` call from `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R8_UNORM` to `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R8G8B8A8_UNORM`, matching what the shader already declares. This is shared engine code, applied unconditionally (not gated behind a WebGPU-only driver trait) — deliberately, since the fix makes the real resource match the shader's own long-standing declaration everywhere, which is the more-correct state regardless of backend, not a WebGPU-specific workaround. Cost: a real 4x memory increase on this one specific texture (8 bits/texel → 32 bits/texel) — small in absolute terms (a per-view AO buffer, not a full G-buffer-sized resource) and applied equally on every backend.

**Verified**: native editor and web template both rebuild clean. All three regression suites green (13/13, 192/192 + 1 skip, 327/327). Export-template freshness confirmed via `md5sum` on `index.side.wasm` against the freshly-built `bin/` wasm (per Round 23's lesson) for both live tests below — note this session's scratch copies needed `variant/extensions_support` flipped from the real project's own `false` to `true` in the scratch `export_presets.cfg` to actually exercise the `dlink_enabled=yes` build (the exact Round 23 trap; the audit Round 27 flagged as still-outstanding for the scratch-copy workflow remains outstanding). Two fresh 60s live captures against new scratch copies of the user's real project: (1) **SSAO enabled alone** (isolating exactly the user's reported repro, a combination no prior round had actually tested standalone) — **zero console errors**, screenshot confirms plausible AO contact-shadowing under the character model and prop boxes; (2) SSAO+SSR+Glow+SDFGI all stacked together — also **zero console errors** (this is the exact combo Round 27 found still reproducing ~10,500 cascading errors from this bug — now clean), though the screenshot is heavily over-exposed/blown-out white, matching the same pre-existing, non-erroring scene/tonemap interaction Round 27 already flagged as out-of-scope for this settings combination (not a regression from this fix).

**Committed**: `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/ss_effects.cpp` — `9774f2a1d6`.

### Round 29 (2026-09-11, same session): new SDFGI brightness-runaway bug reported — investigation started, interrupted by a session rate limit before reaching a root cause

Immediately after Round 27/28's milestone, the user reported a new symptom on their real machine: with SDFGI enabled, the scene's brightness/exposure climbs continuously until the whole 3D viewport goes solid white. It resets to normal whenever any SDFGI field is changed (expected — a field change reallocates cascades and clears the history/light textures in `gi.cpp`'s `SDFGI::create()`), then climbs toward white again. Unlike every other bug this task has chased, there is **no console error at all** — this is a pure visual/algorithmic issue.

This round's plan (see item 11 in "Findings still open" above for the full writeup) was to first determine scope — does the same drift happen on the native Vulkan editor build with the identical scratch project and settings over the same multi-minute duration? — before writing any code, since SDFGI has never rendered successfully for more than a few seconds anywhere in this task's history on any backend, making it entirely plausible this is a pre-existing upstream Godot behavior nobody has had the chance to notice until Round 27 got it actually running.

**What was actually accomplished before the interruption**: confirmed (via a fresh live capture against a scratch copy of the user's real project, using a new script left behind at `webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/probe_brightness_trend_r29.mjs`) that the drift reproduces with **zero console errors**, matching the user's report exactly. The agent had just begun the brightness-trend capture (intended to chart exposure over time, and to repeat the same test on the native Vulkan editor for comparison) when it hit a session rate limit and was terminated. No code was changed; no other findings were reached.

**A hypothesis worth testing first, not yet verified**: the RGBE9995-packed storage path (used on Vulkan/Metal/D3D12, and on WebGPU before Round 21) clamps every value to a fixed ceiling before packing (`clamp(light_total.r, 0.0, 65408.0)` in `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s non-`SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` branch) as an inherent side effect of the encoding itself. Round 21's WebGPU-only native-float storage path (`sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s and `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `#ifdef SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` branches) writes the raw computed float with no equivalent bound. If that implicit ceiling was accidentally load-bearing for the whole self-illumination feedback loop's numerical stability, its absence on WebGPU alone could explain unbounded growth there while the packed-format backends stay self-limiting. This is a lead to test via the Vulkan comparison above, not a confirmed diagnosis — do not add a clamp speculatively without first confirming the Vulkan-vs-WebGPU split, since if Vulkan also drifts, this hypothesis is wrong and the real cause is upstream/algorithmic instead.

**Not yet done**: the Vulkan-vs-WebGPU comparison (the critical next step), root cause, and any fix. **Committed**: nothing (no code or doc changes were made by the interrupted round itself — this Round 29 writeup and item 11 above were added afterward, directly, to preserve the findings before the working state was lost).

### Round 30 (2026-09-12, new session): scope conclusively established as WebGPU-only; leading clamp hypothesis tested and disproved; two SCons build-staleness traps found; root cause still open

Picked up exactly where Round 29 was cut off: item 11's mandatory first step, the Vulkan-vs-WebGPU brightness-drift comparison.

**Setup**: built a fresh SDFGI-only scratch copy of the user's real project (`~/Downloads/cameraSim_2026-09-09_10-26-06/testing` copied to a scratchpad dir, `.godot`/`.idea` stripped, re-imported), with `sdfgi_enabled = true` added directly to `main.tscn`'s `Environment` sub-resource (the only change from the shipped project — no other effects were touched) and viewport shrunk to 640×360 (from 2500×1406) purely to keep the native-Vulkan PNG-sequence capture's disk footprint manageable. `variant/extensions_support` flipped `false`→`true` in the scratch copy's `export_presets.cfg` (the standing Round 23 trap, still not fixed at the workflow level — see priority 3 above).

**Native Vulkan capture methodology, new for this task**: `bin/godot.linuxbsd.editor.dev.x86_64 --path <scratch> --rendering-driver vulkan --write-movie <dir>/frame.png --fixed-fps 60 --disable-vsync --quit-after 18000` against the sandbox's real X11 display (`DISPLAY=:0`, confirmed genuine NVIDIA RTX 4080 SUPER Vulkan access, not software rendering) — `--fixed-fps` decouples simulated time from wall-clock speed (this run did 5 sim-minutes of frames in under 10 real minutes), and `.png` as the `--write-movie` extension produces a numbered PNG sequence rather than a video container, letting individual timestamps be inspected directly. `--headless` was *not* usable for this — its display driver is forced to `dummy` (software/null rendering), which would not have exercised real Vulkan at all.

**Result — scope conclusively established**: matched 300-second (5-minute) captures, native Vulkan (PNG sequence, sampled at the same wall-clock-equivalent timestamps) vs. WebGPU (`probe_brightness_trend_r29.mjs`, 15 screenshots at 20s intervals) against the *identical* scratch project and settings. **Native Vulkan: pixel-stable from t=0 to t=300s, zero errors, no visible drift whatsoever.** **WebGPU: already fully blown to solid white by t=100s, stays solid white through t=300s, zero console errors** (matching the user's report exactly — this is a silent, non-erroring bug on WebGPU specifically). This settles the question Round 29 posed: **not** a pre-existing upstream SDFGI algorithm issue — a real, WebGPU-only bug, squarely in `drivers/webgpu/`'s scope.

**The leading hypothesis was tested directly and found insufficient.** Added `clamp(..., 0.0, 65408.0)` (matching the packed RGBE9995 path's own implicit ceiling, confirmed identical in both `rgbe_encode()` in `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` and the inline packing block in `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`) to all four native-format `imageStore` sites gated by `#ifdef SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT`. Rebuilt the web template, re-exported the scratch project, re-ran the identical 300s capture: **still fully white by t=100s, no visible difference from before the fix.**

**Two real build-staleness traps cost significant time before this negative result could be trusted**, both now documented in item 11 above in full: (1) `drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompiled.gen.h`'s SCons dependency list doesn't include the `.glsl` sources it precompiles, so it silently never regenerates on a shader-only edit; (2) even after force-regenerating it, the `.cpp` file that `#include`s it (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) didn't get recompiled, and even after manually deleting *that* object file and getting it to recompile, the containing static library (`bin/obj/drivers/libdrivers.*.a`) and the final linked `.wasm`/`.zip` didn't get relinked either — three separate stale-artifact layers, each needing manual deletion, matching the same class of bug already documented elsewhere in this doc under "three-level cascading SCons staleness." **In the end, none of this actually mattered for this specific bug** — `gi.cpp` (which directly `#include`s the shader's generated SPIR-V header via `gi.h`) rebuilt correctly the very first time via completely normal, un-forced dependency tracking, and `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s precompile-registry entry (`wgsl_precompile.py`) never defines `MODE_PROCESS_DYNAMIC`, so the real runtime variant was never in the precompiled table to begin with and always takes the live-Tint-translation fallback path regardless. The traps are still worth documenting for the next shader change that *is* affected by them, but they were a real, costly detour here, not the explanation for the fix not working.

**Diagnostic that actually explained the negative result**: lowered the clamp ceiling from `65408.0` to `4.0` (same rebuild/export/checksum-verify/capture cycle) purely to observe behavior, not as a candidate fix. Result: the scene now visibly brightens over the same timeline and pins at a washed-out-but-not-pure-white level by t=100-300s — i.e. it converges to *whatever ceiling is chosen*, rather than settling below it on its own. This proves the per-frame energy growth in the native-storage feedback loop is **genuinely unbounded** — a clamp of any kind only relocates where the runaway visually caps out; `65408.0` is simply high enough that the cap itself still renders as pure white after tonemapping. The `4.0` diagnostic value was reverted; `65408.0` was kept as the final, committed value (a legitimate defensive bound — matches the packed path's own representable range, prevents float overflow/UB if a value ever legitimately approached it) with an explicit code comment is not present but the TASKS.md item 11 entry now says plainly that this does not fix the reported bug, to stop a future round from re-deriving and re-testing the same hypothesis from scratch.

**Refined next hypothesis for whoever continues** (not yet tested): the packed RGBE9995 format's lossy ~9-bit-mantissa quantization may be acting as an *implicit per-frame damping factor* on the self-illumination bounce feedback loop that native float storage, being lossless, simply doesn't have — allowing genuine unbounded growth. If true, the real fix is an explicit decay/normalization term in the feedback math itself (likely shared code, not WebGPU-specific, since it would represent a latent instability the packed format was accidentally masking on every backend) rather than anything storage-format-specific. Next concrete step: instrument actual per-frame magnitude readback (a temporary CPU-side probe or `print_line` via a debug readback path) of `light_total`/`irradiance` at a fixed cascade/probe location on both backends, to directly observe whether Vulkan's value is genuinely converging (supports the damping theory) or something else entirely is different between the two paths.

**Verified**: `shader_corpus` (13/13), `preprocessing_tests` (192/192 + 1 pre-existing skip), `driver_unit_tests` (327/327) all green with the final (`65408.0`) clamp in place. Native editor (`dev_build=yes`) and web template both rebuild clean from a full fresh build. Checksum-verified (`md5sum`) at every capture that the exported build genuinely matched the just-built `bin/` output — the lesson this round's own build-staleness traps reinforced firsthand.

**Committed**: `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/environment/sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`, `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/environment/sdfgi_integrate.glsl` (defensive clamp only, does not fix the reported bug — see above), `webgpu_notes/TASKS.md`.

### Round 31 (2026-09-12, same session): instrumented actual light-magnitude numbers on both backends — quantization-damping hypothesis also disproved, divergence is starker than expected

Directly asked-for follow-up to Round 30: instrument `light_total`/`irradiance` magnitude on both backends and compare, rather than reasoning about the bug only from visual screenshots.

**Instrumentation**: added temporary code to `GI::SDFGI::store_probes()` (`gi.cpp`, immediately after its `compute_list_end()`) that every ~60 frames reads back cascade 0's `lightprobe_data` texture (`RD::texture_get_data(lightprobe_data, 0)`, layer 0 = cascade 0's irradiance plane in the octahedral 2D-array texture) and decodes every texel's magnitude — native format via `Math::half_to_float` on the RGBA16F bytes, packed format via a hand-derived RGB9E5 shared-exponent decode (`scale = 2^(exponent-15-9)`, checked bit-for-bit against `rgbe_encode()`'s own encode math in `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` before trusting it) — then `print_line()`s the max magnitude found across the texture. This was **not committed**; reverted via `git checkout` immediately after capturing results, per its own "remove before merging" comment.

Two build-methodology notes for this readback approach, useful if anyone repeats it: (1) `RD::texture_get_data()`'s `p_layer` parameter addresses a genuine array layer for a `TEXTURE_TYPE_2D_ARRAY` texture consistently on both backends (unlike a `TEXTURE_TYPE_3D` texture, where the WebGPU driver's implementation treats `p_layer` as a depth-slice *z*-origin and only copies back one 2D plane of the volume per call, while the generic/Vulkan path copies the *whole* depth — an asymmetry that would have made a `cascade.light_data` 3D-texture readback backend-inconsistent; `lightprobe_data`'s genuine 2D-array shape sidesteps this entirely, which is why it was chosen over the more obvious `dst_light` site). (2) WebGPU's `texture_get_data()` is asynchronous — the driver comment (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) confirms the first call after a texture write returns empty and only a *later* call returns the mapped data; calling every ~60 frames rather than every frame worked fine in practice (each print reflects a fresh call, `entry->has_data` is reset after each successful read per the driver's own "no auto-requeue" design) but this pattern won't work as a naive single-call synchronous readback on WebGPU.

**Result — the quantization-damping hypothesis is also disproved, and by a wide margin.** Matched ~300-real-second captures (native Vulkan in normal windowed play mode, no `--fixed-fps`, running at its own real frame rate; WebGPU via the existing `probe_alllogs_r26.mjs` full-console-capture probe) against the same SDFGI-only scratch project (`sdfgi_bounce_feedback` left at the `Environment` class default of `0.5`, confirmed via `scene/resources/environment.h`, so the self-illumination feedback path was genuinely active): **native Vulkan's max irradiance is bit-exact identical (`0.79296875`) across all 855 printed samples spanning ~51,300 frames — it reaches this value by the first sample (~frame 60) and never moves again, not by even one quantization step, for the rest of the capture.** **WebGPU's climbs continuously for the entire capture**: `1.51` at frame 120, growing at a roughly-multiplicative rate through about frame 2000-3000, then transitioning to near-*linear* growth (~+2.97 per frame) from there on, reaching `42080` by frame 16680 (~t=278s) with no sign of leveling off before the capture ended.

This is a categorically different result than "less damping" — a merely-less-damped system would still show *some* slow drift over 51,300 frames on Vulkan, not perfect bit-for-bit stasis. It also runs backwards from what the precision theory predicted: WebGPU's native RGBA16F storage has independently-normalized 10-bit mantissas per channel, which if anything is *more* precise than the packed path's single 9-bit mantissa shared across all three channels via one exponent — "more precision causing more divergence" is the opposite of what a damping-via-imprecision mechanism would produce.

**Refined, much more specific hypothesis for whoever picks this up next**: the divergence almost certainly traces to `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s `MODE_PROCESS_DYNAMIC` block (`params.bounce_feedback > 0.001`, roughly lines 223-296) — this samples back the *previous frame's* `lightprobe_texture` (a `sampler2DArray` bilinear read of the same `lightprobe_data` this round instrumented) and blends a fraction of it into the current frame's `light_total` before it's written to `dst_light`, forming a genuine closed multi-frame feedback loop. Tracing the loop: `dst_light` → sampled during `update_probes()`'s `MODE_PROCESS` ray-march → accumulated into `lightprobe_average_texture` (format `R32G32B32A32_SINT`, identical on both backends, no `SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` branch anywhere in that path) → read back fresh (not accumulated further) by `store_probes()`'s `MODE_STORE` to compute `irradiance`/`radiance` → written to `lightprobe_data` → sampled again next frame by the bounce-feedback read, closing the loop. Every step of this cycle is backend-agnostic *except* the packed-vs-native format of `dst_light`/`lightprobe_texture` itself, exactly where Round 21 substituted storage formats — a scale mismatch or read/write inconsistency confined to that one substitution, compounding once per frame around this loop, would produce exactly the observed shape (early multiplicative-looking growth from a per-cycle gain modestly above 1.0, later flattening toward linear as some other term in the loop saturates), while Vulkan's mathematically-equivalent loop (same shader code, packed format decoded transparently by the GPU's texture-sampling hardware) converges to a stable fixed point immediately. **Round 32 confirmed the Vulkan half of this directly, but hit a genuine API limitation on the WebGPU side.** Added the same instrumentation pattern to `update_light()` (reading `cascades[0].light_data`, i.e. `dst_light` itself, immediately after its `compute_list_end()`; reverted after use, not committed) and re-ran matched ~300s captures. **On native Vulkan, `RD::texture_get_data()`'s generic (non-driver) path copies the texture's *entire* depth for a 3D texture** (`copy_region.texture_region_size.z = MAX(1, tex->depth >> i)` in `rendering_device.cpp`), so this read genuinely covered all 2,097,152 texels of cascade 0's full volume — and the result is as clean as Round 31's: **bit-exact flat at `0.7734375` across all 813 samples spanning ~48,780 frames, with zero movement.** This confirms the divergence-vs-stability split already exists at the very first write stage (`sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s own output), not something introduced later in `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s SH accumulation or probe storage.

    **On WebGPU, however, `RD::texture_get_data()` on a `TEXTURE_TYPE_3D` texture is asymmetric with the generic path in a way this round didn't anticipate**: the WebGPU driver (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp:texture_get_data`) always copies extent `{mip_w, mip_h, 1}` from origin `{0, 0, p_layer}` — i.e. exactly *one depth slice*, never the whole volume — and since a 3D texture's `tex->layers` is 1 (array layers and depth are different fields; only `format.array_layers` populates `tex->layers`, confirmed in `rendering_device.cpp`), `p_layer` can only ever be `0`. There is no way to request a different depth slice of a 3D texture through this API on WebGPU. The result reflects this: **all 138 samples across the full ~16,680-frame WebGPU capture read back exactly `0.0`** — cascade 0's voxel-space `z=0` plane is apparently entirely outside the SDF-populated/lit region for this scene, making this a null, uninformative result rather than evidence that WebGPU's `dst_light` stays flat. (Contrast with Round 31's `lightprobe_data` readback, which is a genuine `TEXTURE_TYPE_2D_ARRAY` — `p_layer` addresses a real, full-plane array layer consistently on both backends there, which is exactly why that resource was readable end-to-end while this one only half-worked.)

    **Net conclusion after Round 32**: strong, clean evidence the stability-vs-divergence split originates at or before `dst_light`'s own write in `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl` (Vulkan: flat from the very first frame onward, whole-volume). Still missing at that point: direct confirmation that WebGPU's `dst_light` *does* diverge at this same stage.

    **Round 33 sidestepped the 3D-texture readback limitation entirely and found the actual mechanism — this is no longer believed to be an SDFGI-storage-format issue at all.** Rather than fighting the 3D-texture single-slice limitation, this round instrumented `cascades[0].lightprobe_average_tex` instead — a genuine `TEXTURE_TYPE_2D` texture (not 3D, not array), format `R32G32B32A32_SINT` identical on both backends with **no `SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` branching anywhere near it** — read immediately after `update_probes()`'s single ray-march/SH-accumulation dispatch (upstream of `store_probes()`, which Round 31 instrumented). Result: **native Vulkan is bit-exact flat at `15360` across all 855 samples over ~51,300 frames; WebGPU climbs from `29184` at frame 120 to `472,966,159` by frame 16680**, the same shape (early fast growth, later near-linear) seen at every other stage. This confirms the divergence is present after just the *first* accumulation pass that reads `dst_light`/`light_cascades[]` — as early as it's possible to observe with a texture read.

    **Reading the actual accumulation code (`sdfgi_integrate.glsl` lines ~330-346) found something that reframes the whole bug.** This is a manual (non-atomic) rolling-average implemented by hand: `average -= prev_value; average += ivalue; imageStore(lightprobe_history_texture, prev_pos, ivalue); imageStore(lightprobe_average_texture, average_pos, average);` — each frame's own contribution is loaded, the frame that's aging out of the window (`history_size`, default 30 per `ENV_SDFGI_CONVERGE_IN_30_FRAMES`) is subtracted back out, and the new contribution is added, with **no atomics anywhere in this sequence**. Critically, each frame's own per-probe contribution (`ivalue`) is *explicitly* clamped to a signed 16-bit range before this: `ivec4 ivalue = clamp(ivec4(value * float(1 << HISTORY_BITS)), -32768, 32767); //clamp to 16 bits, so higher values don't break average` (this exact comment is already in the unmodified upstream code — not something Task 9.5 introduced). **This puts a hard, unconditional, backend-agnostic ceiling on what a correctly-functioning version of this rolling average could ever reach: even under constant worst-case per-frame saturation at `32767`, summed over a `history_size`-frame window, `average` cannot exceed `32767 × 30 = 983,010`.** The observed WebGPU value, `472,966,159`, is **~481× that absolute theoretical maximum** — this is not merely "saturating high," the subtract-old/add-new bookkeeping itself must be breaking down, allowing contributions to accumulate without the intended per-window cancellation.

    **Since none of this code path branches on storage format at all**, the divergence can no longer be explained by anything Round 21's RGBE9995→native-float substitution changed directly. The most likely remaining explanation given everything gathered so far: a **read-after-write/hazard-tracking gap specific to this manual `imageLoad`→modify→`imageStore` accumulation pattern spanning *separate frames' compute dispatches*** on the WebGPU driver. This is directly relevant to an existing documented design decision (`CLAUDE.md`): *"Barriers: `command_pipeline_barrier` is a no-op — WebGPU tracks hazards automatically."* Godot's own backend-agnostic dependency tracker (`servers/rendering/rendering_device_graph.*`, `RenderingDeviceGraph::add_compute_list_usage()`) presumably still requests a barrier for this storage-image read-after-write like it would for any driver, but the call is discarded on WebGPU on the assumption that WebGPU's automatic usage-scope tracking covers it. If that assumption doesn't correctly extend across two *separate frames'* compute dispatches (as opposed to within one frame's command encoder), a stale read of `average`/`prev_value` on some frames would break the subtraction-based cancellation this rolling average depends on — plausibly explaining unbounded growth of exactly this shape. **This has not been proven** — it is the most concrete lead so far, not a confirmed root cause, and if true it would likely be a broader concern than SDFGI alone (any other engine code relying on a storage image being correctly read-modified-written across successive frames on WebGPU could have the same latent issue).

    *(Round 34 below both performed this audit and ran a cheap test — disabling `sdfgi_bounce_feedback` — that rules out the closed feedback loop specifically as the root cause, narrowing this further; read on.)*

    **Round 34 audited `command_pipeline_barrier` as planned, and ran a cheap test that rules out one whole class of hypothesis.**

    **Barrier audit**: `RenderingDeviceDriverWebGPU::command_pipeline_barrier()` (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`, under a `// BARRIERS (ALL NO-OPS)` section header) is confirmed to be an unconditional, parameter-ignoring no-op for every barrier type (memory/buffer/texture/acceleration-structure), with the single comment `// No-op: WebGPU handles synchronization automatically.` This is a deliberate, already-documented design choice (`CLAUDE.md`'s own "Barriers" bullet), not an oversight. Separately confirmed that all of one frame's compute/render work is batched into a `LocalVector<WGPUCommandBuffer>` and submitted together in **one** `wgpuQueueSubmit()` call per frame (`command_queue_execute_and_present()`), and that `RD::submit()`/`RD::sync()` (which could otherwise force an explicit CPU-GPU sync point for testing) are hard-restricted to secondary/local `RenderingDevice` instances and cannot be called on the main device SDFGI runs on (`ERR_FAIL_COND_MSG(is_main_instance, ...)` in `rendering_device.cpp`), so there is no simple RD-level API to force an extra synchronization point as a direct test of the hazard-tracking theory.

    **Reasoning about whether the no-op should actually be safe here**: per the WebGPU specification's own memory/execution model, a queue's submitted command buffers execute in a strict content-timeline order, and the specification requires implementations to insert whatever synchronization is necessary so that a later submission's reads correctly observe an earlier submission's writes to the same resource — this is supposed to be true across separate `wgpuQueueSubmit()` calls (i.e. across frames), not just within one. If Dawn (Chromium's WebGPU implementation) correctly implements this guarantee, a cross-frame storage-image read-after-write hazard for a persistent (not recreated per-frame) texture like `cascades[0].lightprobe_average_tex` should already be safe without this driver doing anything — meaning a bare "missing barrier" story is actually weaker than Round 33 made it sound; if this hypothesis is right at all, it would most likely mean a genuine implementation gap in Dawn's automatic hazard tracking for this specific access pattern, not a straightforward omission in this repository's code.

    **A cheap, decisive test that doesn't require touching any C++/driver code at all**: set `sdfgi_bounce_feedback = 0.0` directly in the scratch project's `Environment` resource (previously left at its class default of `0.5`) and re-ran the same WebGPU capture with the *already-built* engine (no rebuild needed — this is a pure content/settings change). With `params.bounce_feedback <= 0.001`, `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s entire `MODE_PROCESS_DYNAMIC` bounce-feedback block (the closed loop that reads back last frame's `lightprobe_texture`) is skipped entirely — `light_total` becomes pure direct+sky light with **no cross-frame self-illumination feedback of any kind**. Result: **the runaway still happens.** It is visibly *slower* (at t=100s the scene is washed out and over-bright but not yet solid white, unlike the fully-blown-white result at the same timestamp with feedback enabled) but by t=300s the scene is nearly as blown out as before. This rules out the closed bounce-feedback loop as the *root* cause — the SH-accumulation/rolling-average mechanism in `update_probes()`/`store_probes()` (audited in Round 33) is diverging on its own, processing plain direct light every frame, with or without any feedback amplifying it. The bounce-feedback loop appears to be an *accelerant* (consistent with positive feedback compounding an already-present per-frame bias faster), not the underlying defect.

    **Where this leaves the investigation**: the defect is now narrowed to something that reproduces on *every* frame's `update_probes()`/`store_probes()` SH-accumulation cycle regardless of what feeds it — i.e. something wrong with how `dst_light`'s per-voxel value gets ray-marched, SH-projected, and rolled into `lightprobe_average_texture`/`lightprobe_history_texture` via the hand-rolled `imageLoad`→modify→`imageStore` pattern Round 33 found, on literally every frame, not just when a self-reinforcing loop is active. Two live hypotheses remain, both still unconfirmed: (a) a genuine cross-frame (or, per this round's reasoning, possibly even within-frame, cross-*dispatch*) storage-image visibility/ordering gap in Dawn's automatic hazard tracking for this exact access pattern, or (b) a **push-constant-value-delivery bug** specific to this driver's ring-buffer push-constant emulation (`_flush_push_constants()`, `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) — e.g. `params.history_index` (`render_pass % history_size`, which selects which ring-buffer slot's old contribution to load-and-subtract) arriving at the shader stale, wrong, or out of the valid `[0, history_size)` range, which — since WGSL storage-texture accesses are bounds-checked and out-of-bounds reads/writes are defined to silently return/discard rather than fault — would cause the "subtract what aged out" step to silently read zero instead of the true old value, letting contributions accumulate every frame with nothing ever correctly cancelled. (b) was not ruled out by this round's test (bounce_feedback=0 doesn't touch push-constant delivery at all) and is arguably the more project-specific, more actionable lead of the two, since push-constant emulation is an existing, previously-audited-for-other-bugs, WebGPU-only mechanism in this exact driver (see the `_flush_push_constants()` ring-overflow-mid-pass-restart machinery Round 34 also read while investigating, though ring overflow itself looks too rare during SDFGI's modest per-frame push-constant usage — `PUSH_CONSTANT_RING_SIZE` is 512KB / 2048 slots per frame — to be the primary trigger on its own).

    **Round 35 ran exactly this test and ruled out push-constant delivery as the cause too — both of Round 34's live hypotheses are now down, and the root cause is genuinely open again.**

    **Test**: had `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `MODE_PROCESS` overwrite probe (0,0)'s SH-band-0 texel of cascade 0 with its own received `params.history_index`/`cascade`/`max_cascades` plus a marker constant, immediately after the normal accumulation store; read it back from `gi.cpp` right after `update_probes()`'s dispatch and compared against the CPU-side `render_pass % history_size` value set for that exact call (both reverted after use, not committed). First attempt (60-frame throttle, 3 samples) showed a suspicious-looking constant "-2" offset — inconclusive on its own, since `RD::texture_get_data()` is asynchronous on WebGPU (a documented "first call empty, later call returns a previously-requested snapshot" pattern) and could plausibly explain a small lag rather than corruption.

    **Refined test**: logged the raw, unthrottled `render_pass` value into a small ring buffer on every single `update_probes()` call (not just at print time), lowered the print throttle, and re-ran a 30-second capture — 14 samples. Result: `render_pass` genuinely increments once per rendered frame as expected (confirmed directly, e.g. `render_pass=39` at `frame=40`), `cpu_history_index` correctly cycles `9 → 19 → 29 → 9 → 19 → 29 → ...` (matching `render_pass % 30`), and **`shader_history_index` cycles in perfect lockstep — `17 → 27 → 7 → 17 → 27 → 7 → ...` — a constant, exact `-8 (mod 30)` offset from the CPU value on every single one of the 14 samples across the full 30 seconds, with zero deviation.**

    **This is conclusive, not ambiguous**: a rock-solid, exactly-periodic offset — where the "wrong" value *actively cycles in lockstep* with the true value rather than ever being frozen, random, or out of the valid `[0, 30)` range — is the signature of a fixed-magnitude **measurement artifact** (this specific readback consistently observing a snapshot from a constant number of calls in the past, an artifact of `texture_get_data()`'s async return-then-requeue pattern interacting with this round's exact polling cadence), not evidence of genuine push-constant corruption reaching the real rendering pipeline. Real corruption would be expected to produce inconsistent, non-periodic, or out-of-range values — not a value that stays perfectly synchronized (with a fixed lag) to the true cycling quantity for an entire 30-second capture. **Push-constant delivery for `history_index` is therefore considered ruled out as the root cause.**

    **Where this leaves the investigation**: both concrete hypotheses raised across Rounds 33-34 (a Dawn-level cross-dispatch hazard-tracking gap, and a push-constant-delivery bug) are now either weakened by spec-level reasoning (the former) or directly ruled out by this test (the latter). See Round 36 below — auditing the actual Tint-generated WGSL found a real, high-confidence root-cause candidate, unrelated to either of those hypotheses.

**Verified**: instrumentation reverted (`git checkout -- servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp`), tree clean of temporary debug code. No fix attempted this round — purely diagnostic. **Committed**: `webgpu_notes/TASKS.md` only (the instrumentation itself was never committed).

### Round 32 (2026-09-12, same session): traced the divergence one stage further upstream to dst_light itself on Vulkan; hit a genuine WebGPU 3D-texture readback limitation trying to get the matching number

Direct follow-up ask: instrument `cascade.light_data` (direct_light's own per-voxel output) immediately after `update_light()`'s dispatch on both backends, the same way Round 31 instrumented the downstream `lightprobe_data`.

**Instrumentation**: same pattern as Round 31 (temporary, reverted after use, never committed) — added to `GI::SDFGI::update_light()` immediately after its `compute_list_end()`/`draw_command_end_label()`, reading back `cascades[0].light_data` every ~60 frames and printing the max decoded magnitude (same native-RGBA16F / packed-RGB9E5 decode as before).

**Result on native Vulkan**: `RD::texture_get_data()`'s generic (non-WebGPU-driver) path builds its copy region from `tex->depth` directly (`rendering_device.cpp`, `copy_region.texture_region_size.z = MAX(1, tex->depth >> i)`), so this read genuinely covers the *entire* 3D volume — all 2,097,152 texels of cascade 0 (128³). Result: **bit-exact flat at `0.7734375` across all 813 samples spanning ~48,780 frames (~300 real seconds), zero movement at any point.** This pushes the confirmed-stable boundary one full stage earlier than Round 31 — the stability isn't something that emerges during SH accumulation or probe storage, it's present from `dst_light`'s very first write.

**Result on WebGPU, and the limitation that undermines it**: the WebGPU driver's `texture_get_data()` (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) always copies extent `{mip_w, mip_h, 1}` from origin `{0, 0, p_layer}` — exactly one depth slice, never the whole volume — and a 3D texture's `tex->layers` field is always `1` (only `format.array_layers` populates it; depth is a separate field), so `p_layer` can only ever be `0`. This is a real, unconditional API asymmetry between the two texture_get_data code paths for `TEXTURE_TYPE_3D` textures specifically, not something tunable from the call site. The result: **all 138 samples across the full WebGPU capture (~16,680 frames) read back exactly `0.0`** — cascade 0's `z=0` voxel plane is apparently entirely outside the SDF-populated/lit region of this scene. This is a null result, not evidence of anything about whether WebGPU's `dst_light` diverges — contrast with `lightprobe_data` (a genuine `TEXTURE_TYPE_2D_ARRAY`, where `p_layer` addresses a real full-plane layer consistently on both backends), which is exactly why Round 31's readback worked end-to-end and this one only half-worked.

**Net position after this round**: very strong evidence the packed/Vulkan path is stable from the earliest possible write stage onward (two independent resources, `dst_light` and `lightprobe_data`, both bit-exact flat for the entire multi-minute capture). Still no direct WebGPU-side number at the `dst_light` stage to confirm the divergence starts there too (as the bounce-feedback hypothesis predicts) rather than first appearing somewhere in `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s accumulation. Closing that gap needs a readback approach that isn't limited to one 3D depth slice — see item 11 for two concrete options (a purpose-built single-point compute+SSBO readback, or repositioning scratch-project geometry to overlap voxel `z=0`).

**Verified**: instrumentation reverted (`git checkout -- servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp`) immediately after capturing results; both native editor and web template rebuilt clean afterward to keep `bin/` consistent with the committed (clamp-only) source. No fix attempted — purely diagnostic. **Committed**: `webgpu_notes/TASKS.md` only.

### Round 33 (2026-09-12, same session): found a backend-symmetric resource that sidesteps Round 32's readback limitation, and reading the actual accumulation code reframed the whole investigation

Also using the newly-documented and newly-committed live-repro methodology (`webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/LIVE_REPRO_METHODOLOGY.md` and its `capture_console.mjs`/`capture_native_vulkan.sh` scripts, written this same round to consolidate the ad hoc probe scripts scattered across Rounds 15-32) for both captures below.

**Instrumentation**: rather than fighting the `TEXTURE_TYPE_3D` single-slice limitation Round 32 hit, this round targeted `cascades[0].lightprobe_average_tex` instead (`gi.h`/`gi.cpp`) — a genuine `TEXTURE_TYPE_2D` texture (not 3D, not array), format `R32G32B32A32_SINT`, with **zero `SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` branching anywhere near its creation or use**. For a plain 2D texture, `RD::texture_get_data()`'s `p_layer=0` genuinely covers the whole texture on both backends (there's no depth dimension to slice), so this sidesteps Round 32's asymmetry entirely. Added the same temporary print pattern as prior rounds (reverted after use, not committed) right after `update_probes()`'s `compute_list_end()` — i.e. immediately after the single ray-march/SH-accumulation dispatch that reads `light_cascades[]`/`dst_light`, upstream of `store_probes()` (which Round 31 instrumented).

**Result**: matched ~300s captures again — **native Vulkan is bit-exact flat at `15360` across all 855 samples spanning ~51,300 frames.** **WebGPU climbs from `29184` at frame 120 to `472,966,159` by frame 16680**, the same early-fast-then-near-linear shape seen at every other pipeline stage examined so far. This confirms the divergence is present as early as it's possible to observe with a single texture-readback call — right after the very first accumulation pass following a read of `dst_light`.

**Reading the actual GLSL this round (`sdfgi_integrate.glsl` lines ~330-346) found the real mechanism, and it reframes the entire investigation.** The SH history/average is a hand-rolled, non-atomic rolling sum:

```glsl
ivec4 ivalue = clamp(ivec4(value * float(1 << HISTORY_BITS)), -32768, 32767); //clamp to 16 bits, so higher values don't break average
ivec4 prev_value = imageLoad(lightprobe_history_texture, prev_pos);
ivec4 average = imageLoad(lightprobe_average_texture, average_pos);
average -= prev_value;
average += ivalue;
imageStore(lightprobe_history_texture, prev_pos, ivalue);
imageStore(lightprobe_average_texture, average_pos, average);
```

Each frame's own per-probe contribution is *explicitly* clamped to a signed-16-bit range before this — the `//clamp to 16 bits, so higher values don't break average` comment is already present in the unmodified upstream Godot source, not something this fork added. This puts a hard, unconditional, **backend-agnostic** ceiling on what a correctly-functioning version of this accumulator could ever produce: with `history_size` defaulting to 30 (`ENV_SDFGI_CONVERGE_IN_30_FRAMES`), even under constant worst-case per-frame saturation at `32767`, the rolling sum over a 30-frame window cannot exceed `32767 × 30 = 983,010`. **The observed WebGPU value, `472,966,159`, is ~481× that absolute theoretical maximum** — not merely "saturating high," the subtract-old/add-new bookkeeping itself must be failing to correctly cancel aged-out contributions.

Since none of this accumulation code branches on storage format at all, **the divergence can no longer be explained by anything Round 21's RGBE9995→native-float substitution changed directly** — that substitution may simply be what exposed a pre-existing, more general bug (by changing what values/timing reach this accumulator), rather than being the bug itself. The leading hypothesis is now a **read-after-write/hazard-tracking gap** in how this manual `imageLoad`→modify→`imageStore` pattern is synchronized *across separate frames'* compute dispatches on the WebGPU driver specifically — directly relevant to an existing documented design decision in this fork's own `CLAUDE.md`: *"Barriers: `command_pipeline_barrier` is a no-op — WebGPU tracks hazards automatically."* Godot's backend-agnostic dependency tracker (`servers/rendering/rendering_device_graph.*`) presumably still requests synchronization for this storage-image read-after-write the same way on every backend, but the request is a no-op on WebGPU under the assumption that WebGPU's own automatic usage-scope tracking covers it — if that assumption doesn't correctly extend across two *separate frames'* dispatches (as opposed to within a single frame's command encoder), the cancellation this rolling average depends on would break, plausibly producing exactly this shape of unbounded growth. **This is not proven** — it is the most concrete, best-quantified lead found so far, not a confirmed root cause. If real, it would likely be a broader concern than SDFGI alone, since any other engine code relying on a storage image being correctly read-modified-written across successive frames on WebGPU could share the same latent issue.

**Not yet done, flagged as a significant scope consideration for whoever continues**: actually auditing `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`'s `command_pipeline_barrier` and cross-frame storage-image synchronization is a meaningfully larger and more architecturally sensitive undertaking than anything else in this bug's investigation so far — potentially touching core synchronization behavior rather than an isolated SDFGI code path. See item 11 for the concrete first diagnostic step recommended (an `imageAtomicAdd`-based experiment) before attempting any actual driver-level fix.

**Verified**: instrumentation reverted (`git checkout -- servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp`); both native editor and web template rebuilt clean afterward. No fix attempted — purely diagnostic. **Committed**: `webgpu_notes/TASKS.md` only (this round's own methodology-documentation work — `LIVE_REPRO_METHODOLOGY.md` and its three capture scripts — was committed separately, see that commit for details).

### Round 34 (2026-09-12, same session): audited the barrier no-op as planned; a cheap settings-only test rules out the closed feedback loop as root cause

Direct follow-up to Round 33's request: audit `command_pipeline_barrier`'s WebGPU implementation.

**Barrier audit**: confirmed `RenderingDeviceDriverWebGPU::command_pipeline_barrier()` (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`, under a `// BARRIERS (ALL NO-OPS)` section) is an unconditional no-op for every barrier type, with the comment `// No-op: WebGPU handles synchronization automatically.` — exactly as `CLAUDE.md` already documents, a deliberate design choice rather than an oversight. Also confirmed one frame's entire compute/render workload is batched into a single `wgpuQueueSubmit()` call (`command_queue_execute_and_present()`), and that `RD::submit()`/`RD::sync()` — which would otherwise let a forced CPU-GPU sync point be inserted as a direct test — are hard-restricted to secondary/local `RenderingDevice` instances and unusable on the main device SDFGI runs on.

**Reasoning about whether the no-op should actually matter here**: per the WebGPU specification's content-timeline execution model, a queue's submitted command buffers execute in strict order, and implementations are required to insert whatever synchronization is necessary so a later submission's reads correctly observe an earlier submission's writes to the same resource — across separate `wgpuQueueSubmit()` calls, not just within one. If Dawn correctly implements this (as the spec requires), a cross-frame hazard for a persistent texture like `cascades[0].lightprobe_average_tex` should already be safe with no help from this driver at all — meaning the bare "missing barrier" story looks weaker on reflection than Round 33 made it sound. If this hypothesis holds at all, it more likely points to a genuine Dawn implementation gap for this specific access pattern than a straightforward omission in this repository.

**A cheap, decisive test requiring no rebuild at all**: added `sdfgi_bounce_feedback = 0.0` directly to the scratch project's `Environment` resource (previously left at its class default of `0.5`) and re-ran the existing WebGPU capture tooling against the *already-built* engine — a pure content change, no code/compile involved. With `bounce_feedback <= 0.001`, `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`'s entire closed-loop bounce-feedback block (reading back last frame's `lightprobe_texture`) is skipped — `light_total` is pure direct+sky light, with **zero cross-frame self-illumination feedback**. Result: **the runaway still happens.** Visibly slower (t=100s is washed-out/over-bright but not solid white, versus fully blown white at the same timestamp with feedback enabled per Round 30's capture) but by t=300s the scene is nearly as blown out regardless. This rules the closed feedback loop out as the *root* cause: the SH-accumulation mechanism itself (Round 33's `imageLoad`→modify→`imageStore` rolling average) is diverging on plain per-frame direct light alone, with the feedback loop acting as an accelerant on top of an already-present defect rather than being the defect itself.

**Where this leaves things**: two live hypotheses, neither confirmed: (a) a genuine Dawn-level hazard-tracking gap for this storage-image RMW access pattern (weakened somewhat by the spec-level reasoning above, but not ruled out — a browser implementation bug is still possible), or (b) a push-constant-delivery bug in this driver's own ring-buffer emulation (`_flush_push_constants()`) — specifically, `params.history_index` (`render_pass % history_size`, selecting which ring-buffer slot's old contribution to subtract) arriving at the shader stale or out of its valid `[0, history_size)` range, which — since WGSL storage-texture accesses are bounds-checked, silently no-op/return-zero rather than fault on out-of-bounds — would mean the "subtract what aged out" step silently reads zero every time instead of the true old value, letting every frame's contribution accumulate with nothing ever correctly cancelled. (b) is arguably the more actionable lead: it's specific to this driver's own previously-scrutinized push-constant emulation rather than requiring browser-internals knowledge, and Round 34's bounce-feedback test doesn't touch push-constant delivery at all, so it remains fully open. `_flush_push_constants()`'s ring-overflow-mid-pass-restart machinery was also read this round as a related possible risk area, but ring overflow itself looks too rare during SDFGI's modest per-frame push-constant volume (`PUSH_CONSTANT_RING_SIZE` = 512KB / 2048 slots per frame) to be the primary trigger on its own.

**Concrete next step (done in Round 35, see item 11 above)**: directly verify `params.history_index` reaches the shader correctly — have the shader write its *received* value (not a light magnitude) to a small debug resource for readback, and compare against the CPU-side value `gi.cpp` set for that exact dispatch. Result: push-constant delivery checks out clean (a rock-solid, exactly-periodic offset consistent with a readback-timing artifact, not corruption) — this hypothesis is ruled out. The `imageAtomicAdd`-based experiment from Round 33 (to test the hazard-tracking hypothesis) remains a fallback, but item 11 now suggests auditing the actual Tint-generated WGSL for this shader as the more likely next step.

**Verified**: scratch project's `sdfgi_bounce_feedback` setting reverted to the class default (removed the `= 0.0` override) after this test. No code changes this round — audit and a project-settings-only experiment. **Committed**: `webgpu_notes/TASKS.md` only.

### Round 35 (2026-09-12, same session): ran the push-constant-delivery test Round 34 proposed — ruled it out too, root cause still open; also improved the default web export HTML shell (unrelated to Task 9.5)

Two pieces of work this round: an unrelated web-export usability fix, then the planned SDFGI diagnostic.

**Web export HTML shell (unrelated to Task 9.5)**: the default export template (`misc/dist/html/full-size.html`, bundled into every web export template zip via `platform/web/emscripten_helpers.py`) always resized the canvas's own pixel buffer to exactly match the browser window (`canvasResizePolicy` "Adaptive"/2), so rendered content stretched/distorted to fill whatever aspect ratio the window happened to be — CSS had no ability to intervene, since the engine's own JS kept the buffer size and CSS display size identical on every resize. Fixed by forcing `canvasResizePolicy` to "Project" (1, a fixed buffer at the project's configured resolution) via one inline JS line in the HTML shell, then letting CSS scale that fixed-aspect canvas to fill the viewport via `object-fit: contain` (with `!important`, since the engine's resize handler still sets an inline style that would otherwise win). Verified visually: a capture at a deliberately mismatched 1000×1000 viewport against the (16:9-ish) scratch project showed correct top/bottom letterboxing instead of stretching. Committed separately (`feat(web): scale exported canvas to fit the viewport with preserved aspect ratio`).

**Push-constant-delivery test**: implemented the test Round 34's item 11 proposed. Added temporary instrumentation (reverted after use, not committed) to `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `MODE_PROCESS` — overwrite probe (0,0)'s SH-band-0 texel of cascade 0 with the dispatch's own received `params.history_index`/`cascade`/`max_cascades` plus a marker constant, right after the real accumulation store — and to `gi.cpp`'s `update_probes()`, reading that texel back and comparing against the CPU-side `render_pass % history_size` value set for the same dispatch.

First attempt (60-frame throttle, 3 samples over ~6s): a suspicious but ambiguous constant "-2" offset (`cpu=29` vs `shader=27` every time) — inconclusive, since `RD::texture_get_data()` is documented as asynchronous on WebGPU (first call returns empty; a *later* call returns a previously-requested snapshot), so a small constant lag doesn't by itself distinguish "delayed but correct" from "wrong."

Refined the test to settle this: logged the raw, unthrottled `render_pass` into a small ring buffer on *every* `update_probes()` call (not just at print time), and re-ran a denser 30-second capture (14 samples). Confirmed `render_pass` genuinely increments once per rendered frame (e.g. `render_pass=39` at `frame=40`), `cpu_history_index` correctly cycles `9 → 19 → 29 → 9 → 19 → 29 → ...` (matching `render_pass % 30`), and **`shader_history_index` cycles in perfect lockstep at a constant, exact `-8 (mod 30)` offset — `17 → 27 → 7 → 17 → 27 → 7 → ...` — on every single one of the 14 samples across the full 30 seconds, zero deviation.**

**Conclusion**: this is decisive, not ambiguous. A value that *actively cycles in lockstep* with ground truth at a perfectly constant, exactly-periodic offset — never frozen, never random, never out of the valid `[0, 30)` range — is the signature of a fixed-magnitude measurement artifact (this specific async-readback pattern consistently observing a snapshot from a constant number of calls in the past, interacting with this round's polling cadence), not genuine corruption reaching the real rendering pipeline. **Push-constant delivery for `history_index` is ruled out as the root cause of the SDFGI brightness runaway.**

**Net position**: both of Round 34's live hypotheses (a Dawn-level cross-dispatch hazard-tracking gap, and push-constant-delivery corruption) are now either weakened by spec-level reasoning or directly ruled out by this test. The root cause is genuinely open again. Two new candidate next steps recorded in item 11: (a) audit the actual Tint-generated WGSL for this shader/mode's history-buffer read-modify-write arithmetic for a genuine SPIR-V→WGSL translation bug (this project's own history shows Tint-translation quirks are a recurring real bug category here, and this specific shader/mode hasn't been inspected at the WGSL level yet) — the more likely lead — or (b) audit `uniform_set_create()`'s binding of `lightprobe_history_texture`/`lightprobe_average_texture` on the WebGPU driver for a binding/aliasing mismatch.

**Verified**: both instrumentation changes reverted (`git checkout -- servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/environment/sdfgi_integrate.glsl`); native editor and web template rebuilt clean afterward (one transient SCons parallel-build race — a missing `.o` the archiver couldn't find despite it existing on disk — resolved cleanly on retry, not a real regression). **Committed**: `misc/dist/html/full-size.html` (canvas scaling, separate commit) and `webgpu_notes/TASKS.md`; the SDFGI debug instrumentation itself was never committed.

### Task 9.6: Web export canvas scaling — fill viewport with preserved aspect ratio, without breaking mouse/touch input `[DONE]`

**Status**: `DONE`. Unrelated to Task 9.5's SDFGI investigation above — a general usability fix to the default web export HTML shell, found and fixed in the same session.

**Problem**: `misc/dist/html/full-size.html` (bundled into every web export template zip via `platform/web/emscripten_helpers.py`, used unless a project sets a custom HTML shell) left the engine's default `canvasResizePolicy` — "Adaptive" (2) — in place, which resizes the canvas's own pixel buffer to exactly match the browser window on every resize (`GodotDisplayScreen.updateSize()`, `platform/web/js/libs/library_godot_display.js`). Rendered content therefore stretched/distorted to fill whatever aspect ratio the browser window happened to be, with no way for CSS to intervene (buffer size and CSS display size were kept identical by the engine's own JS).

**First attempt (reverted/corrected, not left in the final state)**: forced `canvasResizePolicy` to "Project" (1, a fixed buffer at the project's configured resolution) via one inline JS line, then used `object-fit: contain` on a `width:100vw !important; height:100vh !important` canvas rule to visually letterbox-scale that fixed-aspect buffer up to fill the viewport. This looked correct visually (verified via a Playwright screenshot at a deliberately mismatched square viewport, showing correct top/bottom letterbox bars) — **but broke mouse/touch input alignment**: `object-fit` visually letterboxes the rendered content *inside* the element's box without shrinking the box itself, so `canvas.getBoundingClientRect()` — which `platform/web/js/libs/library_godot_input.js`'s mouse/touch handlers use directly to map `event.clientX`/`clientY` into canvas-space coordinates (`const rw = canvas.width / rect.width; const x = (evt.clientX - rect.x) * rw;`) — kept reporting the *full*, un-letterboxed box. Every click/tap landed offset by the size of the letterbox gap, causing mis-clicks whenever the window's aspect ratio didn't match the project's.

**Fix**: keep forcing `canvasResizePolicy = 1` (a fixed-aspect buffer is still needed as the thing to scale), but replace `object-fit: contain` with a CSS `transform: scale()`, computed and applied via a small inline script (`fitCanvasToViewport()`) once the engine sets the canvas's native CSS pixel size, and re-applied on `window.resize`. Unlike `object-fit`, `transform: scale()` visually scales the *entire element* as a rigid box — so `getBoundingClientRect()` always reports exactly the visually-scaled, on-screen box with no separate "content vs. box" distinction, and the existing input-mapping code in `library_godot_input.js` needed zero changes.

**Verification**: Playwright, viewport shaped to force heavy letterboxing (640×1200 against a ~640×360 native project resolution) — `canvas.getBoundingClientRect()` reported `{x:0, y:420, width:640, height:360}`, exactly matching the expected letterbox math (`(1200-360)/2 = 420`) and confirming no distortion (aspect ratio preserved exactly, `scale:1` since width was already the limiting dimension). A click placed in the letterbox dead zone (well above `rect.y`) produced no in-game reaction; a click at the visible content's center did (triggered a real camera/FOV recalculation print). Screenshot confirmed the letterboxing visually matches the measured rect.

**Committed**: `misc/dist/html/full-size.html`, across two commits (`feat(web): scale exported canvas to fit the viewport with preserved aspect ratio` for the initial object-fit version, `fix(web): correct canvas fit-to-viewport to not misalign mouse/touch input` for the transform-based correction) — kept as two commits rather than amended, per this session's own convention of never amending previously-made commits, even ones corrected shortly after.

### Round 36 (2026-09-13, new session): audited the actual Tint-generated WGSL directly — found a real, high-confidence root cause, unrelated to any prior hypothesis

Direct follow-up to Round 35's recorded next step: audit the actual Tint-generated WGSL for `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `MODE_PROCESS` variant.

**Method**: extracted the real, exact-defines-matching GLSL source by calling `drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompile.py`'s own `parse_glsl_file()`/`assemble_glsl()` functions directly from a small Python script (same methodology Tasks 9.1/9.2 used) — general defines `OCT_SIZE 5`, `SH_SIZE 16`, `SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` plus variant define `MODE_PROCESS`, matching exactly what `gi.cpp`'s real `create()` sets up for `INTEGRATE_MODE_PROCESS` (confirmed by reading that construction code directly, not assumed). Compiled with `glslangValidator -V --target-env vulkan1.1` (genuine SPIR-V 1.3, matching what the runtime driver actually requests) and converted with `bin/tint_convert_cli` (which itself runs all 12+ `spirv_preprocess.cpp` passes before calling into Tint, confirmed by reading `tint_cli/main.cpp`'s pass list — this is a faithful stand-in for the runtime `tint_wrapper.cpp` path, not a bypass of it).

**First read of the output looked completely correct**: the history/average read-modify-write loop Round 33 identified (`average -= prev_value; average += ivalue;` and its `textureLoad`/`textureStore` calls) translates faithfully, 1:1 with the GLSL semantics — correct texture types (`texture_storage_2d_array<rgba16sint>`, `texture_storage_2d<rgba32sint>`), correct addressing (`v_125.xy`/`v_125.z` for the array texture's coords/layer), correct integer bitcasts for the mixed signed/unsigned index arithmetic. **This specific loop is not the bug** — Round 33's hypothesis about *this* code was wrong.

**But something else in the same file's WGSL stood out**: `light_cascades` (declared in GLSL as `uniform texture3D light_cascades[MAX_CASCADES]`, i.e. an 8-element array of separate textures, sampled via `light_cascades[hit_cascade]` where `hit_cascade` is a genuine runtime value set during ray-marching) appears in the WGSL as `@group(0u) @binding(4u) var light_cascades : texture_3d<f32>;` — a **single, non-array texture**, with **every sample call** (`textureSampleLevel(light_cascades, linear_sampler, uvw, 0.0f)`) referencing it with **no index at all**. The same is true of `sdf_cascades`, `aniso0_cascades`, and `aniso1_cascades` — all four cascade-array textures collapse to one binding each, with zero indexing anywhere in the output.

**Confirmed this reflects the real, intended source, not a fluke of extraction**: disassembled the *raw* (pre-preprocessing) SPIR-V with `spirv-dis` and found `%light_cascades = OpVariable %_ptr_UniformConstant__arr_515_uint_8 UniformConstant` (a genuine `OpTypeArray` of 8 texture handles) and `%657 = OpAccessChain %_ptr_UniformConstant_515 %light_cascades %656` where `%656 = OpLoad %uint %hit_cascade` — i.e. a real `OpAccessChain` indexed by a value loaded from the `hit_cascade` variable at runtime, not a compile-time constant. The array and its dynamic indexing are genuinely present in the compiled shader; something in the pipeline is discarding them.

**Found exactly where and why, and confirmed this is pre-existing, documented, deliberate behavior — not a new or hidden bug**: `drivers/webgpu/spirv_preprocess.cpp`'s `flatten_binding_arrays()` pass (its own header comment: *"Tint rejects OpTypeArray/OpTypeRuntimeArray of handle types (image, sampler, sampled_image) regardless of array size. We must fully eliminate these array types from SPIR-V and replace them with scalar element types."*) exists because **core WebGPU has no equivalent of Vulkan/D3D12's descriptor/argument arrays at all** — a single binding can never represent N independently-selectable textures in WGSL, so Tint cannot accept the SPIR-V as-is. This pass's chosen resolution is to strip the array type entirely and rewrite every `OpAccessChain` into it as "a direct variable reference" — i.e. it deliberately discards the index, keeping whichever texture ends up bound at that one binding slot. The C++ binding side already knows and documents this: `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`'s `uniform_set_create()`, `UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE` case: `// Tint flattens binding arrays to single resources. Only provide the first texture for array bindings.` — it only ever binds `uniform.ids[0]`.

**Why this plausibly explains the SDFGI runaway**: `gi.cpp` binds all of `cascades[j].sdf_tex`/`light_tex`/`light_aniso_0_tex`/`light_aniso_1_tex` (for `j` in `0..cascades.size()`) as array elements of one `RD::Uniform` each, in both `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl` (the shadow/occlusion ray-march, `for (uint j = params.cascade; j < params.max_cascades; j++) { ... sdf_cascades[j] ... }`, line ~415) and `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` (the GI ray-march, `light_cascades[hit_cascade]` etc.). With the binding collapsed to "always cascade 0" on WebGPU, **any ray-march step that should be checking cascade 1, 2, or 3 (this scratch project genuinely has 4 active cascades, confirmed via Round 33's `shader_max_cascades=4`) instead silently samples cascade 0's SDF/light/anisotropy data** — a much smaller, near-origin volume, sampled at UV coordinates meant for a completely different cascade's extent. This would produce systematically wrong occlusion/shadowing and wrong indirect-light sampling for anything outside cascade 0's tiny volume, on *every single frame*, fully consistent with everything found in Rounds 30-35: present from the very first write (Round 32), present without any bounce feedback (Round 34, since it's already wrong in the direct-light/shadow computation itself, not just the feedback loop), and unrelated to push-constant delivery (Round 35, since `history_index` was never the issue) or storage format (Round 33, since this bug has nothing to do with `SDFGI_NATIVE_STORAGE_FORMAT` at all — it would affect the packed-format code path identically if it ran on WebGPU, and doesn't affect Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 at all since only WebGPU's `flatten_binding_arrays` pass exists).

**Not yet proven as *the* root cause, and no fix attempted this round** — this is a very strong, well-evidenced candidate given how cleanly it fits every prior round's evidence, but it hasn't been directly verified by, e.g., temporarily forcing `cascades.size() == 1` (which would make this bug a no-op, since there'd be nothing to collapse) and checking whether the runaway disappears. That's the natural next confirmation step before investing in a fix.

**Fix approaches, neither attempted — this needs a scope/priority decision, not a quick patch**:
1. **Per-cascade named bindings + shader-side branching**: declare `MAX_CASCADES` (8) separately-named bindings instead of one array (e.g. `sdf_cascades_0` … `sdf_cascades_7`), bind each cascade's texture individually as its own `RD::Uniform` (not appended to one array uniform), and replace every `sdf_cascades[j]`/`light_cascades[hit_cascade]`-style dynamic access with a small helper function branching on the index (`if (j == 0u) { ... } else if (j == 1u) { ... }`, etc.). Needed in `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`, `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`, and `sdfgi_debug.glsl` (the three files declaring `[MAX_CASCADES]` texture arrays) — a moderately invasive shared-GLSL change, would need `#ifdef`-gating to keep Vulkan/Metal/D3D12 on the simple array form per this fork's established driver-specific-branching convention, and needs care renumbering every binding that comes after these in each shader.
2. **Genuine binding-array splitting in the driver**: teach `flatten_binding_arrays` (or a new pass) to actually split an N-element handle array into N separate WGSL bindings plus SPIR-V-level control-flow selecting among them by the original dynamic index (rather than discarding it), and teach `uniform_set_create()` to bind all N ids to the N derived binding slots instead of only `ids[0]`. Mechanically equivalent to option 1's shape, but done automatically at the SPIR-V/driver level rather than hand-editing shared GLSL source — more consistent with this project's overall architecture (fixes belong in `drivers/webgpu/`, not shared engine shader source, per `CLAUDE.md`) and generic to any future array-of-textures case, but a meaningfully larger and more complex driver change (SPIR-V control-flow synthesis, not just a scalar ID substitution like the pass's current form).

**Verified**: no code changes this round — a WGSL/SPIR-V-level audit only. `spirv-dis` (already present on the system, `/usr/bin/spirv-dis`) used for the raw-SPIR-V disassembly step; no new tooling added. **Committed**: `webgpu_notes/TASKS.md` only.

### Round 37 (2026-09-13, same session): ran the confirmation test, implemented the chosen fix end-to-end, but the fix does NOT resolve the runaway — the array-collapse bug is real and independently fixed, but is not (solely) the runaway's root cause

Direct follow-up to the user's instruction: "perform the confirmation test, then based on the outcomes pick the best of the options to move forward (best is performance AND maintainability for future godot updates)."

**Confirmation test — several attempts before landing on a decisive one**: first tried forcing `sdfgi_cascades = 1` in the scratch project (Round 36's proposed no-op test) — hit an unrelated, pre-existing upstream bug first: with exactly one cascade, `gi.cpp`'s `parent_average = cascades[i].lightprobe_average_tex;` self-aliasing fallback (comment: *"If there is only one SDFGI cascade, we can't use the previous cascade for blending"*) creates a storage-image binding that aliases another binding in the same set — Vulkan tolerates this, WebGPU's stricter validation rejects it (`Writable storage texture binding aliasing`), breaking command buffer submission and rendering the scene solid black (uninformative). Tried `sdfgi_cascades = 2` instead — inconclusive, since cascade index 1 still exercises the collapse-to-cascade-0 bug. Settled on a direct driver-level experiment instead: temporarily changed `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`'s `uniform_set_create()` `UNIFORM_TYPE_TEXTURE` case from `uniform.ids[0]` to `uniform.ids[uniform.ids.size() - 1]` (bind the *last* array element instead of the first), rebuilt, and re-ran the standard 300s WebGPU capture at the original `sdfgi_cascades = 4` baseline. **Result: the scene was completely visually stable for the full 300 seconds**, matching a normal well-lit scene, zero errors. Reverted the driver change immediately after (`git checkout --`). At the time this looked like airtight confirmation of Round 36's hypothesis.

**Fix approach decision**: weighed the two options Round 36 proposed. Option B (a fully generic SPIR-V-level pass doing real control-flow synthesis — new basic blocks, `OpSelectionMerge`/`OpBranchConditional`/`OpPhi`, handling arbitrary read-modify-write shapes) would also need to cover `sdfgi_preprocess.glsl`'s storage-image arrays (`dst_occlusion[8]`/`src_occlusion[8]`, found this round via a broader scope grep — genuinely runtime-indexed, some read-modify-write) — a much larger, higher-risk class of transformation than this driver's existing 12 simple ID-substitution passes. Chose **Option A**: a shared, reusable GLSL macro (`webgpu_texture3d_array_inc.glsl`, new file) declaring 8 individually-numbered bindings on WebGPU (one combined array on every other backend, unaffected) plus a helper function branching on the runtime index — letting `glslangValidator` generate the branch's control flow for free rather than hand-rolling it at the SPIR-V level. Verified the macro's approach end-to-end with a standalone fixture before touching real shaders: GLSL ternary selection between two opaque texture handles is rejected by `glslangValidator` (`wrong operand types: no operation ':' exists...`), so the macro uses an if/else-branching helper *function* instead (verified this compiles and converts correctly through the full pipeline). Added a matching new cross-driver trait, `RD::SUPPORTS_TEXTURE_ARRAY_BINDINGS` (`rendering_device_commons.h`; `true` on Vulkan/Metal/D3D12, `false` on WebGPU), following the established `SUPPORTS_SHAREABLE_TEXTURE_FORMATS` pattern from Round 21 exactly.

**Implementation**: applied the macro to all four affected shaders — `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl` (1 array, `sdf_cascades`), `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` and `sdfgi_debug.glsl` (4 arrays each: `sdf_cascades`/`light_cascades`/`aniso0_cascades`/`aniso1_cascades`), and `gi.glsl` (the main per-frame scene GI compositing shader — found affected mid-audit, not just the three SDFGI-preprocessing shaders originally suspected; same 4-array pattern).
