On the C++ side, added one shared static helper in `gi.cpp`, `_gi_bind_cascade_texture_array()`, which branches on `RD::get_singleton()->has_feature(RD::SUPPORTS_TEXTURE_ARRAY_BINDINGS)` to construct either one combined `RD::Uniform` (non-WebGPU, unchanged behavior) or 8 separate ones at derived binding numbers (WebGPU) — and replaced all 13 previously-identified inline `for (uint32_t j...) { u.append_id(...); }` loops across 4 call sites (`direct_light`'s cascade setup, `integrate`'s per-cascade uniform set, `debug_draw`'s per-view uniform set, `process_gi`'s main uniform set) with calls to this helper, preserving each site's existing default-white-texture padding logic for cascade slots beyond `cascades.size()`.

**Two real bugs found and fixed while getting this to actually run (both worth recording for whoever touches this again)**:

1. **A genuine binding-number collision with the push-constant emulation ring buffer.** The chosen synthetic binding scheme was originally `100`/`110`/`120`/`130` (sdf/light/aniso0/aniso1, each an 8-wide range) — chosen only to avoid colliding with each shader's own small hand-numbered bindings. This produced real WebGPU validation errors (`Binding doesn't exist in [BindGroupLayoutInternal ...]`) that did **not** occur on a stashed pre-fix build (confirmed by `git stash`-ing the whole fix, rebuilding, and re-capturing — zero errors on the unmodified baseline, proving this was a regression in the fix, not a pre-existing issue). Root cause: `drivers/webgpu/spirv_preprocess.cpp`'s `split_combined_samplers` pass doubles every binding number (`n → 2n`) to make room for samplers split out of GLSL's combined-sampler syntax, *except* it special-cases the literal value `120` as `PC_RING_BUFFER_BINDING` (the push-constant ring buffer's binding, at `set 3`) and deliberately skips doubling it — but that check only looks at the raw binding number, not the set, so `aniso0_cascades`'s first sub-binding (`set 0, binding 120`) got wrongly left un-doubled and collided with whatever real `set 0` binding *did* land on the doubled slot it should have occupied. Fixed by shifting the scheme to `100`/`110`/`130`/`140` (skipping `120` entirely) across all four shader files and both `gi.cpp` call sites; added a permanent `IMPORTANT` warning to `webgpu_texture3d_array_inc.glsl`'s header comment so nobody repeats this. Verified via direct `glslangValidator` → `tint_convert_cli` inspection of the generated WGSL: all 32 texture bindings now double cleanly (`200`-`214`, `220`-`234`, `260`-`274`, `280`-`294`), no anomalies.
2. **Hit the documented `wgsl_precompiled.gen.h` staleness trap** (`LIVE_REPRO_METHODOLOGY.md`'s "Known SCons build-staleness traps" section, written in an earlier round specifically to warn about this) — rebuilt and re-exported after the shader edits without first deleting this file, so the runtime kept serving the *old*, pre-fix precompiled WGSL despite a "successful," fully up-to-date-looking `scons` build. Caught by noticing the freshly-exported build still exhibited the exact same symptom as an intentionally-reverted (`git stash`) baseline build. Fixed by `rm drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompiled.gen.h` before rebuilding; confirmed regeneration picked up the new shader source (`grep` for `sdf_cascades_0`/`binding(200` in the regenerated header).

**Full verification pipeline run after both fixes above**: native editor rebuild clean; web WebGPU template rebuild clean (one pre-existing, unrelated `-Wswitch` warning); `shader_corpus` 13/13, `driver_unit_tests` 327/327, `preprocessing_tests` 192/192 (+1 expected skip) all pass; checksum-verified fresh export (`md5sum` match between `bin/` output and the scratch project's exported `.wasm`) before every capture.

**The actual confirmation-test capture, re-run properly this time — and the critical negative result**: a full 300s WebGPU capture of the scratch project (`sdfgi_cascades = 4`, unchanged baseline) with the complete, correctly-built fix in place produced **zero validation errors, but the scene still goes visibly white starting around t=40s and is fully blown out by t=100s onward** — indistinguishable from the original, pre-investigation bug report. **The fix, despite being independently correct and now verified end-to-end, does not resolve the runaway.**

**This forced a re-examination of Round 36/37's own "confirmation test" result, and it does not hold up**: with `cascades.size() = 4` (this scratch project's setting) and `MAX_CASCADES = 8`, `gi.cpp`'s existing padding logic fills array slots 4-7 with `DEFAULT_RD_TEXTURE_3D_WHITE` (a texture that reads as constant `1.0` everywhere) rather than a real cascade texture. The driver-level "bind last element" hack used for confirmation therefore didn't make the shader read the *correct* cascade for each index — it made *every* array access, regardless of requested index, read this constant-white padding texture instead. A constant-white "SDF" (`sdf_cascades[i].r * 255.0 - 1.0 = 254`, an enormous positive "distance" everywhere) makes every ray-march step conclude "no obstacle, keep marching to the cascade's edge" — i.e. it didn't fix cascade indexing, it **silently disabled the entire SDF ray-march's ability to ever register a hit**, which trivially "stabilizes" the output by making SDFGI's contribution inert, not by making it *correct*. This is a materially different, weaker claim than what Round 36 concluded, and invalidates treating that experiment as proof the array-collapse bug was the runway's root cause — it only proved that *feeding inert data* instead of *wrong-but-real data* stops the runaway, which is compatible with the real bug being somewhere else entirely, downstream of correct-looking cascade sampling.

**Isolated where the runaway actually comes from, with a clean, fast test**: temporarily set `sdfgi_enabled = false` in the scratch project's `main.tscn` (no shader/engine rebuild needed — pure content change against the already-built, already-exported fixed engine) and re-captured 120s. **Result: completely stable, normal-looking scene for the full duration** — visually identical to the healthy t=20s frame. This conclusively confirms: (a) the runaway is genuinely caused by SDFGI (not exposure/tonemapping/anything else in this camera-sim project, which stays behaviorally identical either way), and (b) **the real root cause is still open** — it is *not* (solely) the cascade-array-binding collapse, since that bug is now genuinely fixed (verified via direct WGSL inspection showing correct per-index bindings with no anomalies) and the runaway persists unchanged.

**Where this leaves things**: the array-collapse bug fixed this round is real, independently verified, and worth keeping regardless — `gi.cpp`/`gi.glsl`/`sdfgi_integrate.glsl`/`sdfgi_debug.glsl`/`sdfgi_direct_light.glsl` were all silently reading the wrong cascade's data for out-of-range indices before this fix, on every WebGPU frame, which is simply incorrect regardless of its relationship to the runaway. But the actual runway root cause needs fresh investigation, and the working theory from Rounds 30-36 (that it was fully explained by this collapse) is now known to be wrong. Rounds 33-35's own findings (SH-accumulation rolling-average divergence, ruled-out hazard-tracking/push-constant hypotheses) are still valid and still unexplained — those should be the starting point for whoever picks this back up, now with the added constraint that the cascade-indexing bug is no longer a viable explanation and can be ruled out from consideration. **Not yet done**: reverting the array-collapse fix was *not* done (it's being kept, as noted above, since it's an independent real bug) — but the *runaway* itself is still open and needs a new hypothesis.

**Scope also expanded, not yet acted on**: this round's broader grep also confirmed VoxelGI (`scene_forward_clustered_inc.glsl`'s and `volumetric_fog_process.glsl`'s `voxel_gi_textures[MAX_VOXEL_GI_INSTANCES=8]`) and particles (`particles.glsl`'s `sdf_vec_textures[MAX_3D_TEXTURES=7]`) share the exact same array-of-textures pattern and are equally affected by the (now-understood-to-be-separate-from-the-runaway) binding-collapse bug — not fixed this round, follow-up work, can reuse `webgpu_texture3d_array_inc.glsl` as-is since the macro is generic. `sdfgi_preprocess.glsl`'s `dst_occlusion[8]`/`src_occlusion[8]` storage-image arrays (genuinely runtime-indexed, some read-modify-write) are also confirmed-affected-but-unfixed, same follow-up scope.

**Verified**: scratch project's temporary `sdfgi_enabled = false` edit reverted (restored from a `.bak` copy) after the isolation test; `sdfgi_cascades` confirmed still at its original `4`. All temporary driver-level "bind last" experiment code reverted (`git checkout --`) before implementing the real fix. **Committed**: not yet — this entry, plus the fix itself (`gi.cpp`, `gi.glsl`, `sdfgi_{direct_light,integrate,debug}.glsl`, `webgpu_texture3d_array_inc.glsl` (new), `rendering_device_commons.h`, and the four drivers' trait cases), are staged in the working tree pending the user's decision on how to proceed given the negative result above.

### Round 38 (2026-09-14, new session): a three-way clearing experiment narrows the runaway's cross-frame hazard to `lightprobe_average_tex` specifically, with the reasoning behind why two of the three results needed a second look before trusting them

Direct follow-up to Round 37's closing note: Rounds 33-35's SH-accumulation-divergence findings are the last unrefuted leads, and whoever picks this up next should start from the `lightprobe_average_tex` instrumentation methodology rather than re-deriving it.

**Setup**: rsync'd a fresh scratch copy of the user's real project (`sdfgi_r38`), enabled `sdfgi_enabled = true` directly in `main.tscn` (the project's existing `sdfgi_bounce_feedback`/`sdfgi_min_cell_size`/`adjustment_enabled` settings were left at their real defaults), confirmed `export_presets.cfg`'s `variant/extensions_support=true` (Round 23's dlink-template gotcha) before doing anything else.

**Round 38a — clear both `lightprobe_history_tex` and `lightprobe_average_tex` every frame**: added temporary instrumentation to `gi.cpp`'s `update_probes()` — immediately before the integrate compute dispatch, `RD::texture_clear()` on both textures for every cascade (breaking their intended cross-frame persistence on purpose); immediately after the dispatch, a throttled (~every 60 frames) `RD::texture_get_data()` readback of `lightprobe_average_tex` printing the max `|value|` across all texels/components (same pattern as Round 33). A full 200s/~3,480-frame WebGPU capture came back **completely flat at `max_abs_average=512` for the entire capture, zero console errors** — versus the known baseline (unbounded growth to hundreds of millions, full whiteout by t≈100s) with both textures left to persist normally. This is the first result in 38 rounds to directly demonstrate that the divergence is a property of the *persisted state itself*, not the per-frame computation alone (Round 36 had already shown the single-frame WGSL translation is correct) — but on its own it doesn't yet say *which* of the two textures' persistence is responsible, since both were broken at once.

**Round 38b — clear only `lightprobe_average_tex`, leave `lightprobe_history_tex` real**: same instrumentation, `texture_clear()` restricted to `lightprobe_average_tex` only. With `average` forced to `0` before each dispatch, the shader's own math (`average -= prev_value; average += ivalue;`) reduces to `average = ivalue - prev_value` computed fresh every frame — a direct probe of whether `lightprobe_history_tex`'s real, 30-layer ring-buffer reads are internally self-consistent frame to frame. Result: **flat at exactly `max_abs_average=512` for the entire ~10,680-frame/~178s capture, zero errors** — the same magnitude as Round 38a, and rock-steady the whole time. Since this result depends on `lightprobe_history_tex` actually being read and written correctly (a stale, wrong, or inconsistently-indexed `prev_value` read would show up here as drift or instability in the `ivalue - prev_value` residual), this is real evidence that `lightprobe_history_tex`'s cross-frame persistence is behaving correctly.

**Round 38c — clear only `lightprobe_history_tex`, leave `lightprobe_average_tex` real**: same instrumentation, inverted. Result: **the runaway reappears**, growing essentially linearly (`max_abs_average` climbing from ~30k at frame 120 to ~105M by frame 3,840), closely matching the shape of the original untouched baseline. **This needed a second look before treating it as confirmation that `lightprobe_average_tex` is independently unsafe**: with `lightprobe_history_tex` forced to `0` every frame, `prev_value` is always `0`, so the shader's own math degenerates from a bounded rolling average into `average = average_prev + ivalue` — a pure, unbounded running sum with the subtract-old-contribution term permanently disabled. Given `ivalue` is a real, non-zero, clamped-but-still-substantial SH sample every frame (the observed growth rate, ~27,472/frame on average, is consistent with values near the ±32,767 clamp ceiling), an accumulator with no subtraction *must* grow roughly linearly forever — this is expected consequence of breaking the ring-buffer's own bookkeeping, not new evidence of a WebGPU-specific hazard. **Round 38c's result is therefore not informative on its own** — it doesn't distinguish "average_tex's own storage is corrupted" from "an unmodified accumulator would look like this even on a perfectly correct GPU once you disable its subtraction term."

**Putting the three results together**: the informative comparison is Round 38b (isolates `lightprobe_history_tex`'s read correctness, comes back clean) against the known real-persistence baseline (both textures behaving as designed, still runs away on WebGPU only, never on Vulkan, with identical code in both cases). Since 38b shows `lightprobe_history_tex`'s cross-frame reads are self-consistent, and the baseline shows the *combination* of both textures persisting normally is what actually diverges, the remaining candidate is `lightprobe_average_tex`'s own cross-frame read-modify-write. Structurally, `lightprobe_average_tex` is read and written at the *identical texel on every single consecutive frame* — zero slack between one frame's `imageStore` and the very next frame's `imageLoad` of the same address — whereas `lightprobe_history_tex` writes cycle through a 30-layer ring buffer (`ivec3 prev_pos = ivec3(pos.x, pos.y * SH_SIZE + i, int(params.history_index))`), so any single layer isn't re-read for a full 30 frames. If the true underlying defect is some form of WebGPU/Dawn cross-`wgpuQueueSubmit()` write-visibility latency on this exact storage-texture access shape (a live hypothesis since Round 33/34, previously "leading but weakened/unproven" per Round 34's own spec-level reasoning about content-timeline ordering guarantees), `lightprobe_average_tex`'s zero-slack pattern would be the only one of the two exposed to it — `lightprobe_history_tex`'s naturally wide 30-frame spacing would very plausibly absorb the same latency without ever producing a visible symptom, explaining why 38b comes back clean despite the same class of hazard potentially still being present there too, just unobservable.

**Where this leaves the investigation**: this is the most decisive narrowing since the investigation began — from "the SH-accumulation mechanism as a whole diverges on WebGPU" (Round 33-35) to "specifically `lightprobe_average_tex`'s zero-slack same-texel cross-frame persistence is implicated; `lightprobe_history_tex`'s ring-buffer persistence checks out clean." **Proposed fix, not yet implemented**: ping-pong/double-buffer `lightprobe_average_tex` — give it a second layer and alternate which layer is read-from/written-to by frame parity (`render_pass % 2`), the same fundamental trick `lightprobe_history_tex`'s 30-layer ring buffer already uses, just with a period of 2 instead of 30. This would require: (a) allocating `lightprobe_average_tex` with `array_layers = 2` instead of `1` (`gi.cpp`'s texture-creation block, ~L605-654), (b) adding a new push-constant field (mirroring the existing `history_index`) so the shader knows which layer to read/write this frame, (c) changing `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `lightprobe_average_texture` declaration from `image2D` to `image2DArray` and updating `average_pos` from an `ivec2` to an `ivec3` with the new layer index, and (d) auditing every other reader/writer of `lightprobe_average_tex` in `gi.cpp`/other SDFGI shaders (`sdfgi_debug.glsl` reads it too, at minimum) for the same layer-indexing change. This touches shader ABI (push-constant struct layout) and multiple files, so is scoped but nontrivial — not attempted this round pending confirmation this is the right level of invasiveness versus a narrower alternative.

**Verified**: native editor rebuild clean after every diagnostic edit (`scons platform=linuxbsd target=editor dev_build=yes`) and after the final revert; web WebGPU template rebuilt clean for each of the three configurations (Round 38a/b/c), each export checksum-verified (`md5sum` on `index.side.wasm` against the freshly-built `bin/` output) before capturing, per Round 23's established lesson. All three captures ran the full intended duration (200s/~178-200s real time each) with **zero console errors** in every configuration. All temporary diagnostic instrumentation (`gi.cpp`'s three `texture_clear()` variants and the throttled `texture_get_data()`/`print_line()` readback) was reverted via `git checkout -- servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp` before ending the round; a clean native rebuild after the revert confirms no diagnostic code remains. Regression suites (`shader_corpus`/`preprocessing_tests`/`driver_unit_tests`) not re-run this round since no shipped code changed — only diagnostic instrumentation that was fully reverted. **Not committed**: nothing to commit — the round produced no lasting code change, only this documentation entry and the narrowed hypothesis above.

### Round 39 (2026-09-14, same session): a skip-dispatch slack-gap experiment shows the proposed ping-pong fix for `lightprobe_average_tex` would likely be insufficient on its own — the hazard's rate falls with more temporal slack but does not vanish

Direct follow-up to Round 38's proposed fix: before investing in the multi-file ping-pong/double-buffer change (shader ABI, push-constant field, `image2D`→`image2DArray`), a cheap test was run first to check the fix's core assumption — that adding *any* fixed amount of slack between one frame's write and the next frame's read of the same `lightprobe_average_tex` texel fully resolves the divergence. A 2-layer ping-pong buffer is, in write-to-next-read timing terms, equivalent to skipping the `MODE_PROCESS` dispatch every other frame (so the same texel is only touched every 2nd frame instead of every frame) — so that was simulated directly by instrumenting `gi.cpp`'s `update_probes()` to early-out before the compute dispatch on a schedule, without touching any shader/ABI code, using the same throttled `lightprobe_average_tex` readback as Round 38.

**Variant 1 — skip every other frame (2-frame write-to-next-read gap, timing-equivalent to a 2-layer ping-pong)**: `if (render_pass % 2 == 1) return;` before the dispatch. Result: growth continues, but far slower than baseline — `max_abs_average` reached 8,043,980 by frame 10,680 (~2,670 active dispatches), zero console errors. This is a **negative result for the proposed fix**: a naive 2-layer ping-pong would reduce but not eliminate the runaway.

**Variant 2 — skip 3 of every 4 frames (4-frame gap, double the slack of variant 1)**: `if (render_pass % 4 != 0) return;`. Result: growth continues but slower still — `max_abs_average` reached 1,685,815 by frame 10,680 (~2,640 active dispatches), zero console errors.

**Growth-per-active-dispatch rate, all conditions compared** (active dispatches = frames actually running `MODE_PROCESS`, excluding skipped frames):

| Condition | max_abs_average | frame | active dispatches | rate (Δ/dispatch) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Gap=0 (Round 38c baseline, every frame) | 105,493,964 | 3,840 | 3,840 | ≈27,472 |
| Gap=2 (skip every other frame) | 8,043,980 | 10,680 | 2,670 | ≈1,506 |
| Gap=4 (skip 3 of 4 frames) | 1,678,647 (Δ from frame 120→10,680) | 10,680 | 2,640 | ≈636 |

The rate drops steeply from gap 0→2 (≈18x) and continues to drop from gap 2→4 (≈2.4x further), so slack is genuinely doing something — this is not noise. But the drop is **not converging toward zero within any practically-sized buffer**: going from a 2-deep to a 4-deep ring buffer only roughly halved the residual rate, not eliminated it, and there is no sign of a hard cutoff/threshold gap beyond which the hazard fully disappears (which is what the original "cross-`wgpuQueueSubmit()` content-timeline visibility latency" theory from Round 33/34 would predict — that theory expected a bounded number of frames of guaranteed staleness, after which reads become reliably fresh). A diminishing-but-nonzero-per-doubling curve is more consistent with a **probabilistic race** (e.g. Dawn/Chromium's automatic hazard tracking occasionally reordering or racing the compute-to-compute same-resource read-modify-write across separate queue submissions) than with a fixed-latency visibility delay — more wall-clock gap reduces the *chance* of the race being lost on any given frame, but doesn't structurally prevent it.

**Revised recommendation**: the Round 38 "ping-pong `lightprobe_average_tex`" fix proposal should be considered **downgraded from leading candidate to insufficient-alone**, based on this round's direct evidence. Widening the ring buffer further (e.g. matching `lightprobe_history_tex`'s 30-layer depth) would likely keep reducing the residual rate — extrapolating the ~2.4x-per-doubling trend, a 30-deep buffer would land somewhere in the tens-of-units-per-dispatch range, not zero — but "reduce a bug's growth rate by another couple orders of magnitude without eliminating it" is a poor place to stop for a rendering correctness fix, and a 30-layer buffer for a single non-array 2D texture is a large ABI/memory footprint to spend on a partial mitigation. Two directions are worth considering before writing more mitigation code:
1. **Look for an explicit synchronization primitive instead of relying on slack.** `command_pipeline_barrier` is a documented no-op on WebGPU (this driver relies entirely on automatic hazard tracking — see CLAUDE.md's "Barriers" note); if Dawn's automatic tracking has a genuine gap for same-texture compute-to-compute RMW hazards across separate `wgpuQueueSubmit()` calls, the correct fix is likely an explicit storage-texture usage scope or an inserted read/write dependency (if WebGPU/Dawn exposes one), not adding delay.
2. **Try to reproduce the race in isolation**, e.g. a minimal Dawn/Chromium WebGPU test case outside Godot entirely (a compute shader doing `imageLoad`→`imageStore` on the same storage-texture texel every frame across separate submits, with an intentionally simple accumulator), to determine whether this is a genuine Dawn/Chromium bug worth reporting upstream versus something specific to how this driver structures its command submission (e.g. queue timeline vs device timeline, submission batching, missing `wgpuQueueOnSubmittedWorkDone` usage, etc.).

**Verified**: web WebGPU template rebuilt and export-checksum-verified for both variants (`build_r38fu_web_release.log`, `build_r38fu2_web_release.log`, both clean; `md5sum` matches confirmed each time). Both captures ran the full intended duration with **zero console errors**. All temporary instrumentation reverted via `git checkout -- servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp`; native editor rebuild after the revert confirmed clean. Regression suites not re-run (no shipped code changed). **Not committed**: nothing to commit — this round produced no lasting code change, only this documentation entry and the revised recommendation above.

### Round 40 (2026-09-14, same session): step 1 of plan-of-attack.md's Phase 1 investigation plan — a minimal, from-scratch WebGPU repro of Round 39's exact same-texel pattern does NOT reproduce the divergence, at real-GPU scale, casting real doubt on the "probabilistic Dawn hazard-tracking race" theory

Built `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/` (new, standalone — no Godot code, no engine build involved) per plan-of-attack.md's Tier 1 Phase 1 step 1: a compute shader implementing the exact telescoping accumulator shape from `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` (`average -= prev; average += new; store(history, new); store(average, average)`) against two 1×1 `r32sint` persistent `read_write` storage textures, one texel, read-modify-written every call across **separate `queue.submit()`s with no explicit CPU-GPU sync** — matching the real shader's access pattern with Godot, GLSL, Tint, and the renderer entirely removed. Because the real algorithm telescopes (each call's subtraction exactly cancels the prior call's own addition when reads see the true previous write), the correct final value after N calls is a single known integer (the last value written), not a running sum — so *any* cross-submission hazard-tracking gap shows up as an exact integer mismatch, not statistical noise, making this a strong pass/fail test rather than a fuzzy one. "Slack" (Round 39's variable) is reproduced by inserting N unrelated filler compute dispatches on the same queue between each accumulate call, simulating a real frame's other GPU work sharing the queue timeline.

**Run against this machine's real GPU** (`WEBGPU_REAL_GPU=1`, same Vulkan-backed headless-Chromium flags `scene_smoketest` uses — NVIDIA RTX 4080 SUPER, `google-chrome` reports adapter `nvidia`/`lovelace`): two runs, 1,000 and then 8,000 accumulate calls per slack value, across slack values `0, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32` (first run) and `0, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128` (second run, ~72,000 total accumulate calls plus their filler dispatches). **Zero divergence at every slack value in both runs** — `actual` exactly matched `expected` every single time, including at slack=0 (zero temporal gap, the condition Round 38/39 identified as the worst case in the real shader).

**Why this matters**: WebGPU's spec guarantees operations submitted to the same `GPUQueue` execute in submission order, with automatic hazard tracking inserting whatever the backend needs for correctness — same-queue same-resource RMW across separate submits is explicitly a supported, guaranteed-correct pattern per spec, not an edge case implementations are allowed to race. A genuine "probabilistic race in Dawn's automatic hazard tracking" for exactly this pattern would be a serious, fundamental Dawn conformance bug — and this repro, deliberately built to be as close to a minimal reproduction of that exact claim as possible, found no evidence of it after tens of thousands of same-texel touches at real-GPU scale, including at zero slack. This is a negative result, but a load-bearing one: it does not prove the theory wrong everywhere (Dawn/Chromium version differences, a subtler dependency shape than this repro captures, or something specific to how *this driver* structures its submissions could still be in play), but it substantially weakens "generic Dawn hazard-tracking race" as the leading explanation, and **positively points at plan-of-attack.md step 1's "not reproducible" branch**: re-examine Godot-specific state instead (`FramebufferCacheRD`/`UniformSetCacheRD` cache-key/staleness issues, or Emscripten's single-threaded event-loop submission timing relative to `requestAnimationFrame`) rather than continuing to treat this as a from-scratch Dawn bug to file upstream.

**Not yet done** (still open, per the plan's later steps, now de-prioritized given this result): step 2's binary-search variants (forced `onSubmittedWorkDone()` sync between calls, single-command-buffer read+write pairing, a second Dawn backend) — worth doing only if a Godot-specific cause from step 4 doesn't pan out, since this round's result suggests the "explicit sync fixes it" question is less likely to be the load-bearing one. Also not done: instrumenting the real Godot repro to log `lightprobe_average_tex`'s actual bound `WGTexture`/view pointer across consecutive frames (plan step 4) — this is now the more promising next step. No engine code changed this round; only the new standalone `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/` directory was added (not yet wired into `local_ci.sh` — it's a one-off investigation tool, not a regression-guarding test, so left out of the standard suite for now).

**Files**: `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/` (new: `race_repro.js`, `index.html`, `run_repro.mjs`, `package.json`, `README.md`, `.gitignore`).

### Round 41 (2026-09-15, same session): two more angles on Phase 1 — (a) code reading rules out uniform-set/bind-group cache staleness for this specific accumulator, structurally; (b) rebuilt Round 40's repro through the real `emdawnwebgpu` C API path (not raw browser JS) — still no divergence

**(a) `FramebufferCacheRD`/`UniformSetCacheRD` staleness, read out of the code rather than tested**: plan-of-attack.md step 4 named a cache-key collision or missed invalidation handing back a stale texture view as a candidate Godot-specific cause. Read `gi.cpp`'s `SDFGI::create()` (~L1155): `cascades[i].integrate_uniform_set` is built by a **direct** `RD::get_singleton()->uniform_set_create(uniforms, ...)` call, once, when a cascade is (re)created — not looked up per-frame through `UniformSetCacheRD::get_cache()` or any equivalent keyed cache. The same fixed `RID` is then reused every frame via `compute_list_bind_uniform_set()` (3 call sites, ~L1436/1480/2183-2207). On the WebGPU driver side, `uniform_set_create()` (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp:6020`) builds one `WGPUBindGroup` via `wgpuDeviceCreateBindGroup()` at creation time and stores it in the `WGUniformSet` struct returned to the caller — WebGPU bind groups pin the exact resource views passed at creation for the bind group's lifetime, they don't get re-resolved on each use. Combined: there is no cache lookup happening per-frame at all for this uniform set, and no mechanism by which the *same* `RID`/bind group could ever be handed back pointing at a *different* underlying texture, since `lightprobe_average_tex`/`lightprobe_history_tex` are themselves created once in `create()` and only freed in the cascade's destructor. **This structurally rules out the "stale cached view" theory for this specific accumulator** — worth documenting so it isn't re-investigated as a live lead. (It doesn't rule out `FramebufferCacheRD`/`UniformSetCacheRD` staleness as a bug *elsewhere* in the driver — only that it can't be the mechanism behind *this* symptom, since the relevant bind group here was never going through either cache to begin with.)

**(b) `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/native_emdawnwebgpu_repro.cpp` (new)**: Round 40's `race_repro.js` talks to `navigator.gpu` directly from plain browser JS — it does not exercise the actual path `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` submits through, which is `wgpuQueueSubmit()` (the `webgpu.h` C API) via Emscripten's `emdawnwebgpu` port (`--use-port=emdawnwebgpu`, same as this repo's own `platform=web webgpu=yes` builds). Wrote the identical repro as a standalone C++ program using `webgpu_cpp.h`, compiled with `em++ --use-port=emdawnwebgpu -sEXIT_RUNTIME=0`, driven by a callback chain (`RequestAdapter`→`RequestDevice`→per-slack accumulate loop→`MapAsync` readback→next slack), to test whether the C API→JS glue layer itself has a bug the pure-JS repro couldn't see.

Hit one harness-only snag getting this running: `wgpu::Buffer::MapAsync()`'s lambda-captured `wgpu::Buffer` copy didn't reliably keep the JS-side buffer alive across the async gap in this emdawnwebgpu build (Emscripten 4.0.11) — 100% reproducible `MapAsyncStatus::Aborted`, `"Buffer was destroyed before mapping was resolved."`, regardless of slack/call count, confirmed via the message text once a `SetUncapturedErrorCallback`/message-printing pass was added for diagnosis. Root cause not fully chased down (likely a `Callback`-template lifetime gap specific to `AllowSpontaneous` mode in this port version) since it's a harness bug, not a finding about the investigation; worked around with an explicit `static std::vector<wgpu::Buffer>` keep-alive before `MapAsync()`, documented in the file and `README.md` so it isn't mistaken for a real result if this file is revisited.

**Run against this machine's real GPU** (same flags as Round 40): 8,000 calls at slack `0, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16` (the slack=32/64/128/256 continuation of that same run didn't finish inside a 5-minute wall-clock budget — filler-dispatch volume scales with slack × calls, and at 8,000 calls slack=128 alone is ~1M extra submits marshaled through the wasm↔JS boundary, meaningfully slower per-submit than raw JS), then a second, lighter run at 1,000 calls for slack `32, 64, 128` (slack=256 again didn't finish in 5 minutes at this scale and was not pursued further — diminishing returns, consistent with Round 39's own shrinking per-doubling trend). **Zero divergence in every case** — same exact-match result as Round 40's pure-JS version, now confirmed through the real C API path too.

**Conclusion for Phase 1 so far**: two independent angles (structural code reading of the uniform-set path, and a from-scratch repro through both the raw-JS and real C-API submission paths) have not found any evidence for either of the two leading theories (Dawn hazard-tracking race; stale cached bind group). The remaining unexplored candidate from plan-of-attack.md step 4 is Emscripten's single-threaded event-loop timing relative to `requestAnimationFrame` — though note this driver's actual web builds use `threads=no` (CLAUDE.md build commands), so there is no cross-thread concurrency possible in the shipped configuration either; whatever gap (if any) exists would have to be a same-thread ordering issue, not a data race in the usual sense. **Next concrete step, not yet done**: instrument the real Godot repro directly — log `lightprobe_average_tex`'s actual bound `WGTexture`/view pointer (should be provably constant across frames, per finding (a) above) alongside a frame counter and the integrate dispatch's actual submitted/observed value, in the user's real scratch project, to catch the *actual* divergence event live rather than continuing to build proxies for it.

**Files**: `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/native_emdawnwebgpu_repro.cpp`, `run_native_repro.mjs` (new); `.gitignore`, `README.md` updated (build output `native_repro.{html,js,wasm}` gitignored). No engine code changed.

### Round 42 (2026-09-15, same session): live instrumentation against the user's real scratch project — confirms the texture pointer is provably constant, and captures the runaway itself live with a clean, quantified growth rate

Round 41's remaining concrete next step: instrument the real Godot repro directly rather than continue building proxies. Added a temporary, capped diagnostic to `GI::SDFGI::update_probes()` (`gi.cpp`, right after the `INTEGRATE_MODE_PROCESS` compute list ends): every 20th `render_pass`, up to 60 samples, logs cascade 0's `lightprobe_average_tex` driver-level texture pointer (`RD::get_singleton()->get_driver_resource(RD::DRIVER_RESOURCE_TEXTURE, ...)` — no driver-side code changes needed, this existing public API already exposes exactly the raw pointer the plan asked for) alongside a CPU readback (`texture_get_data()`) of probe (0,0)'s raw SH0 texel.

**Setup**: rsynced the user's real `cameraSim` project (`~/Downloads/cameraSim_2026-09-09_10-26-06/testing/`, matching this repo's own memory of where real testing happens) into a scratch copy, added `sdfgi_enabled = true` to `main.tscn`'s existing `Environment` sub-resource (which already had `sdfgi_bounce_feedback`/`sdfgi_min_cell_size` set but not `sdfgi_enabled` — matching Round 30's own description of this same scratch-copy setup from earlier in this investigation). Rebuilt the native editor (28s incremental) and the full web export template (regenerated via `build.sh`'s "Templates only" option, since Round 23 found stale symlinked templates silently no-op past fixes before), exported the scratch project headlessly, and `md5sum`-verified the exported `index.side.wasm` against the freshly built `bin/` wasm before trusting anything (both Round 23 cautions applied). Captured 90 real seconds of browser console via the existing `webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/capture_console.mjs` (real-GPU Playwright flags, this machine's RTX 4080 SUPER).

**Result**: `tex_ptr=0x896f508` on **every single one of the 60 samples**, spanning `render_pass` 20 through 1200 (the full capture) — the texture pointer genuinely never changes across a real, live, bug-exhibiting session. This is Round 41's structural code-reading conclusion now confirmed empirically rather than just by inspection: whatever is causing the runaway, it is not a stale/swapped texture view.

The `sh0` readback caught the runaway itself, live, with a clean number attached: values alternate between `0` and a monotonically growing figure every other sample (worth a closer look in a future round — plausibly a partial dirty-region update pattern rather than a bug in the diagnostic itself, since the pointer/growth signal on the nonzero samples is completely clean), but the nonzero-sample trend is unambiguous and unbounded: `306688 → 326656 → 346112 → 366080 → 386048 → 405504 → 425472 → 445440 → 464896 → 484864 → 504832 → 524288 → 544256 → 564224 → 583680` (render_pass 640 through 1200, every 40 passes) — a near-constant step of ~19,700 per 40 render_passes (~492/render_pass), no sign of leveling off, matching the shape of every prior round's runaway reports but now tied to a specific, reproducible probe/texel/SH-component (probe (0,0), the 4th `ivec4` component) for whoever picks this up next.

**Reverted**: temporary diagnostic removed via `git checkout -- servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp` immediately after the capture; native editor rebuilt clean (28s) to confirm. No lasting engine code change this round.

**Where this leaves Phase 1**: three independent lines of evidence (Round 40/41's proxy repros; Round 41's structural code reading; this round's live confirmation) all agree the texture-identity/cache-staleness angle is a dead end. The remaining open candidates are (a) the still-unexplained alternating-zero readback pattern above — is probe (0,0) only receiving real light contributions on alternating calls for a legitimate scene-geometry reason, or is that itself a symptom worth chasing; (b) Emscripten's same-thread event-loop/submission-ordering angle, not yet directly tested; (c) going back to Round 38's own conclusion (the hazard is specifically in `lightprobe_average_tex`'s cross-frame RMW) with a *narrower*, more targeted live diagnostic than this round's — e.g. logging the exact `ivalue`/`prev_value`/`average` triple each call computes (matching `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s own variable names) rather than just the final stored value, to see directly which term is wrong when the growth happens, instead of continuing to infer it from the outside.

**Files**: `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp` touched temporarily, fully reverted — no net change. Scratch project and captures were not committed (session-local, `/tmp`).

### Round 43 (2026-09-15, same session): a narrower live diagnostic reads back `ivalue`/`average` directly — finds the growth rate exactly equals `ivalue`, strong evidence the accumulator's subtraction term is silently not taking effect

Direct follow-up to Round 42's "still open" item (c): rather than just watch the final `average` value, read back the raw per-frame `ivalue` this call just wrote into `lightprobe_history_tex` (probe (0,0), SH0, at this call's `history_index` layer) *alongside* `average`, to see which term of `average -= prev_value; average += ivalue;` is actually wrong when the runaway happens — matching `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s own variable names instead of inferring anything from outside. Added the same kind of temporary, capped `gi.cpp` diagnostic as Round 42 (reverted after use, no lasting change), with two iterations to get a clean, self-consistent read.

**First pass (coarse, every-20th-render_pass sampling, matching Round 42's cadence)**: also logged the texture's logical `RID` alongside its driver pointer — both were **100% constant** across every sample (further hardening Round 41/42's "not a recreated/stale texture" conclusion — this rules out RID-reuse-after-free as an alternative explanation for Round 42's "constant pointer" finding, since a coincidentally-reused address would still have shown a *different* RID).

**Second pass (fine-grained, every single `render_pass`, no stride)**: revealed something Round 42's coarser sampling couldn't see — `average` reads back as **exactly `(0,0,0,0)` on every odd `render_pass` and a nonzero, monotonically growing value on every single even `render_pass`, with zero exceptions across 150 consecutive samples** (75/75 odd all-zero, 75/75 even all-nonzero — this is not a statistical tendency, it is deterministic for this entire capture). Because a naive interpretation ("the stored value itself literally alternates to zero and back") is hard to square with the shader's own math (there's no `render_pass`-parity branch anywhere in `gi.cpp` or `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` — grepped for it directly), the more likely reading is that **this specific CPU-side readback path (`texture_get_data()` on `lightprobe_average_tex`) itself has a per-call visibility gap that happens to line up with `render_pass` parity** — the true GPU-side value is plausibly growing every single call, and the odd-sample reads are seeing stale/default data instead of the real one. This doesn't change the diagnosis (`average` genuinely diverges), but it's a caution for how much to trust any single `texture_get_data()` call's *exact* value without a way to cross-check it — noted here so a future round doesn't over-interpret the zero/nonzero alternation itself as scene behavior.

**The quantitatively load-bearing part**: once the scene's light reaches probe (0,0) (`ivalue` switches from `0` to a rock-steady constant `512` at `render_pass=331` and never changes again for the rest of the 150-sample window — no oscillation, no drift, just a flat `512` every single call, odd or even), `average`'s growth rate on the even-sample series is **~512 per `render_pass`** (mean step 989/2 render_passes ≈ 494.7/render_pass, i.e. matching `ivalue` almost exactly, well within what the odd-sample read-reliability caveat above would explain as noise). **This is the key number**: if the accumulator's subtraction (`average -= prev_value`) were taking effect at all once `lightprobe_history_tex`'s 30-layer ring buffer has fully cycled through the light-on period (`render_pass > 331 + 30 = 361`), `prev_value` would itself already be `512` (the same constant `ivalue` from 30 calls ago), and correct behavior would show `average` converging to a stable `512` and *staying flat* — not growing linearly forever. Instead growth continues unabated well past `render_pass=450`, at a rate matching `ivalue` almost exactly. **The natural reading: `average += ivalue` is happening every call, but `average -= prev_value` is contributing close to nothing — consistent with `imageLoad(lightprobe_history_texture, prev_pos)` reading back `0` (or something small/stale) instead of the true value written 30 calls earlier, not with `lightprobe_average_tex`'s own same-frame RMW being the hazard site Round 33/38 originally pinned it on.**

**This refines, not contradicts, Round 33/38's original finding** — `lightprobe_average_tex` is still where the *symptom* is visible (it's the accumulator), but the likely fault now points at reads of `lightprobe_history_tex`'s specific layer, written up to `history_size` (30) calls earlier — the texture with the *most* slack of anything in this whole investigation, not the least. That's a real tension with Round 39's "more slack reduces the rate" finding (which would predict `lightprobe_history_tex`'s 30-frame gap to be the *safest* access pattern, not the most broken one) — worth resolving explicitly in whatever picks this up next, rather than quietly ignored.

**Not yet done, the obvious next step**: none of Rounds 40/41's synthetic repros (`webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/`) ever tested this *specific* shape — a multi-layer array texture, written once per call at a rotating layer index, read back at that *same* layer only once every `history_size` calls (maximal slack, not minimal). Every prior synthetic repro used a flat 1-layer, 1-texel texture with a 1-frame (or explicit small "slack") gap. Extending the repro to a real array-layer, `history_size`-delayed read is cheap (no engine rebuild, reuses the existing WebGPU test harness) and directly tests the refined hypothesis above — this is the natural next round rather than further live instrumentation, since the live diagnostic has now extracted what it usefully can without a more invasive change (e.g., a debug output buffer written by the shader itself, bypassing CPU-readback-reliability questions entirely).

**Reverted**: both diagnostic iterations reverted via `git checkout --` after their captures; native editor rebuilt clean after each. No lasting engine change this round.

**Files**: `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/environment/gi.cpp` touched temporarily (twice), fully reverted — no net change. Scratch project and captures not committed (session-local, `/tmp`).

### Round 44 (2026-09-15, same session): the array-layer/large-slack synthetic repro Round 43 called for — negative, but caught a real math error in the repro's own expected value first

Built `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/array_layer_repro.js` (+ `array_layer_index.html`, `run_array_layer_repro.mjs`) per Round 43's concrete next step: a `texture_storage_2d_array<r32sint, read_write>` history texture with `history_size` layers, written once per call at a rotating layer (`layer = call_index % history_size`) and read back at that *same* layer only `history_size` calls later — the actual access pattern `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` uses, which no prior repro in this directory had tested (all previous ones used a flat 1-layer texture with a 1-call gap).

**First run caught a real bug in the repro itself, not in WebGPU**: with a constant per-call value `V=512` (matching Round 43's observed steady-state `ivalue`) written into a *freshly zero-initialized* texture array from call 0, the repro's own "converged" check expected `average` to settle at plain `V` — but that assumption (carried over from `race_repro.js`'s simpler 1-frame-gap case) doesn't hold here: with every layer starting at 0 and no prior data to cancel against, the first `history_size` calls are a genuine sliding-window fill-up, not a telescoping cancellation, so the mathematically correct plateau is `V * history_size` (`2048` for `history_size=4`, `15360` for `history_size=30`) — exactly what the first run measured, flagged as "REPRODUCED" purely because the check itself was wrong. Caught by inspecting the numbers directly (`2048 = 512×4`, `15360 = 512×30`, exactly) rather than trusting the automated pass/fail. **Fixed the expected-value calculation** (`array_layer_repro.js`'s `expectedPlateau = CONSTANT_VALUE * historySize`, with the derivation now documented in the file's own header comment) and re-ran.

**Corrected result**: `average` reaches the correct plateau (`2048`/`15360`) by the end of the first ring-buffer cycle and **holds it perfectly flat for 10 full cycles** (`history_size=4`: 40 calls; `history_size=30`: 300 calls, `filler=2` unrelated dispatches between every accumulate call, real GPU) — genuinely correct, convergent behavior. **This specific pattern, in isolation, does not reproduce Round 43's live finding.**

**Where this leaves things**: Round 43's live measurement (growth rate matching `ivalue` almost exactly, continuing well past 30+ calls) is not in question — it was measured directly in the real engine, twice, with consistent numbers. What this round shows is that the *array-layer-read-with-large-slack* mechanism, isolated to the same minimal 1-invocation-per-dispatch shape every repro in this directory has used so far, isn't sufficient on its own to trigger it. The one structural property every repro to date shares that the real shader doesn't is **dispatch parallelism**: `sdfgi_integrate.glsl` dispatches `probe_axis_count²` threads per call, all concurrently touching the same `lightprobe_average_tex`/`lightprobe_history_tex` resources at different sub-addresses (different probes' rows) within a single dispatch — every synthetic repro so far (`race_repro.js`, `native_emdawnwebgpu_repro.cpp`, this round's `array_layer_repro.js`) uses `dispatchWorkgroups(1)`, a single invocation, zero intra-dispatch contention on the shared textures. **Next step, not yet done — full implementation spec written, not yet run**: extend the repro to dispatch many concurrent invocations that each read/write a *different* texel of the same two textures (matching the real probe-grid shape), to test whether the hazard needs that concurrent same-resource traffic to manifest — this is the one remaining major structural gap between every repro tried so far and the real shader. See `plan-of-attack.md`'s "Round 45 spec" subsection (Tier 1 Phase 1) for the exact dispatch shape, WGSL kernel, texture dimensions, and pass/fail check — written up in full this session but deliberately not implemented or run yet.

**Files**: `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/array_layer_repro.js`, `array_layer_index.html`, `run_array_layer_repro.mjs` (new).

### Round 45 (2026-09-16, same session): the per-dispatch-parallelism repro Round 44 spec'd — also negative, full-grid checked, on real GPU

Implemented `plan-of-attack.md`'s Round 45 spec exactly: `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/parallel_repro.js` (+ `parallel_index.html`, `run_parallel_repro.mjs`) matches `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s real dispatch shape precisely — `@workgroup_size(8, 8, 1)` dispatched `(37, 3, 1)` (7104 invocations, matching `dispatch_threads(289, 17, 1)`'s own out-of-bounds tail via an in-shader bounds check, not a smaller exact-fit grid), each in-bounds invocation looping 16 rows of a shared `289×272` `history_tex`/`average_tex` pair (`history_tex` a `texture_storage_2d_array<r32sint, read_write>` with `history_size` layers). Unlike every prior round's single-texel check, this one reads back the **entire** `289×272` grid via `copyTextureToBuffer` on the final cycle of each config (strided every-8th-texel on intermediate cycles) and checks every value against the expected sliding-window plateau (`V * history_size`), specifically so a hazard affecting only a subset of texels under contention wouldn't be invisible.

**Result: not reproduced.** Ran on real GPU (RTX 4080 SUPER class, `nvidia`/`lovelace` via Vulkan/Chrome, same flags as every prior round) with `history_size=30`, `filler=0,2,8`, 3 full ring-buffer cycles each (90 calls/config). Every one of the 289×272=78,608 texels matched the expected plateau exactly on every cycle checked, for all three filler values, including `filler=0` (zero slack, matching the real shader's own zero-gap `average_tex` touch pattern) — zero mismatches anywhere.

**Where this leaves things**: all four structural hypotheses this session's synthetic repros could isolate — same-texel zero-slack RMW (Round 40/41), large-slack array-layer RMW (Round 44), and now large-slack array-layer RMW *under full per-dispatch concurrency* (this round) — have all come back negative on real GPU. Per `plan-of-attack.md`'s own routing for this outcome, the two remaining candidates are: (a) the Emscripten event-loop-timing angle (queue submission relative to `requestAnimationFrame`/JS microtask ordering) — still fully untested; (b) the real shader's additional Godot-side complexity no repro has modeled at all (14-binding uniform set, the sky uniform set bound to slot 1 *simultaneously*, `store_probes()`'s separate `MODE_STORE` dispatch touching overlapping resources later the same frame) — at this point probably a better use of time to pursue via shader-side debug-output-buffer instrumentation in the real engine (bypassing `texture_get_data()` readback-reliability questions Round 43 flagged) than to keep adding complexity to an increasingly Godot-shaped synthetic repro that has now failed to reproduce the bug four times running.

**Files**: `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/parallel_repro.js`, `parallel_index.html`, `run_parallel_repro.mjs` (new).

### Round 46 (2026-09-16, same session): ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED — shadow-refresh copy used `depth` instead of `layers`, silently freezing every non-zero layer of a split read_write storage texture's shadow

With four synthetic RMW-shape repros negative (Rounds 40/41/44/45), this round changed approach: rather than another dispatch-shape variant, it asked what's structurally *different* about `lightprobe_history_texture`/`lightprobe_average_texture` that no repro had modeled. Both use `rgba16sint`/`rgba32sint` (`sdfgi_integrate.glsl:50-51`), not `r32sint` like every repro so far. Per WebGPU's spec, only `r32float`/`r32sint`/`r32uint` get guaranteed `read_write` storage-texture access in core; everything else needs the `readonly-and-readwrite-storage-textures` feature. **Confirmed by direct feature-list check**: this session's real-GPU test adapter (Chrome/Vulkan/`nvidia`/`lovelace` — the exact adapter every live SDFGI capture in this investigation has used) does **not** have this feature. Without it, `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` splits every `read_write` storage texture into a write-only storage side plus a separate "shadow" texture (a plain sampled texture), refreshed via an explicit `wgpuCommandEncoderCopyTextureToTexture(orig -> shadow)` inserted right before every `command_bind_compute_uniform_sets()` call (`refresh_rw_shadows`, ~L10292) — a mechanism whose own code comment already documents this exact bug class having silently corrupted a *different* texture once before (`bokeh_dof.glsl`'s `color_image` going solid black from a stale shadow).

**First repro attempt (`shadow_split_repro.js`) was negative** for the simplest case (flat, single-layer textures routed through write+shadow-copy split instead of direct `read_write`, telescoping accumulator, slack 0-16, 500 calls) — held at exactly `512` throughout. This negative result was the clue: it meant the split mechanism itself works fine for a *flat* texture, so the bug (if any) had to be specific to the *multi-layer* case, which `lightprobe_history_texture` is (30 layers) and the flat repro wasn't.

**Code reading found the actual bug**: `_create_rw_shadow_bind_entry()` creates the shadow texture with the correct dimension-aware layer count (`p_orig_tex->dimension == WGPUTextureDimension_3D ? p_orig_tex->depth : p_orig_tex->layers`, itself a fix from an earlier round, "Task 9.5 Round 22") — but the `RWShadowRegistration` pushed right after it (~L6434) stored `p_orig_tex->depth` directly (always `1` for a 2D(-array) texture; `depth` and `layers` are separate fields on `WGTexture`), discarding that same fix. `refresh_rw_shadows()`'s copy (~L10307) builds its `WGPUExtent3D` from `reg.depth` — meaning **the per-bind refresh copy only ever propagates layer 0** of a multi-layer array texture; every other layer's shadow is permanently stuck at its initial zero-initialized contents, no matter what the compute shader writes to the real texture. The same bug (using `dst->depth` instead of `dst->layers`) was also present in the `texture_update()`-driven shadow-sync path (~L7914), for CPU-authored textures with shadows.

This maps exactly onto Round 43's finding: `history_index` (`render_pass % history_size`) rotates through all 30 layers, so only 1-in-30 frames (`history_index == 0`) would ever see a correctly refreshed `prev_value`; the other 29-in-30 frames read stale/zero data from a shadow that was never updated past its initial state — precisely "`average -= prev_value` is contributing close to nothing," Round 43's exact words.

**Fixed**: both sites now use the same dimension-aware `layers`-vs-`depth` selection `_create_rw_shadow_bind_entry()`'s shadow-creation code already used correctly.

**Confirmed with a targeted repro (`array_shadow_split_repro.js`, Round 46b)** — the exact array-layer telescoping accumulator (Round 44's shape) routed through a write+shadow split, run with the buggy copy extent (`depthOrArrayLayers=1`, matching the pre-fix code) and the fixed extent (`depthOrArrayLayers=historySize`) side by side, real GPU:
- **Buggy**: unbounded, continuously-growing average at every `history_size` tried (4 and 30) — `history_size=4`: `2048 → 3584 → 5120 → 6656 → ... → 15872` over 10 cycles (constant-rate growth, never plateauing); `history_size=30`: `15360 → 30208 → 45056 → ... → 148992` over 10 cycles. **This is the same qualitative shape as the real SDFGI brightness runaway** — continuous, roughly-linear, unbounded growth, not a one-time jump or noise.
- **Fixed**: holds exactly at the correct plateau (`2048`/`15360`) for all 10 cycles, both `history_size` values — zero divergence.

**Files changed**: `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` (`_create_rw_shadow_bind_entry`'s registration ~L6434, `texture_update()`'s shadow-sync path ~L7914). New repros: `webgpu_tests/sdfgi_race_repro/shadow_split_repro.js`/`shadow_split_index.html`/`run_shadow_split_repro.mjs` (negative flat-case control), `array_shadow_split_repro.js`/`array_shadow_split_index.html`/`run_array_shadow_split_repro.mjs` (positive confirmation, buggy-vs-fixed).

**Live end-to-end confirmation, same session**: native editor (`dev_build=yes`) and full web WebGPU template (`platform=web webgpu=yes dlink_enabled=yes`) both rebuilt clean with the fix — `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` compiled with 0 errors, `wgsl_precompile.py` reported 196/196 shaders converted, 0 Tint failures. Re-ran the exact `cameraSim` scratch-project methodology from Rounds 27-44: fresh scratch copy, `sdfgi_enabled = true` added to `main.tscn`'s Environment (was previously toggled ad hoc per round, now explicit), `extensions_support=true` in `export_presets.cfg`, fresh export template installed and `md5sum`-verified against the exported `index.side.wasm` (first attempt caught a real template-directory mismatch — the installed template lives at `~/.local/share/godot/export_templates/4.7.2.stable/`, not a `.custom_build`-suffixed directory as might be assumed from the editor's own `--version` string — corrected before trusting the capture). A 300-second real-GPU Playwright screenshot-trend capture (`capture_screenshots.mjs`, 15 shots at 20s intervals) against this exact scene/settings — the same one that ran away to solid white in every prior round of this investigation — shows **zero visible change between t=20s and t=300s and zero console errors for the entire capture**. This is the first time in the entire 46-round investigation this specific repro has stayed stable for a multi-minute capture.

**Scope check, same session**: grepped the entire GLSL shader corpus (`servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/`) for `restrict image2DArray`/`restrict iimage2DArray` (the GLSL declarations that become a multi-layer `texture_storage_2d_array<..., read_write>` in WGSL) — `sdfgi_integrate.glsl`'s `lightprobe_history_texture` is the **only** hit in the entire engine. This bug's blast radius was already fully scoped to SDFGI; no other shader needs re-testing. Regression suite (`shader_corpus`, `driver_unit_tests`, `preprocessing_tests`) not yet re-run, though this is a pure C++ driver change with no WGSL/preprocessing-pass changes.

### Task 9.7: Shader-corpus baseline reconciliation — confirms the `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV`/`expected_failures.json` test tier is unreliable (SPIR-V 1.4 vs 1.3, third independent hit), establishes the real current failure count via `wgsl_precompile.py` instead `[DONE — investigation and documentation only, no code fix]`

**Status**: `DONE` (as a triage/documentation task — the underlying tooling gap it identifies is left open, see "Not fixed" below).
**Context**: plan-of-attack.md's Tier 3 #14 asked to reconcile `webgpu_tests/shader_corpus/expected_failures.json` (dated 2026-05-05, predates all of Phase 8/9) against a fresh `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` capture, per `webgpu_tests/README.md`'s documented procedure.

**What happened**: followed the documented procedure exactly — cleared the test project's stale shader cache (`~/.local/share/godot/app_userdata/WebGPU Shader Coverage Test/shader_cache`, otherwise `ShaderRD` skips recompilation and `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` dumps nothing), ran `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV=/tmp/spirv_dump bin/godot.linuxbsd.editor.x86_64 --path webgpu_tests/test_project --quit-after 10`, and fed the result through `validate_spirv_dump.mjs`. First run (project's default `renderer/rendering_method="forward_plus"`) produced 344 files, 260 failing. Re-ran forcing `--rendering-method mobile` (the actual WebGPU-relevant renderer per Task 8.5/README's "Forward Mobile" target) to strip out the ~140 Forward+-Clustered-only files that are dead code on WebGPU regardless of Tint conversion — still 204 files, **113 failing (0 matching the stale baseline)**, dominated by one generic error: `error: cannot take the address of 'var v' in handle address space`, hitting a huge, unrelated cross-section of ordinary shaders (`BlitShaderRD`, `CopyShaderRD`, `CanvasShaderRD`, `SkyShaderRD`, `SmaaBlendingShaderRD`, etc.).

**This is not a new regression — it's a known, previously-hit dead end, now confirmed a third time.** Tasks 8.6 and 8.8 each independently ran into and diagnosed the *exact same* symptom (Task 8.8, verbatim: *"Vulkan-driver SPIR-V dumps are SPIR-V 1.4 ... not the SPIR-V 1.3 the WebGPU driver actually requests, and converting 1.4 input through our 1.3-tuned pipeline produced a wall of unrelated failures (`cannot take the address of 'var SAMPLER_LINEAR_...' in handle address space`) ... not investigated further here"*) while chasing unrelated shader-specific bugs, and both times correctly declined to chase it further. This session's shader-corpus baseline work walked into the same trap a third time, this time as the primary object of investigation rather than a distraction — confirming it's a real, reproducible tooling gap: **`GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV`'s dump comes from whatever `--rendering-driver`/glslang invocation the native Vulkan editor uses (SPIR-V 1.4), which is a different code path from `RenderingShaderContainerWebGPU::get_shader_spirv_version()` (`rendering_shader_container_webgpu.h:95`, hardcoded to `SPIRV_VERSION_1_3` with its own comment explaining why) — the version the WebGPU driver, `tint_wrapper.cpp`, and `tint_convert_cli` are actually tuned against.** SPIR-V 1.4 changes what glslang emits for the `OpEntryPoint` interface list (every global variable, not just Input/Output) among other differences, and the 12+ preprocessing passes in `spirv_preprocess.cpp` were written and tested against 1.3 output — feeding them 1.4 input produces this large, generic-looking but methodologically-meaningless failure set. **The entire `expected_failures.json`/`validate_spirv_dump.mjs` mechanism, as currently wired to `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` + the native editor, cannot produce a trustworthy signal until this version mismatch is fixed at the tooling level** (either force the dump's glslang invocation to `--target-env vulkan1.1`/SPIR-V 1.3, or post-process the dump through `spirv-opt`/similar before validation).

**Do NOT run `validate_spirv_dump.mjs --update-baseline` against a `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` capture right now** — doing so would bake ~113-229 methodology-artifact "expected failures" into `expected_failures.json` as if they were validated Vulkan-only variants, which would then silently mask any *real* future regression that happens to produce a similarly-generic-looking Tint error (the whole point of the baseline is catching regressions; a baseline this bloated with false-baseline entries defeats that).

**The real, trustworthy current signal instead comes from `wgsl_precompile.py`** (`python3 drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompile.py . /tmp/out.gen.h glslangValidator`), which invokes its own `glslangValidator` call directly (not going through the native editor) and is the actual production precompilation path `scons platform=web webgpu=yes` runs. Result, freshly re-run this session against current `sync/4.7-stable` HEAD: **177 compiled, 12 GLSL-level failures (pre-existing, unrelated to WebGPU/Tint — glslang compile errors for specific define combos: `taa_resolve`, `octmap_downsampler`, `octmap_filter`, `octmap_roughness`, `ssao_blur`, `ssil_blur`, `subsurface_scattering`, `cluster_render` (this one is Task 9.4's tracked subgroup gap), `giprobe_write` — already flagged as "pre-existing and unrelated, not investigated" in Task 9.5's log, not re-investigated here), 4 genuine Tint conversion failures**: `tonemap_mobile.glsl:subpass:frag` and `:subpass_1d_lut:frag` (both `input_attachment` `textureLoad` — subpass rendering is disabled under `WEB_ENABLED` per `render_forward_mobile.cpp`, per CLAUDE.md's "Subpasses" note, so these two variants are plausibly unreachable dead code on the actual WebGPU runtime, matching plan-of-attack.md Tier 3 #15's own "check reachability" note), `voxel_gi_debug.glsl:default:vert` (`var with 'storage' address space and 'read_write' access mode cannot be used by vertex pipeline stage` — a real, fixable bug, needs the read-only/read-write split already used elsewhere in the driver applied to this shader), `sdfgi_debug_probes.glsl:default:vert` (`position must be declared for vertex entry point output` — a real, fixable bug, the `position` builtin isn't surviving the SPIR-V round-trip for this shader). This matches plan-of-attack.md's Tier 3 #15 list closely, with two items (`screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl`, `volumetric_fog.glsl`) that Task 8.3/8.8 previously listed as failing **no longer failing** — fixed by some intervening round without this specific list being updated.

**Not fixed this task**: the `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV`/native-editor SPIR-V-version mismatch itself (would need either a `--target-env` override somewhere in the editor's SPIR-V dump path, or a `spirv-opt`-based downgrade step in `validate_spirv_dump.mjs` before handing files to `tint_convert_cli`) — worth doing at some point so this test tier becomes usable again, but it's a tooling task in its own right, not a shader bug, and out of scope for what was asked here. `expected_failures.json` itself was left untouched (still the stale 2026-05-05/31-entry version) rather than updated with bad data.

**Follow-up (same session, immediately after): all 4 genuine `wgsl_precompile.py` Tint failures resolved — plan-of-attack.md Tier 3 #15 done, `wgsl_precompile.py` now reports 0 Tint failures.**

1. **`voxel_gi_debug.glsl:default:vert` — real fix, `infer_readonly_storage()` extended.** Root cause: this pass (`drivers/webgpu/spirv_preprocess.cpp`) only ever looked for `OpVariable` with the modern `StorageBuffer` (12) storage class when deciding which SSBOs are genuinely never written (and therefore safe to mark `NonWritable`, which Tint's WGSL writer needs to emit `read` instead of `read_write`). `voxel_gi_debug.glsl`'s `CellDataBuffer` SSBO — an ordinary, unremarkable `buffer` block, never written anywhere in the shader — got compiled by `wgsl_precompile.py`'s un-flagged `glslangValidator -V` (no `--target-env`, confirmed by Task 9.4 to default to SPIR-V 1.0/Vulkan 1.0) into the **legacy pre-1.3 SSBO encoding**: `Uniform` (2) storage class + `BufferBlock` decoration on the block *type* (as opposed to `StorageBuffer` storage class + `Block` on modern output), which `infer_readonly_storage()`'s variable scan simply didn't recognize as a storage-buffer candidate at all — so it was silently never marked read-only, and Tint (which does recognize both legacy and modern SSBO encodings as the same underlying `storage` address space) correctly rejected the resulting `read_write` storage buffer used in a vertex stage. **Fix**: added two new small pre-passes to `infer_readonly_storage()` — collect struct types decorated `BufferBlock` (new `DECO_BUFFER_BLOCK = 3` constant), then collect `OpTypePointer(Uniform, T)` where `T` is one of those types — and extended the main `OpVariable` scan to also match `storage_class == SC_UNIFORM` (new constant, `= 2`) against that pointer-type set, treating matches identically to modern `StorageBuffer` variables for the rest of the pass (read/write tracking, `NonWritable` emission are already storage-class-agnostic downstream). Verified directly via `TINT_DEBUG_DUMP_PREPROCESSED` + manual `tint_convert_cli` invocation: `cell_data` now emits `var<storage, read>` instead of `var<storage, read_write>`. **Files**: `drivers/webgpu/spirv_preprocess.cpp` (`infer_readonly_storage()`, new `SC_UNIFORM`/`DECO_BUFFER_BLOCK` constants near the top of the file).
2. **`sdfgi_debug_probes.glsl:default:vert` — the bug was in the precompile registry, not the shader.** `gi.cpp:3796-3799` only ever creates `SdfgiDebugProbesShaderRD` with one of `MODE_PROBES`, `MODE_PROBES+USE_MULTIVIEW`, `MODE_VISIBILITY`, or `MODE_VISIBILITY+USE_MULTIVIEW` defined — there is no real runtime variant with neither mode set. The shader's `main()` (both stage files) only ever writes `gl_Position` inside `#ifdef MODE_PROBES`/`#ifdef MODE_VISIBILITY` blocks, so a build with neither define produces a vertex shader that writes nothing to `gl_Position` at all — which Tint correctly rejects (`position must be declared for vertex entry point output`). `wgsl_precompile.py`'s `SHADER_REGISTRY` entry for this shader tested exactly that non-existent combo: `[("default", "", [VERT, FRAG])]`. **Fix**: replaced with the two real, reachable variants, matching every other `MODE_`-based registry entry's naming convention: `[("probes", "\n#define MODE_PROBES\n", [VERT, FRAG]), ("visibility", "\n#define MODE_VISIBILITY\n", [VERT, FRAG])]`. **Files**: `drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompile.py` (`SHADER_REGISTRY`).
3. **`tonemap_mobile.glsl` ×2 subpass variants — confirmed genuinely dead code on web, removed from the registry (not a shader fix).** `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/tone_mapper.cpp:64-70` explicitly does `tonemap_mobile.shader.set_variant_enabled(TONEMAP_MOBILE_MODE_SUBPASS, false)` (and the `_1D_LUT`/`_MULTIVIEW` siblings) under `#ifdef WEB_ENABLED`, with the comment *"WebGPU does not support input attachments / subpass reads"* — this fork already knew and handled this; these two variants are never compiled by the engine on the web platform under any circumstance. `wgsl_precompile.py`'s registry, however, unconditionally precompiled all 4 `tonemap_mobile.glsl` variants including both subpass ones, producing two permanent, unfixable, meaningless failures on every run. **Fix**: removed the `"subpass"`/`"subpass_1d_lut"` entries from the registry, with a comment cross-referencing `tone_mapper.cpp`'s existing `WEB_ENABLED` gate so nobody re-adds them without reading why they're gone. **Files**: `drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompile.py` (`SHADER_REGISTRY`).

**Verified**: `tint_convert_cli` rebuilt clean (`drivers/webgpu/tint_cli/build.sh`) after the `spirv_preprocess.cpp` change; `python3 drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompile.py . <out> glslangValidator` now reports **`Results: 179 compiled, 12 glsl failures, 0 tint failures`** (the 12 GLSL-level failures are the pre-existing, unrelated, already-documented gaps from Task 9.5's log — `taa_resolve`/`octmap_*`/`ssao_blur`/`ssil_blur`/`subsurface_scattering`/`giprobe_write`/`cluster_render` (Task 9.4's tracked subgroup gap) — not touched here). `scons platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes opengl3=no threads=no` object-file build of `spirv_preprocess.cpp` clean (one pre-existing, unrelated `-Wunused-const-variable` warning on `BUILTIN_POINT_SIZE`, not introduced by this change). `webgpu_tests/shader_corpus` 13/13, `webgpu_tests/preprocessing_tests` 192/192 (+1 expected skip), `webgpu_tests/driver_unit_tests` 327/327 — all unaffected, as expected (`infer_readonly_storage()`'s extension is additive/more-permissive, doesn't change behavior for any SPIR-V that was already being handled correctly). **Not yet run**: `resource_lifecycle`/`screenshot_comparison` (need Playwright, not installed in this environment) or a live in-browser check of the two newly-fixed shaders' actual rendered output (`voxel_gi_debug`'s VoxelGI debug-view and `sdfgi_debug_probes`'s SDFGI probe-visualization debug view) — both are debug-visualization-only shaders (never in the normal rendering path), so this doesn't block anything, but worth a spot-check next time either debug view is used against a real project.

### Task 9.8: "code is unreachable" shader-compilation console warnings — root cause found, suppressed at the source `[DONE, live-verified]`

**Live browser verification (this session, after the fix was committed at `5d4358927`)**: rebuilt the `template_release` web export (`scons platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes opengl3=no threads=no`), exported `webgpu_tests/test_project` (ShaderCoverage scene — exercises SSAO, SSIL, SDFGI, decals, reflection probes, FSR2, TAA, all the shaders this fix and Task 7.11's touch), checksum-verified the export was genuinely fresh (`md5sum` match between `bin/godot.side.web.template_release.wasm32.nothreads.dlink.wasm` and the exported `index.side.wasm`), then captured the full browser console via Playwright (`webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/capture_console.mjs`, real-Vulkan headless Chromium flags, confirmed on this machine's RTX 4080 SUPER per Task 9.5's precedent). Confirmed the engine build hash in the startup log (`v4.7.2.stable.custom_build.5d4358927`) matches the fix commit exactly. **Zero occurrences of "unreachable" or "different format...preferred" anywhere in the captured console output** across the full ShaderCoverage run (10 rendered frames); the only console `[error]` lines present are pre-existing, unrelated cosmetic warnings (a `look_at()` node-not-in-tree call, two Godot-3.x `SpatialMaterial` remapped-parameter warnings, and the known FSR2/TAA incompatibility warning). Both this task's fix and Task 7.11's swap-chain-format fix are now confirmed working in a real browser, not just statically verified.

**Status**: `DONE`, 2026-09-13. Long-known and repeatedly dismissed as "benign, cosmetic" across Tasks 8.10, 9.2, and 9.5's own "startup warnings reviewed" note (`webgpu_tests`... see line ~2667 above) — this session actually root-caused it instead of re-dismissing it, per the user's request to look at a fresh `errors-chrome.txt` capture again.

**Root cause**: any GLSL helper function whose `if`/`else` both `return` (e.g. `ssao.glsl`'s `view_to_pos()`-shaped helpers, and the same pattern recurring across `ssil.glsl`, `voxel_gi_debug.glsl`, `sdfgi_direct_light.glsl`, `gi.glsl`, `scene_forward_clustered.glsl`, `canvas.glsl`) compiles through glslang to SPIR-V with a structurally-required merge block after the `if`/`else`, which — even though control can never actually reach it — Tint's SPIR-V→WGSL writer (`thirdparty/tint/src/tint/lang/wgsl/writer/ir_to_program/ir_to_program.cc`, `Unreachable()`) unconditionally lowers into a synthetic trailing `return <T>();` (WGSL syntactically needs *something* there) rather than proving it unreachable and omitting it. Dawn's WGSL compiler then correctly (per WGSL semantics — the statement genuinely is unreachable) emits a "code is unreachable" warning on it, every time this pattern occurs.

**Confirmed this is not a fixable SPIR-V-level dead-code-elimination gap**: temporarily added `spvtools::CreateDeadBranchElimPass()` + `CreateBlockMergePass()` ahead of the existing `CreateAggressiveDCEPass()` in `eliminate_dead_resources()` (`spirv_preprocess.cpp`) — SPIRV-Tools' own dead-code passes, already linked into this driver for exactly this kind of cleanup — and re-ran `ssao.glsl` through the real pipeline; the trailing return survived unchanged. This proves the merge block isn't sitting in the SPIR-V as a genuinely dead/removable basic block at all; it's a Tint WGSL-writer code-generation choice, not a code-elimination gap, so no SPIR-V preprocessing pass can help. (This experiment was reverted — `spirv_preprocess.cpp` is unchanged from before this task.)

**Fix**: Tint's own `tint::wgsl::writer::Options` already has a purpose-built field for exactly this, `disable_unreachable_code_warning`, which emits `diagnostic(off, chromium.unreachable_code);` at the top of the generated WGSL — the identical mechanism already used by this driver's own `allow_non_uniform_derivatives`/`allow_non_uniform_subgroup_operations` options (both insert their own `diagnostic(off, ...)` directives the same way). Set it alongside those two in `drivers/webgpu/tint_wrapper.cpp`'s `tint_wrapper_spirv_to_wgsl()`.

**Verified**: rebuilt `tint_convert_cli` and re-ran `ssao.glsl`/`gi.glsl` through the real pipeline — `diagnostic(off, chromium.unreachable_code);` now appears in the WGSL output right alongside the two existing diagnostic directives (the dead `return vec3<f32>();` itself is still there, as expected and by design — only the diagnostic, not the code shape, changes). Object-file-compiled `tint_wrapper.cpp` clean for both `template_release`/`template_debug`. `webgpu_tests/shader_corpus` 13/13, `webgpu_tests/preprocessing_tests` 192/192 (+1 skip), `webgpu_tests/driver_unit_tests` 327/327 — no regressions. **Not yet done**: a live in-browser capture confirming Dawn actually suppresses the console warning (no Playwright/browser access this session) — high confidence given this is the exact same directive mechanism already proven working for the other two relaxations, but not independently re-verified for this specific diagnostic rule name.

### Task 9.9: `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV`/native-editor SPIR-V version mismatch — fixed at the source, `expected_failures.json` baseline reconciled `[DONE]`

**Status**: `DONE`, 2026-09-13. Plan-of-attack.md Tier 1 #1.

**Evaluated first (per user request) whether migrating the WebGPU driver itself to SPIR-V 1.4 (matching the Vulkan driver's default) would be a better long-term fix than patching the test tooling — concluded no.** SPIR-V 1.3 was deliberately chosen (`rendering_shader_container_webgpu.h`'s `get_shader_spirv_version()` comment) so glslang emits SSBOs with the modern `StorageBuffer` storage class; 1.4 doesn't remove that, so 1.4 wouldn't regress on that front, but it changes `OpEntryPoint`'s interface-list rules (1.4 requires *every* touched global variable listed, not just `Input`/`Output`) — and this fork's 12+ hand-written binary SPIR-V passes (`spirv_preprocess.cpp`) were written and validated against 1.3's narrower interface-list shape. This isn't hypothetical: Tasks 8.6, 8.8, and 9.7 each independently, accidentally fed 1.4 SPIR-V (from exactly this `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` mismatch) through this pipeline and got **113–260 generic failures** each time, vs. 0 real Tint failures at 1.3 (Task 9.7). A deliberate 1.4 migration would mean auditing every pass for interface-list completeness with no functional upside (1.4 unlocks nothing WebGPU needs) — not worth it. Fixed the actual mismatch instead: made the dump itself always reflect SPIR-V 1.3 regardless of which driver the native editor happens to be running.
