**Root cause**: `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV`'s dump (`RenderingDevice::shader_compile_binary_from_spirv()`, `rendering_device.cpp`) writes whatever SPIR-V bytes the *active* driver's `RenderingShaderContainerFormat::get_shader_spirv_version()` produced — Vulkan's format requests `SHADER_SPIRV_VERSION_1_4` (`rendering_shader_container_vulkan.cpp:104`, stock upstream default) while WebGPU's requests `SHADER_SPIRV_VERSION_1_3`. Since `drivers/webgpu/` isn't even compiled into non-web platforms, the native editor used for this dump workflow can *only* ever be running Vulkan (or Metal/D3D12) — it structurally cannot produce a WebGPU-format dump on its own.

**Fix**: `ShaderRD::compile_stages()` (`servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shader_rd.cpp`, the sole place with both per-stage GLSL source and stage index — its only two callers, `ShaderRD::_compile_variant()` and `ShaderBakerExportPlugin::_process_work_item()`, both already have a shader name to pass through) now, when `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` is set and the active driver's SPIR-V version isn't already 1.3, recompiles a second copy of the same source directly via `compile_glslang_shader()` (the same underlying function `shader_compile_spirv_from_source()` uses) forced to `SHADER_SPIRV_VERSION_1_3`/`SHADER_LANGUAGE_VULKAN_VERSION_1_1` — the WebGPU driver's exact target — and stores it in a new `ShaderStageSPIRVData::dump_override_spirv` field (`rendering_device_commons.h`, purely additive, empty/unused on every other path). `shader_compile_binary_from_spirv()`'s existing dump block now prefers this field over the driver-native `spirv` bytes when present. Guarded behind `#if defined(MODULE_GLSLANG_ENABLED) && defined(TOOLS_ENABLED)` — compiled out entirely for export templates (`GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` is only ever used against editor builds), zero behavior change for any other driver's real (non-dump) shader compilation. Confirmed via `grep`/`nm` that `ShaderBakerExportPlugin`'s cross-compilation shader-baking path (the other caller) isn't actually wired up for WebGPU exports at all currently (`RenderingShaderContainerFormatWebGPU` has no `platform/web/` registration) — so no live bug there today, just future-proofing if that ever changes.

**Bug caught during implementation, fixed before verifying**: the new code's `#if defined(MODULE_GLSLANG_ENABLED) && ...` guard silently evaluated false on first attempt — `shader_rd.cpp` never included `modules/modules_enabled.gen.h` (unlike `rendering_device.cpp`, which does, right before its own identical guard), so the macro was never visible there despite being genuinely defined for this build. Caught via `nm` on the compiled `.o` showing zero reference to `compile_glslang_shader` despite the object compiling without error (an `#if` on an undefined macro is silently false, not a compile error) — a second compile-then-`nm`-check cycle after adding the missing include (plus `rendering_shader_container.h`, needed because `RenderingShaderContainerFormat` is only forward-declared in the header chain `shader_rd.cpp` already pulls in) confirmed the symbol reference finally present.

**Verified end-to-end, live**: cleared the test project's shader cache, ran `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV=/tmp/spirv_dump_test bin/godot.linuxbsd.editor.dev.x86_64 --path webgpu_tests/test_project --rendering-method mobile --quit-after 10` against the rebuilt editor — all 204 dumped `.spv` files' header version words now read `1.3` (confirmed via a small Python SPIR-V-header parse), where every single one previously read `1.4`. Fed through `validate_spirv_dump.mjs`: **201/204 passed (98.5%)**, up from Task 9.7's 113/204 (55%) on the old, mismatched dump — and both remaining categories are real, already-documented, legitimate Vulkan-only variants, not tooling artifacts: `TonemapMobileShaderRD`'s subpass variant (input-attachment `textureLoad`, the same shader Task 9.7 already confirmed is compiled out entirely under `WEB_ENABLED` in `tone_mapper.cpp` — reachable here only because native, non-web builds don't define `WEB_ENABLED`) and `VrsShaderRD`'s multiview variant (`ViewIndex` builtin, the exact same dormant-on-non-VR gap Task 9.3 already documented). Ran `--update-baseline`: `expected_failures.json` shrank from its stale, wrong 2026-05-05/31-entry version to these 3 real entries; re-running against the fresh dump confirms **0 regressions**. `shader_corpus` 13/13, `preprocessing_tests` 192/192 (+1 skip), `driver_unit_tests` 327/327 — no regressions. Compile-checked clean (zero warnings) for all three affected files (`shader_rd.cpp`, `rendering_device.cpp`, `shader_baker_export_plugin.cpp`) across native editor (`target=editor dev_build=yes`) and both web targets (`template_release`/`template_debug`, `dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes`). **Files**: `servers/rendering/rendering_device_commons.h`, `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shader_rd.{h,cpp}`, `servers/rendering/rendering_device.cpp`, `editor/export/shader_baker_export_plugin.cpp`. **Not yet done**: no live in-browser check needed (this fix only affects a native-editor debug/CI dump path, never anything reaching the browser).

### Task 9.10: FSR2 upscaling re-enablement — the atomics/uniformity Tint bug was never actually an ICE; found and fixed, plus two more bugs, but FSR2 stays disabled on WebGPU for a new, precisely-scoped reason `[Superseded by Task 9.11 — formatless-storage blocker resolved, FSR2 gate removed]`

**Status**: 2026-09-14. Plan-of-attack.md Tier 1 #1.

**The original blocker was misdiagnosed.** FSR2 upscaling had been disabled entirely on WebGPU (Round 14, `webgpu_notes/perf_optimization_may3_2026.md`-adjacent history) because its SPD auto-exposure/luminance-pyramid pass's buffer-atomic counter path was believed to trip a Tint "internal compiler error" — size/complexity-triggered, previously estimated at 3-7 days to resolve, possibly needing an upstream Tint fix. Reproducing it directly via `tint_convert_cli` against the dumped `Fsr2ComputeLuminancePyramidPassShaderRD:1.comp.spv` showed this was never an ICE at all: it's a genuine, well-formed WGSL uniformity-analysis validation error, `error: 'workgroupBarrier' must only be called from uniform control flow`.

**Root cause**: Tint's WGSL writer (`thirdparty/tint/src/tint/lang/wgsl/writer/raise/raise.cc`, `ReplaceWorkgroupBarrier`) has a pattern-matcher that recognizes the idiom `call workgroupBarrier; %v = load <ptr to a var<workgroup>>; call workgroupBarrier` as safe and rewrites it into the special `workgroupUniformLoad` builtin the uniformity analysis trusts unconditionally — but only when those three things are literal, adjacent instructions inside one function body (`call->next` must literally be the load, `load->next` must literally be the second barrier call). FSR2's `SpdExitWorkgroup` (`thirdparty/amd-fsr2/shaders/ffx_spd.h`) reads the last-active-workgroup counter (a `FFX_GROUPSHARED`/`shared` variable, `spdCounter`) by calling through two trivial wrapper functions, `SpdWorkgroupShuffleBarrier()` and `SpdGetAtomicCounter()` — ordinary `OpFunctionCall`s that Tint's raise pass does not see through — and only a single barrier preceded the read to begin with (no second barrier followed it), so the shape never matched the pattern at all.

**The fix, two parts**:
1. `ffx_spd.h`'s `SpdExitWorkgroup`, under `#if defined(FFX_GLSL)`, no longer calls through the wrapper functions: it reads `spdCounter` directly and adds a second, functionally redundant `barrier()` right after the read, reproducing the exact `barrier(); load; barrier();` shape inline.
2. That alone wasn't enough — glslang's unoptimized SPIR-V codegen still routes the read through a `Function`-storage-class local variable (`FfxUInt32 counterValue = spdCounter;` compiles to declare + store + load even with nothing else touching it), and the leftover `OpStore` between the load and the second barrier still broke the required adjacency. Added an 18th SPIR-V preprocessing pass, `eliminate_local_single_block_vars` (`drivers/webgpu/spirv_preprocess.h`/`.cpp`), registering SPIRV-Tools' `CreateLocalSingleBlockLoadStoreElimPass()` together with `CreateAggressiveDCEPass()` in the same optimizer run — the DCE pass is required because the single-block pass on its own leaves the now-provably-dead store (and its now-unread local variable) behind; it can only prove a store/variable pair dead within its own single pass, not because of its own preceding rewrite. Wired into both the runtime driver (`drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) and the standalone precompiler (`drivers/webgpu/tint_cli/main.cpp`), right after `eliminate_dead_resources`. Verified directly: the generated WGSL now reads `return (workgroupUniformLoad(&(spdCounter)) != (*(numWorkGroups) - 1u));` — the exact intended rewrite.

**Isolation method**: rather than editing the real GLSL/rebuilding the engine on every iteration, hand-edited the *disassembled* SPIR-V (`spirv-dis`/`spirv-as`) to simulate candidate fixes and fed them straight through `tint_convert_cli` — this confirmed both that manually inlining the barrier+load+barrier into one function fixed it, and, separately, that adding a second barrier call *without* inlining did not (proving the function-call indirection itself, not just the missing second barrier, was blocking the match) — before touching any real source file.

**Two more bugs found and fixed along the way** (both previously unreachable, since FSR2 was never instantiated on WebGPU at all until this session):

- **R16_Snorm sampling-format gap.** FSR2's two SNORM16 lookup textures — a Lanczos resampling-weight LUT and a "maximum upsample bias" LUT (`FFX_FSR2_RESOURCE_IDENTIFIER_LANCZOS_LUT`/`_UPSAMPLE_MAXIMUM_BIAS_LUT`, both read-only, sampled via plain `texture2D`/`textureLod`, never a storage image) — were created as `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R16_SNORM`. This driver's `texture_get_usages_supported_by_format()` (Task 7.10) deliberately reports R16_Snorm as supporting no usage at all — WebGPU has no native R16Snorm texture format, and reinterpreting the same bits as R16Float, the only same-size substitute, would produce garbage — so `texture_create()` returned a null RID the first time FSR2 got far enough at runtime to actually create these two textures. Fixed in `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr2.cpp`'s `create_resource_rd()`: detect the R16_Snorm request, substitute `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R16_SFLOAT`, and convert the raw `int16_t` SNORM sample data (`value = clamp(int16 / 32767.0, -1, 1)`) into half-float bit patterns via `Math::make_half_float()` before upload. Safe specifically because both textures are read-only sampled LUTs with fixed, one-time initial data — no shader-side storage-image format qualifier depends on the exact original bit layout, since `sampler2D` reads don't carry a compile-time format the way `image2D` writes do.
- **Every declared shader mode gets compiled, whether or not it's ever dispatched.** `ShaderRD` compiles every mode variant a shader declares at initialization time, regardless of which single variant `pass.shader_variant` actually selects for dispatch. FSR2's `compute_luminance_pyramid` shader declares two modes (plain image-atomics, and the `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` buffer-atomics fallback); both were always registered unconditionally, so on WebGPU the image-atomics variant got compiled too, even though it will never be dispatched there — and it is fundamentally untranslatable for a completely different, harder reason than the uniformity bug above: its SPIR-V uses `OpImageTexelPointer` (from `imageAtomicAdd` on a texture), which Tint's SPIR-V reader has no case for at all and hard-aborts on (`TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED`, a WASM `unreachable` trap). This was invisible before because FSR2's shaders were never even created on WebGPU (the whole effect was gated off); re-enabling it surfaced this as a brand-new crash. Fixed in `fsr2.cpp`: only push the plain image-atomics mode string when `RD::SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT` is actually true, so WebGPU's shader only ever declares (and only ever compiles) the one variant it can use.

**The actual remaining blocker, found via live browser verification**: with all three fixes above in place, a real Emscripten web export driven through headless Chromium (real Vulkan-backed Dawn, `webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/capture_console.mjs`) showed FSR2 progressing further on WebGPU than ever before — no atomics ICE, no uniformity error, no R16_Snorm failure — but hit a new failure at pipeline-creation time. FSR2's RCAS pass and the accumulate pass's sharpen variant write their final output through a deliberately formatless storage image: `ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h`'s `rw_upscaled_output` has no `layout(...)` format qualifier at all, with AMD's own source comment reading `/* app controlled format */`, because the real backing pixel format depends on whatever HDR buffer format Godot's own renderer is configured to use (`RenderSceneBuffersRD::ensure_upscaled()`'s `get_base_data_format()`, which varies by project settings). WGSL's `texture_storage_2d<F, ...>` requires a single, compile-time-constant format `F` — there's no formatless or runtime-specialized storage-texture type at all — so this has no direct translation. This is the same class of gap as this project's own **Task 8.3**, previously known to affect only `screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl`; FSR2's RCAS/accumulate-sharpen passes are a second, independent instance of it. Worse: the FSR2 SDK itself doesn't handle this specific backend failure gracefully — `create_pipeline_rd()` correctly returns `FFX_ERROR_BACKEND_API_ERROR` via `ERR_FAIL_COND_V`, but something further up the FFX call chain doesn't check that return value and goes on to crash the whole engine (`RuntimeError: table index is out of bounds`) rather than just failing the upscale for that frame — the same "FSR2 doesn't check backend API errors carefully" pattern already seen once before for the RG11B10_UFLOAT storage-capability gap (Round 14). Leaving FSR2 enabled in this state would therefore be a regression (a hard engine crash) relative to the prior safe fallback.

**Final state**: added a new capability flag, `RD::SUPPORTS_FORMATLESS_STORAGE_IMAGES` (`servers/rendering/rendering_device_commons.h`), mirroring `SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT`'s exact precedent — `true` on Vulkan, Metal, and D3D12 (each backend's `rendering_device_driver_*.cpp` got a one-line `case`), `false` on WebGPU. `render_forward_clustered.cpp`'s `VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_FSR2` case now gates on this flag instead of the atomics one. Net effect: FSR2 stays exactly as disabled on WebGPU today as it was before this session — falling back to no upscaling, identical to the original safe behavior — but the real remaining blocker is now precisely identified and scoped to Task 8.3 instead of a vague "Tint ICE, needs 3-7 days, possibly upstream" placeholder, and re-enabling FSR2 the moment Task 8.3 is resolved is just flipping this one flag back.

**Files**: `thirdparty/amd-fsr2/shaders/ffx_spd.h`; `drivers/webgpu/spirv_preprocess.{h,cpp}`; `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`; `drivers/webgpu/tint_cli/main.cpp`; `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr2.cpp`; `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/forward_clustered/render_forward_clustered.cpp`; `servers/rendering/rendering_device_commons.h`; `drivers/vulkan/rendering_device_driver_vulkan.cpp`; `drivers/metal/rendering_device_driver_metal.cpp`; `drivers/d3d12/rendering_device_driver_d3d12.cpp`.

**Verified**: the uniformity fix, isolated and confirmed via hand-edited SPIR-V through `tint_convert_cli` before any real source change (see above). Native `linuxbsd` editor build compiled clean and the `ShaderCoverage` test scene passed on Vulkan after every change in this task. A full 344-shader-variant corpus run (`webgpu_tests/shader_corpus/validate_spirv_dump.mjs` against a fresh `GODOT_DUMP_SPIRV` dump) with an A/B test — building `tint_convert_cli` with and without the new `eliminate_local_single_block_vars` pass and diffing the failure sets — confirmed the pass fixes exactly `Fsr2ComputeLuminancePyramidPassShaderRD:1` with zero regressions elsewhere, and confirmed the corpus's other 13 failures (mostly the `TINT_ASSERT(int_ty->width() == 32)` bug already flagged unfixed earlier in this file, plus other Vulkan-only variants never used by WebGPU) are pre-existing and unrelated to this task. A real Emscripten `template_release` web export was built, exported, and live-tested in headless Chromium (real Vulkan-backed Dawn) across four rounds as each layer was found and fixed in turn: the atomics/uniformity fix alone surfaced the R16_Snorm null-texture crash; fixing that surfaced the image-atomics-variant-always-compiled crash; fixing that surfaced the RCAS/accumulate-sharpen formatless-storage-image crash; reverting to the new `SUPPORTS_FORMATLESS_STORAGE_IMAGES`-gated `SCALE_NONE` fallback reproduced the original, clean, 8-error baseline (the same pre-existing cosmetic warnings Task 9.8/9.9 already catalogued — node-not-in-tree, two `SpatialMaterial` remaps, the FSR2/TAA-incompatibility warning) with zero crash-related output. **Not yet done**: Task 8.3 itself (the formatless-storage-image translation gap, now confirmed to affect two independent shaders); a live-in-browser screenshot capture of this task's changes timed out in this sandbox both before and after the final revert (consistent with this sandbox's previously-documented slow/software-rendering screenshot-capture limitation, not something newly introduced here) — console-log verification was relied on instead, per this file's own established precedent for this sandbox.

### Task 9.11: FSR2's formatless-storage-image blocker (Task 8.3's second instance) resolved — plus an independently-discovered FP16/Tint crash that would have replaced it `[DONE — pending full engine/browser re-verification]`

**Status**: 2026-09-14. Plan-of-attack.md Tier 1 #2/#3.

**The `rw_upscaled_output` format is not actually variable in this engine.** Task 9.10 left FSR2 gated off behind a new `RD::SUPPORTS_FORMATLESS_STORAGE_IMAGES` flag because its RCAS pass and accumulate pass's non-sharpening variant write through `rw_upscaled_output` (`thirdparty/amd-fsr2/shaders/ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h` ~L265), a storage image AMD's SDK deliberately leaves without a `layout(...)` format qualifier (`/* app controlled format */`) since a generic FFX integration could bind any HDR backbuffer format there. Tracing the actual Godot-side binding end to end shows this format is fixed, not variable, in this fork: the resource is always `RenderSceneBuffersRD::ensure_upscaled()`'s `RB_TEX_COLOR_UPSCALED` texture (`render_scene_buffers_rd.cpp`), created at `get_base_data_format()`. That accessor returns `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT` unconditionally when `force_hdr` is set, and otherwise returns `preferred_data_format` — which is set from the virtual `RendererSceneRenderRD::_render_buffers_get_preferred_color_format()`, whose **base-class implementation unconditionally returns `RD::DATA_FORMAT_R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT`** (`renderer_scene_render_rd.cpp:1160`) and is **never overridden by `RenderForwardClustered`** — confirmed by grep, `RenderForwardClustered` has no override of its own, and FSR2 is never referenced anywhere in `RenderForwardMobile` (a repo-wide grep for `get_upscaled_texture`/`ensure_upscaled`/`fsr2_effect`/`SCALE_FSR2` turns up only `renderer_scene_render_rd.cpp`, `render_scene_buffers_rd.{h,cpp}`, and `forward_clustered/render_forward_clustered.{h,cpp}`). So exactly the same situation as the already-fixed SSR-filter half of Task 8.3: a texture that's formatless in the general FFX/GLSL sense but has exactly one concrete format in every code path this fork can actually reach.

**Fix, part 1 (the formatless gap)**: hardcoded `layout(set = 1, binding = FSR2_BIND_UAV_UPSCALED_OUTPUT, rgba16f)` on `rw_upscaled_output` in `ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h`, mirroring `screen_space_reflection_filter.glsl`'s existing fix exactly. Since `RD::SUPPORTS_FORMATLESS_STORAGE_IMAGES` then had exactly one remaining call site (this FSR2 gate) and no other consumer, removed it entirely rather than leave a dead capability flag: the enum value (`rendering_device_commons.h`) and all four drivers' `has_feature()` cases (`vulkan`/`metal`/`d3d12`/`webgpu`). `render_forward_clustered.cpp`'s `VIEWPORT_SCALING_3D_MODE_FSR2` case now unconditionally sets `scale_type = SCALE_FSR2` — no capability gate needed any more.

**Fix, part 2 (a second, previously-undiscovered blocker found while verifying part 1)**: hand-compiled every FSR2 compute shader through the host `glslangValidator` (`--target-env vulkan1.1`, matching the WebGPU driver's actual SPIR-V 1.3 target) with FSR2Effect's exact `#define` set, then fed the SPIR-V straight through `bin/tint_convert_cli` in single-file mode — the same isolation technique Task 9.10 itself established, and necessary here because FSR2's shaders aren't in `wgsl_precompile.py`'s `SHADER_REGISTRY` at all (they only ever go through the runtime Tint-fallback path in `tint_wrapper.cpp`, never the ahead-of-time precompiler). With `FFX_HALF` (FSR2's fp16 shader mode) defined, **3 of the 5 shaders that declare an FFX_HALF variant crash Tint's SPIR-V reader outright**: `ffx_fsr2_reconstruct_previous_depth_pass.glsl`, `ffx_fsr2_autogen_reactive_pass.glsl`, and `ffx_fsr2_tcr_autogen_pass.glsl` all hit `TINT_ASSERT(int_ty->width() == 32)` (`thirdparty/tint/src/tint/lang/spirv/reader/parser/parser.cc:745`) — the exact same assert already flagged as a pre-existing, unrelated corpus failure in Task 9.10's own verification notes above, now confirmed to also apply to FSR2 shaders in FP16 mode. `ffx_fsr2_reconstruct_previous_depth_pass.glsl` additionally hits a second, distinct crash on the same code path, `TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED unhandled SPIR-V instruction: OpUConvert` (`parser.cc:2493`). FSR2's accumulate pass has its own separate FP16 mode list and independently hits the same `int_ty->width() == 32` assert. Both are hard aborts (SIGILL), not graceful Tint errors — exactly the same class of "kills the whole engine instance" failure Task 9.10 already catalogued for the image-atomics variant. Only 2 of the 5 shared-mode shaders (`ffx_fsr2_lock_pass.glsl`, `ffx_fsr2_depth_clip_pass.glsl`) convert cleanly with `FFX_HALF` defined.

**Why this would have mattered in practice**: `RD::Features::SUPPORTS_HALF_FLOAT` is unconditionally `false` on the WebGPU driver (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`: "WebGPU shader-f16 extension not reliably available"), so `FSR2Effect`'s own `capabilities.fp16Supported` is always false there and the FFX_HALF variant is never *dispatched* on WebGPU — but `FSR2Effect`'s constructor (`servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr2.cpp`) unconditionally declared the FFX_HALF mode string in the mode-list `Vector` passed to `ShaderRD::initialize()` for all 5 shaders (`modes_with_fp16`) plus the accumulate pass's own `accumulate_modes_with_fp16`, regardless of `fp16Supported` — and, per Task 9.10's own already-documented finding, `ShaderRD` compiles *every* declared mode variant at shader-initialization time, not only the one `pass.shader_variant` selects for dispatch. `FSR2Effect`'s constructor also runs unconditionally at renderer setup (`fsr2_effect = memnew(RendererRD::FSR2Effect)`), independent of whether FSR2 is ever actually selected as the scaling mode — so simply removing the Part 1 gate above, on its own, would have swapped the old (already-diagnosed) formatless-storage crash for this new, previously-undiscovered FP16 crash the very first time a WebGPU build initialized its renderer, FSR2 selected or not.

**Fix, part 2 continued**: in `FSR2Effect`'s constructor, changed both `modes_with_fp16` and `accumulate_modes_with_fp16` to only push their `FFX_HALF`-defining mode strings when `capabilities.fp16Supported` is true — the exact same conditional-declare pattern the constructor already uses for `modes_atomic_fallback`/`use_image_atomics` (Task 9.10). The existing `pass.shader_variant = capabilities.fp16Supported ? 1 : 0` selector expressions (and the accumulate pass's `? 2 : 0` plus `sharpen_pass.shader_variant = pass.shader_variant + 1`) needed no changes: they already resolve correctly against either a 1-entry (unsupported) or 2-entry/4-entry (supported) mode vector.

**Verified**: no full engine/browser rebuild was done for this specific shader-conversion claim (see below); verification was via the hand-compile-then-`tint_convert_cli` method described above. Confirmed `rw_upscaled_output` now resolves to `texture_storage_2d<rgba16float, write>` in the generated WGSL for both the RCAS pass and the accumulate pass's non-FP16, non-sharpening variant (the one that actually writes it). Confirmed all 5 `modes_with_fp16`-sharing shaders plus the accumulate pass convert cleanly through Tint with `FFX_HALF` left undefined — i.e. exactly what `capabilities.fp16Supported == false` now actually produces on WebGPU after the Part 2 fix. Separately, a native `linuxbsd` `target=editor dev_build=yes` build compiled clean with all of this session's changes in place (the `rendering_device_commons.h` enum removal, all four drivers' `has_feature()` edits, `render_forward_clustered.cpp`'s gate removal, and `fsr2.cpp`'s two conditional-mode edits); `webgpu_tests/driver_unit_tests` (327/327) and `webgpu_tests/shader_corpus` (13/13) both still pass with no regressions. **Not yet done**: a real Emscripten `template_release` web export + live browser verification that FSR2 now actually upscales correctly end-to-end on WebGPU (Task 9.10's own live-browser round-trip methodology should be repeated here) — this session's verification covers "these shaders no longer crash Tint" and "the codebase still compiles/passes its existing test suites," not a live, on-screen confirmation of FSR2's visual output.

### Task 9.12: Live re-verification of 9.11 surfaced two new crashes — a compute-pass intra-pass sync-scope violation (FSR1) and a second, unaudited `imageAtomicMin`/`imageAtomicMax` call site (FSR2) `[DONE — pending live browser re-verification]`

**Status**: 2026-09-14. Exactly the "not yet done" live re-verification Task 9.11 called for — the user built both a fresh web export and re-ran their own real Chrome test project (per this fork's established manual-build workflow, not the repo's sandboxed test scene) and got two new, distinct crash logs, one per effect, both still ending in a fully black screen (2D and 3D).

**FSR1: intra-pass texture sync-scope conflict, not a Tint bug.** `errors-chrome-fsr-1.txt` showed a Dawn *validation* error, not a Tint crash: `[Texture (unlabeled 1920x1080 px, TextureFormat::RGBA16Float)] usage (TextureBinding|Storage(write-only)) includes writable usage and another usage in the same synchronization scope`, repeated once per frame, followed by every subsequent command buffer being permanently `[Invalid CommandBuffer] is invalid due to a previous error` (the whole frame goes black because nothing after the first violation ever successfully submits). Root cause: `FSR::process()` (`servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr.cpp`) runs EASU and RCAS as two dispatches inside *one* `compute_list_begin()`/`compute_list_end()` pair, with only a `compute_list_add_barrier()` between them — and `RenderingDeviceDriverWebGPU::command_pipeline_barrier()` is a documented no-op (CLAUDE.md's "Barriers" note: "WebGPU tracks hazards automatically"). That's true *across* passes, but this driver opens exactly one `WGPUComputePassEncoder` for the whole compute list (`command_bind_compute_pipeline`) and never closes it between the two dispatches, so EASU's storage-write of `upscale_texture` and RCAS's later sampled-read of the *same* texture both land in one synchronization scope — which WebGPU's spec forbids regardless of dispatch order, no barrier can fix it (there's no WebGPU barrier primitive for this; the only fix is a pass boundary). This is the compute-side analog of the render-pass conflict Task 9's "proactive encoder isolation" (`command_begin_render_pass`, see `git log`/webgpu_notes history around March 24, 2026) already solved for render attachments — that fix never had a compute-pass counterpart because nothing had exercised this exact write-then-sample-same-texture-same-compute-list pattern before FSR1's real re-enablement.

**Fix**: added the compute-pass equivalent of that isolation mechanism. `WGCommandBuffer::RenderState` (`drivers/webgpu/webgpu_objects.h`) gained `current_compute_pass_textures`/`add_current_compute_pass_texture()`/`has_current_compute_pass_texture()`, tracking (deduped) which textures capable of *both* `WGPUTextureUsage_StorageBinding` and `WGPUTextureUsage_TextureBinding` have been bound so far in the currently-open compute pass; reset whenever a fresh compute pass actually begins (`command_bind_compute_pipeline`'s `active_encoder != COMPUTE` branch). `command_bind_compute_uniform_sets` (`drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) now runs a new `split_compute_pass_if_conflicting()` lambda before binding each uniform set: if any of that set's dual-capable textures was already recorded against the current pass, it ends the compute pass, begins a fresh one on the *same* command encoder, and rebinds the current pipeline/gap-groups/`last_bound_state` (mirroring the existing `refresh_rw_shadows` mid-pass-restart machinery already in this function for the RW-shadow-copy case) — then records the set's dual-capable textures against whichever pass is now current. Unlike the render-pass isolation fix, this doesn't need an eager `wgpuQueueSubmit()` first: ending and beginning a pass within the same encoder starts a brand-new synchronization scope on its own, so the split *prevents* the conflict outright rather than just limiting the blast radius of one that's about to happen anyway.

**FSR2: a second `imageAtomicMin`/`imageAtomicMax` call site Task 9.10/9.11 never audited.** `errors-chrome-fsr-2.txt` showed the exact same class of crash Task 9.10 already fixed once (`TINT_UNIMPLEMENTED unhandled SPIR-V instruction: OpImageTexelPointer`, a WASM `unreachable` trap) — but Task 9.10's fix only ever looked at `ffx_fsr2_compute_luminance_pyramid_pass.glsl`'s SPD atomic counter (`imageAtomicAdd`). A repo-wide `grep -rn imageAtomic thirdparty/amd-fsr2/shaders/` turns up a second, completely independent call site: `ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h`'s `StoreReconstructedDepth()`, called only from `ffx_fsr2_reconstruct_previous_depth_pass.glsl`'s `ReconstructPrevDepth()` — it scatter-writes each source pixel's depth into up to 4 bilinear-neighbor destination texels (reprojecting into the previous frame's depth buffer), and uses `imageAtomicMin`/`imageAtomicMax` specifically because multiple source pixels can scatter into the *same* destination texel and the algorithm needs a deterministic "keep the nearest" reduction across them. This pass was never covered by Task 9.10's `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` gating (that gating is per-shader, on the luminance-pyramid shader's own mode list only) and is unconditionally dispatched by FSR2 every frame regardless of `SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT` — so it crashes the engine instance the moment FSR2 initializes on WebGPU. (The sibling `SetReconstructedDepth()`, used by the lock pass, was already atomics-free — only the reconstruct-depth pass's scatter path was affected.)

**Fix**: unlike the luminance-pyramid counter (which had a natural buffer-atomics substitute), there's no buffer-backed equivalent for a full per-pixel 2D atomic reduction without a much larger rewrite (a whole extra atomic-storage-buffer resource, a clear pass, and a buffer→texture unpack pass). Took the smaller, in-keeping-with-this-fork's-existing-tradeoffs fix instead: `StoreReconstructedDepth()` (`ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h`) now branches on the same `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS` define, falling back to a plain `imageStore()` when defined. This changes the scatter's conflict resolution from deterministic-nearest to last-write-wins for the (rare, edge-only) texels multiple source pixels scatter into — an accepted approximation, since `fReconstructedDepthBilinearWeightThreshold` already filters out low-confidence contributions before any store happens, and this value only feeds a heuristic reprojection-confidence signal, not a directly-visible output. `fsr2.cpp`'s `FFX_FSR2_PASS_RECONSTRUCT_PREVIOUS_DEPTH` setup now builds its own `modes_reconstruct_depth` mode list (previously just reused the shared `modes_with_fp16`) combining the existing FFX_HALF gate with a new `use_image_atomics_reconstruct = RD::get_singleton()->has_feature(RD::Features::SUPPORTS_IMAGE_ATOMIC_32_BIT)` gate, prepending `NO_IMAGE_ATOMICS`'s `#define` string to every mode-string entry when unsupported — both axes are independent (currently both false on WebGPU, true elsewhere) and the existing `pass.shader_variant = capabilities.fp16Supported ? 1 : 0` selector needed no change (same 1-or-2-entry-vector shape as before).

**Verified**: native `linuxbsd target=editor dev_build=yes` compiled clean (validates `fsr2.cpp`'s and the shared FFX header's changes, which are shared engine code compiled on every platform). A full Emscripten `platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes` rebuild also compiled clean (the only way to validate the `drivers/webgpu/` changes — `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`, `webgpu_objects.h` — at all, since this driver isn't compiled into native builds; see CLAUDE.md). **Not yet done**: a fresh live-browser round-trip (re-export the freshly built template into the user's real test project, re-run in real Chrome, per this fork's own established manual workflow that surfaced both of today's bugs in the first place) to confirm both FSR1 and FSR2 now render correctly rather than merely "no longer crash at compile/validation time" — this task's own verification, like Task 9.11's, covers compilation but not a live on-screen result.

**Files**: `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr.cpp` (diagnosis only, no changes needed there — the bug is entirely in the driver's pass-lifetime handling, not in how FSR1 uses the API); `drivers/webgpu/webgpu_objects.h`; `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`; `thirdparty/amd-fsr2/shaders/ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h`; `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr2.cpp`.

### Task 9.13: Live re-verification round 2 — FSR1/2D fixed, FSR2/3D still crashing: a `dilatedDepth`-naming false positive in the depth-texture reclassification heuristic `[DONE — live-verified, FSR2 confirmed working correctly on WebGPU]`

**Status**: 2026-09-14. The user rebuilt and re-tested after Task 9.12's fixes: 2D/UI renders again (confirms Task 9.12's FSR1 fix), but 3D still spams errors continuously (fresh `errors-chrome-fsr-1.txt`/`errors-chrome-fsr-2.txt` supplied). `errors-chrome-fsr-1.txt` this round is unrelated browser noise (an `angular.js`/jquery `TypeError` from some other open tab, not this project — ignored). `errors-chrome-fsr-2.txt` (5730 lines) reduces to exactly one root cause, repeated every frame: `GPUValidationError: None of the supported sample types (Float|UnfilterableFloat) of [Texture (unlabeled 1440x810 px, TextureFormat::R32Float)] match the expected sample types (Depth)` on `bgl:Fsr2DepthClipPassShaderRD:0:set1` binding 4, cascading into permanent `[Invalid BindGroup]`/`[Invalid CommandBuffer]` spam for the rest of the log (1414 occurrences each) exactly like Task 9.12's FSR1 case — a black screen, not a crash-to-desktop.

**Root cause**: `_reclassify_single_component_depth_textures()` (`drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`, Task 7.13/9.5-Round-6's naming heuristic that upgrades a `texture_2d<f32>` binding to `texture_depth_2d` when its WGSL name contains "depth" and it's only ever read one component at a time) already has three deliberate exclusions for known false positives (storage-converted textures, RW-shadow-copy textures, and anything with "half" in the name, e.g. SSR's `source_depth_half`) — but had no exclusion for FSR2's `r_dilatedDepth` (`ffx_fsr2_callbacks_glsl.h`, read via `texelFetch(r_dilatedDepth, ...).r` in `ffx_fsr2_depth_clip.h` — single-component, matching the heuristic exactly). `r_dilatedDepth` is an ordinary internal r16f buffer FSR2 itself computes and writes (`rw_dilatedDepth`/`StoreDilatedDepth`, from `ffx_fsr2_reconstruct_previous_depth_pass.glsl`) — never a real depth-attachment texture — that merely has "Depth" in its name because it holds a dilated *copy* of depth values, exactly the same false-positive shape as SSR's `source_depth_half`. Confirmed directly: hand-compiled `fsr2_depth_clip_pass.glsl` through `glslangValidator`/`tint_convert_cli` (the SPIR-V-preprocessing-and-Tint half of the real pipeline; this reclassification pass itself runs only in the real driver afterward, so it can't be exercised through `tint_convert_cli` — verified by inspecting its WGSL output directly instead) and found `r_dilatedDepth` at `@binding(4u)` (`FSR2_BIND_SRV_DILATED_DEPTH`'s raw macro value of 2, doubled to 4 by this driver's combined-sampler-splitting pass — every FSR2 binding number in the real compiled shader is exactly 2× its `#define`) declared as `texture_2d<f32>` and read via `textureLoad(r_dilatedDepth, *(iPxInput), 0i).x` — structurally identical to `r_input_depth` (the real scene depth buffer, correctly reclassified, at binding 18 = 9×2) at the adjacent line. Both match "depth in name + single-component read"; only `r_dilatedDepth` should be excluded.

**Fix**: added a fourth disqualifying-signal check to `_reclassify_single_component_depth_textures()`, "dilated" anywhere in the name, mirroring the existing "half" check exactly. Every "Dilated"-prefixed FFX resource (`DilatedDepth`/`DilatedMotionVectors`/`DilatedReactiveMasks`) is one of FSR2's own synthetic intermediate buffers by construction, never a real hardware depth buffer, so this is safe as a general (not FSR2-specific-code-path) exclusion the same way the existing three are.

**Verified**: hand-compiled `fsr2_depth_clip_pass.glsl` through `glslangValidator --target-env vulkan1.1`/`tint_convert_cli` and confirmed both `r_dilatedDepth` and `r_input_depth` are present in the WGSL with the exact same structural shape described above (this confirms the *diagnosis*; the actual reclassification fix itself lives in driver code `tint_convert_cli` doesn't exercise, so it could not be verified through that tool). A full Emscripten `platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes` rebuild compiled clean. **Live-verified**: the user re-exported and re-tested in their real project — FSR2 now works correctly on WebGPU with no errors. **FSR2 is fully done as of this task** (Tier 1 #3, closing the loop opened by Task 9.10).

**Files**: `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`.

### Task 9.14: FSR1 — the actual root cause of Task 9.12's "sync-scope violation" fix not sticking: the render target's color texture deliberately has no `TEXTURE_USAGE_STORAGE_BIT` on WebGPU `[DONE — live-verified, FSR1 confirmed working correctly on WebGPU]`

**Status**: 2026-09-14. With Task 9.13 confirmed live, the user asked to sort out FSR1 next. `errors-chrome-fsr-1.txt` (61530 lines) reduces to two error signatures repeated together every frame, in this order: (1) a driver-agnostic, generic-RD-layer error, `ERROR: Image (binding: 0, index 0) needs the TEXTURE_USAGE_STORAGE_BIT usage flag set in order to be used as uniform.` at `uniform_set_create` (`rendering_device.cpp:4883`), cascading into `Condition "rid.is_null()" is true` and `Parameter "uniform_set" is null` (422 times each); (2) immediately after, the exact same `GPUValidationError: [Texture (unlabeled 1920x1080 px, TextureFormat::RGBA16Float)] usage (TextureBinding|Storage(write-only)) includes writable usage and another usage in the same synchronization scope` class of error Task 9.12 already fixed once for FSR1's EASU/RCAS `upscale_texture` ping-pong — appearing to still be broken despite that fix.

**Root cause, both errors are the same bug**: `FSR::process()`'s RCAS pass (`servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr.cpp`) binds `p_destination_texture` (the viewport's actual render target color texture, `texture_storage->render_target_get_rd_texture_slice()`) directly as its compute-shader storage-image output — exactly like it writes `upscale_texture` in the EASU pass. But `TextureStorage::render_target_get_color_usage_bits()` (`servers/rendering/renderer_rd/storage_rd/texture_storage.cpp`) has an existing, deliberate `#ifndef WEBGPU_ENABLED` exclusion of `TEXTURE_USAGE_STORAGE_BIT` from the render target's color texture specifically on WebGPU — the comment there explains why: this texture also declares an sRGB `shareable_formats` view (for gamma-correct sampling elsewhere), and Dawn rejects combining `StorageBinding` usage with sRGB view-format sharing on the same texture. That comment was written when "FSR is not available on WebGPU/Forward Mobile" was still true, so it never anticipated FSR1 actually trying to write here. Confirmed by tracing `render_target_get_rd_texture_slice()` → `rt->color`, which is Godot's own offscreen-managed RD texture (`_update_render_target()`), never the swap-chain-backed surface itself — so this has nothing to do with the canvas/swap-chain surface's own `WGPUTextureUsage_RenderAttachment`-only configuration (a different, unrelated constraint that was the first hypothesis investigated and ruled out).

Task 9.12's sync-scope fix (`split_compute_pass_if_conflicting()`) is not actually broken: it's a downstream victim of this same bug. Because `uniform_set_create()` for RCAS's destination-image bind fails at the generic RD layer (before ever reaching the WebGPU driver), `compute_list_bind_uniform_set()` aborts early and the driver's `command_bind_compute_uniform_sets()` is never called for that bind at all. But RCAS's *other* bind (set 0, the sampled read of `upscale_texture`) still runs first and correctly detects the EASU/RCAS conflict on `upscale_texture` and splits the compute pass — and that split's mid-pass-restart machinery unconditionally re-establishes every previously-bound group from `last_bound_state`, including the *stale* group-1 binding left over from EASU (`upscale_texture` as a storage image) which the failed destination-image bind never got the chance to overwrite. The result: the freshly-split pass ends up with group 0 (sampled `upscale_texture`) and group 1 (storage-write `upscale_texture`, stale) *both* active at RCAS's actual dispatch call — recreating the exact conflict the split was designed to prevent. Fixing the STORAGE_BIT root cause fixes this for free, since the destination-image bind will now succeed and properly overwrite the stale group-1 state before RCAS dispatches.

**Fix**: `FSR::process()` now queries `RD::get_singleton()->texture_get_format(p_destination_texture).usage_bits` at runtime and checks `TEXTURE_USAGE_STORAGE_BIT` directly, rather than assuming WebGPU specifically (any backend that can't write here in the future hits the same fallback). When absent, RCAS writes into a new intermediate texture (`p_render_buffers`'s `"FSR"/"rcas_output"`, same format as the actual destination, `STORAGE_BIT | CAN_COPY_FROM_BIT` only — no sRGB shareable-format request, so no conflict) instead of `p_destination_texture` directly, and a plain `RD::texture_copy()` (a GPU blit, not a shader -- needs no `TextureBinding`/no synchronization-scope conflict at all) moves the result into the real destination after `compute_list_end()`. `render_target_get_color_usage_bits()` gained `TEXTURE_USAGE_CAN_COPY_TO_BIT` (unconditionally, on every backend -- a plain copy-destination usage carries none of `STORAGE_BIT`'s sRGB-view restriction, so this is free) so `texture_copy()`'s destination-side check passes. This is the same "never compute-write the render target directly, write to an internal buffer and let something else move it across" pattern FSR2/MetalFX Temporal already use for CLAUDE.md's own documented reasons -- FSR1 just didn't need it until this fork ran it on WebGPU for the first time.

Confirmed the destination's *base* format (`dest_format.format`, what `texture_get_format()` returns, as opposed to `shareable_formats`) is genuinely storage-compatible on its own -- the sRGB conflict is specifically about combining `StorageBinding` with an *additional* sRGB view format declared on the *same* texture, which the plain single-format intermediate texture never requests, so it hits no equivalent restriction.

**Verified (Round 1)**: native `linuxbsd target=editor dev_build=yes` compiled clean; a full Emscripten `platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes` rebuild also compiled clean.

**Round 2 — Round 1's fix had its own bug, found via live re-verification**: the user rebuilt/re-exported and hit a brand-new error, `GPUValidationError: Format (TextureFormat::RGBA8Unorm) of [Texture (unlabeled 1920x1080 px, TextureFormat::RGBA8Unorm)] expected to be (TextureFormat::RGBA16Float)` on `bgl:FsrUpscaleShaderRD:*:set1`, cascading into the same `[Invalid BindGroup]`/`[Invalid CommandBuffer]` spam pattern as every other task in this run (675 occurrences each; confirmed via the same grep-for-unique-signatures approach as prior tasks that this is the *only* new root cause in the fresh log). **Root cause**: `fsr_upscale.glsl`'s `fsr_image` storage-image binding -- shared verbatim by both the EASU and RCAS passes, since they're the same shader/pipeline distinguished only by a push-constant -- is hardcoded `layout(rgba16f, ...)`, so it can *only* ever be bound to an RGBA16F-viewable texture, regardless of what pass or what the real final destination format is. Round 1's intermediate texture was created with `dest_format.format` (i.e. `p_destination_texture`'s own format, RGBA8Unorm in this project's actual LDR viewport configuration) to keep it `texture_copy()`-compatible with the final destination -- which is exactly backwards: it needs to match the *shader's* fixed format instead, and the mismatch to the real destination then needs bridging by something that can convert between formats, which a raw GPU `texture_copy()` fundamentally cannot do (it requires byte-identical formats on both sides).

**Fix**: create the intermediate ("FSR"/"rcas_output") texture at `p_render_buffers->get_base_data_format()` (RGBA16_SFLOAT) instead -- the same format source `upscale_texture` already uses, satisfying the shader's binding regardless of pass -- with `STORAGE_BIT | SAMPLING_BIT` usage (no longer `CAN_COPY_FROM_BIT`, since the move to the real destination is no longer a raw copy). Replaced the `RD::texture_copy()` call with a real shader blit, `RendererRD::CopyEffects::get_singleton()->copy_to_fb_rect()` (into `FramebufferCacheRD::get_singleton()->get_cache(p_destination_texture)`), mirroring the exact call shape `renderer_scene_render_rd.cpp`'s own bilinear/nearest final-output fallback already uses for the identical kind of into-the-render-target blit -- a normal color-attachment write handles the RGBA16F→RGBA8Unorm (or whatever the real format is) conversion for free as part of ordinary rasterization, unlike a byte-level copy. Reverted `texture_storage.cpp`'s Round-1 addition of `TEXTURE_USAGE_CAN_COPY_TO_BIT` (no longer needed, since the render target is now reached via a normal attachment write rather than a copy destination) — the file's other Round-1 comment updates (explaining why `STORAGE_BIT` is still WebGPU-excluded) were kept, adjusted to describe the blit instead of the copy.

**Verified (Round 2)**: native `linuxbsd target=editor dev_build=yes` compiled clean; `driver_unit_tests` 327/327, `shader_corpus` 13/13 (both unaffected, as expected -- this bug and its fix are entirely in driver-agnostic shared code, same as Round 1). A full Emscripten `platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes` rebuild compiled clean. **Live-verified**: the user re-exported and re-tested in their real project — **FSR1 now works correctly on WebGPU with no errors.** Both FSR1 and FSR2 are confirmed fully working on WebGPU as of this task.

**Files**: `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/effects/fsr.cpp`; `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/storage_rd/texture_storage.cpp`.

### Task 9.15: Tier 1 #4 — vertex-stage read_write storage demotion moved to a Tint IR-level transform; format-remap text passes made memcpy-safe; dead binding_array WGSL-text pass removed; RW-storage-split/read-only-to-sampled/depth-reclassification assessed and confirmed correctly text-based (no further migration attempted) `[DONE — Phases 1-2 live-verified; Phases 3-4 assessed, no code change; see plan-of-attack.md]`

**Status**: 2026-09-14. Picking up Tier 1 #4 ("Move WGSL string patching to AST-level transforms", plan-of-attack.md) per Task 7.18's complaint: `RenderingDeviceDriverWebGPU::_remap_unsupported_wgsl_storage_formats()` (`drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`) post-processed Tint's generated WGSL text with several in-place fixed-length `memcpy` replacements — correct only as long as the old and new format-name strings happen to be byte-identical in length, which is true today purely by coincidence of WGSL's texel-format spelling, and would silently corrupt output the moment that stopped holding (e.g. a future Tint WGSL-writer change, or a currently-untested format pairing).

**Scope for this pass** (see the approved plan at the time, and `plan-of-attack.md`'s Tier 1 #4 entry, for the full 5-item inventory of string-based passes — items 2-5, binding_array unwrapping, RW-storage-texture splitting, read-only-to-sampled conversion, and the ~365-line depth-texture reclassification heuristic, are **not** touched here and remain scoped as Phases 2-4 follow-up work):

1. **Vertex-stage `read_write` → `read` storage-buffer demotion — migrated to a Tint `core::ir::Module` transform.** New files `drivers/webgpu/tint_ir_transforms.h`/`.cpp` (compiled in the Tint C++20 environment alongside `tint_wrapper.cpp`, never included from the C++17 driver side) implement `DemoteVertexStageReadWriteStorage(core::ir::Module&)`, modeled on Tint's own in-tree `Bgra8UnormPolyfill` transform (`thirdparty/tint/src/tint/lang/core/ir/transform/bgra8unorm_polyfill.cc`). `tint_wrapper.cpp` now bypasses `tint::SpirvToWgsl()`'s convenience composition and calls `tint::spirv::reader::ReadIR()` → `DemoteVertexStageReadWriteStorage()` → `tint::wgsl::writer::WgslFromIR()` directly, so the transform runs on every caller (both the runtime driver and `tint_convert_cli`'s build-time precompilation path) with zero extra plumbing — it self-detects relevance by checking `core::ir::Function::IsVertex()` on the module's entry point, rather than needing an external flag threaded across the C++17/C++20 boundary. The text-based demotion block in `_remap_unsupported_wgsl_storage_formats()` was removed (replaced with a comment pointing here).

   This is a structural fix, not just a safer-looking one: unlike the old string version, it doesn't stop at renaming the variable's own declared type — a storage-buffer access like `vbuf.data[i]` produces its *own* explicit pointer-typed IR result (computed once, from the base variable's access mode, when Tint's SPIR-V reader first built the IR), and demoting only the variable leaves that derived result stale, which Tint's own IR validator then rejects as inconsistent. Discovered via a synthetic test (`glslangValidator` on a hand-written vertex-stage SSBO shader) failing with exactly that validator error on the first attempt; fixed by having the transform recursively walk `Value::ForEachUseUnsorted()` from each demoted variable, narrowing any further pointer-typed result it flows into (i.e. following access-chains) generically rather than hardcoding `ir::Access` — so it also covers any other pointer-producing instruction Tint might introduce. Confirmed via two synthetic repros: a shader that only *reads* a `read_write`-declared SSBO in the vertex stage now correctly demotes to `var<storage, read>` end-to-end with no validator error; a shader that (unrealistically, for any real Godot shader) actually *writes* to a storage buffer from the vertex stage still correctly fails — WGSL forbids that outright regardless of the declared access mode, exactly as it did before this change, so this is not a regression, just a restriction the demotion was never able to route around in the first place.

2. **The two remaining 16-bit storage-format remap blocks in `_remap_unsupported_wgsl_storage_formats()`** (tier1-conditional SNORM/UNORM→float, and the always-applied 16-bit→32-bit remap) **converted from fixed-length `memcpy` to `String::replace()`**, matching the pattern the existing 8-bit remap block already used safely. This directly eliminates Task 7.18's literal complaint for the parts of the pipeline that must remain text-based (unlike the vertex demotion, whether a format needs remapping depends on `has_texture_formats_tier1`, a runtime/native-precompile-time capability check that isn't knowable from the IR alone at a shared, cache/precompiled-table-safe point — see the "why not IR for this too" note below) — a future Tint spelling change now just fails to match (no-op replace) instead of corrupting output.

**Why the format-remap passes stay text-based (not IR) for now**: `_spv_to_wgsl_cached()` has three separate WGSL-retrieval paths (in-memory cache, build-time-precompiled table, fresh Tint conversion), and `_remap_unsupported_wgsl_storage_formats()` runs unconditionally *after* whichever path returns WGSL — including the precompiled-table path, which at native precompile time has no access to the runtime `has_texture_formats_tier1` capability bit. Moving the format remap fully into the IR (inside `tint_wrapper_spirv_to_wgsl()`, like the vertex-demotion fix) would require it to run identically for the precompiled table (baked once, capability-agnostic) and the runtime path (capability-aware) — not possible without either baking the wrong choice into the precompiled table or adding a second post-hoc WGSL round-trip parse. The vertex demotion had no such constraint (it's unconditional, not capability-gated), which is exactly why it could move for free and the format remaps could not, yet.

**Verified**: rebuilt `bin/tint_convert_cli` clean (no compile errors after fixing an initial missing-include issue — `tint_wrapper.cpp` needed `core/ir/module.h` directly once it stopped going through `tint::SpirvToWgsl()`'s complete-type guarantee). `webgpu_tests/shader_corpus` 13/13 (unchanged — none of the corpus fixtures happen to exercise a vertex-stage `read_write` SSBO, so this only confirms no regression, not the new code path's positive case; that was confirmed separately via the two synthetic `glslangValidator` repros above). `webgpu_tests/driver_unit_tests` 327/327, `webgpu_tests/preprocessing_tests` 192/192+1 skip (both unaffected, as expected — this change is Tint-conversion/WebGPU-driver-only). Native `linuxbsd target=editor dev_build=yes` and full Emscripten `platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes` rebuilds both compiled clean.

**Live-verified**: the user rebuilt/re-exported and confirmed the engine and WebGPU export both still work exactly as expected — no regressions from either phase's changes (Phase 1's IR-level vertex demotion, or Phase 2's dead-code removal below). No vertex-stage read_write storage buffer is known to exist in any live-tested scene, so Phase 1 remains a maintainability/correctness-by-construction improvement rather than a fix for a previously-observed bug; Phase 2's removal is confirmed inert-as-expected (nothing depended on the dead code).

**Files**: `drivers/webgpu/tint_ir_transforms.h` (new), `drivers/webgpu/tint_ir_transforms.cpp` (new), `drivers/webgpu/tint_wrapper.cpp`, `drivers/webgpu/rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`, `drivers/webgpu/SCsub`, `drivers/webgpu/tint_cli/build.sh`.

**Phase 2 (same day, continuing Tier 1 #4) — the `binding_array<T, N>` unwrapping pass (inventory item 2) turned out to already be dead code, not something needing IR migration.** Started implementing an IR-level replacement the same way as Phase 1 (a `UnwrapBindingArrays()` transform collapsing `core::type::BindingArray` vars to their element type and eliminating the `Access` instructions that index into them, `ReplaceAllUsesWith()` + `Destroy()`) — this compiled and worked correctly against a hand-written synthetic case, but before wiring it in permanently, checked whether the WGSL-text-level pass it was meant to replace ever actually fires on any real shader. It doesn't: `spirv_preprocess::flatten_binding_arrays()` (`drivers/webgpu/spirv_preprocess.cpp`) is a *SPIR-V-level* preprocessing pass — one of the shared passes both `shader_create_from_container()` and `_create_module_with_spec_constants()` already run (via `_spv_to_wgsl_cached()` → `_translate_spirv_to_wgsl()`) *before* Tint's reader ever sees the bytes — that comprehensively eliminates every `OpTypeArray`/`OpTypeRuntimeArray` of a handle type at the SPIR-V level already, complete with proper pointer-type deduplication (two `OpTypePointer`s that become identical after array→element substitution get correctly merged to one ID, not left as SPIR-V-invalid duplicates). Confirmed empirically two ways: (1) grepped the existing ahead-of-time precompiled WGSL table (`wgsl_precompiled.gen.h`, every declared shader mode variant in the engine, 45k+ lines) for `binding_array<` — zero occurrences; (2) regenerated that table from scratch via `wgsl_precompile.py` *after* removing the WGSL-text fallback entirely — still 196 compiled/0 tint failures, still zero `binding_array<` occurrences. Since the fallback could never fire, the synthetic IR transform was also unreachable dead code by the same logic (a third redundant layer doing a job the SPIR-V pass already does, more thoroughly) — reverted it out of `tint_ir_transforms.{h,cpp}`/`tint_wrapper.cpp` rather than keep untested, unexercised code around. **Fix**: removed both copies of the WGSL-text-level `binding_array<` unwrapping block (`shader_create_from_container()` and `_create_module_with_spec_constants()`) outright, replaced with a comment explaining why and pointing at `flatten_binding_arrays()` as the actual (already-correct, already-structural) fix. Net effect: this phase turned out to be subtractive rather than additive — real dead-code removal, not a migration, which is arguably a better outcome for Tier 1 #4's actual goal (less fragile text-scanning surface area, not more machinery). **Verified**: `tint_convert_cli` rebuild clean; `shader_corpus` 13/13; a full `wgsl_precompile.py` run across the entire engine corpus post-removal: 196 compiled, 0 tint failures (unchanged), 0 `binding_array<` occurrences (unchanged); `driver_unit_tests` 327/327, `preprocessing_tests` 192/192+1 skip; native `linuxbsd target=editor dev_build=yes` and full Emscripten `platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes` rebuilds both compile clean.

**Live-verified (Phase 2)**: same rebuild/re-export/re-test round as Phase 1 above — user confirmed engine and WebGPU export both work exactly as expected after this phase's changes.

**Phase 3 (same day) — RW-storage-texture read/write splitting and read-only-to-sampled conversion: assessed, confirmed correctly text-based, no migration made.** Applied the same "does this actually still fire, and is a migration architecturally sound" scrutiny Phase 2 established, to the next two items in the inventory (`shader_create_from_container()`'s `!has_rw_storage_textures && strstr(wgsl_str, "read_write>")` block, and its `!has_rw_storage_textures && strstr(wgsl_str, ", read>")` sibling). Unlike Phase 2's binding_array pass, these genuinely fire — they're gated behind `has_rw_storage_textures`, a real runtime WebGPU feature query (`readonly-and-readwrite-storage-textures`, which Firefox and Safari lack) queried via `EM_ASM_INT` against the browser's actual `GPUAdapter`/`GPUDevice` at driver-init time (`rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` ~L1144-1153) — not something knowable at native `tint_convert_cli` precompile time, which builds one shared WGSL table used by every browser regardless of which one ends up loading it. This is the exact same architectural wall Phase 1 hit and documented for the format-remap passes: moving this into the unconditional IR transform inside `tint_wrapper_spirv_to_wgsl()` would either bake one browser's capability choice into the precompiled table for every other browser too, or require a second post-hoc WGSL round-trip re-parse (parse the retrieved WGSL back into an IR, apply the transform, re-emit) purely to make an IR-level decision at a point where the WGSL already exists as text anyway. Also checked both blocks for Task 7.18's literal fixed-length-memcpy fragility: the only `memcpy` calls present are bounded, truncation-guarded copies into fixed-size local buffers during the initial declaration-scanning phase (e.g. `char buf[256]; int len = ...; if (len > 255) len = 255; memcpy(buf, ..., len); buf[len] = '\0';`, used only to materialize a parsed substring into a local `String` before further processing) — not in-place same-length text replacement, so there's no live correctness bug here to fix either. **Conclusion: both passes are already correctly shaped for what they need to do; no code change made.**

**Phase 4 (same day) — depth-texture reclassification heuristic (`_reclassify_single_component_depth_textures()`, ~365 lines): assessed in depth; a real architectural insight found, but no migration attempted this pass.** Unlike Phase 3's two passes, this one runs unconditionally (no capability gate) on every backend, so it's architecturally *eligible* for IR migration in a way Phase 3 is not. Checked for memcpy fragility: only one `memcpy` in the whole function, at the very end (`memcpy(result, cs.get_data(), cs.length() + 1)`), the same safe "commit the final rebuilt `String` into an exactly-sized malloc'd buffer" idiom used throughout this file — not the Task 7.18 pattern. While reading the function's full false-positive-exclusion history (Task 9.5 Round 5's `p_storage_converted_keys` set, the "half" exclusion, the "dilated" exclusion from Task 9.13), found a genuine structural insight: `p_storage_converted_keys` (textures to skip because the read-only-to-sampled pass — Phase 3 above — just relabeled them) only exists to patch around this heuristic's own *position in the pipeline*. That exclusion set is only ever non-empty on Firefox/Safari, because only there does a genuine storage texture (e.g. `voxel_gi.glsl`'s r32f "depth" dynamic-GI buffer) get textually relabeled from `texture_storage_2d<r32float, read>` to the literal `texture_2d<f32>` spelling this heuristic's candidate-scan looks for; on Chrome the same texture never changes its WGSL type spelling and is structurally never a candidate in the first place. If this heuristic's precondition instead ran at the true Tint IR level — before any WGSL-text conversion exists at all — a genuine `core::type::StorageTexture` IR value could never be type-confused with a genuine sampled `Texture2D` IR value, and the entire `p_storage_converted_keys` plumbing (threaded from `shader_create_from_container()` through to this function, and duplicated as an always-empty stub in `_create_module_with_spec_constants()`) would become structurally unnecessary rather than something to patch around after the fact — mirroring exactly the kind of "the string version needs a workaround the IR version wouldn't" pattern Phase 1's vertex-demotion fix already demonstrated once.

**Why no migration was attempted this pass despite that insight**: acting on it isn't a small, low-risk edit like Phase 1's — it would require either (a) also migrating Phase 3's two passes into the IR (already shown above to be architecturally blocked without breaking precompiled-table capability-agnosticism), since this heuristic's correctness currently depends on running *after* Phase 3's text-level output, or (b) fully re-implementing this heuristic's three independent layers — the naming check (Godot's own GLSL identifier-naming convention, which is irreducibly a name/string comparison no matter which API surface reads it), the single-component-access structural signal (genuinely more robust to check via IR use-analysis than by parsing `textureSample(...)` call argument text, and the one part of this heuristic that would most benefit from migrating), and the call-site rewriting (inserting/changing arguments to match `texture_depth_2d`'s different builtin-call signatures from `texture_2d<f32>`'s) — entirely at the IR level. That's a multi-day, standalone engineering effort in its own right (matching the plan's original whole-item estimate), touching rendering-critical logic that has already needed three rounds of hard-won, live-bug-driven false-positive fixes (Task 9.5 Round 5, and the "half"/"dilated" exclusions from real regressions found via live browser testing). A rushed version of this, attempted without dedicated design and test time, risks a *silently wrong* depth-sampling regression (corrupted-looking output, not a compile error) rather than a caught build failure — a materially worse failure mode than what Phase 1/2 were risking. **Conclusion: no code change made; the `p_storage_converted_keys`-elimination insight above is the concrete starting point if this is ever picked up as its own dedicated effort.**

**Tier 1 #4 status after this session**: Phase 1 (vertex demotion → IR, format-remap memcpy→String::replace) and Phase 2 (binding_array dead-code removal) are done and live-verified. Phases 3-4 have been thoroughly assessed rather than left unexamined — Phase 3 is confirmed correctly text-based with no fragility to fix; Phase 4 is confirmed not fragile either, with a documented architectural path for a future full migration that this session deliberately did not rush. **Verified (Phases 3-4 assessment)**: no code changed, so no new build/test run was needed beyond re-confirming the existing Phase 1-2 verification results still hold (they do — nothing in this assessment touched any file).

### Task 9.16: Add a Linux benchmark suite runner `[NOT STARTED]`
**Status**: `NOT_STARTED`
**Effort**: unscoped — see notes below; could be a quick port or could uncover a real Forward+ bug (unbounded)
**Dependencies**: none to start; scoping this properly benefits from Task 9.5 (Forward+ live-run investigation) being further along, since it's the leg most likely to surface something new

**Motivation**: the README's benchmark numbers (`Key Stats`, `Performance Benchmarks`) are inherited from the upstream `dwalter/godotwebgpu` project and only cover WebGL vs. WebGPU/Forward Mobile. This fork's whole point is Forward+ over WebGPU, so the README should eventually carry a third column: WebGL (Compatibility) vs. WebGPU Forward Mobile vs. WebGPU Forward+, measured on this fork at 4.7.2. That requires a benchmark runner that works on this project's actual dev platform (Linux), not just macOS.

**Current state of the benchmark tooling** (`webgpu_tests/benchmark/`):
- Already has 7 scenes (`scene_a_sprites` through `scene_h_batching`/`scene_h_batched`) with GDScript autoload profilers (`benchmark_profiler.gd`) that print `BENCHMARK_RESULT|...|avg=...|p1=...|p99=...` to console.
- `run_benchmark.sh` drives export+serve+capture for `native`/`webgl`/`webgpu`, but is **hardcoded to macOS**: `bin/godot.macos.editor.arm64`, `open -a "Google Chrome"`, BSD `sed -i ''` syntax. It will not run as-is on this Linux dev box.
- Prior results in `webgpu_notes/manualy_run_benchmark_scenes.md` and `webgpu_notes/native_vs_web_benchmarks.md` were gathered manually this same macOS way, and only cover WebGL vs. WebGPU/Forward Mobile — no Forward+ leg exists anywhere yet.
- `webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/` already has a working Playwright setup in this repo, which is the natural way to automate FPS capture (read the console `BENCHMARK_RESULT` line) instead of manually eyeballing a Chrome window — worth reusing rather than re-inventing.

**Scope for this task, when actioned**:
1. Port `run_benchmark.sh` to Linux: swap `bin/godot.macos.editor.arm64` for `bin/godot.linuxbsd.editor.x86_64` (or whatever this build's actual binary name is), replace `open -a "Google Chrome"` with a Linux Chrome/Chromium launch (or better, drive it headlessly via Playwright the way `screenshot_comparison` does), and fix the BSD-specific `sed -i ''` calls to GNU `sed -i` syntax.
2. Decide how results are captured: manual eyeballing (matches the macOS script's current UX, cheap to port) vs. Playwright-automated console-log scraping (more work up front, but removes the human-in-the-loop step and makes the runner scriptable/repeatable — probably worth it if this is going to be re-run every time the README needs updating).
3. Add the missing third leg: a Forward+ export/run path per scene. Check whether the benchmark project's `project.godot` needs a `rendering_method="forward_plus"` variant (vs. the mobile renderer used today) and whether SDFGI needs to stay explicitly disabled in that variant (it's broken — see the SDFGI section of `README.md` and Task 9.5).
4. Run all 7 scenes × 3 renderers (WebGL / WebGPU Forward Mobile / WebGPU Forward+), capture FPS, and update the README's `Key Stats` and `Performance Benchmarks` tables with real, fork-specific numbers instead of the inherited upstream ones.

**Known risk, called out explicitly so it isn't lost when this is picked up**: legs 1-2 (WebGL, WebGPU/Forward Mobile) are known-working paths already benchmarked once before — re-running them is mechanical. Leg 3 (WebGPU/Forward+) is not proven under load — Task 9.5 is still finding real correctness/stability bugs after 44+ investigation rounds (see the unresolved SDFGI brightness-runaway bug). Pushing instance/particle/light counts up via these benchmark scenes on Forward+ for the first time has a real chance of surfacing a new crash/hang/corruption bug rather than a clean FPS number, which would turn this from a benchmarking task into an open-ended debugging one. Scope/estimate this task's effort only for the runner + legs 1-2; treat the Forward+ leg's first run as a discovery step that might spawn its own follow-up task rather than assuming it'll just produce a number.

Diff native vs. WebGPU visually and numerically (per the methodology doc's guidance on reading engine data back out for non-visual comparisons) for any drift, however subtle — SDFGI's bug was a slow brightness ramp, not an obvious glitch, so short captures or a quick eyeball pass may not be enough; favor longer captures for anything accumulator/history-buffer-based.
5. File any confirmed new issue as its own numbered task (10.5+) with severity/repro steps, following the Status/Severity/Lines/Issue/Investigation format used elsewhere in this doc.

**Completion Criteria**: `run_benchmark.sh` (or its replacement) runs end-to-end on Linux for all three renderer legs across all 7 scenes without manual macOS-only steps; README benchmark tables reflect real fork-specific numbers.
