### Task 11: Builds fail to load when Extension support isn't enabled in the export settings with error "Aborted(native code called abort())"
**Status**: `ROOT CAUSE FOUND — 4-byte heap-buffer-overflow inside the pinned emdawnwebgpu port's WGPUInstanceImpl constructor, not this fork's code; not fixable here without vendoring a patch or upgrading the toolchain. One real, independent architectural bug (kNullInstanceId/parentPtr) fixed along the way and kept, but it was never the cause of this crash.`
**Effort**: 1 day, needs a real GPU + browser session
**Dependencies**: none, but overlaps the in-progress `emsdk-upgrade` branch — see step 2.2

**Motivation**: exporting with `variant/extensions_support` unchecked (the default, non-GDExtension web export) aborts on load with the generic Emscripten `Aborted(native code called abort())`, while the same project exported with extensions enabled loads fine. The bare message is uninformative on its own; this task is mostly about getting a real diagnostic before touching any code.

**Root cause summary (read this first — Rounds 1-9 below are the full investigation trail, not required reading)**:
- **The bug**: a 4-byte heap-buffer-overflow inside Emscripten 4.0.11's vendored `emdawnwebgpu` port (`emdawnwebgpu_pkg/webgpu/src/webgpu.cpp`), not in this fork's own code. `wgpuCreateInstance()` does `return new WGPUInstanceImpl(desc);` — the `operator new` call computes `sizeof(WGPUInstanceImpl) == 32` bytes, but the constructor's own compiler-generated zero-initialization (folding `EventSource`'s 8-byte `mInstanceId`, `RefCounted`'s 16-byte `mRefCount`+`mIsImportedFromJS`, and `WGPUInstanceImpl`'s own 12-byte `mCapabilities = {}` default-member-initializer into one combined `memset`) writes **36 bytes**, overflowing by 4. Confirmed with a full AddressSanitizer report (`heap-buffer-overflow`, `WRITE of size 36`, `0 bytes after 32-byte region`) — see Round 9.
- **Why it looked like a Godot/WebGPU-driver bug for 8 rounds first**: this 4-byte overflow corrupts whatever heap allocation happens to sit next, and *which* allocation that is depends on the exact binary's memory layout — so the same underlying bug surfaced as several different-looking symptoms depending on what else was compiled into the build: a plain `Aborted(native code called abort())` (Rounds 1-3), a dlmalloc internal-consistency abort (Round 3), a `SAFE_HEAP`-exposed `EventManager::ProcessEvents` assertion (Round 4), and a `RegisterInstance`-succeeds-but-`ProcessEvents`-can't-find-it self-check failure (Round 8) — all the same root cause, just detected at different, confusing later points.
- **Why it correlated with `variant/extensions_support`**: not architecturally — `dlink_enabled`'s different linking arrangement (`MAIN_MODULE`/`SIDE_MODULE` split, `EXPORT_ALL=1`) just happens to usually place the next heap allocation somewhere the stray 4 bytes land harmlessly, while the non-dlink layout more often doesn't. Toggling extensions was never the actual variable.
- **A real, separate, independent bug was found and fixed along the way** (Round 6): `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU::initialize()` (`drivers/webgpu/rendering_context_driver_webgpu.cpp`) imports the JS-pre-initialized device via `WebGPU.importJsDevice(d)` with no `parentPtr`, so the device's async events (buffer-map completions, fence/work-done callbacks, query-pool readbacks) were being tracked under Dawn's `kNullInstanceId` bucket instead of the driver's own `WGPUInstance` — meaning `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents()` was never actually polling the right thing. Fixed by passing the instance through as `parentPtr`. **This fix is real, independently verified (fixed a genuine extensions-on hang), and is kept in the tree — but it was conclusively proven (Round 8) to not be the cause of this task's crash.**
- **Nothing left to fix in this repo for this specific task.** The overflow lives entirely in vendored/pinned toolchain code. Path forward is one of: (a) the in-progress `emsdk-upgrade` (`webgpu_notes/emsdk-upgrade.md`) — check whether a newer Emscripten/Dawn revision already fixes this once that lands; or (b) vendor a permanent local patch to the port file, following this repo's existing pattern for tracking patches to `thirdparty/tint` (`thirdparty/README.md`) — session-local edits to the emsdk cache (as used for diagnostics throughout this investigation) don't persist and aren't part of this repo.
- **Diagnostic technique notes worth keeping for next time**: (1) Emscripten's own JS-side ASan symbolizer (`_emscripten_pc_get_function`) can itself throw and abort the report before printing a stack — define `extern "C" const char *__asan_default_options() { return "symbolize=0"; }` to get raw addresses instead, then resolve them offline with `llvm-symbolizer -e <wasm> -f -C <addr>` (needs a `debug_symbols=yes` build for full names). (2) ASan needs `initial_memory=512` (or similar) — the default 32MB isn't enough for its shadow memory and fails the link with a clear `wasm-ld: error: initial memory too small` otherwise. (3) Editing a vendored file under `~/emsdk/upstream/emscripten/cache/ports/.../src/` does get picked up by a rebuild, but only if something *else* also changed to force SCons to re-invoke the port's own hash check — if a rebuild reports `` `.' is up to date `` despite an intentional vendored-file edit, delete the specific cached `cache/build/emdawnwebgpu/wasm32_libemdawnwebgpu-<hash>-O0` dir and `cache/sysroot/lib/wasm32-emscripten/libemdawnwebgpu-<hash>-O0.a` to force it.

**Round 1 (2026-09-18) — scoping only, no build yet**: checked whether the in-progress `emsdk-upgrade` branch could be implicated (a live hypothesis when this task was written, since the branch's own name suggests a toolchain change). It is not: `emsdk-upgrade.md`'s Phase 0.1 so far is *scoping and version-recording only* (`5e16f308c7`, "Begin emsdk upgrade: Phase 0.1 baseline" — records the current pinned versions for later diffing, changes no code or toolchain), and the actually-active `emcc` in this environment is still **4.0.11**, identical to the `pre-emsdk-upgrade` tag. So step 2.2's hypothesis (b) is ruled out for now — whatever causes the abort, it isn't an already-landed Emscripten version change. That leaves hypothesis (a) (a latent, pre-existing bug, most likely in the unconditional `dynamicLibraries: [...'.side.wasm']` reference in `config.js` per step 2.3, or the `web_extensions`/`web_noextensions` feature-tag pairing per step 2.4) as the leading candidate. Next step is the actual build + browser repro (step 1) — not yet done this round.

**Round 2 (2026-09-18) — could not reproduce against a fresh build from current HEAD**: did step 1's clean repro. Built `scons platform=web target=template_debug dlink_enabled=no webgpu=yes opengl3=no threads=no` from a clean incremental state (3m05s), confirmed no `.side.wasm` is produced for a non-dlink build (as expected). Found the *installed* `web_nothreads_debug.zip` export template in `~/.local/share/godot/export_templates/4.7.2.stable/` was stale (`md5sum` mismatch against the fresh `bin/` output) — exactly the recurring class of bug this doc already documents at Round 15/23/Task 10.1 — and overwrote it with the fresh build before testing, per step 1.2.
- Exported `webgpu_tests/test_project` (extensions unchecked) as both `template_debug` and a from-scratch `template_release` (completely empty export directory, no leftover files at all — ruling out a stale-leftover-`.side.wasm`-from-a-prior-dlink-export theory too, which was tested explicitly and also came back clean).
- Captured full console output for both via `webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/capture_console.mjs` (real-Vulkan-backed headless Chromium, per this doc's established methodology). **Both loaded cleanly — zero abort, zero WASM-level errors.** The release export logged `Build configuration: Emscripten 4.0.11, single-threaded, no GDExtension support.` and ran the ShaderCoverage scene to completion (4 pre-existing, unrelated cosmetic warnings only — the same `look_at()`/`SpatialMaterial` noise already catalogued elsewhere in this doc, not a new finding).
- Also confirmed the user's real `cameraSim_*` test project (`~/Downloads/cameraSim_2026-09-09_10-26-06/testing/export_presets.cfg`) has `variant/extensions_support=true` in its normal preset — i.e. the user's regular testing workflow doesn't exercise the non-extensions path at all, so this specific combination has likely only been hit incidentally.

**Conclusion of Round 2 (superseded by Round 3 below)**: the abort could not be reproduced against a genuinely fresh, checksum-verified, non-dlink build/export of the synthetic `webgpu_tests/test_project`, in either debug or release, with either a dirty or a from-scratch export directory. This turned out to be a false negative from testing the wrong project — see Round 3.

**Round 3 (2026-09-18) — reproduced against the user's real project; root-caused to heap corruption, not a graceful/reportable error**: the user re-tested and reported a *different* surface symptom than the task title — with extensions disabled, their real `cameraSim_*` project (not `webgpu_tests/test_project`) never finishes loading (stuck on the Godot loading screen, no console errors at all). Made a scratch copy of `~/Downloads/cameraSim_2026-09-09_10-26-06/testing` (per `LIVE_REPRO_METHODOLOGY.md`'s scratch-copy guidance, so the user's real files aren't touched), confirmed its `export_presets.cfg` now has `variant/extensions_support=false` (the user had it `=true` for debugging when Round 2 checked it, now switched back), and exported it fresh with the checksum-verified `template_debug` non-dlink template from Round 2.
- First export attempt left the project's pre-existing `builds/index.side.wasm` (leftover from a prior extensions-enabled export) in place, to match the user's actual directory state exactly. Loading it via `capture_console.mjs` (real-Vulkan headless Chromium) **did abort** — `Aborted(native code called abort())`, with a full JS/WASM stack trace this time (unlike the user's own report of zero console output — see the nondeterminism note below).
- Deleted the stale `index.side.wasm` and re-exported clean — **still aborts, identically**. This rules out the leftover-`.side.wasm` theory from Round 1/2 entirely: it's not a factor here, the project itself triggers this independent of any dylib-loading path.
- Symbolicated the stack trace by disassembling the exact built `.wasm` with `wasm-dis` (Binaryen, from the active emsdk) and correcting for the 267-entry function-index offset between V8's `wasm-function[N]` numbering (imports + defined functions in one space) and `wasm-dis`'s `$N` numbering (which restarts at 0 for defined functions, after `$fimport$0..266`). The innermost frame (`wasm-function[81503]` → `$81236`) is exactly libc's `abort()` shim (`call $fimport$231` which is imported as `env._abort_js`, then `unreachable`) — confirming this is a real `abort()` call, not a JS-level assertion or an unrelated WASM trap. The next frame up (`wasm-function[81759]` → `$81492`) is dlmalloc's main allocator routine (`i32.const 244` size-class check, bin-pointer arithmetic against a fixed heap-base constant — the standard Emscripten-vendored dlmalloc shape), consistent with dlmalloc's **internal heap-consistency assertion**, not an allocation-failure abort (this fork's `detect.py` already sets `-sABORTING_MALLOC=0` unconditionally for all web builds, so a plain out-of-memory should return `NULL`, not abort — confirmed this isn't that).
- **Working theory**: something writes out of bounds (buffer/texture overrun, use-after-free, or similar memory-safety bug) somewhere earlier in this project's specific startup path — its character model (`Школяр.glb`) with a Godot-3.x-style `SpatialMaterial` (the `refraction` remap warning already seen with the synthetic project in Round 2, so at least one legacy-material code path is shared) is the most concrete lead, but general enough that it isn't proven yet — corrupting `dlmalloc`'s heap metadata, which then trips an unrelated later `malloc`/`free` call's internal sanity check. This is undefined behavior, not a deterministic code path: it would plausibly explain the user's own report of a **silent hang with zero console output** on a real interactive browser being the *same* underlying bug manifesting differently (heap corruption doesn't reliably reproduce the same way run-to-run or across browser/GPU backends — this session's headless-Chromium-on-RTX-4080-class-GPU repro crashing loudly vs. the user's real session hanging silently are both plausible outcomes of the same corruption, not necessarily two different bugs). Not yet confirmed as the same root cause, though — genuinely could be two separate issues that happen to both gate on `extensions_support=false`.
- **Why this doesn't reproduce on `webgpu_tests/test_project` (Round 2)**: that project is far simpler (no imported character mesh/materials) and likely just doesn't exercise whatever code path corrupts the heap — consistent with memory-safety bugs typically being data/content-shape-dependent, not something a minimal fixture is guaranteed to hit.
- **Not yet done**: the actual root-cause isolation. Disassembly alone identifies *where the crash is detected* (deep inside dlmalloc), not *where the corrupting write happened* (that requires either an instrumented build — Emscripten's `-sSAFE_HEAP=1` catches many OOB accesses immediately at the write site instead of letting them silently corrupt memory until a much later, confusing crash — or bisecting the project's scene/asset graph to find the minimal reproducer). `SAFE_HEAP=1` roughly doubles memory-access cost, so it should only be used for this diagnostic build, never shipped.

**Round 4 (2026-09-18) — SAFE_HEAP rebuild changes the crash entirely; root cause narrowed to a `WGPUInstance` use-after-free/lifecycle bug in the driver, not a buffer overrun**: added `-sSAFE_HEAP=1` to `platform/web/detect.py` temporarily (reverted after this round — never commit it, it roughly doubles memory-access cost), rebuilt `template_debug` (dlink=no threads=no, 3m03s incremental relink), reinstalled over the export template, and re-ran the exact same `cameraSim_*` scratch-copy repro (clean export, no leftover `.side.wasm`).
- The crash is completely different under `SAFE_HEAP`: no OOB-access exception at all (which would have been `SAFE_HEAP`'s own error format) — instead, a genuine Dawn/emdawnwebgpu internal assertion: `Assertion failed: instanceIt != mPerInstanceEvents.end(), at: .../emdawnwebgpu_pkg/webgpu/src/webgpu.cpp,508,ProcessEvents`. `SAFE_HEAP` changes the binary's exact memory layout/reuse timing, so this is consistent with both crashes being the *same* underlying use-after-free — the vanilla build's dlmalloc-corruption abort (Round 3) was memory reuse silently overwriting a freed `WGPUInstanceImpl`'s bookkeeping before a later `malloc`/`free` call tripped over it, while `SAFE_HEAP`'s different allocation pattern this time lands on memory that's freed-but-not-yet-reused, exposing the *actual* lifecycle violation cleanly instead.
- Read the actual emdawnwebgpu source (`~/emsdk/upstream/emscripten/cache/ports/emdawnwebgpu/emdawnwebgpu_pkg/webgpu/src/webgpu.cpp`) to confirm the mechanism: every `WGPUInstanceImpl` registers itself in a global `EventManager::mPerInstanceEvents` map in its constructor (`RegisterInstance`) and unregisters in its destructor (`UnregisterInstance`, `webgpu.cpp:483-499`). `ProcessEvents(instance)` (`webgpu.cpp:502-508`) asserts the instance is still registered. **An instance ID present in a live call but absent from the map means `wgpuInstanceRelease()` already ran for that instance and its `WGPUInstanceImpl` was already `delete`d** — i.e., `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents()` is being called on an already-destroyed instance. This is a real use-after-free, not a false-positive assertion.
- Traced where the instance comes from on the driver side: `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU::initialize()` (`drivers/webgpu/rendering_context_driver_webgpu.cpp:69-71`) creates it via a plain `wgpuCreateInstance(&inst_desc)` (separate from the JS-imported `preinitializedWebGPUDevice`'s own device, which is wired in immediately after via `WebGPU.importJsDevice()`); `~RenderingContextDriverWebGPU()` (`rendering_context_driver_webgpu.cpp:44-58`) releases it. All four call sites in `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp` that call `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents()` (`buffer_map()` ~L1503, the texture-readback path ~L2500, `fence_wait()` ~L3412, and `command_buffer_end()`'s query-pool-readback path ~L3642) fetch the instance **live** via `context_driver->get_instance()` each time rather than caching a raw pointer locally — so this isn't a stale local variable within those functions. (This round guessed the crash landed on `fence_wait()` from timing alone — Round 5 below instruments this directly and shows that guess was wrong.)
- **Leading hypothesis, not yet confirmed**: this points at either (a) `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU` itself being destroyed and a *new* one recreated during startup (only one construction site exists — `platform/web/display_server_web.cpp:1155`, inside `DisplayServerWeb`'s constructor — so this would require `DisplayServerWeb` itself being torn down and recreated once during boot, which needs checking for any rendering-driver-fallback/retry path in `main.cpp`/`os_web.cpp` that could do that), or (b) something more specific to the non-dlink/non-`EXPORT_ALL` build stripping or mis-wiring part of emdawnwebgpu's own JS↔C++ instance-registration glue (its `--js-library`-injected functions aren't obviously exempted from Closure/wasm-opt DCE the way `EXPORT_ALL=1` exempts everything in the dlink build) — which would directly explain why this is `extensions_support`-dependent at all, since that's the one build-flag axis that differs between the working and broken configurations. (b) is the more direct explanation for the `extensions_support` correlation specifically and should be checked first.
- **Checked (b) directly — ruled out**: diffed `grep -o 'emwgpu[A-Za-z_]*'` output between the dlink and non-dlink **debug** builds (both debug, to isolate the `dlink_enabled` variable specifically from any Closure-minification difference release builds would introduce) — the non-dlink build is genuinely missing 10 of 52 `emwgpu*` JS-glue functions (`emwgpuAdapterRequestDevice`, `emwgpuBufferDestroy`, `emwgpuBufferReadMappedRange`, `emwgpuBufferWriteMappedRange`, `emwgpuDeviceCreateComputePipelineAsync`, `emwgpuDeviceCreateRenderPipelineAsync`, `emwgpuDevicePopErrorScope`, `emwgpuInstanceRequestAdapter`, `emwgpuShaderModuleGetCompilationInfo`, `emwgpuWaitAny`) — a real, confirmed effect of `wasm-ld`'s dead-code elimination not applying to the dlink build (which forces `EXPORT_ALL=1`). **But none of the missing 10 are instance-registration-related**, and critically: `EventManager`/`RegisterInstance`/`UnregisterInstance`/`mPerInstanceEvents` (the actual mechanism this bug lives in) are pure C++ inside `webgpu.cpp`, compiled directly into the WASM — not JS glue at all, so JS-side DCE structurally cannot be the cause here. Hypothesis (b) is ruled out.
- That leaves hypothesis (a) — something double-constructs/destroys `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU` (or its `WGPUInstanceImpl`) during startup, specifically when `extensions_support=false` — as the sole remaining lead. Checked `platform/web/display_server_web.cpp` for a second, earlier "probe" instantiation pattern (a common shape in engines that test driver availability before committing to it) around the single `memnew(RenderingContextDriverWebGPU)` call site (`display_server_web.cpp:1155`) — found no such probe/retry path in the code read so far, so if (a) is right, the duplicate construction/destruction isn't obviously visible from a static read and needs live instrumentation to catch.

**Round 5 (2026-09-18) — hypothesis (a) disproven; found the real architectural gap instead**: added temporary `print_line` diagnostics exactly as Round 4 proposed — to `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU`'s constructor (`this`), destructor (`this`, `instance`), and `initialize()` (`this`), plus one at `fence_wait()`'s `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents()` call site (`context_driver`, `inst`) — rebuilt `template_debug` non-dlink (44s incremental, only the two touched files recompiled), reinstalled, and re-ran the identical `cameraSim_*` scratch-copy repro. All instrumentation reverted immediately after this round (`git diff --stat drivers/webgpu/` confirmed clean) — nothing shipped.
- **Result**: exactly one `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU() ctor` and one `initialize()` log line printed, for the same `this` pointer, and **no destructor log ever printed** before the abort. Hypothesis (a) (double construction/destruction) is disproven outright — there is only ever one context driver, it's never destroyed, so the crash cannot be a stale/dangling `context_driver` pointer.
- Also telling: the `fence_wait()` diagnostic **never printed** before the abort — meaning this crash (unlike Round 4's guess) happens *before* the first `fence_wait()` call, at an earlier point in first-frame setup (most likely `buffer_map()` ~L1503 or the texture-readback path ~L2500, both of which also call `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents()`, per the same grep from Round 4 — not yet pinned down to the exact one of these two). The stack-trace shape (8 frames, same as Round 4's `SAFE_HEAP` trace almost frame-for-frame after adjusting for the small size change from the added instrumentation) confirms this is the *same* `ProcessEvents` assertion as Round 4, and — importantly — **it now reproduces on a plain build, with no `SAFE_HEAP` needed at all**, meaning this is a deterministic bug, not one that only surfaces under heap-layout perturbation as Round 4 assumed.
- With (a) and (b) both ruled out, and `context_driver`'s own instance confirmed live and never released, the only way `ProcessEvents(instance)` can fail to find `instance` in the map is if the instance ID being processed *isn't actually our `context_driver`'s instance* — or that instance was never the one meaningfully tracking the device's events in the first place. Read `emdawnwebgpu`'s JS-side `library_webgpu.js`: `importJsDevice: (device, parentPtr = 0) => { ...; devicePtr = _emwgpuCreateDevice(parentPtr, queuePtr); ... }` — **`parentPtr` defaults to `0`**. Our driver's only call site (`rendering_context_driver_webgpu.cpp:86`, `WebGPU["importJsDevice"](d)`) passes a **single argument**, so `parentPtr` is always `0`/null. On the C++ side, `emwgpuCreateDevice(const EventSource* source, WGPUQueue queue)` constructs the device via `EventSource(source)`, whose constructor is `mInstanceId(source ? source->GetInstanceId() : kNullInstanceId)` (`webgpu.cpp:453-454`) — with `source == nullptr`, **the imported device (and its queue) are registered under the special `kNullInstanceId` bucket, completely disconnected from `context_driver`'s own manually-created `wgpuCreateInstance()` instance.**
- **This is a real, independently-confirmed architectural bug, whether or not it's the exact trigger of this specific assert**: every `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents(context_driver->get_instance())` call in the driver processes events for an instance that the actual device's callbacks were never filed under — meaning buffer-map completions, fence/work-done callbacks, and query-pool readbacks are likely *not* being delivered via this path at all in practice, and the driver's own fallback logic (e.g. `fence_wait()`'s own comment: "If it hasn't fired yet, we force-signal to avoid deadlock") may be doing much more of the real work than intended, silently, on every frame. **Not yet fully explained**: precisely why the currently-registered call still fails the `!= end()` check (a registered-but-empty instance shouldn't itself trip this assert) — that detail needs either patching a debug print directly into the vendored `emsdk` cache copy of `webgpu.cpp` (invasive, and that file isn't part of this repo, so any such change is diagnostic-only, never shippable) or accepting the architectural fix below and re-testing empirically instead of fully explaining the assert mechanism first.
**Round 6 (2026-09-18) — fix applied and verified**: implemented Round 5's proposed fix in `drivers/webgpu/rendering_context_driver_webgpu.cpp`'s `initialize()` — changed `WebGPU["importJsDevice"](d)` to `WebGPU["importJsDevice"](d, $0)` with `$0` bound to the C++-side `instance` pointer (`EM_ASM_PTR(..., instance)`), so the imported device's `EventSource` resolves to the driver's own tracked `WGPUInstance` instead of `kNullInstanceId`. Added a comment at the call site explaining why (cross-referencing this task).
- Rebuilt `template_debug` non-dlink (42s incremental) and re-ran the exact `cameraSim_*` scratch-copy repro (clean export, no leftover `.side.wasm`): **zero abort, zero console errors, sustained over a 40-second capture** (previously aborted within ~1-2 seconds every time). `webgpu_tests/driver_unit_tests` still 327/327 (unaffected as expected — this fix doesn't touch shader/SPIR-V code).
- Also rebuilt and re-tested the `dlink_enabled=yes` (extensions-enabled) variant with the same fix applied, against the same project with `variant/extensions_support=true` — **also zero errors**, confirming no regression to the already-working path.
- Attempted a screenshot capture (`capture_screenshots.mjs`/a custom one-shot script) to visually confirm actual rendering rather than just absence of console errors — `page.screenshot()` timed out (60s) on both the fixed `cameraSim_*` export *and* on `webgpu_tests/test_project`'s already-known-good build (which reliably renders per Round 2 and this doc's broader history). Since the timeout reproduces identically on a build already proven to render correctly, this is a pre-existing Playwright/headless-Chromium-plus-real-GPU screenshot-capture flake in this environment, unrelated to this fix — noted here so a future session doesn't re-chase it as a new bug, but it means this round's verification rests on console-cleanliness + sustained runtime, not a visual screenshot.

**Conclusion**: Task 11's abort is fixed. Root cause: `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU::initialize()` created its own `WGPUInstance` via `wgpuCreateInstance()` but imported the pre-initialized JS device via `WebGPU.importJsDevice(d)` with no `parentPtr`, so per emdawnwebgpu's `library_webgpu.js`, the device's async events (buffer-map completions, fence/work-done callbacks, query-pool readbacks) were registered under Dawn's special `kNullInstanceId` bucket rather than the driver's own instance — completely disconnected from every `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents(context_driver->get_instance())` call in `rendering_device_driver_webgpu.cpp`. This wasn't just a startup-time crash: it meant the driver's async-event-polling path had likely never been delivering real callbacks in the non-dlink configuration at all, silently falling through to force-signal/timeout fallback logic every frame — the abort, when it did surface, was Dawn's internal `EventManager` detecting an inconsistency in that disconnected state. Passing the driver's instance as `parentPtr` reconnects the two. **Why this only affected `extensions_support=false`**: not yet fully explained (both configurations shared this exact same bug prior to the fix) — plausibly the dlink build's different WASM memory layout/heap-reuse timing (same class of effect Round 4 observed between the vanilla and `SAFE_HEAP` builds) happened to avoid tripping the specific internal-consistency check Dawn hit here, while non-dlink's layout reliably did. Worth a skeptical eye in the broader re-verification below, since "why did the *old*, definitely-broken code sometimes not crash" is a looser thread than "why does the *new* code work."

**Not yet done — recommended before fully closing this task**:
1. A real (non-headless-tooling-flake) visual/interactive check — open the fixed export in an actual browser window, or resolve the `page.screenshot()` flake, to visually confirm the scene renders instead of relying on console-cleanliness alone.
2. Re-run `./webgpu_tests/local_ci.sh` (at least `--quick`) to catch any regression outside what this round directly touched.
3. A longer live-testing pass against the user's real (non-scratch-copy) project and a broader set of scenes, per Task 10.4's methodology, since this fix touches a code path (async event delivery) that every buffer/texture readback and fence wait goes through — worth specifically re-testing anything that depends on GPU→CPU readback (screenshots, compute shader results, `texture_get_data()`) now that events may be delivered through a genuinely different path than before.
4. Double-check whether this fix changes *timing* of any async readback now that events are actually reaching the intended instance (previously-silent reliance on `fence_wait()`'s force-signal fallback, or `buffer_map()`/`texture_get_data()`'s "check map state directly" fallback, may have been masking that these paths' "real" callback delivery was never wired up correctly before) — Task 7.15 (`WGUniformSet` temp_views may leak) and Task 10.3's re-assessment of it may be worth revisiting in light of this, since both concern lifetime/cleanup around the same general area.

**Round 7 (2026-09-18) — false alarm caused by a build collision, not the fix**: after Round 6, the user rebuilt via `build-linux.sh` (which invokes `scons` for all 8 web template variants against `bin/`/`bin/obj/`) and reported the *exact same* `ProcessEvents` assertion on the resulting `.tpz`. Investigated by re-running the exact repro against a fresh, isolated rebuild (both `template_debug` and `template_release`, `dlink_enabled=no`) done in this session — both loaded cleanly, and the release build additionally logged real output from the user's own project scripts (`Aperture updated:8`, `Focal:50 FOV:...`, `Sensor: 36.0 FOV:...`), confirming genuine end-to-end execution, not just absence of errors. This directly contradicts the user's report if both were testing the same fixed source.
- Root cause of the discrepancy: **this session's own `scons` invocations (Rounds 4-6, plus the Round-7 re-verification builds) were running directly against `bin/`/`bin/obj/` in this same working directory at the same time the user's `build-linux.sh` run was in progress** — confirmed by timestamp overlap (this session's own `template_release` build finished at 10:21:39; the user's resulting `.tpz` is timestamped 10:21, the same minute). `build-linux.sh` builds all 8 variants sequentially via repeated `scons` calls into the same output tree this session was also writing to; two concurrent `scons` processes sharing one `bin/obj/` incremental-build cache can race on the same `.o`/`.a` files or have one process's linker consume another's partially-written output, producing a corrupted or effectively-stale binary independent of what the source tree actually says. This is very likely what happened here, rather than the fix being wrong.
- Confirmed no lingering `scons`/`em++`/`emcc` processes from this session before writing this up.
- **Not yet done**: a clean, solo re-run of `build-linux.sh` (this session running no builds in parallel) to produce a `.tpz` that's guaranteed to reflect the current source, followed by the user re-testing that fresh `.tpz` specifically. Until that's confirmed, treat Round 6's fix as verified only in this session's own isolated builds, not yet in the user's own build pipeline.
- **Process note for future sessions**: don't run `scons` directly against this repo's `bin/`/`bin/obj/` while the user might be running `build-linux.sh` (or vice versa) — check for running `scons`/`em++` processes first, and prefer asking before building if there's any chance of a concurrent build, since a corrupted binary from a build race can look exactly like a fix regression and waste a full debugging cycle re-chasing a already-fixed bug.

**Round 7 correction (2026-09-18, same session) — the user's report was real; a second, separate bug was found by chasing it down**: after Round 7's build-collision theory, the user actually re-ran `build-linux.sh` solo and reported the *exact same* assertion (extensions off) and, separately, a new "just hangs" symptom with extensions on. Re-verification found:
- `~/.local/share/godot/export_templates/4.7.2.stable/*.zip` had silently stopped being symlinks into this repo's `builds/4.7.2.stable/templates/` (confirmed all 7 present files are now regular files) — almost certainly because the user's own "install the `.tpz`" step extracts real file copies, breaking whatever symlinked them before. This meant several of *this session's own* `cp`-into-`builds/.../templates/` update attempts silently updated a disconnected copy while the user's `--export-debug`/`--export-release` continued reading the real, separately-installed files — a second, distinct instance of this doc's recurring stale-template trap (Round 15/23/Task 10.1), this time on the *install* side rather than the build side.
- After correcting the install path directly (`cp` straight into `~/.local/share/godot/export_templates/4.7.2.stable/web_nothreads_debug.zip`, `md5sum`-verified) and re-exporting, **the extensions-off assertion reproduced in this session's own headless test for the first time** — the earlier "could not reproduce" (Round 2, and the first half of Round 6/7) was itself a stale-template false negative on this session's side, layered on top of the user's own real bug. (The extensions-on "hang" the user separately reported was not reproduced here and is not yet understood — possibly a transient/unrelated symptom from testing during the same confused window; not chased further this round since extensions-on has tested clean in every other round.)
- With a confirmed, now-headless-reproducible repro, added direct diagnostics inside the vendored emdawnwebgpu port itself (`RegisterInstance`/`UnregisterInstance`/`ProcessEvents` in the local emsdk cache's `webgpu.cpp` — diagnostic-only, reverted after this round, never shippable since it's not part of this repo) alongside driver-side prints at all four `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents()` call sites. Rebuilt, re-verified the diagnostic strings were actually present in the compiled `.wasm` via `strings` (learned to check this directly after getting burned by the stale-template issue above), reinstalled correctly, and captured the console:
  ```
  RenderingContextDriverWebGPU() ctor this=23943656
  RegisterInstance id=1
  wgpuCreateInstance -> instance=23942288
  WebGPU 1.0 - Forward+ - Using Device #0...
  buffer_map context_driver=23943656 inst=23942288
  ProcessEvents id=1 found=0 registered_count=2
  Aborted(Assertion failed: instanceIt != mPerInstanceEvents.end()...)
  ```
  **This is the actual smoking gun**: `RegisterInstance` registers id `1` (Dawn's `InstanceID` is a small sequential counter, not a pointer — `GetNextInstanceId()`'s atomic counter starting at 1, confirmed by reading `webgpu.cpp`). Moments later, `ProcessEvents` is called with that *exact same* id (`1`) and `registered_count=2` (correctly matching the expected `[kNullInstanceId=0, ourInstance=1]` — `kNullInstanceId` confirmed `=0` in `webgpu.cpp:47`) — yet `find(1)` returns "not found." **A hash-map lookup failing for a key that both call sites agree should be present, with the size matching expectation, is not an architectural/logic bug — it is memory corruption of the map's internal structure between the two calls.** This is the exact same underlying class of bug Round 3 (dlmalloc-corruption abort) and Round 4 (`SAFE_HEAP`-exposed assertion, same symptom, no diagnostics) already pointed at — the Round 6 `importJsDevice`/`parentPtr` fix was real and independently worth keeping (it does fix the `kNullInstanceId` architectural disconnect, and plausibly explains the extensions-on hang some testing round hit), but **it was never the root cause of this specific crash**. The real bug is a heap corruption occurring somewhere between `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU::initialize()`'s instance creation and the first `buffer_map()` call (i.e., within the rest of `initialize()` — the `EM_ASM_PTR` device import, `wgpuDeviceGetQueue()`, `device_info` population — or in whatever runs between there and the first buffer map, such as canvas/surface setup or early `RenderingDevice::initialize()` work).
- Re-added `-sSAFE_HEAP=1` to `platform/web/detect.py` (temporarily, alongside the new diagnostics this time — reverted after this round, never shippable) to try to catch the actual corrupting write directly instead of its downstream symptom. Rebuild in progress as of this write-up.
- **Process lesson, worth repeating alongside Round 7's build-collision note**: after any template rebuild, verify the *installed* file (not just this repo's own `builds/`/`bin/` copies) via `md5sum` and — when testing a diagnostic build specifically — confirm the diagnostic strings are actually present in the exported `.wasm` via `strings … | grep`, before trusting any test result, positive or negative. This round's own false "fix verified" conclusion happened because a stale install silently absorbed several rebuild-and-test cycles undetected.

**Round 8 (2026-09-18, same session) — confirmed genuine heap corruption, independent of the Round 6 fix; not yet root-caused to the actual corrupting write**: pushed the diagnostics from Round 7's correction further. Added a self-check directly inside the vendored `webgpu.cpp`'s `RegisterInstance()` — an immediate `find()` on the same id right after `try_emplace()`, printing `size()`/`bucket_count()`/the `EventManager`'s own address — to establish whether the registration itself was ever sound.
- **First result**: `RegisterInstance id=1 self_check_found=1 size=2 bucket_count=2` — registration is correct and immediately self-consistent. Moments later at the crash site: `found=0`, but with `registered_count`/`bucket_count` still reading 2 and the *same* `EventManager`/map addresses as at registration (ruled out a duplicated-singleton theory this same round, by printing `this`/`&mPerInstanceEvents` at both sites and confirming they match). A key that was correctly present, in the same map instance, at the same address, minutes/frames earlier, is now unfindable — this is not a logic bug in emdawnwebgpu or in this fork's driver code; it is memory corruption of the map's internal bucket storage sometime in between.
- (Learned the hard way, again: the *first* attempt at this exact test produced zero diagnostic output at all, because `~/.local/share/godot/export_templates/4.7.2.stable/*.zip` had stopped being symlinks into this repo's own `builds/` tree — confirmed via `git status`-style `-L` checks — so this session's `cp` into `builds/4.7.2.stable/templates/` was updating a copy nobody actually read. Fixed by `cp`-ing directly into the install path from then on, and — per the process lesson above — verifying the diagnostic strings' presence via `strings` before trusting a result either way.)
- **Isolated whether the Round 6 fix (`importJsDevice(d, $0)`) is itself the trigger**: temporarily reverted just that one line back to `importJsDevice(d)` (no `parentPtr`), keeping every other diagnostic in place, and rebuilt/retested. The *exact same* `ProcessEvents id=1 found=0 registered_count=2` failure reproduced, just later in the timeline (back at the `buffer_map()` call site rather than immediately after `wgpuCreateInstance()`). **This conclusively separates the two bugs**: the Round 6 fix is a real, independent improvement (it does correctly reconnect the imported device's events to the driver's own tracked instance, closing the `kNullInstanceId` architectural gap), but it does not cause, and is not the cure for, this heap-corruption bug — the corruption exists in the unmodified code too, just manifesting at a different point once memory layout shifts (the same phenomenon `SAFE_HEAP` demonstrated back in Round 4, and consistent with Round 3's original dlmalloc-corruption abort being the same bug's very first-observed symptom). Restored the Round 6 fix afterward (it's worth keeping regardless).
- **Where the corruption happens, narrowed but not pinpointed**: consistently somewhere between `RegisterInstance()`'s successful self-check (right after `wgpuCreateInstance()` in `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU::initialize()`) and the first `wgpuInstanceProcessEvents()` call in `buffer_map()` — a window that includes the rest of `initialize()` (the `EM_ASM_PTR` device import, `wgpuDeviceGetQueue()`, `device_info` population), canvas/surface setup, and early `RenderingDevice`/`RenderingServer` initialization up to the first buffer allocation.
- **Why this needs different tooling to go further**: `SAFE_HEAP=1` (re-tried this round, combined with these diagnostics) still did not catch anything before the crash — expected in hindsight, since `SAFE_HEAP` only catches accesses out of bounds of the *whole* heap/an object's *own* typed-array view, not a write that overflows from one legitimately-allocated buffer into an adjacent, unrelated allocation (exactly the shape of bug a buffer overrun into the `EventManager`'s heap-allocated map storage would be). Finding the actual corrupting write needs either an AddressSanitizer-instrumented build (`use_asan=yes` already exists as a proper build option in `platform/web/detect.py:196-198` — untried this round, a much heavier rebuild than anything done so far) or manual bisection of everything that runs in the narrowed window above.
- All diagnostic instrumentation from this round and Round 7 (the driver-side `print_line`s, the vendored `webgpu.cpp` patches, `SAFE_HEAP`) has been fully reverted — confirmed via `git diff`/`diff` against the pre-round backup — leaving only the Round 6 `parentPtr` fix in the working tree. **This task is not resolved.** The original reported abort is real, reproduces on both this session's headless setup and the user's real hardware, and its root cause is a genuine, currently-unlocated memory-safety bug.

**Not yet done — next concrete step**: an `-fsanitize=address` build (`scons ... use_asan=yes`) run through this exact repro (the `cameraSim_*` scratch project, extensions off) to get a precise stack trace for the actual out-of-bounds/use-after-free write, rather than continuing to narrow the window by bisection. This is a meaningfully bigger rebuild (ASan instruments every memory access, substantially slower binary and longer link time) — worth flagging to the user before spending the time, given how many rebuild cycles this task has already consumed this session.

**Round 9 (2026-09-18, same session) — ROOT CAUSE FOUND: a 4-byte heap-buffer-overflow inside the vendored/pinned `emdawnwebgpu` port's own `WGPUInstanceImpl` constructor, not in this fork's code at all**: ran the `use_asan=yes` build the user approved. Two practical obstacles first: (1) ASan's default `-sINITIAL_MEMORY` (32MB) is nowhere near enough for ASan's shadow memory — needed `initial_memory=512`; (2) Emscripten's own JS-side ASan symbolizer (`_emscripten_pc_get_function`) threw a `TypeError` trying to resolve our crash's PC, aborting the report before printing the actual stack — worked around by defining the standard compiler-rt weak hook `extern "C" const char *__asan_default_options() { return "symbolize=0"; }` (temporarily, in `rendering_context_driver_webgpu.cpp`, reverted after this round) to get raw addresses, then resolved every address offline via `llvm-symbolizer -e <wasm> -f -C <addr>` (works directly off the binary's embedded DWARF — needs a `debug_symbols=yes` build, which retains full symbol names, not just `--profiling-funcs`' minimal names).
- **Full, clean ASan report** (`heap-buffer-overflow`, `WRITE of size 36`, `0 bytes after 32-byte region`):
  - Overflow site: `__asan_memset` ← `WGPUInstanceImpl::WGPUInstanceImpl(WGPUInstanceDescriptor const*)` ← `wgpuCreateInstance` ← `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU::initialize()` ← `DisplayServerWeb::create_func(...)`.
  - Allocation site: `malloc` ← `operator_new_impl` ← `operator new(unsigned long)` ← `wgpuCreateInstance` ← `RenderingContextDriverWebGPU::initialize()` ← `DisplayServerWeb::create_func(...)`.
  - **Both frames are inside the same `wgpuCreateInstance()` call, both inside the same vendored `webgpu.cpp` translation unit.** `wgpuCreateInstance()` (`webgpu.cpp:1758-1764`) does `return new WGPUInstanceImpl(desc);` — the allocator call site computes `sizeof(WGPUInstanceImpl) == 32` bytes, but `WGPUInstanceImpl`'s own constructor's compiler-generated zero-initialization (folding together `EventSource`'s `const uint64_t mInstanceId` (8 bytes), `RefCounted`'s `std::atomic<uint64_t> mRefCount` + `bool mIsImportedFromJS` (16 bytes with alignment padding), and `WGPUInstanceImpl`'s own `WGPUInstanceCapabilities mCapabilities = {}` default-member-initializer (12 bytes: a pointer, a `WGPUBool`, and a `size_t`, all 4 bytes on wasm32)) performs a single combined `memset` of 36 bytes — 4 bytes past the actual 32-byte allocation.
- **This is not a bug in this fork's own driver code or Godot's engine code.** `drivers/webgpu/rendering_context_driver_webgpu.cpp`'s `wgpuCreateInstance(&inst_desc)` call (line ~76) is a single, correct, by-the-book call into the public Dawn/emdawnwebgpu C API — the bug is entirely inside the vendored, pinned copy of Google's `emdawnwebgpu` Emscripten port (`webgpu.cpp`, shipped as part of Emscripten 4.0.11's `--use-port=emdawnwebgpu`), specifically a struct-layout/codegen mismatch between where `WGPUInstanceImpl` gets allocated and how its own constructor zero-initializes itself, both computed by the *same* compiler invocation on the *same* file. Whether this is a genuine upstream source bug or an Emscripten/LLVM wasm32 ABI codegen defect in how base-class + default-member-initializer zeroing gets folded into one `memset` isn't nailed down further this round — not needed to act on it.
- **Fully explains everything found in Rounds 1-8**: every one of this task's crash signatures (Round 1-2's generic abort, Round 3's dlmalloc-corruption abort, Round 4's `SAFE_HEAP`-exposed `EventManager` assertion, Round 8's `RegisterInstance`-succeeds-but-`ProcessEvents`-fails self-check) is the *same* 4-byte heap overflow, landing on a different adjacent allocation and getting detected at a different, confusing later point depending on the exact build's memory layout (`SAFE_HEAP`, added diagnostics, the `parentPtr` fix, and `dlink_enabled` all independently shift layout enough to change *where* the 4 stray bytes land and whether that corrupts something load-bearing enough to crash). This also fully explains the `extensions_support` correlation this task was originally filed under: it was never architecturally about extensions/dlink at all — `dlink_enabled=yes`'s different linking arrangement (`MAIN_MODULE`/`SIDE_MODULE` split, `EXPORT_ALL=1`) just happens to usually place the next heap allocation somewhere the stray 4 bytes land harmlessly, while `dlink_enabled=no`'s different layout more often doesn't.
- **Recommended path forward** (not yet done): this doesn't need a source-level fix in this repo's own code at all — it needs either (a) the in-progress `emsdk-upgrade` effort (`webgpu_notes/emsdk-upgrade.md`) to land, on the reasonable expectation that a newer Emscripten ships a newer `emdawnwebgpu`/Dawn revision where this is already fixed upstream, checked against Dawn/Emscripten's own changelogs once that upgrade is underway; or (b) if the upgrade is not imminent, a local, permanent patch to the vendored port file, following this repo's own established precedent for vendoring third-party patches (`thirdparty/README.md`'s pattern for tracking patches to `thirdparty/tint`) — Emscripten ports support project-local overrides/patches via `--use-port=<name>:<option>=<value>` or a fully vendored copy under `thirdparty/`; whichever mechanism is chosen, it needs to survive `emsdk`/toolchain reinstalls, unlike this session's direct edits to the emsdk cache (which do not persist and are not part of this repo).
- All diagnostic instrumentation from this round (the `__asan_default_options` override) has been reverted — confirmed via `git diff` — leaving only the Round 6 `parentPtr` fix in the tree. A final non-ASan rebuild was done to leave the installed export template in a normal, verified, shippable state.

**Status**: root cause conclusively identified. This task cannot be closed by changing this repo's own source — it's tracking a genuine bug in the pinned Emscripten/emdawnwebgpu toolchain version. Recommend re-triaging as "blocked on `emsdk-upgrade`" or spinning off a dedicated local-patch task, per the recommendation above.

**Subtasks**:
1. Establish a clean, current repro
   1.1. Rebuild the `dlink_enabled=no threads=no` web template from a clean tree via `build-linux.sh` (not an incremental scons build) — Round 15/23's SCons-staleness traps (this doc, ~L2666/~L2767) and Task 10.1's note both mean a stale `.wasm`/`.side.wasm` can silently produce a misleading repro either way.
   1.2. `cp` the freshly built `bin/godot.web.template_release.wasm32.nothreads.zip` over the *installed* export template before exporting (Godot's export step reads from the installed template directory, not `bin/` directly — same trap as above) and `md5sum`-verify the exported `.wasm` matches the freshly built one before trusting any result.
   1.3. Export a minimal project (start with `webgpu_tests/test_project`, not the user's full `cameraSim_*` project) with `variant/extensions_support` **unchecked**, serve it, and load it in a real browser per `webgpu_tests/screenshot_comparison/LIVE_REPRO_METHODOLOGY.md`.
   1.4. Capture the *full* browser console, not just the final line — Emscripten prints an `abort(...)` reason and a JS stack immediately above the generic `Aborted(...)` message; that line, not the generic one, is the actual signal.
2. Narrow down where the abort originates
   2.1. Export and load the same minimal project with `variant/extensions_support` **checked** (dlink build) as a control — confirm it does *not* abort, to pin the variable to `dlink_enabled` specifically and rule out browser/project/Emscripten-version noise.
   2.2. Cross-check against `git log --oneline 07e756ac5b32fa32695d9b107b9f7729dbd576e6..HEAD -- drivers/webgpu/ platform/web/` — as of this write-up that range contains exactly one relevant commit (`9d1861713b`, adding `maxInterStageShaderVariables`/`maxInterStageShaderComponents` limit requests, unrelated to dlink). That means either (a) this is a latent bug that predates the commit named in the task title and was only just noticed, or (b) it's specific to the current branch's in-progress Emscripten version bump (`emsdk-upgrade`, `5e16f308c7`) rather than any of this fork's own driver commits. Re-run step 1 against the pre-upgrade emsdk (checked out via the `pre-emsdk-upgrade` tag, set on the prior HEAD by `5e16f308c7`) to test (b) before assuming (a).
   2.3. Inspect `platform/web/js/engine/config.js`'s `Config.prototype.getModuleConfig()` (~L327-352): `'dynamicLibraries': [\`${loadPath}.side.wasm\`].concat(this.gdextensionLibs)` unconditionally references a `.side.wasm` file even when the export has no such file (non-dlink builds never produce or ship one — see `export_plugin.cpp`'s conditional `opt_cache_files.push_back(name + ".side.wasm")`, only under `if (extensions)`). This line is unmodified upstream Godot code, so on its own it shouldn't be new — but confirm whether the *behavior* of passing a `dynamicLibraries` entry that 404s changed between the old and new Emscripten version under test in 2.2 (older versions may silently no-op when the module wasn't compiled with dynamic-linking support at all; a newer version might not).
   2.4. Check `OS_Web::has_feature()`'s `WEB_DLINK_ENABLED`-gated `"web_extensions"`/`"web_noextensions"` pair (`platform/web/os_web.cpp:161-178`) against `EditorExportPlatformWeb::get_export_features()` (`platform/web/export/export_plugin.cpp` ~L365) — confirm the feature tag baked into resource remaps at export time actually matches what the compiled non-dlink binary reports at runtime. A mismatch here would silently exclude a resource (e.g. a shader variant) that the binary then unconditionally expects, which is exactly the shape of bug that turns into a hard abort rather than a graceful `ERR_FAIL`.
   2.5. Once a candidate line is identified, get an actual native stack for the abort itself (not just the JS-side symptom) — rebuild the debug template (`target=template_debug`) for a readable abort message with source location, since `template_release` strips this.
3. Root-cause and fix
   3.1. Trace the confirmed abort site back to whichever of 2.2/2.3/2.4 (or something else entirely) it actually is.
   3.2. Apply the minimal fix — expected shapes: gate `dynamicLibraries` in `config.js` on there actually being a dlink build/extensions to load; fix a feature-tag mismatch between export-time remapping and runtime `has_feature()`; or (if 2.2's branch check confirms it) pin/patch the specific Emscripten-version behavior change.
   3.3. Rebuild only the affected template variant(s) (not the full `build-linux.sh` matrix) to verify quickly, then run the full matrix once before calling this done.
4. Verify
   4.1. Re-export and reload with extensions OFF — confirm a clean load, no abort, actual rendering (not just "no console error").
   4.2. Re-export and reload with extensions ON — confirm no regression to the working path.
   4.3. Run `./webgpu_tests/local_ci.sh --quick` to catch any incidental regression from the fix.
5. Document
   5.1. Record root cause + fix under this task, following the Status/Severity/Lines/Issue/Investigation format used elsewhere in this doc.
   5.2. If 2.2 confirms the emsdk upgrade is implicated, cross-reference/flag it against the Phase 0 baseline notes so the upgrade tracking doesn't lose this finding.

### Task 12: Ensure that webgpu threads work as expected
**Status**: `TODO` — subtask 1.1's characterization now has two concrete, real crash signatures (2026-09-18, see below), not yet acted on
**Effort**: 1 day, needs a real GPU + browser session
**Dependencies**: none

**Motivation**: this fork's documented, tested WebGPU configuration is `threads=no` (`CLAUDE.md`'s build commands; `build-linux.sh`'s threads=yes variants are explicitly called out there as "build here for completeness and untested"). Phase 2's history already flags the reason: `webgpu_notes/TASKS.md` ~L765 records "Worker thread WebGPU isolation — build with `threads=no`" as the workaround actually shipped, not a fix. This task is about actually characterizing and closing that gap rather than continuing to sidestep it.

**2026-09-18 update — two real, distinct crash signatures found (via the user's own `threads=yes` testing on the `emsdk-upgrade` branch, unrelated to that upgrade itself — `threads=no` builds throughout that branch's own testing never touched this code path)**. Both trace directly to `platform/web/detect.py`'s two different `threads=yes` configurations (lines ~298-330):

1. **`threads=yes dlink_enabled=no` ("no-extensions")**: `proxy_to_pthread` stays enabled here (only forced off by `dlink_enabled=yes` or `threads=no`), so `main()` runs proxied onto a pthread worker per this fork's existing `-sPROXY_TO_PTHREAD=1` setup. Console shows a hard `Aborted(Assertion failed)` inside emdawnwebgpu's JS bridge:
   ```
   at assert (tmp_js_export.js:1:4581)
   at Object.getJsObject (tmp_js_export.js:1:109840)
   at Object.makePipelineLayout (tmp_js_export.js:1:115818)
   at Object.makeComputePipelineDesc (tmp_js_export.js:1:116395)
   at _wgpuDeviceCreateComputePipeline (...)
   ```
   `getJsObject` is emdawnwebgpu's JS-side handle→object lookup table; this fires while building a compute pipeline's layout, i.e. resolving a bind-group-layout handle to its real `GPUBindGroupLayout` JS object fails. This is exactly subtask 1.2/1.3's predicted mechanism: the device (`Module["preinitializedWebGPUDevice"]`) is created/imported on the main thread, but `wgpuDeviceCreateComputePipeline` is being called from the proxied pthread worker, whose JS-side object table doesn't have (or can't see) the main thread's handle registrations. Repeats continuously (once per pipeline creation attempt), one `Pthread 0x... sent an error!` line per occurrence — this is a hard abort, not a warning; rendering does not proceed.

2. **`threads=yes dlink_enabled=yes` ("with-extensions")**: `proxy_to_pthread` is forced *off* here (GDExtension support requires it, per `detect.py:312-314`), so this is a different code path entirely — a **separate bug**, not the same one under different conditions. Two distinct JS errors, both from worker threads:
   ```
   TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'buffer')
       at growMemViews (tmp_js_export.js:1:8540)
   ```
   and
   ```
   TypeError: Cannot set properties of undefined (setting '15818196')
       at addEmAsm (tmp_js_export.js:1:37776)
       at postInstantiation (tmp_js_export.js:1:37970)
       at ... loadModule (tmp_js_export.js:1:39187)
   ```
   `addEmAsm`/`postInstantiation`/`loadModule` is Emscripten's `SIDE_MODULE=2` dynamic-library loader path (this fork's `dlink_enabled=yes` mechanism, per `CLAUDE.md`'s architecture note); `growMemViews` is the wasm-memory-view-refresh helper that runs after `memory.grow()`. Both errors are consistent with the side module (containing this driver's own compiled code) being loaded into a newly-spawned pthread worker before that worker's own EM_ASM registration table / memory views are fully initialized — a load-order/initialization race specific to combining `SIDE_MODULE` dynamic linking with the pthread worker pool, distinct from bug #1's cross-thread WebGPU-handle-visibility issue. Ends in "still waiting on run dependencies: loading-workers" repeating forever — the module never finishes loading, not just one failed draw call.

**Not yet done**: root-causing either bug further (this update is subtask 1.1's characterization step, with real logs in hand instead of a prediction — subtasks 1.2-1.4 and everything in step 2 onward remain open), deciding whether both are worth fixing or whether `threads=no` should simply stay the long-term documented/supported configuration (per subtask 2's own framing, "a documented reduced-but-correct mode" is an acceptable outcome, not just a stopgap). Given bug #2 prevents the module from loading at all, it's the more severe of the two if `dlink_enabled=yes threads=yes` is ever meant to be a supported combination.

**Subtasks**:
1. Characterize why `threads=yes` breaks WebGPU today
   1.1. Rebuild `scons platform=web target=template_debug dlink_enabled=no webgpu=yes opengl3=no threads=yes` and export/load a minimal scene, capturing full console output (per `LIVE_REPRO_METHODOLOGY.md`).
   1.2. Re-derive what "Worker thread WebGPU isolation" (the Phase 2 note) actually meant — check whether `-sPROXY_TO_PTHREAD` (forced off today per `detect.py`'s dlink/proxy_to_pthread interaction) moves `main()` off the browser's main JS thread; the device handle from `Module["preinitializedWebGPUDevice"]` (set by the JS shell on the main thread per `CLAUDE.md`'s Device Init note) and `WebGPU.importJsDevice()` may simply not be reachable from a pthread worker without an explicit proxy.
   1.3. Check whether Dawn/emdawnwebgpu's JS bindings support any cross-thread `GPUDevice` handle transfer at all in the Emscripten version this fork targets, and if so what API that requires (e.g. proxying every WebGPU call back to the main thread via `emscripten_sync_run_in_main_runtime_thread` or similar), versus if it's fundamentally unsupported upstream.
   1.4. Identify every other thread-affecting assumption Godot's threaded rendering path makes that WebGPU's browser-side constraints don't satisfy (e.g. `RenderingServer`'s separate render thread mode, any `WorkerThreadPool` usage inside `drivers/webgpu/` itself — grep for thread-affinity assumptions there first).
2. Decide the actual target behavior
   2.1. Determine what "work as expected" should mean concretely for this driver: full multi-threaded rendering thread parity with Vulkan/Metal, or a documented reduced-but-correct mode (e.g. render thread must stay proxied to the main thread while everything else — physics, scripting — still threads normally).
   2.2. If full parity isn't realistically achievable given WebGPU/browser constraints, write that decision down explicitly as this task's outcome rather than leaving it open-ended.
3. Implement
   3.1. Add whatever proxying/shimming step 1 identifies as missing (main-thread-only WebGPU call dispatch, thread-safe handle passing, etc.) in `drivers/webgpu/` and/or `platform/web/js/engine/`.
   3.2. Ensure `detect.py`'s existing `threads` + `dlink_enabled` + `proxy_to_pthread` interaction (`platform/web/detect.py:290-305,311-314`) still holds, or update it if the fix changes what combination is actually supported.
4. Verify
   4.1. Run the existing single-threaded regression suites (`shader_corpus`, `driver_unit_tests`, `preprocessing_tests`) against a `threads=yes` build to confirm no shader-pipeline regression from any WGSL/SPIR-V-path changes.
   4.2. Live-test a real scene (start with an in-repo `benchmark_*` scene, then the user's real test project per `webgpu_tests/README.md`) with `threads=yes`, watching specifically for the symptom the Phase 2 note originally worked around.
   4.3. Confirm `threads=no` still builds and behaves identically to before (this task must not regress the documented, working configuration).
5. Document
   5.1. Update the `CLAUDE.md` build-commands note and this doc's Phase 2 "Worker thread WebGPU isolation" line to reflect the new state (fixed, partially fixed with a documented limitation, or confirmed genuinely unsupported and why).

### Task 13: Ensure that webgpu exports are fully precompiling shaders on export
**Status**: `TODO`
**Effort**: 1 day, needs a real GPU + browser session
**Dependencies**: none

**Motivation**: `drivers/webgpu/wgsl_precompile.py`'s own module docstring scopes it precisely: it precompiles "each shader class" — i.e. the engine's own built-in ubershaders (materials, post-fx, etc.) — at **engine build time** (`scons ... webgpu=yes`), before any specific project exists. It has no project awareness at all. A project's own custom shaders (visual shaders, `.gdshader` files, any material using a shader the engine doesn't ship) are only ever compiled to SPIR-V at the project's own build/import time and hit `_spv_to_wgsl_cached()`'s runtime Tint fallback (`rendering_shader_container_webgpu.cpp`, per `CLAUDE.md`'s shader-pipeline description) the first time they're actually used in the browser — a runtime hitch, and the literal gap this task's title refers to ("fully precompiling ... on export", as opposed to "on engine build"). No existing hook in `platform/web/export/export_plugin.cpp` runs any shader-related step at export time today (checked: no `shader_cache`/`precompile`/pipeline-bake call exists there).

**Subtasks**:
1. Confirm and quantify the gap
   1.1. Export a project with at least one custom `.gdshader`/visual-shader material not present in `wgsl_precompile.py`'s enumerated engine shader classes, load it in a real browser, and confirm via console/timing (or `TINT_DEBUG_DUMP_PREPROCESSED`) that it does take the runtime Tint-conversion fallback path rather than a precompiled hit.
   1.2. Measure the actual cost of one such runtime conversion (single-shader timing, not batch) to establish whether this is a genuine user-visible hitch (frame drop / stutter on first use) or just a theoretical gap — this determines how urgent the rest of the task is.
2. Design the export-time precompile step
   2.1. Determine what's needed to enumerate a project's actual shader set at export time: Godot's resource dependency graph for the exported scenes/materials, or a full-project shader scan — check how other export platforms (if any) already do a comparable "bake everything reachable" pass to avoid re-deriving this.
   2.2. Decide where the precompiled output should live and how it's looked up at runtime — most likely extending `_spv_to_wgsl_cached()`'s existing hash→WGSL lookup (currently backed solely by the build-time-generated `wgsl_precompiled.gen.h`) to also check a second, PCK-embedded table populated at export time, keyed the same way (MurmurHash3 of the SPIR-V, per `wgsl_precompile.py`'s existing algorithm — reuse it rather than inventing a second hash scheme).
   2.3. Decide whether this runs as a new `EditorExportPlatformWeb` export step (in-process, using the editor's own SPIR-V compilation + the same `tint_wrapper.cpp` path used at runtime) or as an external tool invocation similar to `tint_convert_cli`'s batch mode — the former avoids spawning a process per shader but must not block the editor UI; the latter reuses `tint_convert_cli` as-is but needs a way to feed it project-specific SPIR-V.
3. Implement
   3.1. Add the enumeration + compilation step, wired into `EditorExportPlatformWeb::export_project()` (`platform/web/export/export_plugin.cpp:504`) gated behind `webgpu`-relevant export settings only (don't run this for non-WebGPU web exports).
   3.2. Extend `rendering_shader_container_webgpu.{h,cpp}`'s lookup path to consult the new export-time table before falling back to runtime Tint, mirroring the existing build-time-table lookup order.
   3.3. Make sure `tint_convert_cli`'s batch-mode crash isolation (per-shader forked child, stdout/stderr to `/dev/null` — see `CLAUDE.md`'s debugging note) is reused rather than re-implemented, so a single bad project shader can't abort the whole export.
4. Verify
   4.1. Re-export the project from step 1 and confirm the previously-fallback shader now hits the precompiled path (same diagnostic method as 1.1, expecting the opposite result).
   4.2. Run the full existing shader test tiers (`shader_corpus`, `preprocessing_tests`, `driver_unit_tests`) to confirm no regression to the engine-shader precompile path this shares code with.
   4.3. Live-test a project with a nontrivial number of custom shaders/materials, confirming export time grows sanely (not per-shader-process-spawn-dominated) and load-time hitches are gone.
5. Document
   5.1. Add this new export step to `CLAUDE.md`'s shader-pipeline description and `drivers/webgpu/README.md`, since it changes the pipeline diagram from "GLSL → SPIR-V → 12 passes → WGSL (build-time or runtime)" to a three-tier lookup (build-time engine table → export-time project table → runtime fallback).

### Task 14: Attempt to make the loading of webgpu exports less blocking, and the progress bar more representative of how long is left to load
**Status**: `TODO`
**Effort**: 1 day, needs a real GPU + browser session
**Dependencies**: benefits from Task 13 being done first — an unclosed runtime-shader-fallback gap is itself a source of post-"100%" blocking this task would otherwise misattribute elsewhere

**Motivation**: `platform/web/js/engine/preloader.js`'s `animateProgress()` (~L63-97) computes progress purely from bytes downloaded vs. total content-length across tracked fetches (`load_status.loaded`/`.total`, `preloadedFiles`). It has no visibility into, and the progress bar reaches 100% before: WASM compile/instantiate (can be seconds for a large release `.wasm`, especially if the browser can't stream-compile it), the async `GPUAdapter`/`GPUDevice` request cycle (device is pre-initialized by the JS shell per `CLAUDE.md`'s Device Init note, but this still has to complete before the engine can proceed), the precompiled-WGSL table's own memory setup, and (until Task 13 closes it) any runtime Tint shader fallback conversions. Any of these can produce a visible stall after the bar already reads 100%.

**Subtasks**:
1. Instrument and quantify each post-download phase
   1.1. Add timing instrumentation (temporary, or gated behind a debug flag) around: WASM fetch-complete → instantiate-complete, device-request start → resolve, and engine `main()` start → first rendered frame, for a real project export.
   1.2. Run this against both a small and a large (`cameraSim_*`-scale, per the user's real test project) export to see which phases scale with project size vs. stay roughly constant — this determines which phases are worth surfacing in the progress bar at all versus just genuinely fixed overhead.
   1.3. Identify the single largest contributor with the user's real project (this task's premise — "less blocking" — implies there's a known-bad case; confirm what it actually is before designing around a guess).
2. Reduce actual blocking time, not just report it better
   2.1. For WASM instantiate: confirm `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming` is actually taken (`config.js:339-352`'s `instantiateWasm` override already prefers it when available) and isn't silently falling back to the non-streaming `arrayBuffer()` path due to a missing/incorrect MIME type or response headers from whatever's serving the export.
   2.2. For device request: check whether the JS shell's device pre-initialization (`Module["preinitializedWebGPUDevice"]`) is actually kicked off as early as possible (in parallel with the WASM fetch/instantiate), not serialized after it.
   2.3. If Task 13 isn't done yet, treat any runtime shader-fallback stalls it would produce as out of scope here but flag them explicitly rather than silently working around them in the progress UI.
3. Make the progress bar representative of what's left
   3.1. Extend `preloader.js`'s progress model to include estimated weight for the post-download phases identified in step 1 (e.g. reserve a fixed tail percentage for "starting engine" once download hits 100%, rather than jumping straight to a blank/frozen bar).
   3.2. Wire real phase-completion signals (WASM instantiated, device acquired, first frame rendered) into that tail-progress update via `Config.prototype.onProgress` (`config.js:197-217`) rather than a fixed-timer guess, so the bar reflects actual milestones, not an animation.
   3.3. Handle the case where a phase takes far longer than expected (e.g. cold shader-compile fallback) gracefully — a stalled-looking-but-not-frozen indicator (e.g. a secondary "initializing renderer..." label) beats a bar that either freezes at 99% or snaps to 100% then hangs.
4. Verify
   4.1. Re-run the timing instrumentation from step 1 against the same small/large exports, confirm measurable reduction in post-100% blocking time where step 2's fixes applied.
   4.2. Visually confirm (screenshot/video capture per `LIVE_REPRO_METHODOLOGY.md`) the progress bar's behavior across a slow network throttle (to stress the download-progress phase) and a cold cache (to stress the post-download phases) — both should look reasonable, not just the common case.
5. Document
   5.1. Record before/after timing numbers for the user's real project under this task, following this doc's established Verified-with-numbers convention.

### Task 15: `particles.glsl:default:comp` Tint ICE — REGRESSION from the `emsdk-upgrade` Tint resync (Dawn `db49a549...` → `b975919d...`), root-caused and fixed with a 10th vendored Tint patch `[DONE]`
**Status**: `DONE — fixed with vendored Tint patch 0010, live-verified via wgsl_precompile.py, 0 tint failures`
**Severity**: HIGH — this shader permutation compiled fine at build time (falls through to the runtime Tint fallback per the shader pipeline's own design) but would have ICE'd at runtime the first time it was actually needed, i.e. real particle systems would crash under WebGPU with the upgraded toolchain.
**Found by**: the `emsdk-upgrade` branch's Phase 3 Tint resync (`webgpu_notes/emsdk-upgrade.md`) regression sweep — forcing a full `wgsl_precompile.py` regen against real engine shaders after the resync, not caught by the 13-shader `shader_corpus` fixture set or `preprocessing_tests` (both still pass 100% clean).

**Symptom**: `[WGSL Precompile] Results: 195 compiled, 3 glsl failures, 1 tint failures` — the 3 GLSL failures are the pre-existing, already-baselined `expected_failures.json` entries (`TonemapMobileShaderRD:2/3.frag.spv`, `VrsShaderRD:3.vert.spv`, Vulkan-only variants); the 1 Tint failure was new, on `servers/rendering/renderer_rd/shaders/particles.glsl:default:comp`.

**Root cause (fully diagnosed)**: added temporary `fprintf` instrumentation to `atomics.cc`'s `ConvertUsagesToAtomic()` and its `Access` case (removed again once diagnosed) and ran `tint_convert_cli` directly (single-file, un-forked, per `CLAUDE.md`'s debugging note) on the dumped SPIR-V (`WGSL_DEBUG_DUMP=particles`). The trace showed the actual ICE (`TINT_ASSERT(ld->From()->Type()->UnwrapPtr()->Is<core::type::Atomic>())`) firing on a completely unrelated `ptr<storage, mat4x4<f32>, read_write>` value — nothing to do with the shader's one real atomic (`atomicAdd(src_particles.particle_count, ...)`, member 0 of the `SourceEmission` struct).

The real bug: `atomics.cc`'s `ConvertUsagesToAtomic()` re-walks **every** access chain rooted at the same base pointer as an atomic member — not just the atomic member's own chain — because converting one struct member to atomic requires "forking" the whole struct type, and every sibling access into that struct needs its type re-resolved against the forked struct. That re-resolution goes through `TypeForAccess()`, whose index-walking `tint::Switch` only had cases for `Struct` and `Array` — **no case for `Matrix` or `Vector`**, and no `TINT_ICE_ON_NO_MATCH` fallback to catch the gap loudly. `SourceEmission`'s member 4 (`data`, an array of `ParticleEmission` structs) has, at `ParticleEmission`'s member 0, a `mat4x4<f32> transform` — and the shader accesses it one **column** at a time (a 4th access-chain index past the struct member, e.g. indices `[4, <dyn>, 0, 1]` for column 1). Hitting `Matrix` with a remaining index, `TypeForAccess`'s switch silently no-op'd (leaving `cur_ty` stuck at `mat4x4<f32>` instead of drilling into `ColumnType()` = `vec4<f32>`), so the access's "new" computed type (`mat4x4<f32>`) differed from its real original type (`vec4<f32>`) — which `ConvertUsagesToAtomic` reads as "this value changed, needs further atomic-usage fixup," incorrectly queuing an ordinary matrix-column `Load` for atomic conversion and tripping the assert.

This is a genuine, narrow upstream Tint bug — reachable by *any* SPIR-V module where a struct sharing a base pointer with an atomic member also has a sibling access chain indexing into a matrix or vector field, not anything glslang- or Godot-specific.

**Fix**: added a 10th vendored Tint patch, `0010-atomics-type-for-access-matrix-vector.patch`, adding `Matrix`/`Vector` cases to `TypeForAccess()`'s switch (mirroring the exact pattern already used elsewhere in Tint for the same kind of access-chain type-walking, e.g. `core/ir/transform/decompose_access.cc`'s `Matrix`/`Vector`/`Array`/`Struct` switch) plus a `TINT_ICE_ON_NO_MATCH` fallback so any future unhandled type here fails loudly instead of silently miscomputing a type again. Documented in `thirdparty/README.md`'s `## tint` patch list (now 10 patches).

**Verified**:
- `tint_convert_cli` on the dumped SPIR-V now converts cleanly to valid WGSL — confirmed `particle_count : atomic<i32>` and `atomicAdd(&(src_particles.particle_count), -1i)` in the output, with the sibling `transform` matrix untouched.
- `webgpu_tests/shader_corpus`: 13/13 still pass.
- `webgpu_tests/preprocessing_tests`: 192/192 (+1 expected skip) still pass.
- Full `wgsl_precompile.py` real-engine-shader sweep: `[WGSL Precompile] Results: 196 compiled, 3 glsl failures, 0 tint failures` — the `particles.glsl` failure is gone, the 3 GLSL failures are unchanged from the pre-existing baseline (no new drift).
- Full `scons platform=web target=template_release dlink_enabled=yes webgpu=yes opengl3=no threads=no` build with the fix in place — see `webgpu_notes/emsdk-upgrade.md` Phase 3 for the build log outcome.

**Not yet done**: live-verify the actual runtime `atomicAdd` behavior with a real particle-system scene under `WEBGPU_REAL_GPU=1` per `LIVE_REPRO_METHODOLOGY.md` — static precompile passing confirms Tint no longer ICEs and produces the expected WGSL shape, but hasn't been confirmed against a real running particle system yet. This is `emsdk-upgrade.md` Phase 6's job (the broader real-GPU regression sweep), not re-done here in isolation.
